**v3.2.6 Supersession and Consolidation Rules:**

1. v3.2.4 supersedes v3.2.3 as canonical. v3.2.3 is rejected as a complete master plan because it compressed the document too aggressively.
2. v3.2.4 uses v3.2.2 as the full canonical baseline and preserves its detailed operational architecture.
3. v3.2.4 globally clarifies the distinction between West Peek OS, OS domains, machines/departments, AI employees, queues, workflows/loops, records, source systems, adapters, ledgers, governance, approvals, activity, audit, diagnostics, and source-of-truth systems.
4. v3.2.4 treats Relationship OS and Community OS as examples of the global taxonomy, not special one-off clarifications.
5. v3.2.4 treats machines as department-like executable workflow engines inside OS domains.
6. v3.2.4 treats source systems as external or internal record owners that feed West Peek OS through adapters; source systems are not automatically OS domains or machines.
7. Network OS is an external synced source database/repo for relationship, people, company, community, touchpoint, and connection records. It syncs to West Peek OS. It is not replaced by Relationship OS or Community OS.
8. West Peek OS is the canonical firm operating platform and repo. It contains Relationship OS, Community OS, Investment OS, Portfolio OS, Fundraising OS, Event OS, Finance OS, Legal/Compliance OS, Knowledge OS, Builder OS, and other institutional domains.
9. Relationship OS and Community OS are domains inside West Peek OS that operate on synced Network OS data. They do not create separate competing databases.
10. VentureDeals / Deal Math remains a first-class source-system/product integration and machine input to the Investment Math + Decision Spine.
11. Implementation-specific repo build details belong primarily in the separate Odysseus Audit and Implementation Plan. This document retains only canonical operating architecture, product requirements, domain requirements, machine requirements, governance requirements, workflows, acceptance criteria, and high-level phasing needed to preserve the system design.
12. Prior material may not disappear merely because it is repetitive, old, or long. It must be integrated, explicitly superseded, consolidated into a stronger section, or deliberately retired with a stated reason.
13. AI employees are governed senior operators, not passive prompt responders. They must maintain domain-specific opportunity radar and anticipate credible opportunities, risks, savings, revenue lanes, and leverage points.
14. AI-surfaced opportunities enter an Opportunity Review Queue first. They are not treated the same as normal workflow approvals until a human approves pursuit.
15. Regular workflow approvals remain separate from opportunity approvals. A requested action, outbound draft, spend approval, event invite, or routine workflow approval should not be mixed with a surfaced opportunity that still needs strategic go/no-go review.
16. West Peek Rooms is canonized as a pilotable sponsored-community revenue lane and case study for Opportunity Intelligence, primarily through Event OS, Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue, Community OS, West Peek Live, Finance OS, Legal/Compliance OS, and West Peek Productions / media-community entity review.
17. AI cost visibility is a canonical operating requirement. The system must make AI spend visible by day, week, month, employee, machine, domain, model/provider, workflow, recurring loop, and external tool/vendor.
18. The AI workforce must operate under a Budget Governor with firmwide and domain-level cost modes: Normal Mode, Cheapo Mode, Critical-Only Mode, and Strategic Surge Mode.
19. Cost governance must not make the system unsafe. Cheapo Mode and Critical-Only Mode must preserve compliance, LP, IC, deal, external-commitment, revenue-critical, and risk-sensitive work.
20. Every meaningful AI run must pass through cost preflight, mode check, budget check, least-expensive-adequate model/tool selection, actual cost recording, and value/outcome logging.
21. Opportunity Review and Workflow Approval remain separate. Both queues must show estimated and actual cost, but a surfaced opportunity still cannot become execution without pursuit approval.
22. Cloudflare-first deployment, VPS/container fallback strategy, and runtime portability laws belong in the Odysseus Audit and Implementation Plan, not in this canonical master plan.
23. West Peek OS owns the institutional brain. External agent platforms may be execution adapters, but they may not become the source of truth, approval authority, canonical memory, compliance authority, investment decision authority, relationship intelligence authority, or cost governor.
24. Owned AI employees may use model APIs, firm-owned tools, computer-use sandboxes, browser automation, and vendor agent runtimes only through West Peek OS governance, approval, cost, lifecycle, security, and audit controls.
25. External agent runs must be scoped, sandboxed, least-privilege, cost-visible, approval-governed, revocable, and terminated when the task or authorization ends.

26. West Peek OS may evaluate external platforms only through a Vendor Adapter Bench. No outside tool, hosted agent, workflow platform, browser provider, research API, orchestration framework, or observability platform becomes canonical by default.
27. Every outside tool must be classified before use as one of: owned runtime candidate, model provider, browser execution adapter, web research adapter, workflow plumbing adapter, authenticated tool-access adapter, durable job engine, observability/evaluation layer, external hosted-agent runtime, or blocked/not approved.
28. Every vendor/tool candidate must pass purpose fit, data sensitivity review, cost review, permission boundary review, auditability review, portability review, failure-mode review, human-approval compatibility review, and kill-switch compatibility review before meaningful production use.
29. Agent observability and evaluation are canonical operating requirements. West Peek OS must track not only AI cost, but also run traces, prompts or prompt versions where appropriate, model/tool paths, agent decisions, quality signals, human feedback, latency, errors, reversals, hallucination incidents, approval outcomes, and improvement trends.
30. Tool integration must not create shadow automation. Any external workflow, browser session, research call, MCP server, no-code automation, hosted agent, or durable job that touches sensitive firm work must be registered, permissioned, cost-visible, auditable, and revocable inside West Peek OS.
31. Browser execution, web research, workflow plumbing, authenticated tool access, durable jobs, and observability tools are execution/support infrastructure. They may extend West Peek AI employees, but they may not become the firm brain, source of truth, approval authority, memory authority, compliance authority, investment authority, relationship intelligence authority, cost governor, lifecycle authority, or canonical machine registry.
32. The default architectural posture remains: West Peek owns judgment, memory, approvals, governance, cost control, lifecycle discipline, and source-of-truth authority; vendors may provide bounded execution power.

# 23H. Vendor Adapter Bench, Tool Introduction Governance, and Agent Observability Law

## 23H.1 Core Law

West Peek OS may evaluate and use external platforms for browser execution, web research, workflow automation, app integrations, hosted agents, durable jobs, model access, observability, evaluations, and developer acceleration.

No vendor becomes canonical by default.

Every outside tool must be treated as an adapter until it earns a governed role through explicit review, permissioning, cost visibility, audit capture, and revocation controls.

The standing rule is:

```text
West Peek owns judgment, memory, approvals, governance, cost control, lifecycle discipline, and source-of-truth authority.
Vendors may provide bounded execution power.
```

## 23H.2 Vendor Adapter Classification

Before any external platform is introduced, West Peek OS must classify it as exactly one or more of the following:

1. Owned runtime candidate.
2. Model provider.
3. Browser execution adapter.
4. Web research adapter.
5. Workflow plumbing adapter.
6. Authenticated tool-access adapter.
7. Durable job engine.
8. Observability / evaluation layer.
9. External hosted-agent runtime.
10. Developer/coding acceleration adapter.
11. Blocked / not approved.

A vendor may occupy more than one category, but the categories must be explicit. The system may not use vague labels such as "AI platform" when the real role is browser automation, workflow plumbing, authenticated tool access, or observability.

## 23H.3 Tool Introduction Review

Every vendor/tool candidate must pass a Tool Introduction Review before meaningful production use.

Required review fields:

- purpose fit;
- operating category;
- owner;
- expected users;
- supported machines/domains;
- data sensitivity level;
- credential boundary;
- cost class;
- expected monthly cost range;
- approval threshold;
- auditability;
- portability / exit path;
- vendor lock-in risk;
- terms-of-service or compliance concerns;
- failure modes;
- human approval compatibility;
- kill-switch compatibility;
- lifecycle-control compatibility;
- source-of-truth boundary;
- memory boundary;
- allowed actions;
- prohibited actions.

If those fields are unknown, the tool remains in sandbox evaluation and may not receive sensitive firm data or production authority.

## 23H.4 Agent Observability and Evaluation Requirement

Cost visibility alone is not enough. West Peek OS must be able to see what AI employees and vendor adapters did, why they did it, how much they cost, what they touched, whether they were useful, and whether quality is improving.

The system must maintain or integrate an Agent Observability and Evaluation layer that can track:

- run traces;
- model/provider used;
- tool calls;
- prompt version or instruction set where appropriate;
- agent / machine / domain;
- workflow and loop association;
- inputs and outputs;
- evidence sources;
- approval state;
- human feedback;
- quality score;
- hallucination / factuality incident;
- policy incident;
- latency;
- cost;
- retry/failure events;
- corrective action;
- lifecycle consequence if repeated failures occur.

Observability must connect to the AI Employee Performance + Cost Ledger Machine, the Budget Governor, the Approval Center, the Opportunity Review Queue, the Workflow Approval Queue, the Loop Governor, and the AI Employee Lifecycle Control Panel.

## 23H.5 MCP and Tool Registry Governance

MCP or equivalent tool protocols may be used to connect AI employees to tools, files, databases, browsers, apps, search, workflows, and vendor services.

However, tool protocols do not override West Peek governance.

West Peek OS must maintain a Tool Registry for any connected tool, MCP server, browser capability, vendor action, API integration, workflow, or hosted-agent handoff.

The Tool Registry must record:

- tool name;
- provider;
- category;
- owning machine/domain;
- allowed AI employees;
- allowed scopes;
- prohibited scopes;
- credential source;
- approval requirement;
- cost class;
- logging requirement;
- data boundary;
- output quarantine requirement;
- kill switch;
- review date.

Random tools may not be connected directly to AI employees without registry approval.

## 23H.6 Shadow Automation Ban

No automation may operate in the dark.

Any workflow, browser session, research API call, external hosted-agent run, no-code automation, scheduled job, webhook, MCP server, or authenticated app action that touches West Peek work must be visible inside West Peek OS.

The following are forbidden without explicit registration and approval:

- unsupervised external outreach;
- unlogged sponsor or LP communication;
- unregistered browser automation using firm credentials;
- hidden recurring research loops;
- untracked paid search or scrape jobs;
- ungoverned Gmail/Calendar/Drive workflows;
- vendor agents with direct source-of-truth write access;
- vendor agents storing canonical memory outside West Peek OS;
- browser agents using unrestricted credentials;
- tools that bypass Opportunity Review or Workflow Approval.

## 23H.7 Adapter Permission Model

Every external adapter must operate under least privilege.

Default permissions:

```text
read public data > draft internal output > stage action for approval > execute only after approval
```

External adapters may not begin with broad write access, external-send authority, repository push authority, financial authority, or source-of-truth mutation rights.

Sensitive actions require explicit human approval and audit capture.

Sensitive actions include:

- sending email;
- publishing content;
- contacting LPs, sponsors, founders, investors, vendors, or portfolio companies;
- changing source-of-truth records;
- modifying fund materials;
- accessing LP or investor data;
- using paid data providers;
- submitting forms;
- making payments;
- changing account settings;
- modifying production code;
- committing or pushing to repositories;
- running recurring paid loops;
- using credentials outside an approved session boundary.

## 23H.8 Vendor Output Quarantine

External adapter output is not truth.

Browser outputs, research outputs, scraped data, workflow results, hosted-agent summaries, no-code automation outputs, and MCP tool outputs must enter West Peek OS as untrusted evidence until verified, cited, reconciled, approved, or promoted.

Promotion requirements:

- source capture;
- evidence trail;
- CoVE or verification where required;
- owner review if high stakes;
- memory-promotion approval for canonical memory;
- source-of-truth resolver review when data conflicts.

## 23H.9 Preferred Posture

The operating bias is:

```text
Use fewer tools well.
Prefer the platform Sequoia already understands.
Prefer governed adapters over shadow automations.
Prefer Cloudflare-first owned runtime evaluation in the implementation plan.
Use vendor platforms only when they reduce time, cost, or complexity without weakening governance.
```

Canonical does not choose a specific vendor as the final runtime. Vendor-specific choices, including Cloudflare Agents, Browserbase, n8n, Composio, Pipedream, Exa, Tavily, Firecrawl, Langfuse, LangSmith, Mastra, LangGraph, CrewAI, MyClaw, Twin, OpenClaw, or future equivalents, belong in the Odysseus Audit and Implementation Plan.

# 0A. Canonical Integrity and Non-Compression Mandate

v3.2.4 exists because the canonical plan must be both clear and fully fleshed out. The system cannot be built from a vague summary, and it cannot be trusted if rich operating material is collapsed into short labels.

This version preserves the depth of the v3.2.2 full master plan while correcting the architecture vocabulary that caused confusion around OS domains, machines, source systems, and AI employee assignments.

## 0A.1 Full-content preservation law

```text
A requirement is not preserved because a keyword appears.
A requirement is preserved only when its operating meaning survives.
```

For every material capability, the document should make clear:

- what owns it;
- what domain it belongs to;
- which machine executes it;
- which AI employees operate it;
- what data it reads;
- what data it writes;
- which source system or system of record it depends on;
- which approval gate controls it;
- which audit or activity event proves it happened;
- which diagnostics trace can explain failure;
- how it appears to Sequoia and Scooter without creating cognitive overload.

## 0A.2 Hostile-review integration law

User direction is strategic input, not automatic canon. West Peek OS planning must not copy operator language blindly. Every proposed correction, product idea, workflow, machine, role, or rule must be pressure-tested before becoming canonical.

The system must improve raw input by checking:

- whether the idea creates duplicate sources of truth;
- whether it belongs in West Peek OS, an external repo/product, an adapter, or a manual process;
- whether it is a domain, machine, workflow, dashboard, data object, AI employee duty, policy, or vendor dependency;
- whether it increases legal, privacy, compliance, or operational risk;
- whether it should be built, integrated, bought, mirrored, or deferred;
- whether a simpler architecture preserves the intent better.

```text
Operator input is direction.
Canonical architecture is the hostile-reviewed, clarified, safer, more executable version of that direction.
```

## 0A.3 No-buried-critical-logic law

Critical operating laws must not live only in late reference blocks. The following must be front-loaded as canonical architecture:

- architecture ontology and operating taxonomy;
- OS/domain vs machine/departments distinction;
- source-system vs domain vs machine distinction;
- machine registry;
- investment math spine;
- loop runtime;
- AI employee runtime contract;
- source-of-truth law;
- approval law;
- privacy/model mode law;
- Network OS external database law;
- relationship and connection intelligence law;
- developer diagnostics and audit law.

## 0A.4 v3.2.3 rejection rule

v3.2.3 is not the canonical master plan. It is rejected for canonical use because it reduced a fully fleshed master plan into a much shorter architecture note. The useful taxonomy from v3.2.3 is preserved in v3.2.4, but the reduced artifact itself is not a valid replacement for the complete canonical plan.

## 0A.5 v3.2.5 Opportunity Intelligence correction

v3.2.5 adds a missing performance expectation to the canonical architecture: AI employees should not merely wait for perfect prompts. They should operate like senior domain specialists who notice credible opportunities, prepare decision-ready internal material, and distinguish opportunity review from ordinary approval work.

This correction does not authorize autonomous business action. It authorizes governed thinking, opportunity surfacing, and decision preparation.

```text
AI employees may surface opportunities.
AI employees may prepare internal materials.
AI employees may draft external materials for review.
AI employees may recommend tests and kill criteria.
AI employees may not contact sponsors, spend money, commit the firm, promise access, publish materials, or blur fund/community/production boundaries without approval.
```


## 0A.6 v3.2.6 Cost Visibility and Budget Governor correction

v3.2.6 adds a second missing performance expectation to the canonical architecture: AI employees must not become invisible spend centers.

The AI workforce must be useful, senior, anticipatory, evidence-aware, and cost-aware. Safety alone is not sufficient. Commercial usefulness alone is not sufficient. The system must combine usefulness, evidence discipline, spend discipline, and approval governance.

Canonical correction:

```text
AI employees are governed senior operators, not passive prompt responders and not uncontrolled autonomous spenders.
```

The approved operating standard is:

```text
safe, useful, senior, evidence-aware, cost-aware, visible, mode-governed, and approval-controlled
```

This correction adds the following required operating capabilities:

- AI Workforce Cost Dashboard;
- Cost Command Center;
- Budget Governor;
- firmwide cost mode controls;
- domain-level cost overrides;
- Cost Class taxonomy;
- Cost Preflight;
- Actual Cost Ledger;
- Value / Output Ledger;
- spend alerts;
- recurring loop budget caps;
- Strategic Surge budgets;
- Cheapo Mode and Critical-Only Mode guardrails;
- separation of Opportunity Review cost visibility from Workflow Approval cost visibility.

This correction does not add any deployment-provider rule to canonical. Hosting and runtime strategy belong in the Odysseus Audit and Implementation Plan.

---


## 0A.7 v3.2.7 AI Employee Lifecycle Control correction

v3.2.7 adds a missing governance requirement: AI employees must have a real lifecycle and discipline system.

The system may not treat AI employees as immortal helper characters. Each AI employee is a revocable operating role with status, permissions, performance accountability, audit history, reassignment rules, and human-governed authority.

The correction adds:

- AI Employee Lifecycle, Discipline, and Decommissioning Law;
- AI Employee Lifecycle Control Panel;
- status states: Active, Restricted, Suspended, PIP, Retraining Required, Probation, Retired, Permanently Fired / Decommissioned;
- suspend/restrict/PIP/retrain/retire/fire controls;
- performance incident records;
- permission revocation records;
- role reassignment requirements;
- lifecycle audit events;
- MP/admin authority rules;
- no-orphan-responsibility rule;
- no-silent-reactivation rule.

Canonical law:

```text
AI employees are revocable operating roles, not permanent autonomous entities.
```

# 0B. Architecture Ontology / Operating Taxonomy

This section defines the global vocabulary of West Peek OS. It applies to every domain, machine, workflow, AI employee, source system, and implementation decision in the document.

## 0B.1 The full stack

```text
West Peek OS
├─ OS Domains
│  ├─ Machines / Departments
│  │  ├─ AI Employees
│  │  ├─ Queues
│  │  ├─ Workflows / Loops
│  │  ├─ Records / Objects
│  │  ├─ Approval Gates
│  │  ├─ Audit Events
│  │  └─ Diagnostic Traces
│  └─ Domain Dashboards
├─ Source-System Adapters
├─ Knowledge OS / Memory Promotion
├─ Governance Layer
├─ Approval Center
├─ Activity Feed / Audit Ledger
└─ Developer Diagnostics / Debug Copilot
```

## 0B.2 West Peek OS

West Peek OS is the canonical firm operating platform and repo. It is the governed environment where the firm routes work, assigns AI employees, controls approvals, manages institutional memory, tracks audit events, coordinates source systems, and runs the firm’s operating domains.

West Peek OS does not automatically replace every external product. It governs the firm’s operating layer across products and systems.

## 0B.3 OS domains

An OS domain is a major business capability inside West Peek OS.

Examples:

- Investment OS;
- Relationship OS;
- Community OS;
- Portfolio OS;
- Fundraising OS;
- Event OS;
- Finance OS;
- Legal / Compliance OS;
- Knowledge OS;
- Builder OS;
- Operations OS;
- Opportunity Intelligence / Strategic Initiative Domain.

An OS domain defines the business area. It does not necessarily execute the work by itself. Work is executed through machines/departments inside the domain.

## 0B.4 Machines / departments

A machine is a department-like executable workflow engine inside an OS domain.

A machine owns:

- queues;
- workflows;
- recurring loops;
- AI employees;
- source-system dependencies;
- records/objects;
- approval gates;
- audit events;
- diagnostics;
- failure modes;
- operating cadence;
- acceptance criteria.

Machines are the executable operating units of West Peek OS. In user-facing language, machines may be thought of as departments. In product architecture, a machine is a structured workflow engine with assigned AI employees and governed state.

## 0B.5 AI employees

AI employees are role-based workers assigned to machines. They do not define the architecture. They operate inside it.

AI employees may:

- observe;
- prepare;
- analyze;
- draft;
- route;
- maintain internal records;
- create approval cards;
- create work cards;
- create opportunity briefs;
- create opportunity review cards;
- propose actions.

AI employees may not make material firm decisions, send external communications, make legal commitments, approve investments, override privacy boundaries, or turn unapproved information into institutional truth.

## 0B.6 Queues

A queue is work waiting inside a machine.

Examples:

- Approval Queue;
- Opportunity Review Queue;
- Strategic Initiative Queue;
- IC Prep Queue;
- Deal Math Queue;
- LP Diligence Queue;
- Support Request Queue;
- Relationship Follow-Up Queue;
- Sync Conflict Queue;
- Compliance Review Queue;
- Debug Repair Queue.

Queues prevent work from hiding in chat. Every meaningful work item should have state, owner, deadline or cadence, approval status, and audit trail.

## 0B.7 Workflows and loops

A workflow is a repeatable process owned by a machine. A loop is a recurring or stateful workflow that continues across time.

Examples:

- founder deck intake;
- IC prep;
- LP diligence response;
- portfolio update ingestion;
- event planning;
- relationship moment follow-up;
- memory promotion;
- data-provider freshness review;
- agent performance review.

Loops require state, cadence, stop conditions, owner, approval gates, and failure behavior. A loop that cannot explain its state is not operationally safe.

## 0B.8 Objects / records

Objects are structured entities West Peek OS works on.

Examples:

- Person;
- Organization;
- Company;
- Deal;
- Secondary Opportunity;
- LP;
- Portfolio Company;
- Event;
- Meeting;
- Support Request;
- Decision;
- Document;
- Evidence Object;
- Approval Card;
- Opportunity Brief;
- Opportunity Review Card;
- Strategic Initiative;
- Activity Event;
- Audit Event;
- Machine;
- AI Employee.

Objects may be owned by West Peek OS or mirrored from source systems. Source-of-truth rules determine where the authoritative version lives.

## 0B.9 Source systems

A source system is an external or internal product/repo/service that owns or contributes records, workflows, artifacts, or evidence.

Examples:

- Network OS;
- Google Drive;
- Google Sheets;
- Gmail;
- Google Calendar;
- Pitch Lab;
- West Peek Live;
- VentureDeals;
- Founder Dilution Calculator;
- Fireflies / meeting capture provider;
- fund administrator;
- external VDR;
- data providers;
- GitHub repositories.

A source system is not automatically an OS domain or a machine. It may feed one or more machines through adapters.

## 0B.10 Adapters

An adapter is the sync, import, export, webhook, API, manual-sync, or file-processing bridge between West Peek OS and a source system.

Adapters must define:

- source system;
- data touched;
- sync direction;
- sync cadence;
- source-of-truth status;
- conflict handling;
- privacy labels;
- failure behavior;
- audit event;
- manual fallback.

If a tool has no API, webhook, durable export/import, or reliable sync path, it may be used tactically but should not become core infrastructure.

## 0B.11 Ledgers

A ledger is a durable history layer.

Examples:

- Activity Ledger;
- Audit Ledger;
- Decision Ledger;
- Approval Ledger;
- Data Room Access Ledger;
- Relationship Capital Ledger;
- AI Employee Performance + Cost Ledger + Lifecycle Control;
- Compliance Ledger;
- Memory Promotion Ledger.

Ledgers preserve what happened, who or what triggered it, under what authority, what was approved, what changed, what evidence was used, and how the system can explain it later.

## 0B.12 Governance layer

The governance layer defines constitutional rules, policy rules, model/privacy rules, compliance rules, approval rules, AI employee authority, source-of-truth rules, and knowledge promotion rules.

Governance is not a machine by itself. Governance is a cross-cutting layer enforced by machines and visible through Governance Center, Approval Center, Audit Ledger, and Developer Diagnostics.

## 0B.13 Diagnostics layer

Diagnostics explains why the system did what it did.

Every meaningful machine action should be traceable by:

- actor;
- trigger;
- source data;
- machine;
- AI employee;
- rules applied;
- permission decision;
- privacy mode;
- model mode;
- outputs;
- errors;
- retry state;
- downstream writes.

## 0B.14 Opportunity Radar / Strategic Initiative layer

Opportunity Radar is the cross-domain capability that allows AI employees to notice credible ways West Peek can make money, save money, create leverage, reduce risk, strengthen relationships, generate LP-safe proof, improve operations, or protect the firm.

Opportunity Radar is not random brainstorming. It is governed senior-operator pattern recognition.

Every material opportunity should be converted into an Opportunity Brief before it reaches the humans.

Opportunity Brief fields:

- opportunity title;
- originating domain and machine;
- originating AI employee;
- senior POV used;
- why now;
- right-to-win;
- revenue, savings, leverage, risk-reduction, relationship, proof, or strategic value hypothesis;
- source evidence;
- assumptions;
- unknowns;
- CoVE / verification status;
- commercial or operating upside;
- legal, compliance, brand, privacy, fund, brokerage, or relationship risk;
- owner recommendation;
- suggested first test;
- required approvals;
- external materials needed;
- kill criteria;
- decision requested.

Opportunity Radar has two activation modes:

```text
Proactive radar:
  Machines periodically surface the best qualified opportunities in their domain.

On-demand activation:
  Sequoia or Scooter asks a machine or AI employee to explore an idea immediately.
```

Opportunity Radar must produce signal, not noise. Domain loops should normally surface only the highest-quality opportunities, grouped by value type:

- revenue opportunity;
- cost-saving opportunity;
- relationship leverage opportunity;
- risk-prevention opportunity;
- LP-proof / evidence opportunity;
- strategic partnership opportunity;
- operating simplification opportunity.

Opportunity Radar is governed by the AI Employee Runtime Contract, POV Intelligence Layer, CoVE / verification law, Approval Center, Loop Runtime, and Audit Ledger.

## 0B.15 Opportunity Review vs Workflow Approval distinction

West Peek OS must distinguish two approval families.

### Opportunity Review

Opportunity Review answers:

```text
Should West Peek pursue this surfaced opportunity at all?
```

Examples:

- AI surfaces West Peek Rooms as a sponsored-community revenue lane;
- Events OS proposes sponsoring someone else's event;
- Finance OS proposes a cost-cutting opportunity;
- Portfolio OS proposes a new support product;
- Relationship OS proposes a strategic partnership path;
- Marketing OS proposes a media or content opportunity.

Opportunity Review lives in an Opportunity Review Queue. It routes to the relevant Managing Partner(s), Chief of Staff, and risk reviewers. Approval means permission to explore, test, or package the opportunity. It does not automatically approve outreach, spend, publication, legal commitment, or execution.

### Workflow Approval

Workflow Approval answers:

```text
Do we approve this specific requested action or ordinary workflow step?
```

Examples:

- send this email;
- approve this sponsor deck draft;
- publish this event page;
- invite these attendees;
- spend this amount;
- grant this data-room access;
- send this LP response;
- approve this founder intro;
- route this support plan.

Workflow Approval lives in the standard Approval Center. It requires content, evidence, risk, recipient, owner, and approver fields.

Canonical distinction:

```text
Opportunity Approval = permission to pursue a surfaced idea.
Workflow Approval = permission to execute a specific action.
```

An opportunity can generate many workflow approvals after pursuit is approved. The two queues must remain visually and structurally distinct.

---


## 0B.16 AI Workforce Cost Visibility / Budget Governor layer

The AI Workforce Cost Visibility / Budget Governor layer is the cross-domain capability that makes AI operating cost visible, controllable, mode-governed, and accountable.

It governs how AI employees, machines, loops, workflows, model calls, provider calls, verification passes, external tools, paid data calls, and proactive opportunity-radar activity consume firm resources.

This layer does not exist to make the AI workforce timid. It exists to prevent hidden spend, runaway loops, overuse of frontier models, duplicate work, unnecessary deep research, and low-value recurring output.

### 0B.16.1 Required cost surfaces

West Peek OS must expose AI cost through a Cost Dashboard / Cost Command Center that can show:

- today's AI spend;
- this week's AI spend;
- month-to-date AI spend;
- projected month-end AI spend;
- spend by AI employee;
- spend by machine;
- spend by OS domain;
- spend by workflow;
- spend by recurring loop;
- spend by model/provider;
- spend by external tool/vendor;
- spend by requested work;
- spend by regular workflow execution;
- spend by proactive Opportunity Radar;
- spend by CoVE / verification activity;
- spend by approved, pending, blocked, or rejected work;
- upcoming scheduled or projected expensive work.

### 0B.16.2 Cost classes

Every meaningful AI run must be classified before execution.

Canonical cost classes:

```text
Free
Cheap
Standard
Expensive
Approval Required
Blocked
```

These classes are governance categories, not fixed dollar amounts. Exact thresholds may vary by provider pricing, firm budget, domain, and current mode.

Interpretation:

- Free: negligible-cost internal logic, linking, routing, reminders, or cached retrieval.
- Cheap: classification, extraction, short summaries, simple formatting, lightweight drafts, routine record updates.
- Standard: ordinary briefs, meeting prep, queue review, normal drafting, normal research, ordinary Opportunity Briefs.
- Expensive: deep research, large-context synthesis, long CoVE passes, long-document analysis, multi-agent hostile review, frontier-model judgment.
- Approval Required: recurring expensive loops, paid data calls, paid external tools, frontier-model swarms, broad web-search campaigns, vendor workflows, or any spend above current threshold.
- Blocked: disallowed by policy, budget mode, missing approval, authority limits, privacy rules, fund-safety rules, or compliance gates.

### 0B.16.3 Firmwide cost modes

West Peek OS must support Managing Partner-controlled firmwide cost modes:

```text
Normal Mode
Cheapo Mode
Critical-Only Mode
Strategic Surge Mode
```

The current firmwide mode must be visible in the Command Center and inherited by every machine unless a permitted domain override applies.

### 0B.16.4 Domain-level overrides

The system must support domain-level overrides because the firm may need to conserve cost generally while preserving power in high-value areas.

Example:

```text
Firmwide mode: Cheapo Mode
LP Fundraising OS: Normal Mode
IC Decision Machine: Strategic Surge Mode
Events OS: Paused except approved sponsor-revenue work
Research Machine: Approval Required
```

Domain overrides must be authorized, time-bounded when appropriate, and audit-logged.

### 0B.16.5 Cost preflight

Before meaningful AI work begins, the system should perform Cost Preflight:

- classify work type;
- identify domain, machine, AI employee, loop, workflow, and authority context;
- determine cost class;
- check firmwide mode;
- check domain override;
- check loop budget;
- estimate cost when feasible;
- identify provider/model/tool path;
- choose the least expensive adequate model/tool path;
- determine whether approval is required;
- execute, defer, downgrade, batch, or block.

### 0B.16.6 Actual cost and value recording

After meaningful work, the system must record:

- estimated cost;
- actual cost when available;
- provider/model/tool used;
- input/output usage when available;
- workflow or loop that triggered the work;
- whether work was requested, scheduled, proactive, or approval-generated;
- output produced;
- human decision;
- value outcome;
- follow-up created;
- whether future work should be continued, downgraded, paused, or killed.

### 0B.16.7 No silent spend

```text
No silent spend.
```

No AI employee, machine, loop, workflow, model path, provider, external tool, or data source may become a recurring cost center without visibility, owner, budget, approval state, success metric, review date, and kill condition.

### 0B.16.8 Value ledger principle

The dashboard must not only ask how much the AI workforce spent. It must also ask what the spend produced.

Cost must be evaluated against:

- revenue created or advanced;
- savings created;
- risk reduced;
- time saved;
- relationship leverage created;
- LP proof strengthened;
- IC quality improved;
- compliance confidence improved;
- operational speed gained;
- quality of output;
- human usefulness rating;
- repeatability.

Cheap but useless work is not success. Expensive but valuable work may be justified when approved and bounded.

The following domain map is the canonical business-area map for West Peek OS. Each domain contains one or more machines/departments. Source systems may feed domains but are not automatically domains themselves.

| Command / MP Office Domain | Gives Sequoia and Scooter daily operating control. | Command Center, MP Personal Office, Global Capture, Approval Center. | Calendar, Gmail, +Capture, activity/audit records. |
| Opportunity Intelligence / Strategic Initiative Domain | Cross-domain operating layer that surfaces revenue, savings, risk-reduction, relationship leverage, LP-proof, and strategic opportunities from all machines without turning ideas into unauthorized action. | Opportunity Radar + Strategic Initiative, Opportunity Review Queue, POV/CoVE review, approved pursuit handoff. | Domain loops, source evidence, Network OS, West Peek Live, VentureDeals, PDFs/decks, market research, operator input. |
| Governance Domain | Holds rules, policy, AI workforce updates, approval law, and institutional control. | Governance Center, AI Workforce Broadcast, Compliance Rules, Model Governance, Policy Attestation. | Constitution, counsel-approved policies, user captures. |
| Relationship OS | Turns relationship records into intelligence, warm paths, follow-up, and social-capital management. | Relationship Intelligence, Connection/Warm Path, Relationship Moments, Intro Approval, Relationship Capital Budget, Network OS Sync. | Network OS external synced repo/database, Gmail, Calendar, events. |
| Community OS | Turns community data into segmentation, engagement, programming, sourcing, support, and LP-proof signals. | Community Intelligence, Community Segmentation, Community-to-Event, Community-to-Dealflow, Community Outcome Attribution. | Network OS, West Peek Live, event tools, community forms. |
| Investment OS | Sources, evaluates, underwrites, decides, and learns from early-stage and secondary investments. | Deal Intake, Investment Mandate, VentureDeals / Deal Math, IC Decision, Research, IC Learning Loop. | VentureDeals, Drive, Network OS, Pitch Lab, data providers, meeting records. |
| Portfolio OS | Monitors portfolio companies, supports founders, tracks performance, and manages follow-on/pro-rata analysis. | Portfolio Support, Portfolio Performance, Follow-On Decision, Support Service Levels, Portfolio Update Ingestion. | Portfolio records, founder updates, Network OS, Drive, meetings. |
| Fundraising / LP OS | Manages LP pipeline, fundraising materials, diligence, data-room control, and LP proof. | LP Fundraising, LP Diligence Request, LP Proof Engine, Data Room Control. | Network OS, Drive, VDR, Gmail, Calendar, fund docs. |
| Event OS | Plans and operates West Peek events and converts them into relationship, support, research, and LP-proof value. | West Peek Live / Events, Sponsorship, Event-to-Portfolio Value, Event-to-Relationship Value. | West Peek Live, Network OS, event registration, Drive. |
| Knowledge OS | Promotes approved information into durable institutional memory and searchable evidence. | Memory Promotion, Evidence Ledger, Source-of-Truth Resolver, Knowledge Search. | Drive, Network OS, meeting records, data providers, internal approvals. |

| Brand / Marketing OS | Produces external materials, content, PR, and West Peek voice-quality review under approval controls. | Marketing/PR/Content, West Peek Taste Layer, Claim Substantiation. | Drive, Knowledge OS, LP-safe evidence, brand assets. |

# 0D. Domain / Machine / Source-System Examples

## 0D.1 Relationship OS

Relationship OS is a domain inside West Peek OS. It is not one machine and it is not the database.

```text
Relationship OS
├─ Relationship Intelligence Machine
├─ Network OS Sync + Verification Machine
├─ Connection / Warm Path Machine
├─ Relationship Moments Machine
├─ Intro Approval Machine
├─ Relationship Capital Budget Machine
└─ Relationship Memory Promotion Machine
```

Network OS is the external synced source database/repo for relationship records. Relationship OS reads and enriches synced Network OS data, creates intelligence, routes follow-ups, proposes introductions, manages social-capital risk, and sends durable record updates back through Network OS sync rules.

## 0D.2 Community OS

Community OS is a domain inside West Peek OS. It is not a separate community database.

```text
Community OS
├─ Community Intelligence Machine
├─ Community Segmentation Machine
├─ Community Engagement Machine
├─ Community-to-Event Machine
├─ Community-to-Dealflow Machine
├─ Community-to-Portfolio Support Machine
└─ Community Outcome Attribution Machine
```

Community OS operates on the same Network OS person/company/community database and adds community-specific filters, segments, programming opportunities, contribution signals, event participation, founder-support signals, and LP-proof community evidence.

## 0D.3 Investment OS

Investment OS is a domain inside West Peek OS. It contains the full Investment Math + Decision Spine.

```text
Investment OS
├─ Deal Intake Machine
├─ Investment Mandate + Exclusion Machine
├─ VentureDeals / Deal Math Machine
├─ Research & Intelligence Machine
├─ IC Decision Machine
├─ Portfolio Performance + Follow-On Decision Machine
├─ Fund Construction + Capital Allocation Machine
└─ IC Learning Loop Machine
```

VentureDeals is a source-system/product integration and a machine input. It should produce Deal Math Packets used by IC, secondaries, primary deals, follow-on decisions, and fund construction. IC cannot be considered complete if the math layer is missing, stale, or unresolved.

## 0D.4 Event OS

Event OS is a domain inside West Peek OS. West Peek Live is an external/source product and production layer that feeds Event OS through integration.

```text
Event OS
├─ Event Planning Machine
├─ West Peek Live / Events Machine
├─ Sponsorship Machine
├─ Event-to-Relationship Value Machine
├─ Event-to-Portfolio Value Machine
└─ Event Outcome Attribution Machine
```

## 0D.5 Builder / Systems OS

Builder / Systems OS is the domain that governs products, repos, integration architecture, diagnostics, and build-vs-buy decisions.

```text
Builder / Systems OS
├─ Repo / Product Registry Machine
├─ Systems Integration Machine
├─ Developer Diagnostics Machine
├─ Debug Copilot Machine
├─ Vendor Risk + Build-vs-Buy Machine
└─ Release / Snapshot Machine
```

The separate Odysseus Audit and Implementation Plan owns coding sequence, repo patching, and detailed implementation execution. This canonical plan owns what the system must become and how its operating architecture works.

# 0E. Architecture Clarity Laws

## 0E.1 Domain law

```text
Domains describe business capabilities.
Machines execute work inside domains.
AI employees operate machines.
Source systems provide or receive records through adapters.
Queues organize work.
Loops make work repeatable.
Ledgers preserve history.
West Peek OS governs the whole architecture.
```

## 0E.2 Machine-as-department law

```text
A machine is the West Peek OS equivalent of an operating department.
```

A machine must be concrete enough that Sequoia or Scooter can open it and understand:

- what work lives here;
- who works here;
- which queue matters;
- what gets produced;
- what gets handed off;
- what requires approval;
- what source data it depends on;
- what gets written back;
- what is audited;
- what breaks if the machine fails.

## 0E.3 Source-system law

```text
A source system is not automatically a domain.
A source system is not automatically a machine.
A source system may feed many domains and machines through adapters.
```

Examples:

- Network OS feeds Relationship OS, Community OS, Investment OS, Fundraising OS, Portfolio OS, and Event OS.
- VentureDeals feeds Investment OS, IC Decision Machine, Secondaries Machine, Follow-On Machine, and Fund Construction Machine.
- West Peek Live feeds Event OS, Community OS, Portfolio OS, Relationship OS, and LP Proof Engine.
- Google Drive feeds Knowledge OS, Data Room Control, IC, LP Diligence, Portfolio, Legal, Finance, and Research.

## 0E.4 No duplicate database law

```text
West Peek OS should not create duplicate databases when a source system already owns the record.
```

West Peek OS may mirror, enrich, route, and audit data. It must not create silent competing truth.

## 0E.5 Completeness over compression law

```text
Clarity is allowed.
Compression that removes operating substance is not allowed.
```

A future simplification pass must preserve:

- detailed domain requirements;
- machine requirements;
- AI employee assignments;
- queue/workflow/loop logic;
- source-system dependencies;
- approval gates;
- audit and diagnostics;
- data-object requirements;
- privacy/model mode rules;
- UX requirements;
- failure modes;
- acceptance criteria.

---


# 0. Executive Mandate

West Peek OS is the institutional operating system for West Peek Ventures.

It is not a CRM, dashboard, task manager, data room, agent demo, or document repository. Those are components. West Peek OS is the governed environment through which the firm thinks, decides, raises capital, sources investments, underwrites opportunities, supports founders, operates events, manages relationships, preserves institutional memory, coordinates external providers, and runs a virtual workforce.

The system will be created by forking and transforming Odysseus. Each Managing Partner will operate through a dedicated two-agent personal office consisting of one AI Chief of Staff and one AI Executive Assistant, while all four agents remain governed by the same firm constitution, audit system, and final-authority model.

West Peek OS must also function as a daily operating company of AI employees. Agents are not decorative chat personas. Each role must have a recurring seven-day work schedule, a defined start and end window, owned state, handoffs, logs, approval gates, and manual on-demand invocation from the partners.

```text
West Peek OS
=
Odysseus Fork
+
West Peek Constitution
+
Venture Fund Operating Model
+
Firm Knowledge Graph
+
Agent Workforce
+
West Peek Tool Integrations
+
Human Approval and Audit System
```

West Peek OS must allow two human Managing Partners to operate a credible, disciplined, high-touch venture firm without immediately hiring a conventional full-time team.

The system must be warm, accessible, fun, high-touch, and unconventional externally while remaining rigorous, secure, permissioned, auditable, and institutionally credible internally.

The core build law is:

```text
Odysseus First
Audit First
Transform Second
```

No major component should be rebuilt until the Odysseus fork is audited and classified as Keep, Modify, Remove, or Add.

---

# 1. West Peek Ventures Institutional Profile

## 1.1 Fund model

West Peek Ventures is being designed as a traditional United States venture capital fund.

- Target fund size: $30 million to $50 million
- Economics: 2% management fee and 20% carried interest
- Geography: United States
- Human Managing Partners: Scooter Taylor and Sequoia Taylor
- Ownership: Scooter 51%; Sequoia 49%
- Final authority: Scooter
- Visibility: both Managing Partners see the complete firm and may personalize filters without losing access

## 1.2 Investment strategy

The working allocation model is:

- 60% pre-seed and seed primary investments
- 40% Series C or later direct private-company secondary investments

The early-stage portfolio is internally sector-agnostic. External LP positioning emphasizes healthcare, education, consumer, and the future of work. West Peek may review all credible opportunities consistent with its legal, ethical, reputational, and institutional exclusion policies.

Excluded or restricted categories will be formalized in the Investment Policy and may include adult businesses, illegal drugs, prohibited activities, sanctioned entities, and other categories inconsistent with institutional venture practice.

The fund is flexible about leading, co-leading, or following. Portfolio construction, ownership targets, reserves, initial check sizes, and concentration limits will adjust to final fund size and opportunity quality.

## 1.3 Founder advantages

West Peek’s strategy is not a manufactured deck thesis. It is built around two distinct founder advantages.

### Scooter advantage

- early-stage founder relationships
- community building
- front-office presence
- founder support
- sourcing
- events
- storytelling
- ecosystem development

### Sequoia advantage

- ten years of late-stage private-company secondary-market work
- FINRA-registered broker experience
- existing partnerships and inbound
- secondary transaction judgment
- operations
- institutional systems
- internal tool building

Brokerage activity remains operationally and legally separate from West Peek Ventures. West Peek OS must enforce separation, conflicts controls, attribution, disclosure, and audit trails.

## 1.4 Community advantage

West Peek built a community of more than 5,000 people before writing an institutional fund check.

The community includes:

- founders
- investors
- intrapreneurs
- entrepreneurs
- operators

The community is open to anyone. A future invite-only West Peek Mastermind is outside v1 scope and should remain an extension point rather than shaping the initial architecture.

Community is not a marketing sidecar. It is operating infrastructure for sourcing, diligence, founder support, hiring, business development, customer introductions, investor introductions, events, reputation, and institutional learning.

---

# 2. Strategic Positioning

## 2.1 LP thesis

West Peek is designed to bookend the private-market lifecycle.

```text
Pre-seed and seed
+
Series C and later secondaries
```

The early-stage sleeve seeks asymmetric upside through community-powered access, founder relationships, and hands-on support.

The secondary sleeve seeks selective exposure to later-stage private companies with more operating history, better price discovery, and potentially nearer-term liquidity pathways.

The strategies must not be presented as automatically diversifying or lower-risk. West Peek OS must produce evidence for all fundraising claims and distinguish narrative from verified results.

## 2.2 Working LP proposition

West Peek Ventures is a community-powered venture fund investing across the private-market lifecycle. It backs United States companies at pre-seed and seed, where its founder network and operating platform can generate access and support. It also invests in Series C and later private-company secondaries, drawing on Sequoia’s secondary-market experience and established transaction relationships.

## 2.3 Proof system

West Peek OS must continuously prove:

- how opportunities were sourced
- which opportunities came through community
- founder history before investment
- support provided before and after investment
- introductions made
- resulting outcomes
- event-generated relationships and opportunities
- secondary opportunity provenance
- pricing and stage
- portfolio allocation and concentration
- decision rationale
- track-record attribution
- conflicts and disclosures
- claims approved for LP use

The governing fundraising law is:

```text
Fundraising language must never outrun the evidence.
```

---

# 3. Product Definition

## 3.1 What West Peek OS is

West Peek OS is:

- the Managing Partner command center
- the firm’s governed AI workspace
- the firm’s knowledge and decision system
- the agent workforce control plane
- the operating layer connecting all West Peek tools
- the approval system for outbound actions
- the audit record for meaningful firm activity
- the institutional memory of the firm

## 3.2 What West Peek OS is not

It is not:

- a greenfield generic VC SaaS product
- a public community platform
- a replacement for licensed legal, tax, audit, fund-administration, or broker-dealer functions
- an autonomous investment decision maker
- an agent theater environment
- an ungoverned email bot
- a place where conversation automatically becomes truth

## 3.3 Primary users

Initial human users:

- Scooter Taylor, Managing Partner
- Sequoia Taylor, Managing Partner

All other firm roles are represented initially by governed AI operating functions.

## 3.4 Experience principle

Both Managing Partners receive full firm visibility.

The system may personalize:

- default dashboards
- queues
- notifications
- suggested actions
- owned workflows
- saved filters

It may not restrict either Managing Partner from seeing the whole firm.

```text
Same complete command center
Different personalized defaults
```

---

# 4. Odysseus Fork Strategy

## 4.1 Source capabilities to preserve and assess

Odysseus currently presents horizontal capabilities including:

- AI chat
- agents and tool calling
- local and API models
- persistent memory and skills
- deep research
- documents
- email
- notes and tasks
- scheduled work
- calendar and CalDAV
- file uploads
- web search
- PWA/mobile support
- authentication and 2FA

These capabilities are starting infrastructure, not proof of institutional fitness.

## 4.2 Transformation map

| Odysseus capability | West Peek transformation |
|---|---|
| Chat | Firm Command and governed workspaces |
| Agents | AI workforce with role charters and authority limits |
| Memory | Institutional memory with provenance and approval |
| Skills | Firm playbooks, procedures, and role capabilities |
| Research | Investment, LP, market, portfolio, and event research |
| Documents | IC memos, LP materials, reports, playbooks, and records |
| Email | Relationship intelligence, intake, drafts, and approvals |
| Tasks | Firm execution, ownership, deadlines, and escalation |
| Calendar | IC, MP meetings, LP meetings, portfolio cadence, events |
| Notes | Captured observations awaiting review and promotion |
| Scheduling | Approved recurring workflows and monitoring |
| Files | Controlled data room, diligence, legal, and evidence assets |
| PWA/mobile | Managing Partner mobile command and approvals |

## 4.3 Required Odysseus Reality Assessment

Before implementation architecture is frozen, the fork must be audited across:

1. repository architecture
2. database and persistence model
3. authentication and user model
4. authorization and roles
5. tenant assumptions
6. memory model
7. agent definitions
8. tools and permissions
9. workflows and scheduling
10. background jobs
11. documents and files
12. email integration
13. calendar integration
14. model-provider abstraction
15. knowledge retrieval
16. audit logging
17. notifications
18. UI component system
19. mobile behavior
20. secrets and deployment
21. testing and CI
22. backup and restore
23. extension points

Each capability receives one decision:

```text
KEEP
MODIFY
REMOVE
ADD
```

## 4.4 Institutionalization gaps to assume until proven otherwise

Because Odysseus is designed as a personal workspace, the fork must treat the following as unproven:

- multi-user institutional authorization
- role-based permissions
- data-classification boundaries
- immutable decision logs
- human approval queues
- outbound-send controls
- separation of duties
- legal hold and retention
- structured fund entities
- LP and investment workflows
- conflicts management
- portfolio accounting reconciliation
- vendor integration governance
- agent action auditability

---

# 5. Canonical System Architecture

## 5.1 Architectural layers

```text
LAYER 1 — EXPERIENCE
Managing Partner Command Center
Role workspaces
Mobile approvals
Search and chat

LAYER 2 — OPERATING SYSTEMS
Firm, Investment, Secondaries, Fundraising, Portfolio,
Relationship, Community, Event, Knowledge, Market Intelligence,
Marketing, Finance, Legal, Operations, Builder

LAYER 3 — AGENT WORKFORCE
Chief of Staff and specialist operating agents

LAYER 4 — WORKFLOW AND APPROVAL ENGINE
Tasks, state machines, routing, approvals, escalation, SLAs

LAYER 5 — KNOWLEDGE AND DECISION GRAPH
People, firms, companies, funds, deals, events, decisions,
documents, interactions, obligations, evidence, provenance

LAYER 6 — SYSTEMS OF RECORD AND INTEGRATIONS
Network OS, Pitch Lab, West Peek Live, Dilution Dashboard,
email, calendar, fund administrator, file storage, data providers

LAYER 7 — SECURITY AND OPERATIONS
Authentication, permissions, secrets, logs, backups,
monitoring, data retention, incident response
```

## 5.2 Control-plane principle

West Peek OS is the control plane. It should not become the sole owner of every external record.

Authoritative systems may include:

- Network OS for relationship records and relationship workflows
- fund administrator for capital accounts, tax, official books, and notices
- legal counsel for executed legal documents
- broker-dealer systems for brokerage activity
- West Peek Live for virtual event production
- Pitch Lab for founder pitch practice and approved handoffs

West Peek OS should aggregate, orchestrate, reconcile, and preserve context without creating hidden duplicate truths.

## 5.3 Event-driven integration architecture

Preferred integration pattern:

```text
Source event
→ integration adapter
→ identity resolution
→ policy and consent check
→ normalized domain event
→ workflow trigger
→ record update
→ agent action
→ human approval if outbound
→ audit log
```

Examples:

- Pitch Lab founder elects to share
- Network OS creates or updates relationship
- Gmail message indicates LP interest
- calendar meeting completes
- event attendee registers
- fund administrator posts capital-call status
- portfolio company sends an update

## 5.4 Data architecture

Use a hybrid model:

- relational database for transactional and governed records
- object storage for files and evidence
- search index for full-text retrieval
- vector retrieval for semantic discovery
- graph relationships for connected institutional intelligence
- append-only audit records for decisions and agent actions

The knowledge graph should extend authoritative entities rather than replacing transactional systems.

---

---


# 5AA. Operating Systems, Machines, Departments, and Agents — Canonical Distinction

West Peek OS must use these terms consistently.

## 5AA.1 Definitions

```text
West Peek OS = the canonical firm operating platform and repo.
OS / Domain = a major business area inside West Peek OS.
Machine = the department-like executable workflow engine inside an OS/domain.
Department = the plain-English operating grouping users understand; often equivalent to one machine or a cluster of machines.
AI employee = the governed operator assigned to one or more machines.
External source system = a separate repo, SaaS, database, or product synced into West Peek OS through an adapter, mirror, import/export, webhook, API, or manual verification workflow.
Model = the technical engine an AI employee or plugin may use.
```

West Peek OS is the canonical platform. Network OS is a separate external West Peek repo/source system synced into West Peek OS. Relationship OS and Community OS are domains inside West Peek OS that work from synced Network OS data.

An OS/domain is broader than a machine. A machine is how work actually runs. In practical language, machines are the operating departments or workflow engines that do the work.

Example:

```text
Relationship OS
├─ Relationship Intelligence Machine
├─ Network OS Sync + Verification Machine
├─ Connection / Warm Path Machine
├─ Relationship Moments Machine
├─ Intro Approval Machine
└─ Relationship Capital Budget Machine
```

Therefore, Relationship OS is not one machine. Relationship OS is the relationship operating domain. It contains multiple machines that work together around Network OS data.

## 5AA.2 OS/domain law

```text
OS systems describe what institutional capability exists.
Machines describe how the work is executed.
AI employees describe who performs the work.
Queues describe what work is waiting.
Approval gates describe where humans regain control.
Audit and diagnostics describe what happened and why.
```

## 5AA.3 Why the distinction matters

Without this distinction, the system blurs dashboards, departments, workflows, source systems, and agents into one mushy layer. West Peek OS must not do that.

The user-facing intuition is allowed to be simple: machines are basically departments. The architecture must be more precise: a department-like machine owns queues, workflows, AI employees, data dependencies, approvals, audit events, and diagnostics. An OS/domain may contain several such machines.

The product should let Sequoia or Scooter think in simple domains:

```text
Relationship OS
Community OS
Investment OS
Portfolio OS
Fundraising OS
Events OS
Finance OS
Legal / Compliance OS
Builder OS
```

But the backend and execution plan must map those domains to machines, queues, AI employee assignments, approval gates, source-of-truth dependencies, audit events, and diagnostics.

## 5AA.4 Canonical OS-to-machine examples

| OS / Domain inside West Peek OS | Example machines inside it | Canonical source dependency |
|---|---|---|
| Relationship OS | Relationship Intelligence, Connection/Warm Path, Relationship Moments, Relationship Capital Budget, Intro Approval, Network OS Sync | Network OS external repo/database synced into West Peek OS |
| Community OS | Community Intelligence, Community Segmentation, Event Participation Intelligence, Community-to-Deal Signal, Community-to-Portfolio Support Signal | Network OS external repo/database + West Peek Live event/product data |
| Investment OS | Early-Stage Deal, Secondaries Investment, Investment Mandate, VentureDeals / Deal Math, IC Decision, IC Learning Loop | West Peek OS deal records + VentureDeals external product/repo + Drive + Network OS |
| Portfolio OS | Portfolio Support, Portfolio Performance + Follow-On, Support Service Levels, Portfolio Update Ingestion | West Peek OS portfolio records + Network OS + Drive |
| Fundraising OS | LP Fundraising, LP Diligence Request, LP Proof Engine, Data Room Control | West Peek OS LP records + Network OS + Drive/VDR |
| Events OS | West Peek Live / Events, Sponsorship, Event-to-Portfolio Value, Event-to-Relationship Value | West Peek Live external product/repo + Network OS |
| Finance OS | Fund Construction + Capital Allocation, Finance/Fund Admin, Fund Admin Reconciliation | Fund admin/provider records + West Peek OS finance mirror |
| Legal / Compliance OS | Compliance Rules, Data Provider License/Quality, Vendor Risk, Brokerage/Fund Separation | Counsel-approved rules + provider contracts + audit ledger |
| Builder OS | Repo/Product, Systems/Integrations, Developer Diagnostics, Debug Copilot | GitHub/repos + diagnostics logs + canonical docs |

## 5AA.5 Relationship OS answer

Relationship OS is **not** merely a machine.

Relationship OS is the business domain for relationship intelligence. It uses Network OS as the database and contains several executable machines. The main Relationship OS machines are:

- Relationship Intelligence Machine;
- Network OS Sync + Verification Machine;
- Relationship Capital Budget Machine;
- Connection / Warm Path Machine;
- Relationship Moments Machine;
- Intro Approval Machine.

The same pattern applies elsewhere: Community OS is not one machine, Investment OS is not one machine, and Portfolio OS is not one machine. Each OS/domain contains multiple machines.

# 5A. Core Machine Registry

## 5A.1 Machine-first operating law

Machines are the primary operating units of West Peek OS.

AI employees work inside machines. Queues, approvals, memory, audit, diagnostics, dashboards, data objects, and implementation modules attach to machines.

No major firm function should exist only as a loose feature, isolated dashboard, or isolated AI employee.

The machine registry is the canonical map of West Peek OS. Every implementation phase, Odysseus audit workstream, AI employee assignment, workflow queue, and diagnostic trace should map back to one or more machines.

## 5A.3 Machine object schema

Every machine should have a machine object with:

- machine_id
- machine_name
- purpose
- owning domain
- primary human stakeholders
- primary AI employees
- input channels
- queues
- workflows
- approval gates
- privacy labels
- model modes allowed
- source-of-truth dependencies
- external vendors / integrations
- outputs
- audit requirements
- diagnostics requirements
- failure modes
- implementation phase
- acceptance criteria

## 5A.4 Machine-to-implementation law

The Odysseus audit and implementation plan must map existing Odysseus capabilities against this registry. The first build should not attempt to implement all machines at full depth. It should build the First Working Spine and then attach machines progressively.



## 5A.6 Machine-to-employee operating department standard

The machine registry is not sufficient unless each machine can be operated as a department. Every machine must therefore maintain a machine operating card with:

- machine / department name;
- purpose;
- human owner;
- primary AI employees;
- supporting AI employees;
- daily work;
- weekly work;
- intake sources;
- source-of-truth dependencies;
- records written;
- approval gates;
- audit events;
- diagnostics events;
- failure modes;
- escalation path;
- V0.1/V0.2/V0.3/V0.4/V1 implementation status.

The user should be able to open any machine and immediately understand:

```text
Who works here?
What do they do every day?
What queue do they own?
What do they produce?
What do they hand off?
What are they forbidden to do?
What gets audited?
What breaks if this machine fails?
```

## 5A.5 Machine-to-user-experience law

The user should not have to see every machine by default. The default UX remains Today, Approvals, Capture, Meetings, Pipeline, Relationships, and Search. Machine complexity is available through Command Center, Developer Diagnostics, Governance Center, and admin views when needed.

# 5B. Investment Math + Decision Spine

The investment architecture must not scatter fund construction, capital allocation, VentureDeals, IC, secondaries, and follow-on decisions across disconnected machines.

West Peek OS uses one Investment Math + Decision Spine:

```text
Fund Model Layer
↓
Deal Math Layer / VentureDeals Machine
↓
IC Decision Layer
↓
Portfolio Performance + Follow-On Layer
↓
Investment Learning Loop Layer
```

## 5B.1 Spine law

```text
No IC decision is complete until the math works, the assumptions are visible, and the return case is tied to the fund model.
```

This does not mean the OS makes investment decisions. It means every investment decision has a disciplined math packet before humans decide.

## 5B.2 Fund Model Layer

Owned by Fund Construction + Capital Allocation Machine.

Purpose:

- fund size;
- management fee and investable capital assumptions;
- stage allocation;
- secondary sleeve allocation;
- target company count;
- initial check sizes;
- reserve ratio;
- expected follow-on count;
- ownership targets;
- concentration limits;
- deployment pacing;
- exposure by stage, sector, company, and vintage;
- scenario model.

Primary AI employees:

- Preston — finance/fund construction support;
- Wyatt — math normalization and metrics analyst;
- Pierce — investment judgment overlay;
- Poppy — IC packet integration;
- Willow — compliance/conflict review where capital allocation touches restricted matters.

## 5B.3 Deal Math Layer / VentureDeals Machine

VentureDeals is a West Peek-branded Deal Intelligence / Venture Deal Math Dashboard. The uploaded repo is named `secondaries`, but its actual purpose is broader: it supports secondary deals, primary deals, and fund math.

VentureDeals must sit beside Pitch Lab, Network OS, West Peek Live, and the Dilution Calculator in the West Peek integration/product registry.

VentureDeals should support:

- secondary deal economics;
- primary deal economics;
- fund math;
- editable assumptions;
- refreshable industry standards;
- CSV export;
- print/PDF output;
- decision-support disclaimers;
- assumption persistence;
- deal math packet generation.

The Deal Math Layer answers:

- Does the entry price make sense?
- Does the ownership target matter?
- Does dilution break the return case?
- Does the secondary discount create enough margin of safety?
- What exit value is required?
- What MOIC / TVPI / DPI / XIRR is implied?
- What reserve burden does this opportunity create?
- What fund-return contribution is required?
- Is the deal venture-scale enough for West Peek?
- Is this support-only rather than invest-more?

## 5B.4 IC Decision Layer

Owned by IC Decision Machine.

The IC Decision Layer owns judgment, evidence, dissent, final rationale, and decision memory.

It cannot mark an opportunity IC-ready unless the Deal Math Layer has produced one of:

```text
Deal Math Packet — Primary
Deal Math Packet — Secondary
Deal Math Packet — Follow-On / Pro-Rata
Deal Math Packet — Fund Impact
Deal Math Exception Memo
```

A Deal Math Exception Memo is allowed only when math cannot yet be completed. It must name what is missing and why the decision can or cannot proceed.

## 5B.5 Portfolio Performance + Follow-On Layer

Owned by Portfolio Performance + Follow-On Decision Machine.

This layer detects post-investment signals and routes winners or risk cases back through the Investment Math + Decision Spine.

Follow-on decisions must include:

- current ownership;
- pro-rata right status;
- next-round amount;
- implied pro-rata check;
- reserve capacity;
- updated dilution path;
- performance evidence;
- fund concentration impact;
- opportunity cost;
- support-only alternative;
- decision history.

## 5B.6 Learning Loop Layer

Owned by IC Learning Loop Machine.

Every invest/pass/follow-on/pass-on-pro-rata decision creates assumptions and a review cadence.

Review points:

```text
3 months
6 months
12 months
24 months
major financing
follow-on opportunity
shutdown
exit
major thesis change
```

The learning loop asks:

- What did we believe?
- What happened?
- Which assumptions were true?
- Which assumptions failed?
- Which evidence mattered?
- Which evidence was noise?
- Did the math packet matter?
- Did the fund model hold?
- What should change in future IC prompts?

## 5B.7 Deal Math Packet schema

Every Deal Math Packet should include:

- packet ID;
- deal ID;
- company;
- deal type: primary / secondary / follow-on / fund scenario;
- source inputs;
- VentureDeals dashboard link or calculation snapshot;
- assumption set;
- industry-standard baseline used;
- editable deviations from baseline;
- valuation or price;
- check size;
- ownership at close;
- expected exit ownership;
- dilution assumptions;
- pro-rata right status;
- reserve requirement;
- MOIC / TVPI / DPI / XIRR where applicable;
- fund contribution;
- concentration impact;
- secondary discount/premium if applicable;
- margin-of-safety notes;
- math quality status;
- missing inputs;
- reviewer;
- approval status;
- IC readiness status;
- audit trail.

## 5B.8 Deal math readiness statuses

```text
Not Started
Inputs Missing
Draft Math Complete
Needs Wyatt Review
Needs Preston Review
Needs Willow Review
IC-Ready
Exception Memo Required
Rejected / Math Does Not Work
```

## 5B.9 VentureDeals integration law

```text
VentureDeals is not a standalone calculator floating outside the firm OS.
It is the deal-level math workbench for West Peek investment judgment.
```

West Peek OS should not duplicate every calculator UI on day one. It should integrate, deep-link, export/import, or mirror VentureDeals outputs as structured Deal Math Packets.

## 5B.10 Hard failure modes

The system must warn when:

- IC memo exists but no Deal Math Packet exists;
- founder narrative looks strong but math is incomplete;
- ownership target is missed;
- reserve need exceeds available budget;
- valuation requires unrealistic exit value;
- secondary discount is insufficient for risk;
- pro-rata rights are missing;
- fund contribution is too small to matter;
- concentration limit is exceeded;
- follow-on decision is driven by vibes rather than evidence;
- VentureDeals inputs are stale;
- industry standards were changed without review.

# 6. West Peek Constitution

The Constitution is the highest internal behavioral authority beneath executed legal documents.

It should define:

- firm purpose
- ownership and final authority
- Managing Partner visibility
- investment mandate
- external and internal thesis treatment
- exclusion policy
- community principles
- founder treatment standards
- conflicts policy
- brokerage separation
- agent authority
- outbound approval law
- knowledge promotion law
- confidentiality and data classification
- decision-record requirements
- external-provider authority
- override and emergency rules
- audit and retention rules

## 6.1 Core authority model

- Scooter has final institutional authority.
- Sequoia has full Managing Partner visibility and broad operating responsibility.
- Both Managing Partners may direct internal work.
- Agents do not make material firm decisions.
- Any communication or action going to another human requires preapproval.
- Material commitments require Scooter approval unless authority is explicitly delegated.

## 6.2 Knowledge law

```text
Conversation does not equal knowledge.
```

Important information follows:

```text
Capture
→ classify
→ review
→ approve
→ publish to institutional knowledge
→ version and retain provenance
```

## 6.3 Outbound law

```text
Internal preparation may be autonomous.
External communication requires human approval.
```

This applies to email, social posts, invitations, introductions, meeting requests, LP communications, founder responses, legal messages, vendor contact, press outreach, and public publishing.

- Scooter position

## 7.5 Managing Partner Personal Offices

Each Managing Partner receives a dedicated AI personal office inside West Peek OS. This is not a cosmetic preference layer. It is the primary operating interface through which each partner receives briefings, delegates work, prepares decisions, manages commitments, and controls external approvals.

### Scooter Personal Office

- Scooter AI Chief of Staff
- Scooter AI Executive Assistant
- default emphasis on early-stage investing, founders, community, events, fundraising relationships, portfolio support, and public-facing opportunities
- full access to the entire firm
- final-authority queue for material institutional decisions

### Sequoia Personal Office

- Sequoia AI Chief of Staff
- Sequoia AI Executive Assistant
- default emphasis on secondaries, operations, systems, finance, risk, fundraising execution, and institutional infrastructure
- full access to the entire firm
- recommendation, execution, and delegated-approval queues consistent with the Constitution

### Personal-office capabilities

Each office must provide:

- personalized morning and end-of-day briefs
- calendar and meeting preparation
- inbox triage and draft responses
- decision preparation
- commitment and follow-up tracking
- personal priority management
- relationship reminders
- travel and event logistics
- document and briefing preparation
- approval queue management
- delegated agent orchestration
- private working notes
- partner-specific preferences and communication style
- continuity across desktop and mobile

### Shared-firm coordination law

The two personal offices must coordinate without becoming competing command centers.

- The firm record, policies, decisions, tasks, relationships, and approved knowledge remain shared institutional truth.
- Partner-private working notes remain private unless deliberately promoted or shared.
- Each Chief of Staff may propose assignments to specialist agents.
- A central orchestration service detects duplicate requests, contradictory instructions, resource conflicts, and overlapping outbound drafts.
- No personal-office agent may suppress information that either Managing Partner is authorized to see.
- Scooter's final authority remains controlling for unresolved material decisions.
- Every cross-office handoff, conflict, override, and resolution is logged.

### Memory boundaries

Each personal office uses four memory scopes:

1. Partner-private memory — personal preferences, private notes, draft thinking, and non-promoted context.
2. Partner-work memory — current priorities, delegated tasks, meeting preparation, and personal commitments.
3. Shared firm memory — approved institutional knowledge available to both Managing Partners and authorized agents.
4. Restricted matter memory — legal, compliance, conflicts, or transaction-specific records governed by matter permissions.

Conversation does not automatically enter shared firm memory. Promotion requires classification, provenance, and approval under the Knowledge OS.

# 8. Firm OS

Purpose: run the firm as an institution.

## Features

- firm objectives and annual priorities
- quarterly and monthly operating plans
- initiatives and owners
- weekly Managing Partner operating review
- decision journal
- commitments ledger
- risk register
- policy library
- vendor register
- insurance and corporate records
- organizational chart, including AI roles
- capacity and workload view
- operating calendar
- board or advisory materials if applicable
- institutional scorecard

## Weekly MP operating review

The two dedicated AI Chiefs of Staff jointly produce one reconciled institutional review. Each first prepares a partner-specific brief; the orchestration layer then merges them into one shared agenda, removes duplicates, surfaces disagreements, and preserves both partners' positions.

The reconciled review includes:

1. decisions required
2. fundraising and LP pipeline
3. early-stage opportunities
4. secondary opportunities
5. portfolio construction
6. portfolio company health
7. community and founder support
8. events
9. relationship intelligence
10. finance and cash
11. legal and compliance
12. operations and vendors
13. marketing and visibility
14. agent work and approvals
15. risks and unresolved commitments
16. seven-day priorities

Every agenda item exits as Decision, Owner, Deadline, Delegated Action, Deferred Item, or Closed Item.

Purpose: source, evaluate, decide, execute, and learn.

```text
Relationship
→ community member

→ potential investment
```

A pass does not terminate the relationship.

## 9.3 Pipeline stages

## 9.5 Diligence workstreams

- team and founder quality
- market

- legal and regulatory
