## 9.6 Investment Committee system

West Peek OS must include a first-class Investment Committee system. The IC system is not just a memo folder. It is the governed decision environment for evaluating investment opportunities, conducting live meetings, capturing judgment, preserving dissent, and converting discussion into institutional memory.

The IC system has three layers:

```text
IC Preparation
↓
IC Meeting Portal
↓
IC Decision Memory
```

### 9.6.1 IC Preparation

Before a meeting, the system prepares a complete deal room for the company under review. The preparation layer includes:

- company profile
- founder profiles
- source and provenance
- relationship history from Network OS
- Pitch Lab materials if applicable
- founder-support history
- deck, memo, notes, emails, recordings, transcripts, and attachments
- market map
- competitor map
- customer and community diligence
- references
- valuation and round terms
- ownership target
- expected check size
- fund-construction impact
- risk register
- open diligence questions
- conflicts check
- legal or regulatory flags
- decision options
- recommended next step

The AI Investment team prepares the packet, but the packet must label every claim by evidence status:

```text
Verified
Founder-stated
Third-party sourced
AI-inferred
Unverified
Missing
```

No IC memo may bury uncertainty.

### 9.6.2 Investment Committee Meeting Portal

West Peek OS must provide a live IC Meeting Portal that any authorized meeting participant can enter during an investment discussion.

The portal should open directly into the company being discussed and show:

- company name and one-line description
- round, stage, check size, valuation, and ownership target
- source of opportunity
- lead partner
- current recommendation
- memo summary
- key diligence findings
- open questions
- strongest investment case
- strongest counterargument
- red flags
- community advantage evidence
- founder relationship history
- fund-construction impact
- conflicts and approvals
- live agenda
- current speaker or discussion topic
- decision status

The portal must support multiple meeting types:

```text
Early look discussion
Partner screen
Full IC meeting
Follow-up IC meeting
Bridge / extension decision
Reserve decision
Secondary investment IC
Post-mortem / decision review
```


### 9.6.2A IC Decision Portal and Internal Sidecar Architecture

The Investment Committee Meeting Portal is not a video meeting room in v1.

The actual human meeting happens wherever the meeting naturally happens:

```text
Zoom
Google Meet
phone
in person
investor dinner
partner walk-through
external diligence call
```

West Peek OS runs beside that meeting as an internal investment-decision cockpit.

```text
External conversation environment
↓
West Peek OS IC Decision Portal open in a separate internal tab
↓
Walter and approved specialist AI employees assist internally
↓
West Peek OS captures evidence, questions, commitments, risks, decisions, and follow-up
```

The IC Decision Portal should include a meeting link, calendar context, transcript/capture status, and artifacts where available, but it should not require participants to conduct the actual video call inside West Peek OS.

The portal must be useful when the meeting is:

- a formal IC discussion;
- a partner screen;
- a follow-up IC meeting;
- a founder diligence call that may advance to IC;
- a secondaries decision discussion;
- a reserve or follow-on discussion;
- a phone call;
- an in-person conversation;
- an async investment review.

### 9.6.2B No External Guest Portal in v1

External screen-share-only mode is removed from v1.

West Peek OS is internal-only during meetings. External attendees do not receive portal access, do not see internal notes, do not see AI employee panels, and do not interact with West Peek AI employees.

If Sequoia or Scooter wants an external person to see a West Peek OS surface, the human may screen-share the relevant tab from Zoom or Google Meet.

The system does not need:

- a guest-facing founder portal inside IC;
- external participant permissions inside West Peek OS;
- founder-visible IC screens;
- AI employees appearing in Zoom or Google Meet;
- external attendee chat inside West Peek OS;
- complex guest access controls for v1.

The v1 rule is:

```text
The meeting happens elsewhere.
West Peek OS is the intelligence cockpit.
```

### 9.6.2C AI Workspace Access in IC

The phrase “AI employee admission” means scoped internal access to a West Peek OS workspace. It does not mean admission into the external meeting.

Preferred UI labels:

```text
AI Workspace Access
Grant AI Access to This Room
Admit AI Employees to Workspace
```

The IC Portal should show:

```text
Suggested AI Employees
Admitted AI Employees
Available AI Employees
Revoked AI Employees
```

Controls:

```text
Admit Suggested
Customize
Revoke Selected
Revoke All
```

Admitted AI employees receive scoped access only to:

- this IC workspace;
- approved related company records;
- approved related person records;
- approved documents;
- approved meeting notes;
- relevant machine context;
- assigned task outputs;
- their authorized tool set.

They do not receive general access to unrelated founder records, LP notes, legal matters, secondaries records, brokerage-separated material, or partner-private notes.

### 9.6.2D V1 Internal-Only AI Participation Law

For v1:

```text
No AI employee joins Zoom or Google Meet.
No AI employee appears as a meeting participant.
No AI employee speaks to founders, LPs, vendors, sponsors, advisors, or external guests.
No AI employee sends external messages.
No AI employee represents West Peek externally.
```

AI employees may assist internal West Peek users inside the IC Portal.

Examples:

```text
Poppy, what decision are we actually making?
Paige, what evidence is missing?
Willow, any confidentiality issue?
Whitney, explain this market.
Pierce, strongest bull case?
Walter, capture that Scooter wants two customer calls.
```

These interactions remain internal.

### 9.6.2E Revoke All Control

The IC Portal must include a prominent Revoke All AI Employees control.

When activated:

```text
No AI employee can read the IC room notes.
No AI employee can view attached documents.
No AI employee can answer questions in the room.
No AI employee can process follow-up from the room.
No AI employee can update records from the room.
```

Human users can continue taking notes manually. AI access resumes only if an authorized human re-grants access.

The audit log records:

- who revoked access;
- when access was revoked;
- which AI employees were affected;
- what access was removed;
- whether access was later restored.

### 9.6.2F IC Portal vs. General Meeting Intelligence

General Meeting Intelligence is the default internal sidecar for all meetings.

IC Decision Portal is the specialized internal sidecar for investment decision meetings.

Every IC meeting uses meeting intelligence. Not every meeting needs the IC Portal.

General Meeting Intelligence produces:

- better prep;
- better live help;
- better notes;
- better follow-up;
- better relationship memory.

IC Portal produces:

- investment decision record;
- IC memo update;
- diligence status;
- bull case;
- bear case;
- risk analysis;
- dissent ledger;
- final rationale;
- audit trail.

### 9.6.2G IC Portal Core Tabs

The IC Portal should include:

```text
Company
Memo
Diligence
Market
People
Live Help
Decision
Follow-Up
Audit
```

The Live Help tab contains Walter and any AI employees granted AI Workspace Access.

### 9.6.3 Participant-specific AI prep

Each participant receives a private AI preparation panel. The panel helps the person decide how they want to participate in the meeting without forcing a generic script.

For each participant, the assistant should generate:

- what this person should understand before speaking
- the most important questions this person should ask
- what risks they may be best positioned to probe
- what blind spots they should watch for
- where their prior knowledge or relationship history matters
- suggested talking points
- suggested objections
- suggested support for the deal if they are positive
- suggested conditions to invest
- areas where they should defer to another participant

For Scooter, the default lens should emphasize:

- founder quality
- community insight
- early-stage upside
- founder-market fit
- storytelling and access
- founder support potential
- network leverage

For Sequoia, the default lens should emphasize:

- investment discipline
- financial and operating rigor
- fund construction
- secondaries implications if applicable
- process risk
- terms, ownership, and downside
- operational follow-through

For future participants, the lens should follow their role: principal, associate, analyst, scout, LP observer, advisor, legal, finance, portfolio support, or outside expert.

The private prep panel must not rewrite the participant's judgment. It should sharpen their thinking, surface missing questions, and help them enter the meeting prepared.

### 9.6.4 Live meeting facilitation

The IC Meeting Portal should include an AI IC Facilitator. This facilitator does not decide. It manages meeting quality.

It should:

- open the meeting with the decision required
- keep the discussion tied to the agenda
- surface the memo and evidence at the right time
- identify unanswered questions
- flag when the meeting is drifting
- detect missing diligence areas
- invite dissent explicitly
- separate facts from opinions
- separate founder claims from verified evidence
- identify decision options
- track unresolved conditions
- preserve minority views
- summarize the conversation live
- prepare the decision record

It should not:

- pressure the team toward a yes
- over-optimize for consensus
- hide disagreement
- manufacture confidence
- speak externally
- approve an investment

### 9.6.5 Role-based question engine

The portal must include a question engine that generates high-quality IC questions by lens.

Core question lenses:

- founder obsession and ethics
- market size and urgency
- customer reality
- product quality
- distribution strategy
- community advantage
- traction quality
- revenue quality
- retention and churn risk
- competitive dynamics
- pricing power
- business model quality
- financing risk
- dilution and ownership
- exit paths
- legal or regulatory risk
- portfolio fit
- downside survivability
- asymmetric upside
- whether the opportunity can become a $1B+ outcome

The question engine should produce:

```text
Must-ask questions
Smart optional questions
Role-specific questions
Adversarial questions
Founder-friendly questions
Follow-up questions based on live answers
Questions still unanswered after the meeting
```

### 9.6.6 Live evidence and source panel

During the meeting, participants must be able to open the evidence behind any claim.

Evidence objects include:

- founder deck slide
- memo section
- call transcript excerpt
- email
- CRM note
- Pitch Lab packet
- market research source
- customer reference
- financial model cell or assumption
- valuation source
- legal document
- diligence note

Every important claim should have a source link, confidence label, and owner.

### 9.6.7 Decision capture

The IC portal must capture the decision in a structured way:

- invest / pass / continue diligence / defer / conditional approval
- final authority
- votes or positions if used
- Scooter final decision if unresolved
- Sequoia position
- other participant positions
- rationale
- key risks accepted
- open conditions
- follow-up owner
- target close date
- post-decision review date

The system must preserve disagreement rather than smoothing it away.

### 9.6.8 Decision memory

After the meeting, the portal creates institutional memory:

- final IC memo
- meeting transcript or summary
- decision record
- dissent record
- risk register updates
- follow-up tasks
- founder response draft
- LP-reporting-safe summary if applicable
- future review triggers
- lessons learned

For passed companies, it creates a nurture plan instead of dropping the relationship.

For invested companies, it creates the transition into closing, portfolio onboarding, support planning, and monitoring.

### 9.6.9 Access model

IC Decision Portal access is permissioned by internal role and workspace scope.

Initial human access:

```text
Scooter — full access, final authority
Sequoia — full access, full visibility, recommendation and execution authority
```

Future internal participant classes may include:

- internal team member;
- AI employee;
- venture partner;
- scout;
- outside advisor;
- legal reviewer;
- finance reviewer.

External attendees do not access West Peek OS in v1. LP observer, founder guest, or external advisor portal access is a future extension point only and must not shape v1 architecture.

Sensitive discussions, conflicts, legal matters, brokerage-separated matters, and partner-only deliberations must be separable from broader workspace access.

AI employees receive AI Workspace Access only for the specific workspace, matter, and function granted.

### 9.6.10 IC Portal modes

The IC Portal should support internal workspace modes, not external video-room modes.

```text
Async prep mode
Internal sidecar / live support mode
Partner-only deliberation mode
Decision mode
Post-meeting follow-up mode
Post-mortem mode
Revisit / reopen mode
```

The portal may show a Zoom or Google Meet link, transcript status, meeting capture provider status, or calendar metadata. It does not host the external meeting in v1.

No external screen-share-only mode exists in v1.

### 9.6.11 AI agent participation

The following agents should be able to assist the IC portal:

- AI IC Facilitator
- AI Principal
- AI Associate
- AI Analyst
- AI Research Analyst
- AI Legal and Compliance Analyst
- AI Finance Support
- AI Portfolio Support Manager
- Scooter AI Chief of Staff
- Sequoia AI Chief of Staff

Each agent contributes within its lane. No agent has investment approval authority.

### 9.6.12 Output artifacts

Every IC meeting should produce:

- final decision record
- updated memo
- action list
- unanswered questions
- evidence gaps
- follow-up communication drafts
- updated CRM records
- investment pipeline update
- knowledge graph updates
- audit log entry

### 9.6.13 Why this matters

The IC Meeting Portal makes West Peek better by ensuring:

- every deal is discussed from multiple lenses
- each participant enters prepared
- questions are sharper
- evidence is visible
- dissent is captured
- founder relationships are preserved
- decisions compound into institutional memory
- the firm learns from both investments and passes

### 9.6.14 Meeting Capture Adapter and Walter Intelligence Layer

West Peek OS should not build native meeting transcription or meeting-bot infrastructure in v1.

The correct architecture is:

```text
Zoom / Google Meet / phone / in person
↓
Meeting capture provider or native transcript source
↓
West Peek Meeting Capture Adapter
↓
Walter — Meeting Buddy
↓
West Peek OS workflows, approvals, memory, and updates
```

Meeting capture providers may include, subject to audit, cost, privacy policy, consent requirements, and technical fit:

- Fireflies-style meeting capture APIs;
- Recall-style meeting bot infrastructure;
- native Zoom transcript or recording APIs;
- native Google Meet transcript or recording artifacts;
- uploaded audio files;
- manual notes;
- phone-call notes;
- in-person meeting notes.

The meeting capture provider does the plumbing. Walter does the West Peek intelligence.

External capture providers may join meetings, record, transcribe, return artifacts, and send webhooks, but they do not become the source of institutional truth.

The source of institutional truth is the approved West Peek meeting record after Walter processes the transcript or notes and humans approve relevant updates.

Meeting capture modes:

```text
Not recorded
Manual notes only
Native transcript
Third-party notetaker
West Peek-branded bot via provider infrastructure
Audio upload after meeting
```

Every meeting record should show:

- capture mode;
- consent status;
- provider used;
- transcript location;
- recording link if any;
- privacy label;
- retention rule;
- who may access;
- which AI employees may process it;
- whether follow-up drafts may be generated;
- whether knowledge promotion is allowed.

Sensitive meeting defaults:

```text
LP private notes → closed/private processing
IC deliberation → closed/private processing
secondaries / brokerage-sensitive → restricted processing
legal/compliance → restricted processing
board materials → restricted processing
```


## 9.7 Pass and nurture

- respectful response draft
- human approval
- pass taxonomy
- future trigger conditions
- support options
- next check-in
- community status retained
- no ghosting workflow

## 12.4 Portfolio Performance and Follow-On Decision Center

West Peek OS must include a Portfolio Performance and Follow-On Decision Center.

Purpose:

```text
Identify which portfolio companies are pulling ahead, which are stalling, which need support, and which may justify pro-rata or follow-on consideration.
```

The center should show:

- companies outperforming expectations;
- companies showing winner signals;
- companies gaining customer traction;
- companies with fundraising momentum;
- companies with strong founder execution;
- companies with market tailwinds;
- companies needing support but not more capital;
- companies requiring risk triage;
- companies approaching pro-rata or reserve decisions;
- companies where West Peek should consider follow-on IC review.

Follow-on/pro-rata decision inputs:

- KPI trends;
- founder update quality;
- customer traction;
- revenue quality;
- retention and expansion;
- fundraising momentum;
- market movement;
- relationship signals;
- support outcomes;
- ownership math;
- reserve implications;
- dilution implications;
- portfolio concentration;
- price/valuation;
- downside and opportunity cost;
- fund construction impact;
- conflict or compliance review;
- IC decision history.

West Peek OS may prepare an evidence-linked follow-on/pro-rata recommendation packet, but capital decisions remain human-governed.

```text
West Peek OS may recommend, analyze, and prepare.
Humans decide whether to invest more capital.
```

## 12.5 Portfolio Support for Underperforming Companies

West Peek should remain useful even to companies that are not pulling ahead and even when West Peek cannot or should not invest more money.

Non-capital support may include:

- customer discovery help;
- GTM support;
- fundraising preparation;
- warm intros;
- founder coaching;
- hiring help;
- operator introductions;
- advisor introductions;
- strategic triage;
- morale and community support;
- next-step planning;
- graceful bridge or shutdown planning if appropriate;
- peer founder support.

A company may be classified as not eligible for more capital while still receiving high-touch support.

The portfolio support law is:

```text
Not every company gets more money.
Every founder should feel West Peek remained useful, respectful, and high-touch.
```

Relationship OS should help the entire portfolio by finding:

## 12.9 Portfolio Support Acceptance Standard

Portfolio Support OS is acceptable only when it can:

# 12A. Network OS External Database Law for Relationship OS and Community OS

Network OS is the external West Peek repo/source database for relationship and community-person records. It syncs into West Peek OS, but it is not the same repo or product as West Peek OS.

West Peek OS is the canonical firm operating platform. Relationship OS and Community OS are domains inside West Peek OS. They use synced Network OS data to create intelligence, routing, filters, workflows, follow-through, community views, intro recommendations, and institutional memory candidates.

Relationship OS and Community OS must not create separate competing databases for the same people, organizations, touchpoints, events, preferences, asks, offers, or relationship histories.

## 12A.1 Source-of-truth law

```text
Network OS = external synced source database/repo for people, companies, community members, touchpoints, tags, and relationship records.
West Peek OS = canonical firm operating platform/repo that runs the firm.
Relationship OS = West Peek OS domain that uses Network OS data to create relationship intelligence, connection strategy, follow-through, moments, and relationship capital protection.
Community OS = West Peek OS domain that uses Network OS data to create community segmentation, engagement intelligence, sourcing/support/event filters, and outcome loops.
Machines = department-like executable workflow engines inside OS/domains.
AI employees = governed operators assigned to machines.
```

## 12A.2 Network OS owns

- person records;
- organization records;
- contact fields;
- touchpoints;
- intake status;
- relationship owner;
- public/event intake;
- community membership fields;
- tags and categories where technically supported;
- basic relationship history;
- Gmail-triggered relationship intake;
- Google Sheets persistence;
- shared relationship IDs.

## 12A.3 Relationship OS owns

- why the relationship matters;
- what should happen next;
- warm paths;
- intro suitability;
- relationship capital budget;
- moments and milestones;
- promises made;
- follow-up obligations;
- connection recommendations;
- sender assignment;
- relationship risk;
- machine-specific relationship context;
- external-action approval routing.

## 12A.4 Community OS owns

- community segmentation;
- engagement intelligence;
- founder/investor/operator grouping;
- event participation interpretation;
- contribution patterns;
- community-to-deal sourcing signals;
- community-to-portfolio-support signals;
- programming opportunities;
- ambassador/scout potential;
- community-powered LP proof candidates.

## 12A.5 Writeback law

Any durable community or relationship update discovered by West Peek OS must route back to Network OS if it belongs to the relationship database.

Examples:

- updated company;
- role change;
- new email;
- new tag;
- birthday;
- hometown;
- interest;
- investor type;
- founder status;
- operator expertise;
- event attendance;
- offer to help;
- request for help;
- promised introduction;
- relationship owner change.

West Peek OS may hold temporary working context, private notes, restricted matter notes, and non-promoted intelligence. It should not silently create a second canonical CRM.

## 12A.6 Conflict law

If Network OS, email, calendar, event data, Pitch Lab, VentureDeals, or human notes disagree, the Source-of-Truth Resolver creates a conflict card. Porter and Wells handle verification, and humans approve material merges.

# 13A. Relationship Memory and Connection Intelligence Layer

Relationship intelligence is one of West Peek's unfair advantages. It must not be reduced to CRM fields.

## 13A.1 Relationship Memory

Relationship Memory includes:

- birthdays;
- hometowns;
- family context;
- where someone grew up;
- schools;
- past jobs;
- personal interests;
- causes;
- sports teams;
- restaurants;
- milestones;
- communication preferences;
- things they care about;
- things to avoid;
- how West Peek met them;
- who introduced them;
- what they are building;
- who they want to meet;
- who they can help;
- what West Peek promised;
- what they promised;
- sensitive details that must not be used externally.

Relationship Memory does not automatically become external copy. It exists to make West Peek thoughtful, not creepy.

## 13A.2 Relationship Moments

The system should surface moments that deserve attention:

- birthdays;
- work anniversaries;
- new jobs;
- funding announcements;
- company launches;
- personal milestones;
- event participation;
- long-time-no-touch reminders;
- support opportunities;
- community contribution moments.

## 13A.3 Connection Intelligence / Connection Board

The Connection Board surfaces thoughtful high-value introductions.

It should show:

- who should meet;
- why the match exists;
- mutual benefit;
- relationship owner;
- recommended sender;
- warmth;
- relationship-capital cost;
- sensitivity;
- required permission;
- draft-only vs approval-send;
- suggested intro note;
- expected outcome;
- follow-up owner;
- cooldown impact.

## 13A.4 Make Connection workflow

Humans should not have to decide all sender/workflow details.

When Sequoia or Scooter clicks Make Connection, West Peek OS should:

1. identify the correct sender;
2. check relationship capital budget;
3. check recent asks;
4. check permission sensitivity;
5. check whether external communication is allowed;
6. draft the intro;
7. route approval;
8. send only after approval or create Gmail draft depending on preferences;
9. track whether the intro happened;
10. record outcome in Network OS and Relationship OS.

## 13A.5 Sender Assignment Engine

The Sender Assignment Engine uses:

- relationship ownership;
- last touchpoint;
- who promised the intro;
- warmth;
- credibility;
- context;
- partner authority;
- relationship capital budget;
- sensitivity;
- risk;
- whether the sender has enough context to credibly make the intro.

## 13A.6 Sensitive detail law

```text
AI employees may use sensitive relationship details to inform internal judgment.
They may not use sensitive personal details as external intro hooks unless explicitly approved and appropriate.
```

## 13A.7 Key AI employees

- Winnie — Connection Intelligence / Warm Path Finder;
- Porter — Network OS Sync + Verification Mirror;
- Waverly — Community Manager;
- Wells — Knowledge Manager;
- Wren / Walker — Chief-of-Staff escalation and priority judgment;
- Willow — sensitivity, privacy, and compliance reviewer.

# 15. Event OS

Events are a major West Peek operating capability.

## 15.1 Event types

## 15.2 Event classes

### Micro

Up to approximately 15 people. Lightweight workflow emphasizing curation, seating, hospitality, conversation design, and follow-up.

### Small

Approximately 15 to 75 people. Structured programming, registration, facilitation, attendee communications, and relationship capture.

### Medium

Approximately 75 to 300 people. Full project plan, speakers, sponsors, production, vendors, registration operations, and risk management.

### Large

More than approximately 300 people or materially complex virtual/hybrid production. Full production command, sponsorship, speaker management, contingency plans, support staffing, rehearsals, and evidence capture.

Exact thresholds remain configurable.

Capabilities:

# 17. Market and Industry Intelligence Academy

West Peek OS must help partners, employees, advisors, scouts, interns, and future team members become competent quickly in any market, industry, technology, business model, or skill relevant to the firm.

This is not generic learning content. It is an internal intelligence and training system that converts a topic into usable venture judgment.

Its purpose is to let someone enter West Peek OS and quickly answer:

- what is this market?
- why does it matter now?
- how does money move through it?
- who are the customers?
- who are the incumbents?
- where are startups attacking?
- what are the risks?
- what are the regulatory or ethical issues?
- what questions should West Peek ask a founder?
- what would make this investable?
- how should I talk about it in an IC meeting, LP conversation, founder call, or event?

The Academy should support both speed and rigor. A user should be able to get a five-minute primer, a twenty-hour learning plan, an IC-ready market brief, or a teach-back coaching loop.

## 17.1 Core product concept

Working name:

```text
West Peek Market & Industry Intelligence Academy
```

Alternative UI labels:

```text
Market Academy
Industry Briefing Room
Learn a Market
Get Up to Speed
Market Intelligence Coach
```

Primary command:

```text
Bring me up to speed on [market / industry / business model / technology / skill].
```

Example topics:

- AI infrastructure
- dental practice software
- oncology clinics
- healthcare staffing
- consumer social
- creator economy
- education technology
- fintech compliance
- vertical SaaS
- logistics marketplaces
- cybersecurity for SMBs
- private-company secondaries
- fund administration
- oil and gas non-op investing
- infrastructure finance

## 17.1A Adaptive time and depth selector

The Academy must not produce the same briefing for every user. Every learning flow starts by capturing two controls:

```text
How much time do you have?
How up to speed do you need to be?
```

The user may answer in plain English:

- I have 5 minutes.
- I have 20 minutes before a founder call.
- I have 90 minutes today.
- I have one afternoon.
- I have 10 hours this week.
- I need to sound competent in a meeting.
- I need to support diligence.
- I need to lead the IC discussion.
- I need to become dangerous enough to source here.
- I need to teach this to the team.

The Academy then chooses an output depth.

### Depth levels

#### Level 0 — Orientation

Time: 3-5 minutes.

Goal: know what the market is and why it matters.

Outputs:

- plain-English definition
- what problem it solves
- who buys
- who uses
- how money moves
- one concrete example
- three basic questions

#### Level 1 — Meeting competent

Time: 10-20 minutes.

Goal: survive a founder, LP, or internal meeting without sounding lost.

Outputs:

- one-page brief
- market map
- key vocabulary
- common business models
- top diligence questions
- obvious red flags
- three smart things to ask

#### Level 2 — IC ready

Time: 45-90 minutes.

Goal: contribute to an investment discussion.

Outputs:

- market structure
- incumbents and startup wedges
- buyer/user/economic buyer distinction
- regulation and risk notes
- what makes a startup investable
- what would make West Peek pass
- founder-quality questions
- scorecard
- teach-back check

#### Level 3 — Diligence capable

Time: 4-10 hours.

Goal: support diligence and produce work product.

Outputs:

- 80/20 sprint
- source pack
- competitor map
- customer questions
- pricing and unit economics explanation
- business model benchmark
- market timeline
- thesis hypotheses
- unanswered questions
- diligence workplan

#### Level 4 — Thesis builder

Time: 20+ hours.

Goal: form a view West Peek can use for sourcing, IC, LP conversations, events, or content.

Outputs:

- full learning path
- market memo
- investable wedges
- anti-thesis
- founder archetypes
- sourcing targets
- event topics
- LP-facing explanation
- internal thesis review
- teach-to-team module

### Adaptive Academy command examples

```text
Bring me up to speed on oncology clinic software. I have 15 minutes and need to ask good questions on a founder call.
```

```text
Teach me private-company secondaries. I have 6 hours this week and need to understand enough to support Sequoia.
```

```text
Build a Level 3 diligence path for AI revenue-cycle management. I need to be IC-ready by Thursday.
```

```text
I know vertical SaaS. Use that to teach me dental practice management software in 30 minutes.
```

The Academy must track readiness state so the system knows whether a user has merely skimmed a topic, passed a teach-back, completed diligence training, or is qualified to lead a meeting discussion.

## 17.2 User journeys

### Managing Partner before founder meeting

The partner enters:

```text
Bring me up to speed on AI-powered revenue cycle management for specialty clinics before my founder call.
```

The system returns:

- five-minute one-pager
- market map
- business model explanation
- buyer and user distinction
- incumbent landscape
- startup wedges
- diligence questions
- red flags
- what a strong founder should know
- quick talk track for the meeting

### Associate before diligence

The associate enters:

```text
Build me a 20-hour learning sprint on specialty-clinic software so I can support diligence this week.
```

The system returns:

- 10 two-hour blocks
- one high-signal resource per block
- key questions
- 15-minute checks
- practical deliverables
- final diligence readiness test

### IC participant before meeting

The participant enters the IC Meeting Portal and clicks:

```text
Teach me this market fast.
```

The system generates:

- company-specific market primer
- likely investor debate points
- role-specific questions
- what the participant should listen for
- what would change the investment decision

### LP-facing preparation

A partner enters:

```text
Explain why West Peek is looking at future-of-work tools in a way an LP would respect.
```

The system returns:

- LP-safe market thesis
- evidence level
- caveats
- relevant portfolio or pipeline examples
- claims that need support before use externally

### New employee onboarding

A new team member enters:

```text
I know nothing about secondaries. Get me to working literacy.
```

The system returns:

- plain-English explanation
- five-level skill ladder
- vocabulary
- transaction flow
- common mistakes
- West Peek-specific rules
- compliance boundaries
- teach-back loop

## 17.3 Built-in learning flows

The Academy should include reusable, structured flows rather than forcing users to invent prompts from scratch.

### Flow 1 — The 80/20 Sprint

Use when the user needs working competence fast.

Output:

- 10 two-hour learning blocks
- what matters most
- what to ignore for now
- one resource per block
- one concrete exercise per block
- 15-minute check at the end of each block
- final readiness assessment

West Peek enhancement:

- connect the topic to venture investing
- identify diligence implications
- identify potential sourcing angles
- identify likely founder exaggerations
- identify what LPs may ask

### Flow 2 — The One-Page Brief

Use when the user needs a printable market primer.

Output:

- core concepts
- how the concepts connect
- market structure
- revenue model
- customer/user distinction
- incumbents
- startup wedges
- common mistakes
- one real-world example per concept
- key diligence questions

West Peek enhancement:

- label each claim as sourced, inferred, or unknown
- identify what would make the market attractive or unattractive
- include founder-call questions

### Flow 3 — Teach Me Like I Am New

Use when the user is unfamiliar with the market.

Output:

- plain-English explanation
- no jargon unless defined
- everyday analogies
- story-based explanation
- three comprehension questions
- re-explanation if the user misses something

West Peek enhancement:

- translate the topic into investor language only after the user understands the basics
- show how the market makes money
- show how startups could win or fail

### Flow 4 — Skill Ladder

Use for onboarding, employee growth, and repeatable training.

Output:

- five levels from beginner to teachable competence
- what the user can do at each level
- what to study
- estimated time
- proof of readiness for the next level
- West Peek use cases at each level

Recommended levels:

1. Recognition: I understand basic terms.
2. Explanation: I can explain the market simply.
3. Analysis: I can evaluate a company in this market.
4. Diligence: I can lead questions and identify risks.
5. Teaching: I can brief another team member or IC participant.

### Flow 5 — Beginner Mistakes to Skip

Use when someone is new and likely to waste time.

Output:

- five common traps
- why beginners fall into them
- what to do instead
- venture-specific consequences
- how to recognize the trap in a founder pitch

Examples of traps:

- confusing user with buyer
- mistaking market size for demand
- believing every workflow problem is a venture-scale company
- ignoring distribution
- underestimating regulation
- overvaluing AI wrapper features

### Flow 6 — Teach-Back Loop

Use for mastery and meeting prep.

Workflow:

1. AI explains the topic simply.
2. User repeats it back.
3. AI identifies gaps, errors, and shallow reasoning.
4. AI fills the gaps.
5. User repeats again.
6. Loop continues until the user can explain it clearly.

West Peek enhancement:

- include founder-meeting version
- include IC-meeting version
- include LP-safe version
- include “what not to say yet” if evidence is weak

### Flow 7 — Questions That Actually Test Me

Use when memorization is not enough.

Output:

- 10 questions that test understanding
- trick questions
- applied scenarios
- explanation of what each answer reveals
- targeted remediation

West Peek enhancement:

- investor judgment questions
- founder evaluation questions
- market-structure questions
- unit-economics questions
- distribution questions
- moat questions

### Flow 8 — Connect to What I Already Know

Use when the user has a familiar anchor.

Input:

```text
I understand [familiar topic]. Teach me [new topic] through that lens.
```

Output:

- similarities
- differences
- false analogies
- where prior knowledge helps
- where prior knowledge misleads
- new vocabulary mapped to familiar vocabulary

West Peek enhancement:

- compare adjacent markets
- connect early-stage pattern recognition to late-stage evidence
- show how West Peek’s community or secondaries lens changes the analysis

### Flow 9 — Real Scenarios, Real Practice

Use for applied judgment.

Output:

- five realistic situations
- guided walkthrough of the first scenario
- independent attempts for the next four
- correction after each attempt
- final readiness judgment

Examples:

- founder claims the market is huge
- customer says the pain is real but budget is unclear
- incumbent launches similar feature
- regulation changes reimbursement economics
- distribution depends on channel partners

## 17.4 Market Intelligence Brief output

Every market brief should have a standard structure.

Default sections:

1. Plain-English summary
2. Why the market matters now
3. Market map
4. Customer, user, buyer, and beneficiary
5. Workflow and pain points
6. Existing solutions and incumbents
7. Startup wedges
8. Business models
9. Distribution channels
10. Unit economics considerations
11. Regulatory, legal, ethical, or compliance issues
12. Market size and reality check
13. Diligence questions
14. Founder-quality signals
15. Red flags
16. West Peek angle
17. IC debate points
18. LP-safe explanation
19. Evidence table
20. Unknowns and next research tasks

## 17.5 Evidence and source discipline

The Academy must not produce confident nonsense.

Every output should separate:

```text
Sourced facts
Inferences
Hypotheses
Unknowns
Questions requiring research
Claims safe for internal use only
Claims safe for external use after approval
```

The system must show source links or internal document references when available.

If the user asks for a current market view, the Research Agent must search current sources and date the brief.

If sources disagree, the brief must show the disagreement rather than flattening it.

If the system cannot verify a claim, it must say so.

## 17.6 Integration with IC Meeting Portal

The IC Meeting Portal should include an embedded Academy panel.

For each company under discussion, participants can open:

- Market 101

- Competitive landscape

- Risks I may be missing
- Teach-back mode
- Role-specific prep
- What would make this investable?
- What would make us pass?

The portal should generate different prep for:

- Scooter
- Sequoia
- outside IC member
- associate
- analyst
- advisor
- guest founder presenter

External participants should never receive West Peek OS access in v1. If a human screen-shares, only the intentionally shared surface should be visible.

## 17.7 Integration with employee onboarding

The Academy should become part of West Peek onboarding.

Each new employee or contractor receives learning paths for:

- West Peek strategy
- venture basics
- pre-seed and seed investing
- secondaries basics
- community-led sourcing
- founder support
- LP fundraising
- compliance and confidentiality
- event operations
- internal tools
- approval rules

The system should track:

- assigned learning paths
- completed modules
- teach-back quality
- readiness level
- areas needing reinforcement
- permissions unlocked after training

No training completion should automatically grant sensitive permissions. Permission changes require human approval.

## 17.8 Integration with sourcing and thesis development

When West Peek sees repeated activity in a market, the Academy should help turn scattered curiosity into an institutional thesis.

Triggers:

- three or more companies in one market
- repeated founder questions
- repeated LP questions

- secondary opportunity in a new sector
- external market shock

Outputs:

- incumbent map

- LP talking points

## 17.9 Data model

New or extended entities:

- `Market`

## 17.10 Agent support

The Academy requires a dedicated agent role:

```text
Learning and Market Intelligence Coach
```

Mission:

- help West Peek users understand markets quickly and accurately
- convert research into usable investing judgment
- create structured learning paths
- test understanding
- improve team readiness before meetings
- prevent shallow pattern matching

This agent works with:

- Research Analyst
- Investment Analyst

- IC Facilitator
- LP Relations Manager
- Event Planner
- Marketing Lead
- Partner Chiefs of Staff

The agent may prepare internal learning content and internal briefings autonomously. External use of its outputs requires the normal approval process.

## 17.11 User interface

Primary entry points:

- global command bar: “Bring me up to speed on...”
- company page: “Learn this market”
- IC portal: “Prep me for this discussion”
- LP page: “Explain this thesis for this LP”
- event page: “Build audience primer”
- employee onboarding page: “Start learning path”
- knowledge graph: Market node

Core screens:

- Market Home
- Market Brief
- Skill Ladder
- Learning Sprint
- Teach-Back Session
- Scenario Practice
- IC Prep Panel
- Source and Evidence Panel
- Market Map
- Readiness Score

## 17.12 Quality standard

A good Academy output should make the user:

- understand the market faster
- ask better questions
- avoid basic mistakes
- see how the business makes money
- understand buyer behavior
- identify distribution risk
- recognize moat claims
- distinguish hype from evidence
- speak credibly in meetings
- know what remains unknown

A bad Academy output is:

- generic
- overconfident
- unsourced
- too academic
- not connected to investing
- not connected to West Peek’s strategy
- not useful in a meeting
- unable to distinguish fact from assumption

## 17.13 V1 implementation scope

V1 should include:

- command: “Bring me up to speed on [topic]”
- one-page market brief generator
- 80/20 learning sprint generator
- teach-me-like-I-am-new mode
- skill ladder generator
- beginner mistakes mode
- teach-back loop
- test-me question mode
- connect-to-what-I-know mode
- real scenario practice mode
- source/evidence table
- company-page integration
- IC portal integration
- save-to-Knowledge-OS workflow

V1 does not need a full LMS. It needs fast, useful, evidence-aware learning flows integrated into the investment workflow.


# 17A. Research & Intelligence Machine

West Peek OS must treat research as an operating machine, not as occasional AI summaries.

Purpose:

```text
Give West Peek persistent, evidence-based information advantage across companies, founders, markets, LPs, secondaries, portfolio support, and IC decisions.
```

The Research & Intelligence Machine should help West Peek become a world-class investment firm by improving speed, depth, memory, evidence quality, and information asymmetry.

## 17A.1 Research Edge Layers

West Peek research uses multiple layers of signal.

### Layer 1 — Commodity private-market data

Potential sources may include evaluated providers such as PitchBook, Crunchbase, Preqin, CB Insights, Dealroom, Tracxn, Harmonic, and similar private-market datasets.

These sources may provide useful company, funding, investor, market, LP, and private-market context. They do not create durable edge by themselves.

### Layer 2 — Startup discovery and signal data

Potential signals:

- newly formed companies;
- founder movement;
- hiring momentum;
- product launches;
- website changes;
- developer traction;
- social and community signals;
- investor interest;
- category momentum.

### Layer 3 — Public official records

Potential sources:

- SEC filings;
- Form D filings;
- state incorporation records;
- officer/director records;
- trademarks;
- patents;
- grants;
- government contracts;
- regulatory filings;
- nonprofit/foundation filings;
- public company filings.

### Layer 4 — West Peek proprietary data

This is where the strongest asymmetry may live.

Sources:

- Network OS relationships;
- West Peek community;
- event attendance;
- founder conversations;
- Pitch Lab activity;
- founder support requests;
- LP conversations;
- secondaries market pattern recognition;
- intros made;
- promises made;
- meeting transcripts;
- internal notes;
- community expertise;
- warm paths;
- who keeps showing up;
- what operators say privately.

### Layer 5 — Human judgment

West Peek OS should sharpen Sequoia and Scooter. It should not replace them.

The research machine should help answer:

```text
What should we believe?
What should we doubt?
What do we know that others probably do not?
What claim is unsupported?
What would make this a billion-dollar outcome?
What would kill this company?
What does our community know that the market data misses?
```

## 17A.2 Research Adapter Architecture

External data sources should plug into adapters, not the core.

Potential adapters:

```text
PitchBook Adapter
Crunchbase Adapter
Preqin Adapter
CB Insights Adapter
Dealroom Adapter
Tracxn Adapter
Harmonic Adapter
SEC / EDGAR Adapter
Company Registry Adapter
Patent / Trademark Adapter
People / Company Enrichment Adapter
Google Search / News Adapter
Network OS Adapter
Meeting Transcript Adapter
West Peek Live Event Signal Adapter
```

All adapters normalize into the West Peek Research Evidence Layer.

Standard output objects:

- source;
- claim;
- evidence;
- signal;
- company match;
- person match;
- investor match;
- funding event;
- market tag;
- confidence score;
- freshness date;
- license restriction;
- contradiction flag.

Provider disagreement should be preserved, not flattened.

Example:

```text
Company A
Provider 1: Seed, $2.1M raised
Provider 2: Pre-seed, amount undisclosed
Founder deck: $2.5M raised
Status: Conflicting
Action: verify with founder, filings, or investor reference
```

## 17A.3 Evidence Object Standard

Research outputs must become evidence objects, not just prose summaries.

Every material claim should include:

- claim text;
- source;
- source date;
- captured date;
- confidence;
- evidence type;
- related company/person/market/fund;
- internal-only / external-safe status;
- LP-safe status;
- IC-only status;
- contradiction status;
- missing information;
- freshness;
- review date;
- owner.

Research quality law:

```text
No important claim without source, confidence, and use boundary.
```

## 17A.7 Information Asymmetry Labels

West Peek OS should label edge type:

```text
Public data edge
Paid data edge
Relationship edge
Community edge
Timing edge
Interpretation edge
Secondaries edge
Founder-support edge
Event edge
Pattern-recognition edge
```

Example:

```text
Public data edge: recent hiring and incorporation filings
Paid data edge: nonpublic-like signal visible in licensed data source
Relationship edge: founder knows three West Peek community members
Community edge: two operators independently confirm the pain
Interpretation edge: most investors classify this as agtech, but the real buyer is operations software
West Peek edge: we can diligence through community faster than a generic fund
```

## 17A.8 Research AI Employees

Paige — Research Analyst owns source gathering, market/company/founder research, competitor mapping, claim ledger, and unknowns.

Wyatt — Analyst owns metrics extraction, funding/valuation math, ownership scenarios, benchmark tables, and data normalization.

Whitney — Market Intelligence Coach owns market learning, IC prep, teach-back, founder-call prep, and LP-safe explanations.

Pierce — Principal owns investment judgment, bull/bear case, why now, why this founder, what must be true, and pass conditions.

Willow — Compliance / Privacy owns use boundaries, LP-safe vs IC-only claims, privacy, compliance, and brokerage/fund separation.

Wells — Knowledge Manager owns promotion into Knowledge OS, provenance, stale-claim removal, and decision linkage.

Walter — Meeting Buddy owns live meeting claim capture, contradictions, open questions, and routing research updates from conversations.

## 17A.9 Provider Strategy

West Peek should not start by buying every data provider.

Recommended sequence:

1. Build the Research Evidence Layer first.
2. Use free, public, internal, and proprietary West Peek data early.
3. Add one startup/private-market provider after workflow need is clear.
4. Add one people/company enrichment provider after workflow need is clear.
5. Add institutional private-market providers when budget and use case justify them.

Provider evaluation should consider:

- coverage;
- API availability;
- data rights;
- export rules;
- cost;
- refresh rate;
- entity matching quality;
- duplicate/contradiction handling;
- privacy constraints;
- whether data can be stored, cached, or only referenced.

## 17A.10 Research Law

```text
West Peek OS research must not be a pile of AI summaries.

It must be a governed Research & Intelligence Machine that ingests public, paid, proprietary, and human-sourced data; converts it into evidence-linked claims, signals, maps, memos, questions, and decisions; and compounds every meeting, event, founder interaction, LP conversation, IC decision, and portfolio update into institutional intelligence.
```

The goal:

```text
West Peek knows faster.
West Peek understands deeper.
West Peek remembers better.
West Peek acts with better judgment.
```
