# VC Role Operating Model, Agentic Workflows, and Meeting Buddy Layer

## 1. Purpose

West Peek OS must not merely list AI employees. Each AI employee must operate like a real functional role in a venture firm, with recurring daily work, manual run-now requests, owned state, handoffs, approvals, escalation rules, and measurable outputs.

This integrated requirement defines:

- every role currently outlined in the West Peek OS master plan;
- what that role handles in a real VC firm;
- the agentic workflows each role runs;
- the seven-day daily operating rhythm;
- cross-agent handoffs;
- meeting-buddy capabilities for IC, founder, VC, LP, board, vendor, and internal meetings.

## 2. Universal AI Employee Operating Standard

Every AI employee must have:

1. a role charter;
2. a daily start time and end time;
3. an owned work queue;
4. an owned database or state table;
5. read-only access to upstream agent outputs as permitted;
6. no write access to another agent's state unless explicitly authorized;
7. approval gates for outbound or externally visible actions;
8. a daily report to the relevant human owner;
9. escalation rules;
10. audit logs for all meaningful work.

Manual commands can be issued at any time from the West Peek OS command center, partner personal offices, role dashboards, meeting portals, CRM records, deal records, LP records, event records, and knowledge pages.

## 3. Meeting Buddy Layer

West Peek OS must include a generic Meeting Buddy layer that can operate across meeting types. The Investment Committee Meeting Portal is one specialized version of this layer, but the meeting system must support all core venture-firm meetings.

### 3.1 Meeting Buddy Modes

- Investment Committee meeting buddy;
- founder meeting buddy;
- LP meeting buddy;
- other VC / co-investor meeting buddy;
- board meeting buddy;
- portfolio support meeting buddy;
- diligence call buddy;
- partner meeting buddy;
- internal operating meeting buddy;
- vendor / legal / fund administration meeting buddy;
- event planning meeting buddy.

### 3.2 Meeting Buddy Operating Pattern

Before the meeting, the Meeting Buddy prepares:

- meeting objective;
- participant list;
- relevant records;
- history of prior interactions;
- open questions;
- likely agenda;
- risks and sensitivities;
- recommended questions;
- role-specific talking points;
- documents to review;
- desired outcome;
- follow-up actions likely to be needed.

During the meeting, the Meeting Buddy can operate as:

- embedded meeting room companion;
- sidecar in West Peek OS while the meeting happens in Google Meet, Zoom, phone, or in person;
- note-taking and issue-capture interface;
- question prompt engine;
- decision and commitment capture layer;
- risk and unresolved-question tracker.

After the meeting, the Meeting Buddy produces:

- summary;
- commitments;
- decision log;
- follow-up tasks;
- draft emails;
- CRM updates;
- deal/LP/portfolio/event record updates;
- relationship intelligence;
- open questions;
- escalation items;
- next-step recommendations.

No external follow-up is sent without human approval.

## 4. Role-by-Role Operating Model and Workflows

### 4.1 Scooter AI Chief of Staff

Real VC firm function: strategic partner-office operator for Scooter's early-stage, founder, community, partnership, LP, and brand responsibilities.

Handles:

- daily Scooter briefing;
- early-stage pipeline prioritization;
- founder meeting prep;
- community opportunity review;
- partner meeting prep;
- decision queue organization;
- coordination with Scooter AI Executive Assistant;
- coordination with investment, community, events, and fundraising agents;
- identification of decisions that require Scooter as final authority.

Daily workflows:

1. Morning Command Brief: scans pipeline, meetings, approvals, follow-ups, relationship changes, and urgent founder/community matters.
2. Founder Priority Review: identifies the founders Scooter should focus on today.
3. Community Signal Review: surfaces community members, events, or founder needs that may create investment or relationship value.
4. Decision Prep: organizes decisions into approve, defer, reject, delegate, or discuss.
5. End-of-Day Closure: confirms what was completed, what slipped, and what needs tomorrow's attention.

Manual workflows:

- prepare me for this founder meeting;
- summarize this company for Scooter;
- build Scooter's talking points;
- review this deal from Scooter's POV;
- create a partner meeting agenda.

### 4.2 Scooter AI Executive Assistant

Real VC firm function: calendar, inbox, logistics, travel, follow-through, and administrative coordination for Scooter.

Handles:

- calendar triage;
- meeting scheduling drafts;
- pre-read packets;
- inbox summarization;
- follow-up tracking;
- meeting logistics;
- reminders;
- approval packet preparation.

Daily workflows:

1. Calendar Scan: identifies meetings requiring prep, travel, reschedule, or materials.
2. Inbox Triage: flags urgent founder, LP, partner, community, and internal messages.
3. Prep Packet Assembly: compiles meeting prep using records from Investment OS, Relationship OS, Community OS, and Fundraising OS.
4. Follow-Up Tracker: checks promised follow-ups and prepares drafts.
5. Schedule Hygiene: identifies conflicts, low-priority meetings, and missing context.

Manual workflows:

- prepare Scooter for tomorrow;
- draft follow-up from this meeting;
- find open calendar windows;
- assemble this founder pre-read.

### 4.3 Sequoia AI Chief of Staff

Real VC firm function: strategic partner-office operator for Sequoia's secondaries, operations, systems, finance, execution, and fundraising responsibilities.

Handles:

- daily Sequoia briefing;
- secondaries pipeline review;
- operational risk review;
- systems implementation oversight;
- finance/admin review;
- execution queue prioritization;
- approval queue preparation;
- coordination with Sequoia AI Executive Assistant;
- coordination with LP, secondaries, operations, finance, legal, and systems agents.

Daily workflows:

1. Morning Command Brief: scans money work, secondary opportunities, operations, fundraising, approvals, and implementation risks.
2. Secondaries Review: summarizes opportunities, conflicts, missing documents, next actions, and fund-vs-brokerage separation flags.
3. Operating Risk Review: checks overdue tasks, vendor issues, system failures, fund admin/legal/compliance deadlines, and owner gaps.
4. Execution Queue Build: ranks the highest-leverage tasks for Sequoia.
5. End-of-Day Closure: captures decisions, incomplete loops, and next-day handoffs.

Manual workflows:

- prep me for this LP call;
- prep me for this secondary opportunity;
- audit this workflow;
- build my decision packet;
- tell me what is blocked.

### 4.4 Sequoia AI Executive Assistant

Real VC firm function: calendar, inbox, logistics, admin, follow-through, and execution support for Sequoia.

Handles:

- calendar triage;
- email/inbox triage;
- pre-read packet assembly;
- meeting notes;
- follow-up drafts;
- approval reminders;
- task reminders;
- day-planning support.

Daily workflows:

1. Calendar Scan: flags preparation needs, conflicts, and high-value meetings.
2. Inbox Triage: identifies urgent LP, secondary, vendor, legal, internal, and founder messages.
3. Prep Packet Assembly: builds concise pre-reads from Relationship OS, Fundraising OS, Secondaries OS, and Operations OS.
4. Follow-Up Tracker: prepares drafts for promised actions.
5. Approval Monitor: surfaces items waiting on Sequoia.

Manual workflows:

- make my prep packet;
- draft follow-up;
- summarize this thread;
- remind me what I promised;
- show me today's highest-leverage moves.

### 4.5 Principal

Real VC firm function: senior investment judgment support; owns screening depth, founder evaluation, deal prioritization, and investment narrative quality.

Handles:

- early-stage screening;
- second-stage diligence design;
- investment thesis quality;
- founder quality assessment;
- market framing;
- IC recommendation shaping;
- deal prioritization;
- partner decision support.

Daily workflows:

1. Pipeline Review: reviews new and active deals and ranks them by strategic fit and urgency.
2. Founder Quality Review: checks founder obsession, ethics, distribution, customer reality, resilience, and clarity.
3. Deal Path Assignment: recommends pass, nurture, diligence, IC prep, or partner discussion.
4. IC Memo Review: improves investment memos for decision quality.
5. Partner Briefing: gives MPs the highest-conviction opportunities and biggest concerns.

Manual workflows:

- evaluate this founder;
- build the case for investment;
- build the case against investment;
- identify the one thing that would change our mind;
- prepare the IC framing.

### 4.6 Associate

Real VC firm function: diligence operator and pipeline manager; turns promising opportunities into structured diligence packages.

Handles:

- diligence checklist;
- customer/reference research;
- competitor mapping;
- market sizing;
- traction analysis;
- fundraising history;
- cap table/request tracking;
- founder follow-up requests;
- diligence-room organization.

Daily workflows:

1. Active Diligence Sweep: checks every active diligence item and missing evidence.
2. Founder Request Tracker: identifies what has been requested, received, overdue, or unclear.
3. Market/Competition Update: refreshes competitive and category intelligence for active deals.
4. Customer/Reference Prep: drafts reference questions and organizes answers.
5. Diligence Summary: updates deal status and next actions.

Manual workflows:

- run diligence on this company;
- build competitor map;
- summarize traction;
- draft founder data request;
- prepare customer-reference questions.

### 4.7 Analyst

Real VC firm function: research, financial modeling, market maps, metrics normalization, and data-room support.

Handles:

- metrics extraction;
- market comps;
- valuation benchmarks;
- ownership math;
- dilution and fund-return scenarios;
- data-room indexing;
- KPI normalization;
- research tables.

Daily workflows:

1. Metrics Normalization: extracts and standardizes revenue, growth, churn, burn, runway, margin, customers, and fundraising metrics.
2. Ownership/Fund Math: calculates check-size, ownership, reserve, return-contribution, and portfolio-construction impact.
3. Benchmark Sweep: finds comparable companies and category benchmarks.
4. Data-Room Indexing: tags uploaded docs and flags missing files.
5. Analyst Note: creates concise numerical takeaways for each active company.

Manual workflows:

- model this check size;
- calculate ownership and return case;
- extract metrics from this deck;
- compare this company to comps;
- build a diligence table.

### 4.8 Research Analyst

Real VC firm function: market, company, people, sector, and thesis research.

Handles:

- company background;
- founder background;
- market research;
- industry primers;
- emerging themes;
- competitive intelligence;
- source-based claim tracking;
- unknowns ledger.

Daily workflows:

1. Active Deal Research: refreshes research for active deals.
2. Market Signal Scan: identifies category shifts relevant to West Peek's sectors and sector-agnostic opportunities.
3. Founder/Company Background Check: compiles public information and flags inconsistencies.
4. Research Claim Ledger: links claims to evidence and marks unsupported claims.
5. Research Brief Delivery: sends summarized findings to investment agents and MPs.

Manual workflows:

- research this market;
- bring me up to speed on this industry;
- build a one-page brief;
- find red flags;
- verify this claim.

### 4.9 IC Facilitator

Real VC firm function: meeting preparation, facilitation, decision discipline, evidence management, dissent capture, and post-IC recordkeeping.

Handles:

- IC portal setup;
- agenda;
- participant-specific prep;
- question prompts;
- evidence panel;
- decision options;
- dissent ledger;
- decision record;
- follow-up tasks;
- audit trail.

Daily workflows:

1. Upcoming IC Scan: identifies companies scheduled for IC or ready for IC.
2. IC Readiness Review: checks whether memo, market, diligence, financials, conflicts, and open questions are complete.
3. Participant Prep: builds role-specific prep for each participant.
4. Question Engine: generates decision-quality questions and red-team prompts.
5. Post-IC Packaging: writes decision, dissent, evidence, and next steps.

Manual workflows:

- open IC portal for this company;
- run IC Meeting Buddy for this external meeting;
- generate questions for Scooter;
- generate questions for Sequoia;
- capture decision and dissent.

### 4.10 Generic Meeting Buddy

Real VC firm function: meeting intelligence layer for all recurring and ad hoc firm meetings.

Handles:

- founder meetings;
- LP meetings;
- VC/co-investor meetings;
- board meetings;
- diligence calls;
- portfolio support calls;
- vendor calls;
- internal operating meetings;
- IC meetings as a specialized mode.

Daily workflows:

1. Meeting Readiness Scan: reviews calendar and identifies meetings needing prep.
2. Context Packet Build: compiles relevant records, prior notes, relationship history, open tasks, and goals.
3. Question Prep: generates role-specific questions and talking points.
4. Live Sidecar Support: captures notes, commitments, objections, decisions, and follow-ups.
5. Post-Meeting Conversion: turns the meeting into CRM updates, tasks, draft follow-ups, deal updates, LP updates, and knowledge entries.

Manual workflows:

- be my meeting buddy for this call;
- prep me for this board meeting;
- help me ask better founder questions;
- capture notes from this conversation;
- turn this meeting into follow-ups.

### 4.11 Scout Coordinator

Real VC firm function: manages scouts, community referrals, founder referrals, and inbound sourcing loops.

Handles:

- scout/referral pipeline;
- source attribution;
- referral follow-up;
- community signals;
- inbound founder routing;
- source-quality tracking;
- warm intro coordination drafts.

Daily workflows:

1. Referral Intake Sweep: checks inbound referrals and community-sourced companies.
2. Source Attribution: tags who sourced the deal and through what channel.
3. Quality Scoring: tracks source quality over time.
4. Follow-Up Drafting: prepares warm follow-up drafts for human approval.
5. Scout Relationship Review: identifies scouts/referrers who need attention.

Manual workflows:

- review this referral;
- find who introduced this company;
- rank our best sources;
- draft scout follow-up;
- build a source-quality report.

### 4.12 Fundraising Associate

Real VC firm function: LP pipeline creation, LP research, qualification, materials coordination, and meeting preparation.

Handles:

- LP sourcing;
- LP enrichment;
- LP scoring;
- LP pipeline stages;
- LP meeting prep;
- data-room readiness;
- follow-up tracking;
- relationship status.

Daily workflows:

1. LP Candidate Review: scans sources and new candidates from LP Sourcer.
2. LP Fit Scoring: reviews thesis fit, check-size fit, emerging-manager friendliness, activity, geography, and relationship path.
3. LP Pipeline Hygiene: updates stage, next step, owner, and last touch.
4. LP Meeting Prep: prepares briefs for upcoming LP calls.
5. LP Follow-Up Queue: drafts follow-ups for approval.

Manual workflows:

- find LPs like this;
- prep this LP call;
- score this LP;
- update LP pipeline;
- identify warm paths.

### 4.13 LP Sourcer

Real VC firm function: top-of-funnel LP discovery.

Handles:

- candidate discovery;
- source capture;
- duplicate detection;
- basic category classification;
- staging-table creation.

Daily workflows:

1. Source Sweep: scans approved LP discovery sources.
2. Candidate Extraction: extracts names, firms, type, geography, source URL, and reason for relevance.
3. Duplicate Check: compares to existing LP database.
4. Staging Write: writes raw candidates to LP staging table.
5. Handoff: notifies Enrichment & Scoring.

Manual workflows:

- find healthcare-focused family offices;
- find emerging-manager-friendly LPs;
- find foundations with venture exposure;
- find fund-of-funds backing first-time managers.

### 4.14 Enrichment & Scoring Agent

Real VC firm function: turns raw LP candidates into evaluated prospects.

Handles:

- background research;
- firm/domain resolution;
- likely email pattern generation;
- thesis-fit scoring;
- check-size estimation;
- recent-activity analysis;
- warm-path hints.

Daily workflows:

1. Read LP Staging: pulls candidates from LP Sourcer output.
2. Research Candidate: enriches public background and firm profile.
3. Resolve Domain: identifies official firm domain and likely email format.
4. Score LP: scores thesis fit, check size, emerging-manager fit, recent activity, geography, and relationship potential.
5. Handoff: writes scored records for verification and CRM sync.

Manual workflows:

- enrich this LP;
- score this family office;
- find likely email pattern;
- explain why this LP fits.

### 4.15 CRM Sync & Verification Mirror Agent

Real VC firm function: keeps LP pipeline human-readable and technically verified.

Handles:

- CRM updates;
- Google Sheets mirror;
- email verification status;
- sync state;
- deduplication;
- field normalization.

Daily workflows:

1. Read Scored LPs: pulls from enrichment database.
2. Verify Contact Data: runs approved email verification checks.
3. Sync CRM: creates or updates LP records.
4. Mirror to Sheet: updates human-readable pipeline tracker.
5. Handoff: writes verification and sync status for Warm Path Finder and Outreach Composer.

Manual workflows:

- sync this LP to CRM;
- show verification status;
- mirror this pipeline to Sheets;
- dedupe LP records.

### 4.16 Warm Path Finder

Real VC firm function: discovers relationship paths and personalization signals before outreach.

Handles:

- shared connections;
- podcast/interview references;
- conference activity;
- thesis alignment;
- recent fund closes;
- portfolio overlap;
- community overlap;
- observational fallback sentence.

Daily workflows:

1. Read Verified LPs: pulls verified LPs from CRM Sync.
2. Search for Warm Signals: finds credible personalization or relationship context.
3. Build Warm Path Sentence: writes one concise sentence explaining why this LP is relevant.
4. Shared Topic Tagging: assigns the outreach theme.
5. Handoff: sends approved candidates to Outreach Composer.

Manual workflows:

- find a warm path to this LP;
- write personalization for this investor;
- find shared themes;
- identify who could introduce us.

### 4.17 Outreach Composer

Real VC firm function: drafts compliant LP outreach and follow-up sequences.

Handles:

- outreach drafts;
- follow-up drafts;
- template selection;
- personalization insertion;
- compliance pre-check;
- send scheduling requests;
- morning outreach report.

Daily workflows:

1. Read Approved LP Candidates: pulls high-score verified LPs with warm paths.
2. Filter Contacts: excludes low-confidence, generic, restricted, and previously vetoed records.
3. Compose Drafts: creates plain-text messages using counsel-approved templates.
4. Compliance Lint: routes drafts through compliance linter.
5. Approval Queue: queues messages for Sequoia or assigned MP approval before sending.

Manual workflows:

- draft outreach to this LP;
- write follow-up;
- summarize today's top LPs;
- prepare approval queue;
- pause outreach sequence.

### 4.18 Compliance Linter

Real VC firm function: checks outbound fundraising, investor, and public-facing drafts against firm policy and counsel-approved rules.

Handles:

- prohibited language detection;
- return-promise detection;
- fund-term disclosure checks;
- attachment/data-room warnings;
- solicitation-risk flags;
- required disclaimers if counsel approves;
- escalation to legal/human review.

Daily workflows:

1. Draft Intake: reviews queued fundraising, LP, PR, marketing, and investor-facing drafts.
2. Policy Check: compares against locked compliance policy.
3. Risk Labeling: marks pass, needs edits, blocked, or counsel review.
4. Edit Suggestions: proposes safer language.
5. Audit Log: records rule hits and final status.

Manual workflows:

- lint this LP email;
- check this post;
- flag risky language;
- rewrite safer.

### 4.19 LP Relations Manager

2. Meeting Prep: creates briefs for upcoming LP meetings.
3. Objection Tracker: logs concerns and prepares responses.
4. Materials Tracker: ensures requested materials are ready or assigned.
5. Relationship Health: scores priority and next action.

Manual workflows:

- prep this LP meeting;

- draft follow-up;
- build LP update;
- show fundraising risks.

Real VC firm function: rapidly upskills partners, employees, and IC participants on markets, sectors, business models, and investment concepts.

1. Active Deal Market Prep: identifies markets tied to upcoming meetings or IC.
2. Time-Adaptive Briefing: creates 5-minute, 20-minute, 1-hour, 4-hour, or deep-dive paths depending on user need.
3. Knowledge Gap Tracking: tracks who needs to learn what before a meeting.
4. Teach-Back Review: evaluates user explanations and fills gaps.
5. Academy Archive: stores reusable market briefs.

Manual workflows:

- bring me up to speed on this market in 20 minutes;
- make me IC-ready on this industry;
- teach me this like I am new;
- test my understanding;
- create a one-page market brief.

## 5. Seven-Day Daily Operating Rhythm

Each agent must have a defined daily work window. The default model is:

- Morning scan and preparation;
- Midday execution and handoffs;
- Afternoon approval preparation and reporting;
- Evening archive, cleanup, and next-day setup;
- Weekend lighter but still active monitoring, research, drafting, and cleanup.

Agents can be manually invoked at any time, but scheduled work must stay within assigned windows unless a human requests an immediate run or emergency escalation.

## 6. Core Cross-Agent Workflow Families

### 6.1 LP Fundraising Pipeline

LP Sourcer -> Enrichment & Scoring -> CRM Sync & Verification Mirror -> Warm Path Finder -> Outreach Composer -> Compliance Linter -> Human Approval -> LP Relations Manager -> Fundraising Associate -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.2 Early-Stage Deal Pipeline

Scout Coordinator / Community Manager / Pitch Lab / Network OS -> Associate -> Analyst -> Research Analyst -> Principal -> IC Facilitator -> MPs -> Portfolio Support Manager or Pass/Nurture workflow -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.3 Secondaries Pipeline

Sequoia AI Chief of Staff -> Secondaries intake workflow -> Legal Analyst conflicts review -> Analyst valuation/ownership work -> Research Analyst company research -> Principal investment framing -> IC Facilitator -> MPs -> Fund Admin Coordinator / Finance Support -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.4 Founder Meeting Workflow

Meeting Buddy -> Scooter AI Chief of Staff or Sequoia AI Chief of Staff -> Associate/Research Analyst prep -> live sidecar -> post-meeting notes -> CRM update -> deal update -> follow-up drafts -> approval -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.5 Board Meeting Workflow

Meeting Buddy -> Portfolio Support Manager -> Finance Support / Analyst KPI prep -> Research Analyst market context -> talking points -> live notes -> commitments -> board follow-up -> portfolio record update -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.6 Event Workflow

Community Manager / Marketing Lead -> Event Planner -> PR Lead / Content Manager -> Operations Manager -> Meeting Buddy for planning calls -> approval gates -> West Peek Live execution -> post-event CRM capture -> Portfolio/Investment/Fundraising handoffs -> Knowledge Manager.

### 6.7 Weekly Partner Operating Workflow

Scooter AI Chief of Staff + Sequoia AI Chief of Staff -> Operations Manager -> Finance Support -> Fundraising Associate -> Principal -> Community Manager -> Event Planner -> Systems Operator -> weekly review packet -> decision log -> agent task assignments -> Knowledge Manager.

## 7. Approval Law

Internal work may be performed autonomously inside the system. External-facing work must be routed for human approval before sending, publishing, inviting, committing, promising, spending, or representing a decision.

Approval is required for:

- LP outreach;
- founder responses;
- investor updates;
- public posts;
- press communication;
- event invitations;
- meeting invites;
- vendor commitments;
- legal positions;
- fund terms;
- investment decisions;
- brokerage/fund handoffs;
- any communication that could bind or represent West Peek.

## 8. VC Role Operating Model Acceptance Criteria

West Peek OS v1.5 is acceptable only if:

1. every AI employee has a role charter;
2. every role has daily work, not just a title;
3. workflows have upstream and downstream handoffs;
4. meeting buddy works across founder, LP, VC, board, IC, vendor, and internal meetings;
5. IC Portal remains a specialized meeting-buddy mode;
6. LP fundraising agents produce real pipeline work but require human approval for external actions;
7. agents have owned state and audit logs;
8. manual run-now requests are available from relevant records and dashboards;
9. no agent can silently make external commitments;
10. the MPs can see what each role did, what is pending, what needs approval, and what matters next.

# West Peek AI Workforce Machines and Employee Roster

## 1. Governing Concept

West Peek OS does not contain a loose set of AI helpers. It contains operating machines.

An operating machine is a coordinated group of AI employees, databases, queues, schedules, approval gates, reports, and handoffs that performs a real business function inside the firm.

Each machine must have:

- a business owner;
- assigned AI employees;
- a daily start and end window;
- one or more owned databases;
- a staging queue;
- a human approval queue when external action is involved;
- internal audit logs;
- daily output;
- manual run-now commands;
- failure escalation.

The LP Fundraising Agent Subsystem is the first clear example. The same operating-machine model should apply across the full firm.

## 2. Core West Peek Operating Machines

### 2.1 LP Fundraising Machine

Purpose: build, qualify, personalize, track, and advance the LP pipeline.

Handles:

- LP discovery;
- enrichment;
- fit scoring;
- email verification;
- warm-path research;
- outreach drafting;
- compliance linting;
- human approval;
- relationship follow-up;
- meeting prep;
- data-room requests;
- LP objection tracking.

Primary AI employees:

- Piper — LP Sourcer;
- Perry — Enrichment and Scoring Agent;
- Porter — CRM Sync and Verification Mirror;
- Winnie — Warm Path Finder;
- Penn — Outreach Composer;
- Willow — Compliance Linter;
- Wesley — LP Relations Manager;
- Wren — Sequoia AI Chief of Staff.

Default daily flow:

```text
Piper
→ Perry
→ Porter
→ Winnie
→ Penn
→ Willow
→ Human Approval
→ Wesley
→ Wren
→ Knowledge Manager
```

Human controls:

- Sequoia can veto, pause, resume, approve, reject, or reassign.
- Scooter has final firm authority if fundraising claim, strategy, or legal exposure is implicated.
- No outbound LP message leaves the system without human approval unless counsel later approves a specific automated workflow.

```text
→ human approval or review
→ finance/legal archive
→ Knowledge Manager
```

### 2.12 Brand, Content, Marketing, and PR Machine

Purpose: turn West Peek's point of view, community, events, founder support, and fund narrative into approved public-facing assets without creating legal or reputational risk.

Handles:

- marketing campaigns;
- content calendar;
- social drafts;
- essays;
- newsletters;
- event recaps;
- founder resources;
- PR opportunities;
- media prep;
- launch plans;
- brand consistency;
- compliance routing.

Primary AI employees:

- Percy — Marketing Lead;
- Prue — PR Lead;
- Pippa — Content Manager;
- Willow — Compliance Linter;
- Waverly — Community Manager;
- Parker — Event Planner;
- Wesley — LP Relations Manager for LP-safe language.

Default daily flow:

```text
Knowledge / event / community / LP signal
→ Percy
→ Pippa / Prue
→ brand and compliance review
→ human approval
→ publish queue
→ performance and relationship capture
→ Knowledge Manager
```

### 2.13 Systems, Data, and Integration Machine

Purpose: keep the forked Odysseus system, Network OS, Pitch Lab, West Peek Live, Dilution Dashboard, email, calendar, data stores, and automations healthy.

Handles:

- sync failures;
- job failures;
- stale tokens;
- auth issues;
- data integrity;
- duplicate records;
- release readiness;
- audit logs;
- integration status;
- security/access review.

Primary AI employees:

- Wilson — Systems Operator;
- Porter — CRM Sync and Verification Mirror;
- Pax — Operations Manager;
- Wren — Sequoia AI Chief of Staff;
- Knowledge Manager.

Default daily flow:

```text
System health scan
→ Wilson
→ Porter / Pax
→ issue classification
→ fix recommendation or escalation
→ human review when needed
→ system log and daily report
```

## 3. Named AI Employee Roster

West Peek OS should give the AI employees names so the system feels like an actual operating staff. Names should start with W or P for brand cohesion.

The system should use illustrated or generated avatar portraits, not photographs of real people without rights and consent. The right implementation is a consistent West Peek avatar style: polished, editorial, warm, diverse, non-photorealistic or clearly AI-generated. This avoids identity, consent, and trust issues.

## 4. Employee Roster UI Requirements

The Employee Roster should be a first-class screen in West Peek OS.

Each employee profile should show:

- name;
- avatar;
- role;
- machine assignment;
- current status;
- working hours;
- current queue;
- last output;
- owned database;
- allowed tools;
- approval limits;
- escalation owner;
- daily report history;
- manual commands;
- audit log.

Status options:

- working;
- waiting on handoff;
- waiting on human approval;
- blocked;
- paused;
- completed today's run;
- failed and escalated.

Manual commands:

- run now;
- pause;
- resume;
- re-run last task;
- explain current work;
- show queue;
- show last output;
- escalate to Scooter;
- escalate to Sequoia;
- send to legal review;
- archive result;
- convert to task;
- convert to meeting prep;
- convert to memo.

## 5. Avatar Policy

West Peek OS may use avatars to make the AI workforce easier to navigate, but it should not use photographs of real people unless the firm owns the rights and has explicit consent.

Recommended approach:

- illustrated avatar portraits;
- consistent West Peek visual system;
- diverse but non-tokenizing character design;
- professional, warm, modern, VC-firm appropriate;
- no fake claims that the agents are humans;
- clear label: AI employee.

The goal is to make the workforce legible, not deceptive.

## 6. Daily Workforce Scheduling Model

Every AI employee has a daily work window, including weekends.

The schedule model should support:

- start time;
- end time;
- recurrence pattern;
- priority level;
- maximum tasks per run;
- manual run-now trigger;
- pause/resume controls;
- owner override;
- failure retry limit;
- escalation path;
- daily report time.

Agents should keep working through their assigned window, but not outside their authority.

They can:

- research;
- classify;
- draft internally;
- update internal records;
- prepare recommendations;
- generate tasks;
- prepare meeting materials;
- prepare approval packets;
- summarize;
- score;
- route;
- monitor.

They cannot, without human approval:

- contact outside humans;
- send emails;
- publish content;
- schedule external meetings;
- make promises;
- commit capital;
- approve investments;
- approve legal language;
- spend money;
- change fund terms;
- share confidential information.

## 7. Machine Dashboard Requirements

West Peek OS should have a Machine Dashboard separate from the Employee Roster.

The dashboard should show:

- all machines;
- today's status;
- queued work;
- completed work;
- blocked work;
- pending approvals;
- handoff failures;
- data errors;
- top recommendations;
- human decisions needed;
- machine-specific KPIs.

Example machine KPIs:

LP Fundraising Machine:

- LPs sourced;
- LPs enriched;
- LPs verified;
- warm paths found;
- drafts pending approval;
- meetings booked;
- LP objections logged.

Early-Stage Deal Machine:

- new companies reviewed;
- deals moved to diligence;
- deals moved to nurture;
- IC-ready companies;
- pass reasons captured;
- founder follow-ups pending.

Meeting Intelligence Machine:

- meetings prepped;
- meetings summarized;
- commitments captured;
- follow-ups drafted;
- CRM updates completed;
- unresolved open loops.

Market Academy Machine:

- briefings generated;
- user readiness scores;
- teach-back sessions;
- markets archived;
- IC prep packets supported.

## 8. Implementation Notes for the Odysseus Fork

The Odysseus fork should implement AI employees as persistent agent profiles, not one-off prompts.

Each agent profile needs:

- identity metadata;
- machine membership;
- permissions;
- tools;
- memory scope;
- schedule;
- owned database or namespace;
- input queues;
- output queues;
- approval rules;
- reporting rules;
- escalation rules.

Agent state should not be one giant shared soup. The correct model is:

```text
Firm Knowledge Graph
+ shared read access where appropriate
+ agent-owned working state
+ machine-owned queues
+ human approval records
+ immutable audit log
```

Agents may read from prior-agent databases by approved cross-agent query, but they should not directly write to another agent's owned state. Handoffs should happen through queues and canonical records.

## 9. Acceptance Criteria

West Peek OS v1.6 planning baseline is acceptable when:

- all AI employees have names;
- every role maps to a real VC-firm function;
- every role belongs to at least one operating machine;
- every machine has a purpose, workflow, handoff model, and output;
- the employee roster UI is specified;
- the avatar policy is non-deceptive;
- daily work schedules are modeled;
- manual run-now requests are supported;
- external actions remain approval-gated;
- audit logging is mandatory;
- implementation notes are tied back to the Odysseus fork.

# First-Name AI Employee Roster and Concurrent Agent Operations

## 1. Roster Naming Law

West Peek OS must distinguish real humans from AI employees at a glance.

Real people use full legal or professional names. The founding Managing Partners are:

```text
Sequoia Taylor — real human Managing Partner
Scooter Taylor — real human Managing Partner
```

AI employees use first names only, plus a visible AI badge in the product UI.

```text
Walker — AI Chief of Staff for Scooter
Wendy — AI Executive Assistant for Scooter
Wren — AI Chief of Staff for Sequoia
Willa — AI Executive Assistant for Sequoia
```

The system must not present AI employees as human employees, contractors, or real people. Every AI employee profile must include:

```text
First name
AI Employee badge
Machine assignment
Role title
Current work state
Allowed tools
Approval limits
Audit log
Escalation owner
```

## 2. Duplicate First-Name Rule

If two AI employees naturally share the same first name, West Peek OS may use a first name plus middle initial or machine suffix, not a full human-style name.

Approved examples:

```text
Parker — Event Planner
Perrin — Fund Admin Coordinator
Winnie — Warm Path Finder
Whitney — Market Intelligence Coach
```

The product should still display them as AI employees, not humans.

## 3. Concurrent Agent Operations Law

AI employees can operate in multiple places at once because they are software agents, not human staff. However, concurrency must be governed.

An AI employee may have multiple simultaneous task instances, such as:

```text
Walter supporting an LP meeting buddy session
Walter preparing a board meeting packet
Walter summarizing a founder call
```

But each task instance must have its own:

```text
Task ID
Machine context
Matter context
Permissions scope
Input sources
Output destination
Human owner
Approval gate
Audit trail
Start time
End time
Status
```

The UI should show this as one AI employee with multiple active work cards, not as multiple fake people.

## 4. Concurrency Risk Controls

Concurrent AI work creates operational risk if unmanaged. West Peek OS must prevent:

```text
Cross-matter leakage
Using one founder's private data in another founder's meeting
Mixing LP and founder contexts
Mixing brokerage and fund contexts
Conflicting recommendations from stale state
Duplicate outreach drafts
Multiple agents touching the same external party without coordination
Unapproved external action
```

The system therefore needs concurrency locks and context boundaries.

## 5. Context Boundary Model

Every agent task runs inside a context container.

Core context containers:

```text
Firm-wide context
Partner-private context
LP context
Founder/company context
Investment opportunity context
Secondaries context
Portfolio company context
Event context
Board meeting context
Legal/compliance context
Brokerage-separated context
```

Agents may read across containers only when permissions allow and the task requires it. External outputs must cite which context containers were used.

## 6. Work Queue Model

Each AI employee has one identity but many queues.

Example:

```text
Walter
- IC Meeting Buddy queue
- Founder Meeting Buddy queue
- LP Meeting Buddy queue
- Board Meeting Buddy queue
- Internal Partner Meeting queue
```

Each queue can run concurrently, but queue outputs cannot merge unless an approved workflow explicitly combines them.

## 7. Priority and Collision Rules

When concurrent tasks collide, priority order is:

```text
1. Human safety / legal / compliance escalation
2. Live meeting support
3. Time-sensitive LP/fundraising tasks
4. Active investment decision support
5. Partner daily briefs
6. Scheduled recurring machine work
7. Long-form research or academy work
8. Cleanup, enrichment, and knowledge hygiene
```

If two tasks need the same external action, the system must merge them into one human approval item instead of creating duplicate drafts.

## 8. Human Control Rule

Concurrent does not mean autonomous external execution.

AI employees may work in parallel internally. They may not independently:

```text
Send external emails
Send calendar invites
Publish content
Contact LPs
Contact founders
Contact other VCs
Contact media
Contact vendors
Make commitments
Represent investment interest
Represent fund terms
Represent legal or compliance conclusions
```

Those actions require human approval unless a future counsel-approved automation policy explicitly permits a narrow exception.

## 9. Product UX Requirement

The AI Employee Roster should show each employee as one profile with multiple live work cards.

Example:

```text
Walter — AI Meeting Buddy
Status: Working
Active tasks: 4
1. IC prep for Company A
2. Founder meeting sidecar for Company B
3. LP call prep for Family Office C
4. Board packet summary for Portfolio Co D
```

Each card should be clickable into inputs, outputs, permissions, and audit log.

## 10. Implementation Requirement

The Odysseus fork must support agent task instances, not just agent personas.

Required backend concepts:

```text
Agent profile
Agent role
Agent machine assignment
Agent task instance
Agent queue
Context container
Permission scope
Tool scope
Approval item
Audit event
Output artifact
Task lock
Collision detector
```

This is mandatory for West Peek OS to feel like a real AI workforce instead of a chatbot with role prompts.

# MODEL GOVERNANCE, LLM PLUGINS, AI EMPLOYEES, AND CLOSED-CIRCUIT PRIVACY

## 1. Why This Requirement Exists

West Peek OS must support two different AI patterns:

1. **LLM Plugin Mode** — a user intentionally asks an external or internal model to help with a bounded task.
2. **AI Employee Mode** — a named AI employee performs assigned work inside a governed operating machine with state, schedules, permissions, logs, handoffs, and approval gates.

These are not the same thing.

The fork must make the difference obvious in the user interface, data architecture, permission model, audit logs, and security settings.

The system must also allow West Peek to move between:

- external model assistance when useful;
- approved API model use with data controls;
- local/private model use;
- fully closed-circuit mode where no firm data is sent to external LLM APIs.

The governing security law:

```text
West Peek data must never leave the environment by accident.
```

## 2. Core Definitions

### 2.1 LLM Plugin

An **LLM plugin** is a model endpoint used for one bounded task.

Examples:

- Gemini for broad web-aware analysis.
- Perplexity for quick market scan and source discovery.
- Claude for long-form document reasoning.
- OpenAI for structured reasoning, drafting, extraction, or coding help.
- Local Llama / Qwen / Mistral / Mixtral / other local models for private reasoning.

A plugin has no independent job, schedule, memory ownership, or firm role unless wrapped by West Peek OS governance.

It answers the user’s immediate request.

### 2.2 AI Employee

An **AI employee** is a named operator inside West Peek OS.

An AI employee has:

- first-name identity;
- role;
- assigned machine;
- daily work schedule;
- owned database/state;
- queue;
- allowed tools;
- permission scope;
- escalation owner;
- approval limits;
- audit trail;
- recurring outputs;
- manual commands;
- memory scope.

An AI employee may use one or more models under the hood, but the user should not have to think about the raw model unless choosing privacy or quality mode.

```text
Model = engine.
AI employee = governed operator.
Machine = business workflow.
```

## 3. When an LLM Plugin Is Enough

Use an LLM plugin when the task is:

- one-time;
- low-risk;
- user-directed;
- not part of a recurring workflow;
- not writing to a system of record;
- not making decisions;
- not contacting anyone;
- not handling sensitive/private firm data unless in approved private mode.

Examples:

- “Summarize this public article.”
- “Explain this market concept.”
- “Brainstorm event themes.”
- “Rewrite this paragraph.”
- “Compare these three public companies at a high level.”
- “Give me a one-page primer on dental SaaS.”
- “Help me understand this legal term, but do not store it.”

Default output is ephemeral unless the user saves it.

## 4. When an AI Employee Is Required

Use an AI employee when the task is:

- recurring;
- scheduled;
- attached to a machine;
- needs memory or state;
- writes to CRM, deal records, LP records, task systems, meeting logs, or knowledge graph;
- involves handoffs between agents;
- requires approval gates;
- affects firm priorities;
- could create compliance, legal, relationship, or reputational risk;
- needs auditability.

Examples:

- LP sourcing pipeline.
- IC prep.
- founder meeting prep and follow-up.
- portfolio support tracking.
- legal/compliance issue spotting.
- fund admin deadlines.
- event planning.
- weekly partner packet.
- market academy employee readiness tracking.
- secondaries pipeline conflict review.

## 5. Model Routing Architecture

West Peek OS must include a **Model Router** between users/AI employees and the available model providers.

The router decides which model class may be used based on:

- data sensitivity;
- user privacy mode;
- matter type;
- agent role;
- task type;
- required capability;
- cost budget;
- latency requirement;
- provider availability;
- external-data requirement;
- firm policy.

### 5.1 Model Classes

```text
Class A — Local/private models
Runs inside West Peek-controlled infrastructure.
Best for sensitive firm data, internal records, private memos, LP lists, deal materials, meeting notes, and confidential workflows.

Class B — Approved external API models
External model providers allowed only under policy, logging, redaction, and matter-level permission controls.
Best for high-quality reasoning where data sensitivity permits.

Class C — Web/research answer engines
Used for public-market, industry, company, news, and source discovery.
Must not receive confidential West Peek data unless explicitly approved and redacted.

Class D — User-selected plugin mode
User manually chooses a model for a bounded task.
System must show what data will be sent before execution when sensitive context is involved.
```

## 6. Privacy Modes

The Odysseus fork must expose a simple privacy control that is understandable to non-technical users.

### 6.1 Open Research Mode

Purpose: public-market research and source discovery.

Allowed:

- public web search;
- external LLM APIs;
- public company/market data;
- public documents;
- non-confidential prompts.

Blocked by default:

- LP lists;
- private CRM notes;
- founder confidential materials;
- deal memos;
- IC notes;
- board materials;
- secondaries records;
- legal/conflict materials;
- nonpublic investment rationale.

### 6.2 Approved External Model Mode

Purpose: use high-capability external models for firm work when approved.

Controls:

- matter-level permission required;
- sensitive fields redacted by default;
- user sees outbound data preview for high-risk matters;
- logs provider, model, prompt hash, document IDs, and output ID;
- no silent transmission of private records;
- legal/compliance blocks can override user preference.

### 6.3 Hybrid Private Mode

Purpose: local/private model handles sensitive material; external models may be used only on sanitized summaries.

Example:

```text
Private local model reads IC memo.
System creates sanitized market question with no company name, founder names, LP names, or confidential facts.
External model researches public market data.
Local model merges public research back into private memo.
```

### 6.4 Closed-Circuit Mode

Purpose: maximum privacy.

Rules:

- no external LLM APIs;
- no external model calls;
- no public web calls unless separately allowed as browser-only public research with no private context;
- no prompt, file, CRM record, meeting note, LP record, IC memo, founder material, or internal task leaves West Peek infrastructure;
- local models only;
- local embeddings only;
- local retrieval only;
- local document processing only;
- local audit logs.

Closed-circuit mode can be activated at:

- global workspace level;
- machine level;
- matter level;
- document level;
- meeting level;
- AI employee level;
- manual task level.

The UI label should be unmistakable:

```text
Closed Circuit: ON
No external LLM calls. No firm data leaves West Peek OS.
```

## 7. Sensitivity Labels

Every object in West Peek OS should carry a sensitivity label.

```text
PUBLIC
INTERNAL
CONFIDENTIAL
HIGHLY CONFIDENTIAL
LEGAL / COMPLIANCE
LP PRIVATE
FOUNDER CONFIDENTIAL
IC CONFIDENTIAL
SECONDARIES RESTRICTED
BROKERAGE-SEPARATION SENSITIVE
BOARD CONFIDENTIAL
```

Model routing must respect these labels.

Default policy:

```text
PUBLIC → external models allowed
INTERNAL → external models allowed only with firm policy
CONFIDENTIAL → local/private by default
HIGHLY CONFIDENTIAL → closed-circuit by default
LEGAL / COMPLIANCE → closed-circuit unless counsel-approved
LP PRIVATE → closed-circuit by default
FOUNDER CONFIDENTIAL → closed-circuit by default
IC CONFIDENTIAL → closed-circuit by default
SECONDARIES RESTRICTED → closed-circuit by default
BROKERAGE-SEPARATION SENSITIVE → closed-circuit by default
BOARD CONFIDENTIAL → closed-circuit by default
```

## 8. Data Egress Control

West Peek OS must have a **Data Egress Firewall**.

Before any model call, tool call, webhook, email, integration, or API request sends data outside the environment, the firewall checks:

- what data is leaving;
- where it is going;
- which user or agent requested it;
- which matter it belongs to;
- which sensitivity label applies;
- whether the destination is approved;
- whether redaction is required;
- whether human approval is required;
- whether the action violates closed-circuit mode.

If blocked, the system returns:

```text
Blocked: This request would send [data category] to [destination]. Current matter is [privacy mode]. Use local/private model or request approval.
```

## 9. Redaction and Sanitization Layer

Before approved external-model use, the system should be able to create sanitized prompts.

Redactable fields:

- founder names;
- company names;
- LP names;
- emails;
- phone numbers;
- addresses;
- financial terms;
- cap table details;
- valuation details;
- proprietary thesis language;
- nonpublic metrics;
- meeting notes;
- legal conclusions;
- broker-dealer references;
- counterparty names;
- confidential attachments.

Sanitization output should include a redaction report:

```text
Sent externally:
- public market category
- non-identifying company profile
- generalized traction band
- generalized question

Not sent externally:
- company name
- founder names
- revenue details
- investor names
- deal terms
- internal recommendation
```

## 10. Model Use Ledger

Every model invocation must be logged.

Log fields:

```text
Invocation ID
Timestamp
User or AI employee
Machine
Matter
Model provider
Model name
Privacy mode
Sensitivity label
Input source IDs
Prompt hash
Output ID
External data sent: yes/no
Redaction applied: yes/no
Human approval required: yes/no
Human approval received: yes/no
Cost estimate
Latency
Failure status
Escalation owner
```

The ledger should be searchable by:

- provider;
- matter;
- AI employee;
- machine;
- sensitivity label;
- external calls;
- blocked calls;
- cost;
- date range.

## 11. AI Employee Model Policy

Each AI employee should have allowed model classes.

Example:

```text
Walter — Meeting Buddy
Default: local/private
May use external: only for public participant/company research after sanitization
Closed-circuit required: board meetings, IC deliberation, LP private notes

Piper — LP Sourcer
Default: external web/research models allowed because top-of-funnel uses public data
Closed-circuit required: existing LP CRM, relationship notes, private scoring rationale

Willow — Legal / Compliance
Default: closed-circuit
May use external: never by default; requires explicit legal approval

Winnie — Market Intelligence Coach
Default: public research allowed for generic market education
Closed-circuit required when tied to confidential deal, IC memo, or proprietary thesis

Pierce — Principal
Default: local/private for investment judgment
May use external: only for sanitized public-market research
```

## 12. User Interface Requirements

### 12.1 Model Mode Toggle

Visible in the command bar:

```text
Model Mode:
[Auto Governed] [Local Only] [Approved External] [Open Research] [Closed Circuit]
```

Default should be **Auto Governed**.

Auto Governed means West Peek OS chooses the safest allowed route based on data sensitivity and task type.

### 12.2 Per-Matter Privacy Panel

Each deal, LP, portfolio company, event, meeting, or legal matter should show:

```text
Privacy mode
Sensitivity label
Allowed models
Blocked models
Allowed tools
External calls allowed: yes/no
Last external model call
Last blocked egress event
Approval owner
```

### 12.3 External Call Confirmation

For sensitive matters, the user must see:

```text
This action may send data to an external LLM provider.
Data category: [summary]
Provider: [provider]
Redaction: [on/off]
Reason: [why requested]
Approve / Use local model / Cancel
```

### 12.4 Closed-Circuit Badge

Closed-circuit matters must show a persistent badge:

```text
CLOSED CIRCUIT
Local/private processing only
```

## 13. External Model Kill Switches

West Peek OS must include kill switches at multiple levels.

```text
Global external LLM kill switch
Machine-level external LLM switch
AI-employee external LLM switch
Matter-level external LLM switch
Document-level external LLM switch
Meeting-level external LLM switch
User-session external LLM switch
```

When global kill switch is on:

- all external LLM calls blocked;
- all external embedding calls blocked;
- all external OCR/document-intelligence calls blocked unless explicitly whitelisted;
- all scheduled agents must use local/private models or pause with a clear blocked status;
- no silent fallback to external models.

## 14. Closed-Circuit Readiness Requirements

To become truly closed circuit, West Peek OS must support:

- local LLM serving;
- local embeddings;
- local vector database;
- local document parsing;
- local OCR where possible;
- local speech-to-text if meeting transcription is needed;
- local text-to-speech if voice agents are used;
- local model router;
- local agent runtime;
- local logs;
- local encrypted storage;
- local backups;
- no hidden telemetry;
- no external model fallback.

Odysseus already appears directionally aligned because it is designed as a self-hosted, local-first AI workspace with local model/API support, agents, tools, memory, documents, email, tasks, calendar, and privacy-oriented positioning. Final implementation still requires a source audit before relying on any specific privacy guarantee.

## 15. Model Decision Examples

### Example A — Public Market Primer

User: “Bring me up to speed on dental SaaS in 20 minutes.”

Allowed:

- Open Research Mode;
- Perplexity/Gemini/web tools;
- public sources;
- market academy archive.

AI employee:

- Winnie may run the Academy flow.

### Example B — Confidential Founder IC Memo

User: “Help me sharpen the investment case for this founder.”

Required:

- local/private model;
- no external LLM unless sanitized;
- IC confidential label;
- invocation log.

AI employees:

- Pierce, Priya, Wyatt, Paige, Poppy.

### Example C — LP Prospect Discovery

Agent: Piper finds public LP candidates.

Allowed:

- public web/research models;
- EDGAR/IAPD/990/podcasts/fund pages;
- no existing private relationship notes sent externally.

### Example D — LP Relationship Notes

Agent: Wesley prepares for an LP call using CRM notes.

Required:

- closed-circuit by default;
- local/private model;
- no external LLM;
- LP Private label.

### Example E — Board Meeting Buddy

Agent: Walter preps board meeting notes.

Required:

- closed-circuit;
- board confidential label;
- no external LLM;
- no external transcription unless explicitly approved.

## 16. Implementation Tasks for the Odysseus Fork

1. Audit existing Odysseus model-provider architecture.
2. Identify where model calls are created.
3. Insert West Peek Model Router before every model invocation.
4. Insert Data Egress Firewall before every external API/tool call.
5. Add sensitivity labels to records/documents/matters/meetings.
6. Add privacy-mode state to workspace/machine/matter/document/meeting/task.
7. Add model use ledger.
8. Add external-model kill switches.
9. Add redaction/sanitization layer.
10. Add UI indicators for model mode and closed-circuit state.
11. Add AI employee model policies.
12. Add local-only fallback behavior.
13. Add blocked-call reporting to Systems Operator.
14. Add compliance reporting for external model use.
15. Add tests proving closed-circuit mode blocks external calls.

## 17. Acceptance Criteria

West Peek OS v1.9 is acceptable only if:

- users can distinguish plugin use from AI employee work;
- external model use is visible and governed;
- closed-circuit mode can be activated globally and per matter;
- sensitive matter data is local/private by default;
- external calls are logged;
- blocked calls are logged;
- no AI employee silently sends sensitive data to external models;
- no external provider receives private records unless explicitly allowed;
- scheduled agents pause or reroute safely when external model access is disabled;
- the system can show a complete model-use audit trail.

## 18. Final Law

```text
External models are optional engines.
West Peek OS is the governing system.
AI employees are governed operators.
Private firm data stays private unless West Peek explicitly decides otherwise.
```

# FRONTIER KNOWLEDGE CAPTURE, PRIVATE REUSE, AND KNOWLEDGE AIRLOCK ARCHITECTURE

## 1. Why This Requirement Exists

West Peek OS needs to use the power of frontier LLMs without turning private firm data into uncontrolled provider input.

The goal is not to pretend West Peek can download the full internal knowledge of OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, Perplexity, or any future model. That is not how model APIs work. The goal is to build a controlled intelligence pipeline:

```text
Frontier models + public web + approved public sources
→ structured public intelligence outputs
→ West Peek review / provenance / confidence checks
→ private West Peek Knowledge Bank
→ local / closed-circuit reuse through retrieval and local models
```

The practical law:

```text
Do not try to extract the model's brain.
Use frontier models to manufacture structured, source-aware public intelligence packets.
Store those packets privately.
Reuse them locally.
Refresh them only when needed.
```

This lets West Peek benefit from frontier reasoning and broad public knowledge while keeping confidential firm records, LP data, founder materials, IC memos, board notes, relationship intelligence, secondaries records, and legal/compliance matters inside the West Peek-controlled environment.

## 2. Two privacy concepts that must work together

West Peek OS now has two separate but connected privacy layers.

### Layer 1 — Model Privacy Gate

This controls which brain is allowed to touch a task.

```text
Local Mode
Frontier Mode
Lockdown Mode
```

#### Local Mode

Default for private firm work.

Allowed engines:

```text
Local Qwen
Local Llama
Local Mistral
Local DeepSeek
Local Gemma
Future approved local models
Private fine-tuned or privately hosted models
```

Used for:

```text
IC memos
Founder confidential materials
LP notes
Relationship intelligence
Secondaries records
Board notes
Internal strategy
Legal/compliance prep
Private meeting summaries
Portfolio company data
Partner decision rationale
```

Goal:

```text
Nothing leaves West Peek-controlled infrastructure.
```

#### Frontier Mode

Used intentionally for approved external models.

Examples:

```text
OpenAI
Anthropic
Gemini
Perplexity
OpenRouter
Future approved APIs
```

Before use, West Peek OS must show:

```text
FRONTIER MODE ACTIVE
DATA MAY LEAVE WEST PEEK INFRASTRUCTURE
```

The user must see:

```text
Provider
Purpose
Data being sent
Matter context
Sensitivity label
Retention / policy note if configured
Approval status
```

No silent routing.

#### Lockdown Mode

Maximum privacy.

```text
No external LLM APIs
No web
No Perplexity
No Gemini
No Claude
No OpenAI
No OpenRouter
No external embeddings
No external document processors
No external transcription
```

Used for:

```text
Restricted secondaries matters
Sensitive legal/compliance matters
Founder confidential records
LP confidential records
Private partner strategy
Board materials
Brokerage/fund separation-sensitive matters
```

Goal:

```text
Local only. Closed circuit. No exceptions unless explicitly lifted by authorized human.
```

### Layer 2 — Privacy Airlock System

This controls what is allowed through the wall even when Frontier Mode is used.

```text
Local Core
↓
Outbound Airlock
↓
Frontier Chamber
↓
Inbound Airlock
↓
Knowledge Promotion Gate
↓
Local Core
```

## 3. Local Core

The Local Core is the private West Peek vault.

Contains:

```text
Firm Knowledge Graph
Network OS relationship data
LP pipeline
Founder pipeline
IC memos
Secondaries records
Portfolio records
Meeting transcripts and notes
Internal decisions
Partner notes
Event intelligence
Legal/compliance records
Fund admin records
Financial records
Documents
Private memory
Agent state databases
```

Default rule:

```text
Local Core data does not leave by default.
```

## 4. Outbound Airlock

Before anything leaves the Local Core, West Peek OS asks:

```text
What is being sent?
Why is it being sent?
Which provider receives it?
What matter does it relate to?
What sensitivity label applies?
Is the content public, sanitized, abstracted, or confidential?
Who approved it?
What should be logged?
```

### Allowed outbound patterns

Good outbound request:

```text
Ask a frontier model to explain public dynamics of dental practice management software using no private West Peek deal data.
```

Better outbound request:

```text
Ask a frontier model to produce a public market primer on dental practice management software, using only public facts and requiring citations/source links where possible.
```

Bad outbound request:

```text
Send the founder's full pitch deck, private revenue, investor notes, and IC memo to an external LLM by default.
```

### Sanitized abstraction pattern

When private context is useful but cannot leave, the Local Core creates an abstracted prompt:

Private input:

```text
Company X has $1.2M ARR, 18% monthly growth, 7 enterprise customers, churn issue in Segment B, founder reference concern, and a disputed customer concentration issue.
```

Outbound abstraction:

```text
Analyze a generic early-stage B2B vertical SaaS company with low-seven-figure ARR, strong growth, limited enterprise customer count, segment-specific churn risk, and customer concentration concerns. Do not assume any named company.
```

The frontier model can help with general reasoning, but it never receives the actual private company identity or confidential details.

## 5. Frontier Chamber

The Frontier Chamber is a temporary work zone.

It can use approved external models for:

```text
Public market primers
General industry education
Public-source synthesis
Generic diligence question generation
Non-confidential brainstorms
Public trend scans
Public company comparables
Public LP landscape research
General memo structure suggestions
Generic risk frameworks
```

It cannot directly access:

```text
Full knowledge graph
Full CRM
Full LP database
Full founder records
Full IC archive
Full secondaries pipeline
Full portfolio records
Private meeting archive
Agent private databases
```

Every frontier call must produce a Model Use Ledger event.

Ledger fields:

```text
timestamp
requesting user or AI employee
action ID
task ID
matter ID
provider
model
mode
purpose
input sensitivity
output sensitivity
redaction applied
approval source
retention policy note
result stored? yes/no
candidate knowledge created? yes/no
```

## 6. Inbound Airlock

The Inbound Airlock is more important than the outbound side.

After a frontier model responds, West Peek OS asks:

```text
Should any of this become firm knowledge?
What claims did it make?
What sources support those claims?
What is uncertain?
What is outdated risk?
What should expire?
What should be reviewed by a human?
Where should it be stored?
```

Default:

```text
Conversation ≠ Knowledge
External output ≠ Truth
AI output ≠ Firm memory
```

## 7. Knowledge Promotion Gate

Nothing becomes permanent firm knowledge automatically.

Flow:

```text
Frontier response
→ Candidate Intelligence Packet
→ Source/provenance check
→ Confidence/risk labels
→ Human or delegated approval
→ Knowledge Bank promotion
→ Local reuse
```

Promotion options:

```text
Approve
Approve with edits
Send for review
Archive as raw output
Reject
Delete
Refresh later
```

Promoted knowledge must include:

```text
topic
summary
claims
sources
source dates
model used
created date
reviewer
confidence
expiry date
related markets
related deals
related LPs
related portfolio companies
usage permissions
```

## 8. The Knowledge Bank is how West Peek captures frontier power privately

The West Peek Knowledge Bank stores frontier-assisted intelligence as structured private assets.

It includes:

```text
Market Primers
Industry Maps
Company Comparables
Thesis Briefs
Diligence Question Banks
Risk Pattern Libraries
LP Landscape Briefs
Founder Support Playbooks
Portfolio Support Playbooks
Event Topic Briefs
IC Prep Packs
Beginner Explainers
Skill Ladders
Teach-Back Assessments
```

Each asset becomes reusable in Local Mode and Lockdown Mode through retrieval.

That means a future private task can run like this:

```text
Private founder deck stays local
Local model reads private deck
Local model retrieves West Peek's stored public market primer
Local model combines private facts + stored public intelligence
No external LLM sees the private deck
```

This is the core answer to the privacy/power problem.

## 9. Public Intelligence Forge

West Peek OS should include a dedicated machine called the Public Intelligence Forge.

Purpose:

```text
Use frontier LLMs and public sources to build private reusable intelligence packets before private matters need them.
```

Core jobs:

```text
Market map generation
Industry primer creation
Thesis research
Diligence question-bank creation
Public comparable company scan
LP landscape research
Regulatory/public policy scan
Sector trend scan
Public data extraction
Source collection
Brief refresh
```

Operators:

```text
Paige — Research Analyst
Whitney — Learning + Market Intelligence Coach
Pierce — Principal
Wells — Knowledge Manager
Wilson — Systems Operator
Willow — Compliance / Legal Review
```

Workflow:

```text
User or scheduled agent requests topic
→ classify topic sensitivity
→ confirm public-only mode
→ frontier model/public web research
→ create Candidate Intelligence Packet
→ source/provenance check
→ human or delegated approval
→ promote to Knowledge Bank
→ make available to local agents
```

Example command:

```text
Forge a public intelligence packet on veterinary clinic roll-up software for IC use. Use public sources only. No private deal data.
```

Output:

```text
5-minute primer
20-minute briefing
2-hour deep dive
market map
business model patterns
key metrics
common diligence traps
questions for founders
questions for customers
red flags
investment thesis angles
source list
expiry date
```

## 10. Public mode should be used for refresh, not default reasoning

Public / frontier mode is useful when West Peek needs:

```text
current market changes
new financing announcements
recent fund closes
current LP activity
new regulation
recent competitor launches
new pricing data
current public-company metrics
current conference/podcast signals
current public founder/company news
```

But after refresh, useful knowledge gets pulled back into the Local Core through the Inbound Airlock.

Pattern:

```text
Need current information
→ use public/frontier mode
→ ingest and structure output
→ promote approved knowledge
→ return to private mode
```

## 11. Closed-circuit mode still gets smarter over time

Closed Circuit does not mean frozen intelligence.

Closed Circuit can use:

```text
local models
private knowledge graph
promoted public intelligence packets
approved source archives
uploaded documents
private notes
meeting records
internal decisions
market briefs previously imported
local embeddings
local retrieval
```

So the private system improves as West Peek imports and approves more public intelligence.

It does not improve by sending confidential data to frontier models.

It improves by building a private library of structured intelligence.

## 12. The right mental model

Wrong model:

```text
We need the frontier model to know all West Peek data so it can be powerful.
```

Right model:

```text
We need West Peek OS to privately know West Peek data and selectively import frontier-assisted public intelligence.
```

Wrong model:

```text
Use external LLMs for everything until privacy becomes an issue.
```

Right model:

```text
Use local/private reasoning by default. Use external models as research engines through an airlock.
```

Wrong model:

```text
Conversation becomes memory.
```

Right model:

```text
Conversation becomes candidate knowledge. Candidate knowledge becomes firm memory only after approval.
```

## 13. How this applies to West Peek's operating machines

### LP Fundraising Machine

Public/frontier use:

```text
public LP discovery
public podcast/interview summaries
fund close announcements
public foundation 990 analysis
public fund-of-funds research
public firm thesis extraction
```

Private/local use:

```text
LP relationship notes
email drafts using private relationship context
Sequoia/Scooter meeting history
approval decisions
internal ranking changes
fundraising strategy
```

### Early-Stage Deal Machine

Public/frontier use:

```text
market primer
competitor scan
industry benchmark scan
public founder/company background
public customer segment research
```

Private/local use:

```text
founder deck
confidential metrics
IC memo
partner notes
customer references
investment decision
```

### Secondaries Machine

Public/frontier use:

```text
public company/news scan
public market context
public valuation environment
sector trend research
```

Private/local use:

```text
seller details
share class
counterparty details
pricing
ROFR documents
fund-vs-brokerage separation
legal review

```

### Meeting Buddy

Public/frontier use:

```text
public person/company background before the meeting
public industry context

```

Private/local use:

```text
relationship history

commitments
sensitive notes

```

### IC Portal


Private/local use:

```text
company memo
```

## 14. Data labels for routing

Every record needs a sensitivity label:

```text
PUBLIC
PUBLIC_IMPORTED
INTERNAL
CONFIDENTIAL
RESTRICTED
LEGAL_PRIVILEGED
BROKERAGE_SEPARATION_SENSITIVE
PORTFOLIO_CONFIDENTIAL
LP_CONFIDENTIAL
FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
```

Routing rules:

```text
PUBLIC → may use frontier/public mode
PUBLIC_IMPORTED → local reuse allowed; refresh through airlock
INTERNAL → local by default; sanitized external only with approval
CONFIDENTIAL → local only unless explicit human approval
RESTRICTED → lockdown default
LEGAL_PRIVILEGED → lockdown default; counsel-approved exceptions only
BROKERAGE_SEPARATION_SENSITIVE → lockdown default
PORTFOLIO_CONFIDENTIAL → local/lockdown default
LP_CONFIDENTIAL → local/lockdown default
FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL → local/lockdown default
```

## 15. Model choice policy

West Peek OS should decide model use at the task level.

Each task gets:

```text
Task type
Matter ID
Sensitivity label
Need for current data
Need for frontier reasoning
Need for local/private containment
Allowed model class
Required approval
Logging requirement
```

Example model routing:

```text
Generic public market primer → Frontier allowed
Private IC memo analysis → Local only
Sensitive secondaries memo → Lockdown
LP public-source discovery → Frontier allowed
LP relationship strategy → Local only
Founder deck summary → Local only
Public competitor scan → Frontier allowed
Post-meeting private summary → Local only
```

## 16. User-facing UX

Every user should see a visible mode indicator:

```text
LOCAL MODE
FRONTIER MODE
LOCKDOWN MODE
```

When Frontier Mode is requested, show:

```text
What will be sent
What will not be sent
Which provider will receive it
Why external help is useful
How the result will be handled
Whether it can become knowledge
```

When Lockdown Mode is active, show:

```text
External APIs disabled
Web disabled
Frontier models disabled
Only local models and local knowledge available
```

## 17. Acceptance Criteria

This architecture is acceptable only if:

- private data never silently leaves West Peek OS;
- external LLM calls are visible, logged, and governed;
- users can switch Local, Frontier, and Lockdown modes;
- every external call passes through the Outbound Airlock;
- every returned answer passes through the Inbound Airlock;
- no external model response becomes firm memory automatically;
- promoted knowledge has provenance, confidence, reviewer, and expiry metadata;
- Local and Lockdown modes can reuse imported public intelligence without external calls;
- private records can be combined with public intelligence locally;
- each operating machine has a model-use policy;
- Systems Operator can audit model use and blocked calls;
- Knowledge Manager can review candidate knowledge before promotion;
- users can refresh public knowledge when needed without exposing private matter context.

## 18. Final Law

```text
Nothing leaves without approval.
Nothing becomes knowledge without approval.
Frontier models help build public intelligence.
West Peek OS owns the private knowledge bank.
Closed circuit gets smarter through approved imports, not through leaking private data.
```
