# External Helper Touchpoints + Separate Connected Product Law

## 1. External Helper Architecture

West Peek OS must treat external helpers as governed external counterparties, not AI employees and not internal staff.

External helpers include:

- outside counsel
- fund formation counsel
- investment deal counsel
- compliance counsel
- fund administrator
- CPA / tax advisor
- auditor
- banking partner
- insurance broker
- valuation provider
- payroll / HR provider
- registered agent
- vendor / dev contractors
- fractional CFO / controller
- PR agency
- event production vendors
- placement / fundraising advisors if ever used

They remain outside the system, but West Peek OS manages every touchpoint with them.

## 2. Core Principle

External helpers do not live inside West Peek OS as users by default.

They are represented as:

- external organization records
- external person records
- matter records
- task records
- document-request records
- approval records
- deadline records
- communication logs
- file-exchange records
- risk and escalation records

West Peek OS is the command center. External helpers remain providers.

## 3. External Helper Portal Model

West Peek OS should support controlled provider portals when useful.

Portal access should be matter-specific and time-bound.

A legal provider should not see the whole firm. A fund administrator should not see founder confidential materials unless required. A CPA should not see IC debate. A vendor should not see LP records.

Access is granted by:

- provider
- individual contact
- matter
- file folder
- permission level
- expiration date
- human approver
- audit log

Default access is no access.

## 4. External Helper Touchpoint Types

Each provider relationship should support these touchpoints:

### Intake

- provider profile
- contact list
- engagement scope
- signed agreement status
- billing terms
- responsible MP
- internal owner
- active matters
- restricted matters

### Tasking

- task title
- matter context
- required output
- source documents
- deadline
- priority
- internal owner
- provider owner
- status
- blocker
- approval needed

### Document Exchange

- request list
- uploaded files
- missing files
- version history
- reviewed status
- privilege/confidentiality label
- export/download log
- approval before sharing

### Communication

- email thread capture
- meeting notes
- call summaries
- unanswered questions
- promised follow-ups
- escalation notes

### Review and Approval

- provider output received
- AI summary prepared internally
- human review required
- counsel/provider clarification needed
- approved / rejected / revised
- final artifact archived

### Billing and Budget

- invoices
- estimates
- retainer balance
- budget vs actual
- matter-level spend
- approval before payment
- finance/admin handoff

### Risk and Deadline Tracking

- filing deadlines
- tax deadlines
- audit requests
- legal/compliance dates
- capital-call dates
- closing dates
- unresolved legal questions
- high-risk open items

## 5. External Helper Command Center

West Peek OS should include an External Helpers view with:

- provider roster
- active matters by provider
- open requests
- overdue provider tasks
- documents waiting on West Peek
- documents waiting on provider
- invoices awaiting approval
- upcoming deadlines
- unresolved legal/compliance questions
- escalation items
- recent provider activity

This gives Sequoia and Scooter one place to see what outside professionals are doing and what they need from West Peek.


## 5A. External Helper AI Wrangler Assignment Law

Every external helper relationship must have at least one named internal AI employee assigned as a provider wrangler.

The external helper does not become an AI employee. The AI employee becomes West Peek's internal coordinator for that provider, matter, and workstream.

The AI wrangler's job is to help Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor give the provider what they need, get useful work back from the provider, and keep the matter from going stale.

Each provider record must show:

- assigned AI wrangler or wranglers
- responsible human owner
- provider contacts
- active matters
- open document requests
- documents waiting on West Peek
- documents waiting on provider
- upcoming deadlines
- invoice status
- last provider touchpoint
- next required action
- escalation level
- approval requirements

### Core AI Wrangler Responsibilities

For every external helper, assigned AI employees should:

- prepare provider packets before meetings or requests
- summarize relevant internal context without exposing unrelated data
- identify missing documents and owner gaps
- draft provider emails for human approval
- prepare questions for legal, fund admin, CPA, audit, banking, or vendors
- track provider commitments and West Peek commitments
- maintain document-request lists
- check deadlines and escalate risk
- summarize provider outputs in plain English
- route provider outputs to the right internal machine
- archive final documents in the Provider Evidence Vault
- update the Knowledge Manager when a provider output becomes institutional knowledge

### Provider-to-AI Assignment Map

| External helper type | Primary AI wrangler | Supporting AI employees | Human owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fund formation counsel | Willow | Pax, Perrin, Wren | Sequoia Taylor / Scooter Taylor |
| Compliance counsel | Willow | Wren, Pax, Penn | Sequoia Taylor / Scooter Taylor |
| Investment deal counsel | Willow | Pierce, Priya, Wyatt, Wren | Matter owner + Scooter Taylor for final authority |
| Secondaries counsel | Willow | Wren, Wyatt, Paige, Perrin | Sequoia Taylor |
| Fund administrator | Perrin | Preston, Pax, Wesley, Wren | Sequoia Taylor |
| CPA / tax advisor | Preston | Perrin, Pax, Wren | Sequoia Taylor |
| Auditor | Preston | Perrin, Wells, Pax | Sequoia Taylor |
| Banking / treasury partner | Preston | Pax, Wren | Sequoia Taylor |
| Insurance broker | Pax | Willow, Preston | Sequoia Taylor |
| Valuation provider | Wyatt | Preston, Paige, Pierce | Sequoia Taylor / Scooter Taylor |
| Registered agent | Pax | Willow | Sequoia Taylor |
| PR agency | Prue | Percy, Pippa, Willow | Scooter Taylor / Sequoia Taylor |
| Event vendors | Parker | Waverly, Walter, Pax | Scooter Taylor / Sequoia Taylor |
| Dev contractors / technical vendors | Wilson | Pax, Wren, Wells | Sequoia Taylor |
| Fractional CFO / controller | Preston | Perrin, Pax, Wren | Sequoia Taylor |

### External Helper Workflow

```text
Provider need or deadline appears
→ assigned AI wrangler opens matter queue
→ wrangler identifies required documents / decisions / owners
→ wrangler prepares packet for Sequoia or Scooter
→ human approves what can be shared
→ provider receives approved request or package
→ wrangler tracks response
→ provider output is summarized internally
→ human reviews / approves / requests changes
→ final output is archived
→ deadlines, tasks, and knowledge graph update
```

### Provider Packet Standard

Before any provider request or meeting, the assigned AI wrangler should prepare a provider packet with:

- matter name
- provider name and contacts
- purpose of request
- context summary
- documents to share
- documents not to share
- sensitivity label
- open questions
- decisions needed from West Peek
- deadline
- human approval required
- suggested email or meeting agenda

### Provider Output Review Standard

When a provider sends work product, the AI wrangler should not treat it as automatically final.

The wrangler prepares:

- plain-English summary
- key decisions or obligations
- deadlines triggered
- documents affected
- money implications
- legal/compliance implications
- questions for the provider
- internal owner recommendation
- archive location

The human owner then approves, rejects, revises, or sends it back for clarification.

### Escalation Rules

The assigned AI wrangler must escalate when:

- a deadline is inside 7 days and the provider is blocked
- a provider asks for sensitive documents
- a provider request involves LP data, founder data, IC records, secondaries, legal privilege, or brokerage-sensitive information
- a provider output conflicts with an internal record
- an invoice exceeds expected budget
- a provider misses a promised deadline
- a filing, closing, capital call, audit, or tax deadline is at risk
- a provider suggests a change that affects fund economics, governance, compliance, or external commitments

### Boundary Rule

AI wranglers help West Peek manage external helpers.

They do not let external helpers command West Peek OS.

They do not expose unrelated firm context.

They do not approve legal, tax, audit, banking, compliance, or fund-administration outputs on behalf of Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor.

- upload requested documents
- comment on assigned matters
- respond to specific requests
- view narrow task context

They cannot:

- browse the full knowledge graph
- see unrelated LP/founder/deal records
- trigger outbound communications
- modify investment records
- access private partner notes
- access brokerage-sensitive information
- access unrestricted meeting archives
- access AI employee internal reasoning or hidden logs

## 9. Provider Evidence Vault

Every external-helper matter should generate a Provider Evidence Vault containing:

- engagement letter
- scope of work
- provider contacts
- document requests
- files shared
- files received
- email/call logs
- meeting notes
- decisions
- invoices
- approvals
- final outputs
- unresolved questions
- closeout summary

This vault becomes institutional memory.

## 10. Separate Connected Product Law

Network OS, West Peek Live, and Pitch Lab remain separate products.

They must not be rebuilt into the Odysseus fork by default.

They must be:

- deeply connected
- reachable from West Peek OS
- surfaced in command center context
- synchronized through APIs, webhooks, shared identity, and structured handoffs
- linked by firm-wide search and knowledge graph references
- governed by the same privacy and approval rules where applicable

But they retain their own product boundaries.

## 11. Product Boundary Rules

### Network OS

Network OS remains the relationship-intelligence product.

West Peek OS should:

- read relationship context from Network OS
- push approved updates back to Network OS
- open Network OS records from inside West Peek OS
- use Network OS as relationship source of truth unless audit proves otherwise
- never duplicate relationship systems unnecessarily

Network OS handles:

- people
- relationships
- CRM-like intelligence
- Gmail-trigger intake
- relationship follow-through
- founder/community/LP/contact records

### Pitch Lab

Pitch Lab remains the founder-facing pitch practice and support product.

West Peek OS should:

- receive founder-approved Pitch Lab handoffs
- respect founder privacy and consent
- open Pitch Lab records from company/founder profiles
- route shared Pitch Lab outputs into founder support and investment workflows

Pitch Lab handles:

- pitch practice
- founder story work
- AI Scooter practice experience
- founder readiness artifacts
- optional handoff to West Peek

### West Peek Live

West Peek Live remains the event and production product.

West Peek OS should:

- plan events internally
- hand execution details to West Peek Live
- receive attendee, speaker, sponsor, and engagement intelligence from West Peek Live
- route post-event intelligence into Network OS, Fundraising OS, Community OS, Investment OS, and Knowledge OS

West Peek Live handles:

- virtual events
- conferences
- event pages/production
- attendee engagement
- speaker programming
- event media outputs

## 12. Integration Pattern

West Peek OS should function as the command layer.

Separate products should function as specialized systems.

Integration should happen through:

- single sign-on if possible
- deep links
- embedded panels where useful
- API sync
- webhook events
- shared entity IDs
- cross-product search index
- knowledge graph references
- structured handoff packets
- approval-aware data writes

## 13. Cross-Product Handoff Packets

Every handoff between West Peek OS and a separate product should use a structured packet.

Packet fields:

- source product
- destination product
- entity type
- entity ID
- human owner
- sensitivity label
- consent status
- summary
- source links
- requested action
- approval status
- timestamp
- audit log ID

Example:

Pitch Lab → West Peek OS:

- founder approved share
- company profile
- pitch summary
- founder story packet
- requested support
- consent timestamp

West Peek Live → West Peek OS:

- event completed
- attendee list
- engagement signals
- speaker notes
- follow-up opportunities
- content assets

West Peek OS → Network OS:

- approved relationship update
- follow-up task
- meeting summary
- new classification
- relationship warmth signal

## 14. Final Operating Law

West Peek OS is the command center.

Network OS, Pitch Lab, and West Peek Live are separate connected products.

External helpers are governed counterparties with structured touchpoints.

AI employees prepare, monitor, summarize, coordinate, and escalate.

Humans approve external actions and final institutional decisions.

# Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue Machine

## 1. Strategic Addition

West Peek OS must support a new revenue and relationship lane inside Event OS:

```text
Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue Machine
```

This machine exists because West Peek is not only a venture fund with events. West Peek has a real community-origin story, a founder/operator/investor audience, and the ability to create high-trust rooms that brands may want to sponsor.

This machine turns West Peek's event capability into a professional sponsorship engine while preserving the firm's taste, reputation, community trust, and investment focus.

## 2. Core Origin Story to Preserve

West Peek's events and community story must include the Scooter origin narrative:

```text
Scooter Taylor originally ideated a small Zoom kickback during COVID.
As interest grew, the team planned for a larger room.
Over 4,000 people showed up.
That unexpected community signal became the seed of West Peek's broader community engine.

External sponsor-facing use must keep this simple and must confirm exact origin-story wording with Scooter before publication or outreach.
```

This story matters because it proves the community did not begin as a fund-marketing strategy. It began as culture, trust, gathering, energy, and real demand for connection.

## 2A. West Peek Rooms case study / pilot lane

West Peek Rooms is canonized as a pilotable sponsored-community revenue lane and a proof case for the Opportunity Intelligence mandate.

West Peek Rooms is not merely an event idea. It is a structured opportunity that AI employees should be capable of surfacing, pressure-testing, packaging, and routing for approval.

The approved operating thesis:

```text
Brands do not need another ad.
They need the right room.
```

West Peek Rooms gives aligned sponsors a warm, high-trust way to show up with founders, operators, investors, creatives, and culturally fluent builders through curated community programming, content capture, and measurable recap/reporting.

Canonical first-pilot recommendation:

- pilot format: The Tastemaker Table;
- expected audience: approximately 12-30 curated founders, operators, investors, and builders;
- target price: $10,000 Room Partner;
- minimum acceptable pilot: $7,500 only for the right logo/case study;
- deliverables: co-branded invite or event page, sponsor recognition, photo/video recap, two or more content/social assets, and post-event report;
- first target categories: fintech/founder finance, founder tools, AI/startup software, legal/accounting, premium hospitality, wellness/performance, and startup ecosystem partners.

West Peek Rooms should be evaluated through a cross-domain packet:

```text
Opportunity Radar surfaces the revenue lane.
Events OS evaluates format and feasibility.
Community OS evaluates audience and community fit.
Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue evaluates sponsor fit and target list.
Finance OS evaluates margin, pricing, and operating burden.
Legal/Compliance OS evaluates fund safety, entity separation, and pay-to-play optics.
West Peek Taste Layer evaluates whether the room feels premium, useful, warm, and credible.
Approval Center routes the Opportunity Review before any outreach occurs.
```

Entity and fund-safety default:

```text
West Peek Rooms should likely be housed through West Peek Productions / media-community operations rather than directly through the fund, subject to counsel/accounting confirmation.
```

No sponsor may be offered investment access, diligence influence, portfolio endorsement, guaranteed introductions, pay-to-play dealflow, or proximity to West Peek Ventures investment decisions.

## 3. Sponsorship Positioning

The sponsorship story is:

```text
West Peek creates high-trust rooms for founders, investors, operators, creators, and community builders.
Brands can sponsor those rooms when their values, customers, products, and cultural goals align with West Peek's audience.
```

The product is not simply logo placement.

The product is:

```text
access to a curated community
association with founder energy
real-world gathering
warm brand integration
high-context storytelling
credible cultural proximity
community goodwill
founder/operator/investor attention
```

## 4. Machine Purpose

The Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue Machine should help West Peek:

- identify brands that align with West Peek's community and values;
- build sponsorship packages;
- prepare landing pages, one-pagers, and decks;
- research brand partnership teams;
- score sponsor fit;
- draft outreach for approval;
- manage sponsor conversations;
- prepare sponsor proposal packets;
- track sponsor deliverables;
- coordinate event execution with West Peek Live;
- capture post-event proof and sponsor ROI;
- convert successful sponsors into repeat partners.

## 5. Sponsor Categories

The machine should search across sponsor categories that naturally fit the West Peek ecosystem:

```text
founder tools
banking and fintech
law firms
accounting and tax firms
fund administration
cloud and developer tools
AI tools
productivity tools
workspace / coworking brands
travel and hospitality brands
wellness and lifestyle brands
premium food and beverage brands
beauty and fashion brands
media and creator economy platforms
education and upskilling platforms
hiring and talent platforms
insurance and benefits providers
event technology platforms
```

The machine should not chase every brand with a marketing budget. It should prioritize brands whose association strengthens West Peek rather than cheapens it.

## 6. Sponsorship Product Types

West Peek OS should support multiple sponsorship products:

### Private Dinner Sponsorship

Small, curated, intimate dinners for founders, investors, operators, LP prospects, or community leaders.

### Founder Workshop Sponsorship

A brand sponsors a tactical founder education session such as fundraising, hiring, growth, AI workflows, finance, or storytelling.

### West Peek Live Virtual Event Sponsorship

A brand sponsors virtual programming, panels, conferences, founder showcases, or community gatherings.

### LP / Investor Salon Sponsorship

A carefully managed sponsorship opportunity for brands that serve investors, family offices, founders, or allocators.

### Community Meetup Sponsorship

A brand supports a local or thematic community event.

### Flagship Summit Sponsorship

Larger sponsorship packages around major West Peek events.

### Content + Event Bundle

A sponsor supports an event plus recap content, founder resource, or educational distribution.

## 7. Sponsor Fit Scoring

Every sponsor candidate should be scored before outreach.

Suggested scoring factors:

```text
Audience fit
Brand alignment
Founder/community usefulness
Check size potential
Reputation risk
Repeat partnership potential
Event type fit
Geographic relevance
Strategic relationship value
Conflict risk
```

Score output:

```text
A — Strong fit, prioritize
B — Possible fit, research more
C — Weak fit, only pursue if specific event match
D — Do not pursue
```

## 8. Required Sponsorship Assets

The machine must help assemble and maintain sponsor-ready materials.

### West Peek Events Landing Page

Purpose: public-facing sponsorship and event partnership entry point.

Should include:

- West Peek community story;
- Scooter's COVID Zoom origin story;
- what West Peek events are;
- who attends;
- event formats;
- sponsor opportunities;
- brand fit language;
- example event concepts;
- inquiry form;
- contact/approval routing.

### West Peek Sponsorship One-Pager

Purpose: fast PDF-style overview for potential sponsors.

Should include:

- who West Peek is;
- why the community matters;
- audience profile;
- event formats;
- sponsor benefits;
- example packages;
- next step.

### Scooter Origin Story One-Pager

Purpose: founder/community/storytelling asset centered on Scooter.

Should include:

- the COVID Zoom party origin;
- accidental virality / 5,000-person moment;
- how that evolved into community;
- why Scooter is credible as a community convener;
- how West Peek turns gathering into founder support and opportunity.

### Sponsorship Deck

Purpose: deeper sales asset.

Should include:

- West Peek thesis;
- community proof;
- event formats;
- audience segments;
- sponsor packages;
- case-study style story;
- sample activations;
- sponsor fit criteria;
- next steps.

### Event-Specific Sponsor Proposal

Purpose: customized packet for a specific brand and event.

Should include:

- proposed event concept;
- why the brand fits;
- audience alignment;
- activation options;
- deliverables;
- price range;
- approval and production timeline.

## 9. AI Employees Assigned

The Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue Machine is not owned by one agent only.


## 10. Sponsorship Scout Workflow

### Daily Scheduled Work

Wynn runs a daily sponsor scan during the Event OS operating window.

Inputs:

- upcoming event concepts;
- West Peek audience segments;
- brand category targets;
- prior sponsor conversations;
- event calendar;
- public brand partnership signals;
- founder/community relevance.

Steps:

```text
Discover brands
→ classify category
→ research sponsor/partnership fit
→ find partnership contacts where publicly available
→ identify public evidence of event/community sponsorship
→ score brand fit
→ detect reputation/conflict risks
→ write sponsor candidate record
→ hand off to Percy/Parker for package strategy
```

Outputs:

- sponsor candidate list;
- sponsor fit score;
- recommended event type;
- contact research;
- warm path hints;
- sponsor-specific angle;
- risk notes.

### Weekly Sponsorship Pipeline Review

Every week, the machine produces:

- top sponsor targets;
- highest-fit categories;
- events needing sponsor strategy;
- assets needed;
- sponsor conversations in progress;
- pending human approvals;
- packages ready to send;
- revenue pipeline estimate.

## 11. Sponsor Outreach Workflow

Default flow:

```text
Sponsor candidate discovered
→ fit score
→ sponsor angle drafted
→ sponsorship packet selected
→ outreach draft prepared
→ compliance/brand review
→ Sequoia/Scooter approval
→ send or schedule
→ meeting buddy prepares sponsor call
→ proposal packet generated
→ contract/vendor review if needed
→ event execution
→ post-event sponsor report
```

AI may draft and queue outreach.

AI may not send sponsor outreach externally without human approval.

## 12. Sponsor Meeting Buddy

Walter should support sponsor calls with a dedicated sponsor mode.

Before call:

- sponsor profile;
- brand fit thesis;
- likely budget range if known;
- suggested event concept;
- activation ideas;
- questions to ask;
- terms to avoid promising;
- follow-up goal.

During call:

- capture sponsor goals;
- capture budget hints;
- capture desired audience;
- capture event constraints;
- capture deliverables discussed;
- flag promises requiring review.

After call:

- sponsor summary;
- proposal draft;
- task list;
- contract/legal flags;
- follow-up draft;
- CRM update;
- event machine update.

## 13. Sponsor Boundary Rules

West Peek must protect trust.

Sponsors should not get:

```text
private founder data
LP private information
IC materials
portfolio confidential information
private community data exports
unapproved access to attendees
investment influence
control over fund decisions
misleading affiliation claims
```

Sponsor arrangements must be reviewed for:

```text
reputation risk
conflicts
regulated language
privacy issues
event safety
brand mismatch
contract obligations
payment terms
cancellation terms
content rights
```

## 14. Revenue Tracking

The machine should track:

- sponsor target;
- sponsor category;
- proposed event;
- package value;
- probability;
- stage;
- owner;
- next action;
- payment status;
- contract status;
- deliverables;
- post-event ROI report;
- renewal potential.

This creates a sponsorship pipeline separate from LP fundraising and separate from investment pipeline.

## 15. Final Brand Sponsorship Law

West Peek events are not merely community programming.

They are:

```text
community infrastructure
founder support infrastructure
relationship intelligence infrastructure
brand partnership opportunities
potential experiential revenue products
```

The machine may create revenue, but it must never compromise West Peek's trust, taste, founder-first posture, or investment credibility.

# WEST PEEK PRODUCTIONS + BRAND SPONSORSHIP ENGINE

## 0. Version Lock

**Version:** v2.3  
**Date:** June 23, 2026  
**Status:** Canonical Planning Baseline Updated

This integrated requirement upgrades the Brand Sponsorship + Experiential Revenue Machine into a cross-entity system spanning:

```text
West Peek OS
West Peek Events
West Peek Live
West Peek Productions
Network OS
External Helper Architecture
Brand Sponsorship Machine
Content / PR / Marketing Machine
```

The strategic correction is simple:

```text
West Peek is not merely hosting sponsored events.
West Peek can produce sponsor-ready cultural business experiences.
```

That makes West Peek Productions relevant.

West Peek Productions should not be treated only as a future television/film studio. In the near term, it can operate as the production and content-capture layer for West Peek-sponsored rooms, salons, dinners, founder events, virtual sessions, recap videos, short-form clips, sponsor assets, and brand storytelling.

---

## 1. Strategic Rationale

Brands are increasingly interested in creator-led and community-led experiences when the host has trust, taste, and a credible audience. West Peek’s asset is not merely attendance. The asset is:

```text
Scooter’s convening credibility
+ West Peek’s founder / investor / operator community
+ West Peek Live’s production capacity
+ West Peek Productions’ storytelling layer
+ West Peek OS’s sponsor workflow and relationship intelligence
```

This allows West Peek to package:

```text
community
vibe
founder/operator access
cultural credibility
content capture
measurable sponsor deliverables
```

as a repeatable sponsorship product.

The origin story is central:

```text
Scooter hosted a Zoom party during COVID expecting around 50 people.
Roughly 5,000 people showed up.
That moment became proof that Scooter can convene culture, not merely attend it.
```

This is the narrative bridge between West Peek’s community history and sponsor-ready experiential programming.

---

West Peek OS must preserve clean boundaries between the venture fund, community products, event operations, sponsor revenue, and production/media work.

# POV Intelligence Layer

## Purpose

West Peek OS must not default to generic assistant behavior for serious firm work.

The POV Intelligence Layer determines the professional standard required by a task before any AI employee, operating machine, meeting buddy, or model backend begins work.

This is not persona theater. It is a quality-control and governance system.

It answers:

```text
What kind of senior operator would produce excellent work here?
What reviewer would catch the risks?
What hostile reviewer would find the weakness?
What evidence standard is required?
What failure modes must be avoided?
What approval gates apply?
```

## Canonical Law

```text
West Peek OS must not require Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to manually ask for expert framing.

For any meaningful task, West Peek OS automatically selects the highest-useful professional POV and reviewer stack required to complete the work at an elite standard.

The selected POV is not performative persona language.
It defines standards, assumptions, failure modes, review criteria, evidence requirements, and approval requirements.
```

## Placement in the Architecture

The POV Intelligence Layer sits inside the West Peek OS Common Sense Governor.

Updated governor stack:

```text
Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor

1. Task Classification
2. POV Intelligence Layer
3. Loop Suitability Gate
4. Expert Standard Selection
5. Maker / Checker Split
6. Hostile Review Governor
7. Claim Discipline
8. Objective Verification Gate
9. Human Approval Gate
10. Narrative Truth Mode
11. Agent Security Tax
12. Comprehension Debt Guard
13. Privacy / Model Gate
14. Knowledge Promotion Gate
```

The POV Intelligence Layer runs before execution. It informs which AI employee acts, which operating machine receives the task, which model mode is allowed, and which review gates are required.

## Inputs

For each serious task, the system classifies:

```text
Task domain
Output type
Intended audience
Internal vs external use
Risk level
Irreversibility
Data sensitivity
Revenue impact
Reputation impact
Legal/compliance exposure
Required craft standard
Canonical documents that govern the task
Available AI employees
Available external helpers
Available model backends
Required approval category
```

## Outputs

The layer generates a POV Card.

Each POV Card includes:

```text
Task
Lead POV
Supporting POVs
Reviewer POV
Hostile Review POV
Definition of Done
Evidence Standard
Failure Modes to Avoid
Privacy Mode
Approval Requirement
Human Owner
Assigned AI Employees
Assigned Machine
```

The POV Card is attached to the task record and visible in the task detail screen.

## Example POV Cards

### Fundraising / LP Work

```text
Task:
Prepare a West Peek LP intro packet for a family office.

Lead POV:
Top-tier emerging-manager fundraiser + venture GP.

Supporting POV:
Fund operator + LP relationship manager.

Reviewer POV:
Skeptical family-office allocator + compliance-aware fund counsel lens.

Hostile Review POV:
Allocator looking for reasons not to trust the fund, team, strategy, claims, or process.

Definition of Done:
Clear thesis, credible proof, no unsupported claims, no sloppy fund language, no return promises, no regulatory landmines, next step obvious.

Evidence Standard:
All traction, community, team, SPV, advisory, event, and sourcing claims must link to source records or be marked as narrative positioning.

Privacy Mode:
Local or Approved External depending on materials used. Private LP notes remain local.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval before anything is sent externally.
```

### Sponsor / Brand Partnership Work

```text
Task:
Draft the West Peek Rooms sponsor one-pager.

Lead POV:
Brand partnerships strategist with deep creator-event and experiential-marketing experience.

Supporting POV:
VC firm operator + community-led brand builder + West Peek Productions producer.

Reviewer POV:
Brand marketing director deciding whether this is worth $20K.

Hostile Review POV:
Sponsor buyer who thinks this is vague community fluff and wants hard deliverables.

Definition of Done:
Clear sponsor value, credible Scooter origin story, clean package menu, concrete deliverables, brand-safe positioning, strong CTA, investment-compliance boundary intact.

Evidence Standard:
No invented attendance metrics, demographics, sponsor outcomes, or brand claims. The 5,000-person Zoom origin story must be identified as West Peek/Scooter narrative unless independently documented.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval before sponsor outreach or publication.
```

### Early-Stage Deal Work

```text
Task:
Evaluate a pre-seed company for IC.

Lead POV:
Top-tier seed GP with founder-market judgment.

Supporting POV:
Principal + analyst + market researcher + founder-support operator.

Reviewer POV:
Skeptical investment committee member.

Hostile Review POV:
LP asking why this is venture-scale, why this founder can win, and why West Peek has an edge.

Definition of Done:
Clear invest/pass/nurture recommendation, founder assessment, market map, customer/problem validation, distribution analysis, ownership math, IC questions, anti-thesis, evidence gaps.

Evidence Standard:
Founder claims must be separated from verified evidence. Unknowns must remain visible.

Approval Requirement:
MP decision required before investment action or external representation of interest.
```

### Secondaries Work

```text
Task:
Evaluate a Series C+ secondary opportunity.

Lead POV:
Secondaries operator + late-stage private-market investor.

Supporting POV:
Fund counsel lens + valuation analyst + transfer/ROFR process operator.

Reviewer POV:
Compliance-aware fund manager.

Hostile Review POV:
Counterparty-risk reviewer looking for transfer, pricing, conflict, disclosure, or settlement failure.

Definition of Done:
Source classified, brokerage/fund separation checked, company/stage verified, share class reviewed, ROFR/transfer restrictions identified, price compared to known data, conflict status logged, IC packet prepared.

Evidence Standard:
No assumptions about transferability, pricing, seller authority, or company approval without documentation.

Privacy Mode:
Lockdown or Local Mode by default.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval and legal review before external action.
```

### Meeting Buddy Work

```text
Task:
Prepare Sequoia and Scooter for a founder meeting.

Lead POV:
Elite VC meeting prep operator.

Supporting POV:
Founder psychologist + market diligence lead + relationship-intelligence analyst.

Reviewer POV:
Managing Partner who has only 20 minutes and needs signal fast.

Hostile Review POV:
Founder who can tell when investors are generic, lazy, or underprepared.

Definition of Done:
Meeting objective, founder/company context, relationship history, likely founder needs, sharp questions, red flags, desired outcome, follow-up plan.

Evidence Standard:
Separate known facts from assumptions and suggested questions.

Approval Requirement:
No external follow-up without human approval.
```

### Product / Repo Work

```text
Task:
Modify the West Peek OS fork.

Lead POV:
Principal Product Architect + senior full-stack engineer.

Supporting POV:
Senior security engineer + QA lead + VC operating-system product manager.

Reviewer POV:
Production owner responsible for uptime, privacy, and real user workflows.

Hostile Review POV:
Technical investor and incident reviewer assuming the system will fail under real-world usage.

Definition of Done:
Source-root confirmed, lifecycle mapped, no stubs, no fake validation, user journeys complete, auth/data/security risks considered, artifact delivered from correct root, local validation delegated or run as required.

Evidence Standard:
Do not claim validation unless it actually ran.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval for deployment, external integrations, privacy-mode changes, or data migrations.
```

### Public Writing / Content Work

```text
Task:
Draft a public West Peek essay or announcement.

Lead POV:
Elite editor + brand strategist + experienced founder storyteller.

Supporting POV:
VC firm operator + compliance reviewer + West Peek voice steward.

Reviewer POV:
Smart reader who wants substance, not hype.

Hostile Review POV:
Reader who detects cringe, exaggeration, fake vulnerability, unsupported claims, or regulatory sloppiness.

Definition of Done:
Compelling, useful, believable, voice-aligned, factual, and safe for publication.

Evidence Standard:
No invented metrics. Narrative details can be shaped, but factual claims must remain true.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval before publication.
```

### Trading / AI Hedge Fund Research

```text
Task:
Explore an AI trading bot concept.

Lead POV:
Quant PM + systematic trading engineer.

Supporting POV:
Trading risk officer + security engineer + compliance-aware operator.

Reviewer POV:
Risk manager protecting capital and infrastructure.

Hostile Review POV:
Reviewer assuming the bot will overfit, leak keys, hallucinate profit, overtrade, or ignore drawdown risk.

Definition of Done:
No guaranteed returns, no live capital without authority envelope, no exchange withdrawal permissions, no strategy without backtest and paper test, no scale-up without review.

Evidence Standard:
Backtest/paper-trade evidence must be labeled; simulated results are not real returns.

Approval Requirement:
Human approval before live capital, credentials, or exchange access.
```

## Machine-Level Behavior

Every operating machine must request or generate a POV Card before serious work begins.

Affected machines include:

```text
LP Fundraising Machine
Early-Stage Deal Machine
Secondaries Investment Machine
IC Decision Machine
Generic Meeting Intelligence Machine
Founder + Community Machine
Portfolio Support Machine
Event + West Peek Live Machine
Brand Sponsorship + Productions Machine
Market + Industry Intelligence Academy Machine
Firm Operations Machine
Finance + Fund Admin + Legal Machine
Brand + Content + PR Machine
Systems + Data + Integration Machine
Knowledge + Institutional Memory Machine
Public Intelligence Forge
```

## AI Employee Behavior

Each AI employee must obey the POV Card for the task instance they are working on.

The same AI employee may use different POV standards in different task instances.

Example:

```text
Walter as Founder Meeting Buddy:
Lead POV = elite VC meeting prep operator.

Walter as Legal Call Buddy:
Lead POV = legal operations coordinator.

Walter as IC Meeting Buddy:
Lead POV = IC process manager and decision-quality facilitator.
```

This allows AI employees to operate flexibly while keeping task standards explicit and auditable.

## UI Requirements

Task screens must show:

```text
Selected POV Card
Lead POV
Reviewer POV
Hostile Review POV
Definition of Done
Evidence Standard
Privacy Mode
Approval Requirement
Assigned AI Employees
Review Status
```

The user may override the POV Card, but the system must warn if the override lowers the required standard for a high-risk task.

## Approval and Review Logic

The POV Intelligence Layer can increase, but not silently decrease, review rigor.

Examples:

```text
A public sponsor one-pager can require brand + compliance review.
A founder-facing email can require Scooter approval.
A secondary investment packet can require legal/conflict review.
An LP outreach draft can require compliance linter + human approval.
A private IC memo can require Local or Lockdown Mode.
```

## Failure Modes the Layer Prevents

```text
Generic assistant output for high-stakes work
Unsupported claims in fundraising or public materials
Overly casual legal/compliance handling
Investor decks that sound good but cannot survive diligence
Meeting prep that misses the real power dynamics
Product work that ignores security, auth, data, or lifecycle risks
Sponsor materials that sell vague vibes instead of concrete deliverables
AI employees working from inconsistent standards
Private data being routed to external models without the right lens
Memory pollution from unreviewed outputs
```

## Acceptance Criteria

West Peek OS v1 implementation should be considered incomplete unless:

```text
Meaningful tasks create POV Cards automatically.
AI employees can read and follow POV Cards.
Operating machines can require different POV standards.
POV Cards attach to task records and audit logs.
High-risk categories require reviewer and hostile-review POVs.
External-facing outputs require an approval path.
Privacy Mode is selected as part of the POV Card.
Users can view and override POV Cards with warning logic.
Completed outputs can be checked against the Definition of Done.
```

## Final Principle

```text
West Peek OS should not ask Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to remember to demand excellence.

The system should infer the professional standard, assemble the right expert lens, and hold the work to that standard by default.
```

# Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor

## 1. Purpose

West Peek OS must not require Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to repeatedly remember every operational safeguard before asking the system to do serious work.

The OS should not wait for prompts like:

```text
Do a hostile review.
Double check your work.
Run claim verification.
Separate maker from checker.
Use a state file.
Do not hallucinate.
Ask before irreversible actions.
Do not run forever.
Do not grade your own homework.
```

Those safeguards must become default institutional behavior.

The **Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor** sits above every AI employee, machine, workflow, loop, meeting buddy, repo task, fundraising workflow, sponsor workflow, investment process, and external-helper workflow.

Its job is simple:

```text
Make West Peek OS behave like a competent senior operator by default.
```

This is not personality. This is not “AI common sense” as a vague hope. It is encoded operational judgment.

## 2. Core governing law

```text
West Peek OS must not require Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to manually request basic operational safeguards.

For meaningful work, safeguards are default system behavior.

Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor may reduce friction for low-risk creative work, but they should not have to manually request truth checks, approval gates, hostile review, state persistence, or verification when those protections are obviously required.
```

## 3. Relationship to the POV Intelligence Layer

The v2.4 POV Intelligence Layer determines the professional standard and expert lens required for a task.

The v2.5 Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor determines how the work should be governed.

They operate together:

```text
POV Intelligence Layer
= Who should be thinking? What professional standard applies?

Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor
= What safeguards, loops, gates, state, review, and approvals are required?
```

Example:

```text
Task: Draft a West Peek sponsor deck.

POV Intelligence Layer:
Lead POV = brand partnerships strategist + creator-event operator.
Reviewer POV = skeptical sponsor buyer.
Compliance POV = truth and sponsorship-boundary reviewer.

Operational Intelligence Governor:
Needs claim check.
Needs sponsor-compliance review.
Needs human approval before external use.
Needs source-backed metrics only.
Needs Narrative Truth Mode if Scooter origin story is compressed.
Does not need code validation.
Does not need recurring loop.
```

## 4. What the Governor decides before work begins

For every meaningful assignment, West Peek OS should classify:

```text
Task type
Domain
Output type
Audience
Risk level
Irreversibility
Data sensitivity
Revenue impact
Reputation impact
Legal/compliance exposure
Need for recurrence
Need for objective verification
Need for state persistence
Need for hostile review
Need for human approval
Need for model privacy restrictions
Need for knowledge promotion
```

Then it assigns the correct operating path:

```text
One-off answer
Draft artifact
Meeting buddy support
Workflow task
Recurring duty
Agent loop
Approval item
Research packet
Knowledge candidate
Provider request
Investment process step
External communication draft
```

## 5. Loop Suitability Gate

Not every task should become an agent loop.

Loops are powerful, but they create cost, drift, security exposure, and false-completion risk if used casually.

Before creating a loop, West Peek OS must check:

```text
1. Does the task repeat weekly or more often?
2. Is there an objective verifier or gate?
3. Can the agent inspect the relevant environment, sources, logs, or data?
4. Can the agent run, test, or verify its work where applicable?
5. Is there a bounded token, time, cost, or iteration cap?
6. Is there a hard stop?
7. Is there a human approval gate before irreversible action?
8. Is the data sensitivity compatible with the model/privacy mode?
```

Routing:

```text
If yes → loop candidate.
If partly → scheduled checklist, supervised workflow, or human-reviewed recurring task.
If no → one-off assignment, manual prompt, checklist, or human review.
```

## 6. Minimum Viable Loop Standard

Every approved West Peek OS loop must have:

```text
1. Automation trigger
2. Assigned AI employee
3. Skill / SOP
4. State file or owned state database
5. Objective gate
6. Hard stop / budget cap
7. Audit log
8. Human approval rule
9. Failure escalation route
10. Pause / kill switch
```

For repo/product loops, also require:

```text
Worktree or workspace isolation
Diff review
Source-of-truth artifact rule
Validation boundary
No fake completion
No merge/deploy without human approval
```

For fundraising/outreach loops, also require:

```text
Compliance linter
Source provenance
Deduplication
Do-not-contact suppression
Human approval before send unless explicitly authorized later
Outbound audit log
```

For sponsor/event loops, also require:

```text
Sponsor fit rules
Brand-safety review
No private data leakage
No implied investment access
Deliverable tracker
Post-event proof packet
```

For external-helper loops, also require:

```text
Provider matter ID
Document request list
Shared file log
Deadline tracker
Invoice/budget implications
Human approval before sharing sensitive materials
```

## 7. Review tiers

West Peek OS should automatically select a review tier.

### Tier 0 — Common Sense Pass

Always on.

Checks:

```text
Did we answer the actual request?
Did we invent anything?
Did we skip a required artifact?
Did we imply validation that did not run?
Did we miss an approval gate?
Did we contradict canonical docs?
Did we use the right privacy mode?
Did we route to the right machine or AI employee?
```

### Tier 1 — Claim Check / CoVE Discipline

Default for factual, strategic, repo, legal-adjacent, financial, fund, LP, sponsor, public-facing, investment, market, health, trading, or compliance-sensitive work.

Checks:

```text
Claim
Evidence
Verification path
Confidence
Source
Expiry date when relevant
```

### Tier 2 — Hostile Review

Default for high-impact work.

Used for:

```text
Canonical plans
Investment analysis
IC materials
LP materials
Fundraising language
Sponsor decks
Public claims
Repo/product architecture
Automations
Agent workforce design
Legal/compliance-sensitive workflows
Trading or financial systems
External communications
```

Questions:

```text
What breaks?
What is missing?
What assumption is hiding?
What would embarrass West Peek later?
Where could this hallucinate?
Where is the approval boundary?
What would a skeptical LP reject?
What would a founder distrust?
What would counsel flag?
What would a brand sponsor object to?
What would a production user hate?
```

### Tier 3 — Maker / Checker Split

Required when work is complex, risky, irreversible, public-facing, compliance-sensitive, or involves code/automation.

Rule:

```text
The AI employee that creates the work should not be the only reviewer of the work.
```

Examples:

```text
Pippa drafts sponsor copy → Willow reviews compliance → Prue reviews reputation → Sequoia approves.

Wyatt produces fund math → Preston checks financial logic → Pierce checks investment implication → Sequoia/Scooter approve.

Wilson prepares a repo patch → separate checker validates structure → human runs local validation.
```

### Tier 4 — Objective Gate Required

Used for code, data, automations, trading, deployments, migrations, recurring loops, financial calculations, and high-risk sync jobs.

No “looks good.”

The gate must be objective.

Examples:

```text
Tests pass
Build passes
Lint passes
Typecheck passes
ZIP reopens
Required files present
Schema validates
Hash manifest exists
No placeholder strings found
Email compliance linter passes
Deduplication check passes
Provider packet complete
Capital-call checklist complete
Risk envelope satisfied
```

## 8. No Ralph Wiggum Loops Law

West Peek OS must prevent agents from exiting early, quietly failing, or declaring work complete because they feel done.

Law:

```text
No AI employee may declare completion solely by self-assessment when an objective gate is available.
```

If no objective gate exists, output status must be honest:

```text
Draft complete — judgment review required.
Research complete — source review required.
Recommendation prepared — MP decision required.
Meeting summary prepared — participant review optional.
Sponsor copy prepared — compliance/human approval required.
```

Not:

```text
Validated.
Approved.
Final.
Complete.
```

unless the relevant gate has actually passed.

## 9. State file / state database requirement

Recurring work must not restart from zero.

Every recurring AI employee workflow must have owned state.

State may include:

```text
last run time
inputs processed
outputs produced
handoff target
errors
blocked items
approval status
next action
suppression lists
decisions made
open questions
human owner
source references
```

Examples:

```text
LP Sourcer owns candidate discovery state.
Warm Path Finder owns personalization state.
Event Planner owns sponsor/event deliverable state.
Meeting Buddy owns meeting-specific task instances.
Legal Helper Wrangler owns provider matter state.
Knowledge Manager owns promotion queue state.
Systems Operator owns integration health state.
```

No recurring AI employee should operate only from chat memory.

## 10. Hard stop / budget cap

Every loop or long-running workflow needs a stop condition.

Examples:

```text
Run for 30 minutes.
Process max 80 candidates.
Retry max 3 times.
Generate max 5 drafts.
Stop after first failed verification gate.
Stop before external send.
Stop before irreversible action.
Stop if data sensitivity conflict appears.
Escalate if no progress for 2 runs.
```

This prevents agent sprawl and runaway cost.

## 11. Human Approval Gate

West Peek OS must distinguish internal work from irreversible or external action.

Internal work may be autonomous when authorized.

Human approval is required for:

```text
External emails
Calendar invites
LP outreach
Founder outreach
Sponsor outreach
Media outreach
Vendor commitments
Legal document sharing
Fundraising materials sent externally
Investment interest communicated externally
Public publishing
Money movement
Capital calls
Contract signatures
Portfolio/company commitments
Access grants
Data exports
Deployments
Merges
Credential or permission changes
```

## 12. Agent Security Tax

Every AI employee, tool connector, model backend, and loop must pay the security tax.

Required fields:

```text
Permission scope
Allowed tools
Forbidden tools
Secret handling rule
Log sanitation rule
Skill admission gate
Connector boundary
Write-action approval rule
Sensitive-data handling rule
30-day permission review
Kill switch
Escalation owner
```

Security concerns include:

```text
Generated code shipping unreviewed
Credentials leaking into logs
Permission scope creep
Prompt/skill injection
Unreviewed external sends
Unapproved data export
Private records entering frontier models
Provider portals exposing too much
Agent loops continuing after context changes
```

## 13. Comprehension Debt Guard

West Peek OS exists to reduce unnecessary cognitive load, not to remove Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor from judgment.

Law:

```text
West Peek OS reduces unnecessary thinking.
West Peek OS does not remove MP authority over judgment calls.
```

The OS should separate:

```text
Mechanical correctness → automate/check
Research synthesis → brief/recommend
Judgment → escalate/prepare decision
Irreversible action → approval required
```

For important work, the system should provide a compressed decision brief:

```text
What happened?
What matters?
What is recommended?
What is uncertain?
What could go wrong?
What decision is needed?
What happens if we do nothing?
```

This prevents passive dependence on AI while still reducing overwhelm.

## 14. Narrative Truth Mode

West Peek OS must not treat all writing modes the same.

There is a difference between:

```text
Factual reporting
Legal/compliance materials
Investor materials
Fundraising materials
Sponsor materials
Personal essay
Marketing story
Composite case study
Fictionalized brand essay
Teaching story
```

Truth policy:

```text
Do not mislead the reader about what kind of truth they are receiving.
```

Narrative Truth Mode may allow:

```text
Anonymization
Composite characters
Altered names
Compressed timelines
Adjusted non-material details
Scenario blending for teaching
```

Only when clearly disclosed.

Suggested disclosure:

```text
Some names, details, and scenarios have been adjusted for storytelling. The lessons are real.
```

Narrative Truth Mode does **not** allow:

```text
Fake investment performance
Fake fund metrics
Fake testimonials presented as literal
Fake credentials
Fake legal facts
Fake financial claims
Fabricated results
Invented material facts presented as real
Misleading LP claims
Misleading sponsor claims
Misleading founder/portfolio claims
```

Not allowed for:

```text
Fundraising claims
Investment materials
Legal documents
Compliance documents
Financial performance
Trading performance
Medical claims
Case studies presented as exact
Testimonials presented as literal
```

Allowed for:

```text
Blog posts
Personal essays
Founder education
Brand storytelling
Composite teaching stories
Anonymized lessons
Creative narrative content
```

## 15. Mode-aware truth policy

Every output should have a truth mode when relevant:

```text
Factual Mode
Marketing Mode
Narrative Truth Mode
Composite Teaching Story
Fictionalized Brand Essay
Legal/Compliance Mode
Investor Materials Mode
Internal Draft Mode
Research Hypothesis Mode
```

Each mode carries different rules for:

```text
Evidence standard
Disclaimer requirement
Allowed compression
Allowed uncertainty
Approval gate
Publication readiness
```

## 16. Knowledge Promotion Gate integration

The Governor must coordinate with the Knowledge Promotion Gate.

Default:

```text
Conversation ≠ Knowledge
AI output ≠ Firm truth
Draft ≠ Approved memory
```

Flow:

```text
Conversation / task output
→ Candidate Insight
→ Claim extraction
→ Source/provenance check
→ Human or delegated approval
→ Knowledge Bank / Knowledge Graph
```

Options:

```text
Approve
Edit
Reject
Archive
Expire
Mark as hypothesis
```

## 17. Operating-machine application

### LP Fundraising Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Loop suitability required.
Compliance linter required.
Human approval before external send.
Source provenance required.
No invented email verification.
No unsupported LP fit claims.
Suppression lists respected.
```

### Brand Sponsorship + Productions Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Sponsor claims must be source-backed or clearly framed.
Scooter origin story must remain truthful.
No private attendee data exported.
No implied investment access.
No sponsor influence over investment decisions.
Human approval before sponsor outreach.
Narrative Truth Mode allowed only for disclosed storytelling assets.
```

### Early-Stage Deal Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Founder claims must be sourced.
Market claims must be sourced or labeled as hypothesis.
Diligence gaps must be visible.
IC recommendation requires hostile review.
No investment decision marked final without MP decision.
```

### Secondaries Investment Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Brokerage/fund separation check required.
Conflict review required.
Legal/compliance issue spotting required.
Pricing assumptions labeled.
No external model use for confidential opportunity data unless explicitly approved.
```

### IC Decision Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Participant prep must match permission scope.
External screen-share-only mode must hide internal deliberation.
Dissent must be captured.
Decision rationale must be archived.
Unanswered questions must remain visible.
```

### Generic Meeting Intelligence Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Meeting summaries are draft records until reviewed or accepted.
Commitments must be separated from suggestions.
External follow-up drafts require approval.
Sensitive meeting data follows matter privacy mode.
```

### External Helper Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Provider access is matter-specific, time-bound, logged, and revocable.
Assigned AI wrangler prepares packets and tracks missing items.
Sensitive file sharing requires human approval.
Provider outputs are reviewed before becoming canonical.
```

### Repo / Product Machine

Governor rules:

```text
Source-of-truth rules apply.
No fake validation.
Maker/checker split for risky changes.
Objective gates required when available.
Human approval before merge/deploy.
```

## 18. UI requirements

Every serious task should display a compact Governance Card.

```text
Governance Card

Task type:
Assigned machine:
Assigned AI employee(s):
POV Card:
Review tier:
Privacy mode:
Truth mode:
Loop status:
State file/database:
Objective gate:
Hard stop:
Human approval required:
Escalation owner:
Status:
```

For recurring loops, show:

```text
Last run
Next run
Items processed
Items skipped
Errors
Approvals waiting
Gate results
Cost/time used
Kill switch
```

## 19. Acceptance criteria

This module is complete only when West Peek OS can:

```text
Classify task risk before execution.
Select POV stack and governance stack.
Decide whether a task should be a loop.
Require minimum viable loop fields.
Apply review tiers.
Enforce maker/checker split when needed.
Require objective gates when available.
Prevent self-assessed false completion.
Track recurring workflow state.
Apply hard stops and budget caps.
Route irreversible actions to approval.
Apply security tax to agents and tools.
Separate draft, reviewed, approved, and canonical states.
Apply Narrative Truth Mode only where appropriate.
Prevent AI outputs from becoming firm memory automatically.
Show governance status in the UI.
```

## 20. Final law

```text
West Peek OS is not merely an agent launcher.

It is an operating system that decides when to use agents, how to constrain them, how to check them, when to stop them, when to escalate, and what may become institutional truth.
```

# PRACTICAL OPERATING HARDENING, CONTINUITY, AND FIRM-LEVEL GAP CLOSURE

## Purpose

West Peek OS v2.6 promotes the system from an ambitious institutional architecture into a harder operating standard. The purpose is to ensure that West Peek OS does not become a beautiful plan that is too complex to operate. The system must behave predictably every morning, every week, every quarter, under stress, with imperfect data, limited human time, external helpers, AI employees, separate connected products, and sensitive firm information.

This integrated requirement adds the missing hardening layers that make the system safer, calmer, more useful, and more buildable.

## Governing Law

West Peek OS may hold enormous complexity internally, but it must expose only the most important decisions, risks, approvals, and opportunities to Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor at the right time.

West Peek OS must not require the Managing Partners to remember common operational safeguards. The system must select the right standard, privacy mode, approval rule, evidence requirement, taste bar, compliance review, and verification level before work begins.

---

# 1. Continuity Hardening Layer

## Purpose

West Peek OS must be able to continue operating in a reduced local/offline mode if frontier AI becomes unavailable, internet access fails, accounts are lost, subscriptions become too expensive, vendors change policies, cloud systems go down, or a device is lost.

This is not a new idea. It is a hardening standard.

## Emergency Sovereignty Package

West Peek OS must maintain an emergency sovereignty package containing:

```text
1. Active copy on the primary machine or primary private server
2. Encrypted external SSD recovery copy
3. Offsite encrypted copy
4. Local AI runtime
5. Local model weights or model manifest
6. Offline knowledge snapshot as of latest backup date
7. Prompt and operating-command library
8. Canonical West Peek OS documents
9. Odysseus fork source snapshot
10. Network OS export or integration snapshot
11. Pitch Lab handoff schema and approved exports
12. West Peek Live event/export schema and approved exports
13. Provider/contact continuity file
14. Restore checklist
15. SHA/integrity manifest
16. Last verified date
```

## Reduced Offline Mode

If West Peek OS loses internet or frontier model access, it must still support:

```text
- local search across approved knowledge snapshots
- local AI chat with available local model
- meeting prep from stored records
- relationship summaries from last synced Network OS data
- IC packet review from local documents
- task and approval queue viewing
- emergency provider contact lookup
- fund/legal/admin deadline review
- restore checklist execution
```

## Continuity Drill Cadence

```text
Weekly: backup status check
Monthly: local model boot check
Quarterly: restore dry run
Semiannual: external SSD/offsite copy verification
Annual: full continuity drill and vendor dependency review
```

## Separate Connected Product Continuity

Network OS, Pitch Lab, and West Peek Live remain separate products. West Peek OS must maintain continuity interfaces for each, including:

```text
- export schema
- sync status
- last successful pull/push
- deep-link map
- API/webhook health
- fallback CSV/JSON export path
- recovery owner
- provider credentials location
- data classification map
```

---

# 2. Decision Rights Matrix

## Purpose

Approval gates are not enough. West Peek OS must know exactly what each AI employee, external helper, and human may do by domain.

## Permission Classes

```text
AUTO-DO
AI may complete internally and log.

DRAFT-ONLY
AI may prepare but not send, publish, commit, contact, or execute externally.

RECOMMEND-ONLY
AI may analyze and recommend but may not draft an outbound artifact without human request.

RESEARCH-ONLY
AI may collect and summarize approved sources.

ASK-BEFORE-MODEL
AI must request approval before using an external LLM or web tool.

ASK-BEFORE-MEMORY
AI must request approval before promoting output to firm memory.

ASK-BEFORE-CONTACT
AI must request approval before contacting anyone.

ASK-BEFORE-SPEND
AI must request approval before committing cost, vendor work, paid tools, deposits, or sponsorship obligations.

NEVER
AI must not perform the action under any circumstance.
```

## Domain Matrix

| Domain | AI Can Auto-Do | AI Must Ask | AI Must Never Do |
|---|---|---|---|
| Internal briefs | generate summaries, classify urgency, prepare packets | promote to permanent knowledge | invent facts or hide uncertainty |
| Email | draft, summarize, classify | send, schedule send, forward externally | impersonate a human without review |
| LP fundraising | research public LP data, draft profiles, score fit | contact LPs, send materials, discuss terms | make promises, imply returns, misstate fund terms |
| Early-stage deals | intake, summarize, research, draft diligence questions | indicate investment interest externally | promise investment or access |
| Secondaries | research public company data, prepare internal checklist | use brokerage-derived data for fund workflow | blur brokerage and fund records |
| Sponsor/events | research brands, draft packages, prepare proposals | send sponsor pitches, promise attendee access | sell access to deal flow, LPs, IC, or investment decisions |
| Legal/compliance | flag risks, prepare counsel packets | send legal drafts externally | present as legal advice |
| Fund admin/tax/audit | assemble packets, track deadlines | share documents with providers | expose unrelated firm data |
| Public content | draft, suggest, package | publish | fabricate metrics, testimonials, or investment claims |
| Knowledge | propose candidate insights | promote memory | silently store sensitive facts |
| Models/data | classify sensitivity | use frontier models with non-public data | send highly sensitive/local-only data externally |
| Money/vendor actions | prepare comparisons, draft budget | spend, approve invoice, sign contract | commit funds without human approval |

---

# 3. Data Classification Matrix

## Purpose

Every record, document, note, packet, model prompt, and agent output must carry a data classification. Model routing, storage, sharing, logging, and memory promotion follow classification.

## Classification Levels

```text
PUBLIC
Already public or explicitly approved for public use.

INTERNAL
Internal West Peek operational material, not confidential enough for lockdown.

PRIVATE
Relationship, strategy, or internal context that should not leave approved systems.

SENSITIVE
Material that could harm West Peek, a founder, LP, partner, sponsor, or portfolio company if exposed.

HIGHLY SENSITIVE
Legal, board, secondaries, LP private notes, internal debate, or partner-private material.

REGULATED / COMPLIANCE-SENSITIVE
Fundraising, securities, secondaries, broker-dealer-adjacent, performance, LP, legal, tax, or audit records.

LOCAL-ONLY
May not be sent to external LLMs or external tools except under explicit human-approved exception.

NEVER STORE
Secrets, passwords, API keys, one-time codes, raw credentials, and ephemeral sensitive data.
```

## Model Routing Rule

```text
PUBLIC → Frontier allowed with logging.
INTERNAL → local/private preferred; frontier allowed only if purpose-approved.
PRIVATE → local/private default; frontier requires redaction and approval.
SENSITIVE → local/private default; external use blocked unless exceptional approval.
HIGHLY SENSITIVE → local-only unless counsel/MP exception.
REGULATED → compliance sentinel review before external use.
LOCAL-ONLY → no external LLM or web tool.
NEVER STORE → secrets vault only or discard.
```

---

# 4. Compliance Sentinel

## Purpose

West Peek touches venture fundraising, secondaries, sponsor revenue, founder support, public content, LP communications, and external helper workflows. Compliance issue-spotting must be permanent system behavior.

The Compliance Sentinel is not legal counsel. It is a flagging layer that routes risky work to human/counsel review.

## Detection Categories

```text
investment advice risk
broker-dealer language risk
fundraising claim risk
performance advertising risk
testimonial or endorsement risk
solicitation risk
promissory return language
guaranteed income language
confidential deal leakage
MNPI concern
portfolio confidential information risk
LP private information risk
sponsor access misrepresentation
fund/sponsor/entity revenue confusion
AI-generated financial advice risk
```

## Required Output

When flagged, the system must say:

```text
Compliance-sensitive. Human/legal review required before external use.
```

It must also show:

```text
- flagged phrase or action
- risk category
- why it matters
- safer alternative language
- required reviewer
- approval status
```

---

# 5. Receipts Layer / Evidence Vault

## Purpose

Every serious claim should be backed by receipts. This applies to LP materials, sponsor decks, IC memos, public content, fundraising claims, community claims, portfolio support claims, event metrics, and strategic planning.

## Claim Status Labels

```text
KNOWN
Supported by source evidence.

BELIEVED
Reasonable internal belief, not yet proven.

ASSUMED
Working assumption for planning.

ESTIMATED
Based on calculation, approximation, or incomplete inputs.

ASPIRATIONAL
Future goal or intended direction.

STORYTELLING-ADJUSTED
Narrative Truth Mode detail; disclosed as adjusted.

UNVERIFIED
Do not use externally without verification.
```

## Receipt Types

```text
source document
email
calendar event
contract
screenshot
file
web citation
calculation
prior decision record
user approval
validation log
provider output
meeting transcript/summary
external helper confirmation
```

## External Use Rule

Investor, LP, sponsor, public, and legal/compliance-sensitive materials must attach receipts or mark claims clearly as assumptions, estimates, aspirations, or narrative-adjusted.

---

## Purpose

West Peek is not trying to feel like generic enterprise SaaS or generic venture content. Taste is a business asset.

The Taste Intelligence Layer protects quality, elegance, credibility, emotional resonance, and brand fit.

```text
West Peek decks
LP materials
sponsor one-pagers
landing pages

West Peek Live event pages
West Peek Rooms assets
public essays
founder resources
portfolio support materials
hospitality/cultural concepts
speaker/event experiences
```

```text
Does this feel premium?
Does this feel try-hard?
Does this sound generic?
Would a serious person trust this?
Would a tastemaker share this?
Is the design emotionally coherent?
Is the writing sharp or just correct?
Is this too much?
Is this not enough?
Does this fit West Peek’s warm, accessible, unconventional brand?
```

## Taste Review Output

```text
Taste verdict: Pass / Needs refinement / Too generic / Too much / Not credible / Brand mismatch
Primary issue
Suggested fix
Human approval required if public-facing
```

# 7. Cognitive Load Budget

## Purpose

West Peek OS exists to reduce overwhelm. It must not become a cockpit with 900 blinking lights.

## Rule

West Peek OS may hold infinite complexity internally, but its default UI may only surface the decisions that matter now.

## Default Limits

```text
Morning: max 3 priorities per MP
Midday: max 3 checks per MP
End-of-day: max 3 closure prompts per MP
Approval Inbox: grouped by urgency and risk
Command Center Mode: explicit opt-in for full detail
```

## Escalation Rule

The system may break the limit only for:

```text
legal/compliance emergency
money movement risk
external deadline
data/security incident
partner-level decision
fundraising commitment risk
investment decision deadline
```
