# Prompt Enhancer Portal + Intent-to-Execution Interface

## Purpose

West Peek OS must not assume that Sequoia Taylor, Scooter Taylor, future employees, advisors, or external collaborators will always know how to write perfect prompts.

The system should accept messy human intent and convert it into a strong, governed, task-ready assignment.

```text
Prompt Library = reusable templates.
Prompt Enhancer Portal = live intent translation and prompt improvement system.
```

The Prompt Enhancer Portal is a first-class product surface inside West Peek OS.

It helps a user move from:

```text
"I need help with this thing."
```

to:

```text
A well-scoped task with the correct expert POV, privacy mode, evidence standard, output format, assigned AI employee, assigned machine, approval rules, and definition of done.
```

This is not a novelty prompt toy. It is the front door for safe, high-quality AI work.

---

## Why This Matters

West Peek OS is agentic and operational. Bad prompts can create:

```text
wrong task framing
wrong privacy mode
wrong model routing
wrong AI employee assignment
wrong evidence standard
wrong output format
wrong approval gate
wrong memory promotion behavior
wrong level of polish
```

The Prompt Enhancer Portal prevents that.

It does not require the user to know the system architecture. It quietly adds the structure needed for elite execution.

---

## Core Law

```text
West Peek OS must not require users to be expert prompters.

Users provide intent.
West Peek OS converts intent into governed work.
```

---

## User Experience

The user enters a rough request:

```text
"Help me prep for this founder meeting."
```

West Peek OS returns an enhanced prompt / task card:

```text
Task: Prepare founder meeting packet for [Founder / Company].
Lead POV: Seed-stage VC GP + founder-facing operator.
Supporting POV: Market researcher + meeting strategist.
Hostile Reviewer: Skeptical IC member looking for weak founder-market fit.
Privacy Mode: Local or Hybrid Private depending on attached materials.
Assigned Machine: Generic Meeting Intelligence Machine + Early-Stage Deal Machine.
Assigned AI Employees: Walter, Walker, Priya, Paige.
Inputs Needed: calendar invite, CRM record, deck if available, prior notes.
Output: 1-page prep packet, 10 questions, risk flags, desired meeting outcome, follow-up draft.
Approval Required: human approval before any external follow-up is sent.
Definition of Done: meeting packet is useful within 5 minutes and all claims are sourced or marked unknown.
```

The user can then choose:

```text
Run this
Edit prompt
Make shorter
Make deeper
Use local only
Add hostile review
Turn into recurring workflow
Save as reusable prompt
```

---

## Prompt Enhancer Portal Modes

### 1. Quick Enhance

Turns rough instructions into a stronger prompt without over-processing.

Use for:

```text
small research asks
meeting prep
drafting help
summaries
brainstorms
quick analysis
```

### 2. Executive Prompt Builder

Creates a full task card with POV, standard, output format, risks, and approval gates.

Use for:

```text
LP materials
founder diligence
IC prep
sponsor decks
legal/compliance-sensitive drafts
public-facing content
market intelligence packets
```

### 3. Machine Assignment Builder

Turns an ask into a machine workflow.

Example:

```text
"Find brands that might sponsor West Peek Rooms."
```

Becomes:

```text
Brand Sponsorship + Productions Machine
→ Sponsorship Scout
→ Marketing Lead
→ Compliance Linter
→ Sponsor Meeting Buddy
→ Human approval queue
```

### 4. Agent Instruction Builder

Creates role-specific instructions for a named AI employee.

Example:

```text
"Give this to Parker."
```

Becomes:

```text
Parker: build the event operating plan, sponsor deliverables checklist, venue options, production needs, and approval gates for The Tastemaker Table.
```

### 5. Privacy-Safe Prompt Builder

Builds prompts that avoid leaking private data to frontier models.

Example:

```text
"Use Claude to help with this founder deck."
```

System response:

```text
This contains founder confidential material.
Recommended mode: Local or Hybrid Private.
Safe frontier alternative: ask external model for a public market primer only, then combine locally with the confidential deck.
```

### 6. Public Intelligence Forge Prompt Builder

Creates prompts for frontier models using public data only.

Example:

```text
Forge a public intelligence packet on AI tools for medical practice administration.
Use public sources only.
Do not include West Peek private records.
Return market map, competitors, questions, red flags, and source list.
```

### 7. Reusable Prompt Productizer

Turns a good one-time prompt into a reusable internal template.

Stores:

```text
prompt title
machine
AI employee
privacy mode
inputs
outputs
definition of done
approval rule
examples
version history
```

---

## Prompt Enhancer Inputs

The portal asks only useful questions. It should infer defaults where safe.

Possible inputs:

```text
What are you trying to do?
Who is the audience?
How much time do you have?
How polished should it be?
Is this private, public, sensitive, or compliance-related?
Should this be quick, deep, or institutional-grade?
Should an AI employee handle this?
Should it become a recurring workflow?
Should the result be saved as knowledge?
```

The portal should not interrogate the user with unnecessary questions. When the risk is low, it should proceed.

---

## Prompt Enhancer Outputs

Every enhanced prompt can output as one of these formats:

```text
Simple prompt
Task card
AI employee assignment
Machine workflow
Meeting Buddy request
IC Portal request
Public Intelligence Forge request
Academy learning request
External helper packet request
Sponsor prospecting request
Repo/product implementation request
Reusable prompt template
```

---

## Integration With POV Intelligence Layer

Prompt Enhancer must call the POV Intelligence Layer before serious work begins.

It selects:

```text
Lead POV
Supporting POV
Reviewer POV
Hostile Review POV
Definition of Done
Failure Modes
Evidence Standard
Truth Mode
Taste Standard
Privacy Mode
Approval Gate
```

This prevents generic assistant behavior.

---

## Integration With Model Privacy Gate

Prompt Enhancer must label the prompt before routing:

```text
Local Mode
Frontier Mode
Lockdown Mode
Hybrid Private
Public Intelligence Only
```

If a request includes sensitive firm data, founder data, LP data, IC materials, board notes, legal issues, secondaries records, or brokerage-sensitive material, the portal must default away from frontier model exposure.

---

## Integration With Machines

Prompt Enhancer should be able to route work to the correct machine.

Examples:

```text
"Find LPs" → LP Fundraising Machine
"Prep this founder call" → Generic Meeting Intelligence Machine + Early-Stage Deal Machine
"Help me understand this market" → Market + Industry Intelligence Academy
"Build a sponsor deck" → Brand Sponsorship + Productions Machine
"Check this external counsel request" → External Helper Machine
"Make this public-facing" → Brand + Content + PR Machine + Compliance Sentinel
"Turn this into a workflow" → Operational Intelligence + Common Sense Governor
```

---

## Integration With AI Employees

Prompt Enhancer can assign named AI employees.

It must show:

```text
Assigned employee
Why assigned
Task scope
Permission scope
Required output
Approval gate
Deadline or schedule
Handoff destination
```

Example:

```text
Assigned: Wynn
Role: Sponsorship Scout
Reason: This is sponsor prospecting.
Task: Find 25 brands aligned with West Peek Rooms.
Output: sponsor prospect table with category, fit score, buyer persona, likely offer angle, and source notes.
Approval: no outbound contact without human approval.
```

---

## Prompt Enhancer UI Requirements

The portal should have:

```text
Messy Request Box
Enhance Button
Risk/Privacy Indicator
POV Card Preview
Suggested Machine
Suggested AI Employees
Output Format Selector
Depth Selector
Time Available Selector
Approval Requirement Badge
Run / Edit / Save Template / Send to Queue controls
```

Suggested controls:

```text
Make clearer
Make more senior
Make safer
Make more concise
Make more detailed
Make local-only
Add hostile review
Add citations/evidence
Add compliance review
Add taste review
Turn into workflow
Turn into recurring agent task
Save to prompt library
```

---

## Prompt Library Relationship

The Prompt Library still exists, but it becomes downstream of the portal.

```text
Prompt Enhancer Portal
→ creates improved prompts
→ optionally saves reusable prompts
→ tracks version history
→ logs usage/results
→ promotes high-performing prompts
```

The library becomes a curated repository of proven prompts, not a static pile of prompt examples.

---

## Prompt Quality Scoring

The portal should score each enhanced prompt across:

```text
clarity
specificity
privacy safety
evidence standard
output usefulness
role assignment
approval safety
risk coverage
done definition
```

Low-scoring prompts are not blocked automatically unless risky. They are improved or routed for human clarification.

---

## Prompt Enhancer Data Model

Entities:

```text
PromptRequest
EnhancedPrompt
PromptTemplate
PromptVersion
PromptRun
PromptOutcome
PromptQualityScore
PromptRiskLabel
PromptPrivacyLabel
PromptMachineAssignment
PromptAgentAssignment
PromptApprovalRule
```

Each prompt run records:

```text
original request
enhanced prompt
model/machine/agent used
privacy mode
input data labels
output generated
approval status
whether saved to library
whether promoted to knowledge
user rating
result quality
```

---

## Failure Modes to Avoid

The Prompt Enhancer must avoid:

```text
over-engineering tiny asks
asking too many questions
routing private data to frontier models
assigning the wrong machine
creating fake certainty
making outputs too generic
saving weak prompts as templates
allowing prompt injection through pasted content
creating prompts that bypass approval gates
turning everything into a recurring workflow
```

---

## Acceptance Criteria

The Prompt Enhancer Portal is accepted when:

```text
- a messy request can become a task-ready prompt
- serious tasks receive a POV Card
- privacy mode is selected before routing
- sensitive data is not silently sent externally
- the correct machine and AI employees are suggested
- the user can edit before running
- enhanced prompts can be saved as reusable templates
- prompt outcomes can be scored
- approval gates are attached when needed
- prompt history is auditable
```

# Integrated Requirement Status

COMPLETE — PROMPT ENHANCER PORTAL ADDED AS A FIRST-CLASS WEST PEEK OS PRODUCT SURFACE.

# FIELD CAPTURE, RELATIONSHIP INTAKE, CLOUD COMMAND UI, PRIVACY/AIRLOCK CLARITY, AND WEEKEND AGENT OPERATIONS

**Status:** CANONICAL INTEGRATED REQUIREMENT  
**Date:** June 23, 2026  
**Purpose:** Address Scooter Taylor's operating concern: when West Peek is moving fast at events, conferences, founder meetings, VC meetings, LP conversations, dinners, and community gatherings, the system must make it effortless to capture raw context, classify it, route it, and preserve it in the correct place without overwhelming Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor.

## 1. Scooter Concern — System Requirement

When Scooter Taylor or Sequoia Taylor is at an event, conference, dinner, founder meeting, LP conversation, VC meeting, or fast-moving community moment, West Peek OS must support instant capture.

The user should not need to know:

- which system owns the record;
- which AI employee should handle it;
- whether it is a task, relationship note, deal note, meeting note, sponsor lead, event follow-up, LP signal, or portfolio support item;
- whether it belongs in West Peek OS, Network OS, Pitch Lab, West Peek Live, or another connected system;
- whether a task should be created;
- whether human approval is needed.

The system must handle that.

Canonical law:

```text
Fast human capture must be enough.
West Peek OS is responsible for parsing, routing, assigning, and preserving the captured information.
```

## 2. New Product Surface — Field Capture Inbox

West Peek OS must include a **Field Capture Inbox**.

This is the fastest possible capture surface for real-world moments.

Supported capture methods:

- mobile quick note;
- voice memo;
- dictated note;
- photo of badge/business card;
- screenshot;
- forwarded email;
- copied text;
- calendar-linked meeting note;
- Slack/text paste if later integrated;
- post-event batch upload;
- manual desktop entry.

The UI should allow Scooter or Sequoia to enter messy raw context such as:

```text
Met Jordan from BrightPath at the conference. Already in Network OS. We agreed I should intro them to Maya, Chris, and Elena. Also they may be a fit for the education event. Follow up next week.
```

The system then creates a structured capture packet.

## 3. Field Capture Packet Standard

Every field capture becomes a packet before anything is stored permanently.

Packet fields:

```text
capture_id
captured_by
captured_at
capture_location_optional
source_type
raw_input
inferred_people
inferred_companies
inferred_event
inferred_relationships
possible_existing_records
suggested_system_destination
suggested_machine
suggested_ai_employee
suggested_tasks
suggested_followups
privacy_label
approval_required
confidence_score
human_review_status
routing_status
```

This packet lives first in the Field Capture Inbox.

Nothing is lost.

Nothing is silently over-promoted.

## 4. AI Employee Assignment — Intake Router

West Peek OS must assign a named AI employee to manage the Field Capture Inbox.

Canonical AI employee:

```text
Wells — Knowledge Manager
Primary owner of capture preservation and source placement.
```

Supporting AI employees:

```text
Porter — CRM Sync + Verification Mirror
Handles Network OS matching, duplicate detection, and relationship record sync.

Waverly — Community Manager
Handles community/member/event-related routing.

Walter — Meeting Buddy
Handles meeting-derived notes, commitments, and follow-up extraction.

Walker — Scooter AI Chief of Staff
Handles Scooter-owned follow-up prioritization.

Wren — Sequoia AI Chief of Staff
Handles Sequoia-owned follow-up prioritization.

Priya — Associate
Handles founder/company/deal follow-up.

Wesley — LP Relations Manager
Handles LP-related follow-up.

Parker — Event Planner
Handles event/sponsor/community programming follow-up.
```

## 5. Routing Logic

The Intake Router must classify every capture into one or more routing categories.

Routing categories:

```text
Relationship update
Task / follow-up
Founder opportunity
Existing founder update
LP prospect or LP relationship update
VC/co-investor relationship
Portfolio support request
Community signal
Event follow-up
Sponsor lead
External helper item
Knowledge archive item
IC / investment diligence item
Meeting note
No-action archive
```

A single capture may route to multiple places.

Example:

```text
Met founder already in Network OS and agreed to intro them to X, Y, Z.
```

System actions:

```text
1. Match founder to existing Network OS profile.
2. Create/update relationship note.
3. Create intro follow-up tasks.
4. Assign draft responsibility to correct AI employee.
5. Route external intro drafts to human approval.
6. Update founder/company relationship context.
7. If relevant, notify Community Machine, Deal Machine, or Portfolio Support Machine.
8. Preserve original capture packet in Knowledge Machine.
```

## 6. Source-of-Truth Placement Law

West Peek OS must not duplicate system ownership blindly.

Canonical source-of-truth rule:

```text
Relationship identity and relationship history live in Network OS.
West Peek OS may display, orchestrate, summarize, and route relationship intelligence, but Network OS remains the relationship system of record.
```

The capture may create multiple records:

- Network OS profile update;
- West Peek OS task;
- West Peek OS meeting note;
- West Peek OS deal note;
- West Peek OS event/sponsor lead;
- West Peek OS knowledge archive entry.

But each record must know which system owns the canonical version.

## 7. Separate Connected Product Boundary Reaffirmed

Network OS, Pitch Lab, and West Peek Live remain separate products.

They are not rebuilt inside the Odysseus fork.

They must be:

```text
deeply connected
reachable from West Peek OS
synced through approved APIs/webhooks where useful
linked through shared entity IDs
visible through panels/cards/deep links
subject to privacy and approval rules
```

### Network OS

Source of truth for:

```text
people
relationships
relationship notes
contact history
Gmail-trigger intake
community/founder/LP/contact intelligence
relationship follow-through
```

### Pitch Lab

Source of truth for:

```text
founder pitch practice
founder consent to share
shared pitch/story packets
founder readiness signals
```

### West Peek Live

Source of truth for:

```text
event execution
attendee engagement
speaker/session data
video/live-session production data
post-event engagement signals
```

West Peek OS is the command center and orchestrator, not a replacement for these products.

## 8. What It Looks Like in the UI

West Peek OS should be cloud-based and reachable from a private employee navigation link on `westpeek.ventures`.

Example navigation:

```text
westpeek.ventures
→ NAV: Team / Employee Portal / WP OS
→ login
→ West Peek OS Command Center
```

The nav item must be employee/internal-facing. It should not expose private data or imply public access.

### Default UI Principle

West Peek OS may hold massive complexity internally, but the default UI must be calm.

Canonical law:

```text
The system should hide complexity until it is needed.
```

### Main Left Navigation

Recommended main navigation:

```text
Today
Capture Inbox
Approvals
Relationships
Meetings
Deals
Fundraising
Events
Portfolio
Machines
AI Employees
Academy

```

### Today Screen

The Today screen should show only:

```text
What needs attention now
What changed since last login
What is waiting for approval
What meetings need prep
What AI employees completed
What is blocked
Top 3 recommended actions
```

### Capture Inbox Screen

The Capture Inbox should show:

```text
New captures
AI-parsed summary
suggested destination
suggested tasks
confidence level
approval needed
one-click actions
```

One-click actions:

```text
Approve routing
Edit routing
```

### Relationship Detail Panel

When a capture relates to a person, the UI should show:

### Machine Context Panel

If the note affects a machine, the UI should show the target machine.

Example:

```text
This capture affects:
- Relationship Machine

```

## 9. Mobile-First Capture UX

For Scooter and Sequoia at events, the mobile experience matters more than the desktop experience.

Mobile capture must support:

- one-tap voice note;
- quick text note;
- badge/card photo;
- meeting/event tag;
- person/company search;
- “already in Network OS” toggle;
- “needs follow-up” toggle;
- “private/sensitive” toggle;
- submit without filling a long form.

The system can ask clarifying questions later. The capture moment must stay fast.

Canonical law:

```text
Never make the human do structured data entry while the relationship moment is happening.
```

## 10. AI Parsing and Assignment Workflow

```text
Human captures raw note
→ Field Capture Inbox
→ Intake Router parses entities/actions
→ Source-of-Truth Resolver checks existing records
→ Privacy Classifier labels sensitivity
→ Task Extractor identifies commitments
→ Routing Engine assigns machine/AI employee
→ Approval Engine determines if human approval is needed
→ Destination Sync prepares updates
→ Human reviews only exceptions and external actions
→ Approved updates sync to correct systems
→ Knowledge Manager archives capture packet
```

## 11. Do Not Miss Anything Rule

Every capture must end in one of these states:

```text
Routed
Waiting on human review
Waiting on external info
Duplicate merged
Archived no action
Blocked
Rejected
```

No capture may remain in raw unprocessed limbo beyond the system-defined SLA.

Suggested SLA:

```text
High confidence / low risk: processed within same operating window.
Medium confidence: queued for daily review.
Low confidence or high sensitivity: escalated to human review.
```

## 12. Privacy Explanation — Plain English

West Peek OS has two different questions to answer:

```text
1. Which AI brain is allowed to help?
2. What information is allowed to cross the wall?
```

That is why the system needs both a **Model Privacy Gate** and a **Double Airlock**.

### Local Mode

Local Mode means West Peek OS uses local/private models and private stored knowledge.

Use this for:

```text
founder confidential data
LP notes
IC memos
board notes
secondaries
legal/compliance
private relationship notes
internal decisions
```

Plain English:

```text
The work stays inside West Peek-controlled infrastructure.
```

### Frontier Mode

Frontier Mode means West Peek intentionally uses outside AI providers such as OpenAI, Claude, Gemini, Perplexity, OpenRouter, or similar.

Use this for:

```text
public market research
public source discovery
generic primers
public competitor maps
public podcast/interview summaries
non-confidential brainstorming
```

Plain English:

```text
The outside model can help, but only with data approved to leave.
```

### Lockdown Mode

Lockdown Mode means:

```text
No frontier models.
No external APIs for reasoning.
No web unless explicitly allowed.
Local/private only.
```

Use this for:

```text
sensitive deals
secondaries pricing
legal issues
private LP notes
board materials
confidential founder materials
brokerage/fund separation-sensitive records
```

Plain English:

```text
Nothing leaves. The system works from local/private data and approved internal knowledge only.
```

## 13. Double Airlock System

The Double Airlock protects data in both directions.

```text
West Peek Local Core
→ Outbound Airlock
→ Frontier Chamber
→ Inbound Airlock
→ Knowledge Promotion Gate
→ West Peek Local Core
```

### Local Core

Contains:

```text
Network OS relationship data
LP records
founder records
IC memos
secondaries records
portfolio data
meeting notes
agent databases
legal/compliance records
private knowledge graph
```

### Outbound Airlock

Before anything leaves West Peek OS, the system checks:

```text
What is being sent?
Why is it being sent?
Which provider will see it?
What is the privacy label?
Is private data included?
Can it be sanitized?
Who approved it?
Is there a safer local alternative?
```

If the answer is unsafe, the call is blocked.

### Frontier Chamber

The external LLM works only on the approved payload.

It does not receive the whole firm memory.

It does not receive the whole relationship graph.

It does not receive private founder/LP/IC records unless explicitly approved under a special exception.

### Inbound Airlock

When the external model returns an answer, West Peek OS does not automatically believe it.

The system checks:

```text
What claims did it make?
Are sources attached?
What is public fact vs model inference?
What is uncertain?
Does it conflict with stored knowledge?
Should it expire later?
Should it become reusable knowledge?
```

### Knowledge Promotion Gate

External output becomes firm knowledge only after approval.

```text
External answer
→ candidate intelligence
→ approve / edit / reject / archive
→ Knowledge Bank
```

Canonical law:

```text
Nothing leaves without permission.
Nothing becomes permanent truth without promotion.
```

## 14. How West Peek Uses Frontier Power Without Leaking Private Data

West Peek OS does not download the full brain of frontier LLMs.

Instead, it uses frontier models and public web sources to create reusable **Public Intelligence Packets**.

Example:

```text
Create a public market intelligence packet on dental practice management software.
Use public sources only. Do not use private West Peek data.
```

The output becomes a candidate packet.

After review, it is stored privately in West Peek OS.

Later, in Lockdown Mode, the local model can use:

```text
private founder deck
+ approved public market packet
+ West Peek investment criteria
```

without sending the founder deck to any external model.

This is how West Peek gets the power of frontier research while keeping private work private.

## 15. Weekend and Seven-Day Agent Operations

AI employees work seven days per week inside defined operating windows.

But weekend work must not create bottlenecks or unsafe action buildup.

### Weekend Operating Principle

```text
AI employees may keep preparing, sorting, researching, drafting, and queuing work on weekends.
They may not create irreversible external action without approval.
```

### Weekend Queue Types

Weekend work should be separated into:

```text
Auto-process internally
Needs weekday review
Needs urgent human review
Approval-ready drafts
Blocked waiting on human
Low-priority archive
```

### Weekend Digest

The system should send a calm weekend digest, not 50 alerts.

Weekend digest should include:

```text
urgent only
approval-ready items
events/meetings within 48 hours
sensitive-risk flags
top 3 recommended actions
what can safely wait until Monday
```

### Anti-Bottleneck Rule

The system should continue preparing work up to the approval boundary.

Examples:

```text
Draft the intro email, but do not send.
Prepare the LP list, but do not outreach.
Update internal relationship notes, but queue uncertain syncs.
Build the sponsor proposal, but route for approval.
Prepare meeting packet, but ask before contacting attendees.
```

### Weekend Escalation Rules

Escalate only if:

```text
a meeting/event is within 48 hours
an approval expires or creates reputational risk
an external helper deadline will be missed
an LP/founder relationship may be damaged by silence
security/privacy issue is detected
a system failure blocks scheduled work
```

Do not escalate for normal backlog accumulation.

## 16. Cloud-Based Deployment Requirement

West Peek OS should be a cloud-accessible authenticated web application hosted at a domain of West Peek’s choosing.

Requirements:

```text
authenticated login
role-based access
private employee nav link from westpeek.ventures
mobile-first capture
desktop command center
secure API integrations
privacy mode controls
audit logs
admin console
```

Possible domain patterns:

```text
os.westpeek.ventures
team.westpeek.ventures
command.westpeek.ventures
internal.westpeek.ventures
```

Public website nav should link to the employee portal without exposing internal data.

Example:

```text
westpeek.ventures → Team Login → os.westpeek.ventures
```

## 17. Scooter / Sequoia Low-Overwhelm UX Law

West Peek OS must not become a giant cockpit.

Default UX should answer:

```text
What happened?
What matters?
What needs my approval?
What can wait?
What should I do next?
```

The system should not show every machine, every agent, every queue, and every internal workflow unless the user enters Command Center Mode.

Canonical law:

```text
Complexity belongs behind the scenes.
Judgment belongs in front.
```

## 18. Acceptance Criteria

This integrated requirement is implemented when West Peek OS can:

```text
1. Capture a messy mobile note from an event.
2. Identify people, companies, commitments, and next actions.
3. Match existing Network OS records.
4. Suggest correct destination systems.
5. Assign AI employees.
6. Create internal tasks and approval-ready drafts.
7. Update or queue Network OS sync.
8. Preserve the original capture packet.
9. Show the item calmly in the UI.
10. Explain privacy mode and data-routing decisions in plain English.
11. Run weekend agent work without spamming the humans.
12. Keep private data out of frontier models unless explicitly approved.
```

## 19. Final Field Capture and Cloud Command Law

```text
West Peek OS must make fast capture easy, routing intelligent, privacy visible, and complexity calm.
```

# INTENT-DRIVEN RELATIONSHIP OS, COMMAND CENTER, PRIVACY AIRLOCK, AND UI SKETCHES

**Status:** CANONICAL INTEGRATED REQUIREMENT  
**Date:** June 23, 2026  
**Purpose:** Convert the Relationship OS, Field Capture flow, Network OS integration, privacy architecture, and Command Center from conceptual components into explicit product behavior and UI surfaces.

---

## 1. GOVERNING PRINCIPLE

West Peek OS must be intent-driven, not menu-driven.

Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor should not have to manually decide which machine owns a task, which AI employee should execute it, which system should store it, which sender should make an introduction, or which privacy mode applies.

The human provides intent. West Peek OS converts intent into governed work.

### Canonical Law — Intent-Driven Work

```text
Humans provide intent.
West Peek OS selects the machine, assigns employees, determines routing, prepares outputs, applies privacy and governance rules, and surfaces only the approval or judgment needed.
```

### What this means in practice

Bad product behavior:

```text
Go to Relationship OS.
Choose connection type.
Pick sender.
Choose intro style.
Pick employee.
Pick approval mode.
Route to Network OS.
Remember to update the touchpoint.
```

Correct West Peek OS behavior:

```text
“Scooter met Jordan. We need to introduce him to Maya and Chris next week.”
```

West Peek OS then determines:

- this is relationship-related;
- Jordan exists or needs matching in Network OS;
- this is an introduction workflow;
- Scooter is the likely sender because he met Jordan;
- Wendy should package the approval for Scooter;
- Porter should prepare the Network OS update;
- Walker should only review if high-priority or sensitive;
- approval should go to Scooter;
- delivery follows Scooter’s default delivery preference;
- the touchpoint should be logged after send;
- a follow-up reminder should be created.

---

## 2. MACHINE VS. AI EMPLOYEE CLARIFICATION

Machines and AI employees are not the same thing.

### Machines

Machines are workflows, departments, or operating engines.

A machine owns:

- queues;
- stages;
- routing rules;
- data objects;
- triggers;
- handoffs;
- approval gates;
- status labels;
- reporting;
- audit logs.

Examples:

- Relationship OS;
- Field Capture Machine;
- LP Fundraising Machine;
- Event Sponsorship Machine;
- IC Decision Machine;
- Meeting Intelligence Machine;
- External Helper Machine;
- Privacy Center / Airlock Machine.

### AI Employees

AI employees are named operators who execute work inside one or more machines.

An AI employee owns:

- role;
- assigned queues;
- work product;
- daily schedule;
- permissions;
- approval limits;
- escalation rules;
- reporting duties.

### Canonical Law — Machine / Employee Distinction

```text
Machines are workflows.
AI employees execute inside machines.
Humans do not manually operate machines. Humans provide intent, approve meaningful actions, and make judgment calls.
```

---

## 3. W / P AI EMPLOYEE NAMING ENFORCEMENT

All West Peek AI employees must use first names beginning with W or P.

No new AI employee may be added with a name outside this convention unless the naming law is explicitly changed by Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor.

### Intake Employee Name

The prior proposed name “Ivy” is rejected because it violates the W / P naming rule.

The approved intake employee name is:

```text
Winton — Field Intake Coordinator
```

Rationale:

- begins with W;
- contains “int,” linking it to intake;
- sounds operational rather than decorative;
- does not collide with existing employees;
- is easy to reference in product copy: “Winton is processing this.”

---

## 4. WINTON — FIELD INTAKE COORDINATOR

Winton is the dedicated Field Intake Coordinator for West Peek OS.

Winton is not a replacement for Wendy, Willa, Walker, Wren, Porter, Wells, Waverly, or Network OS.

Winton owns the first structured pass over messy incoming signal.

### Winton owns

- raw field intake;
- fast capture parsing;
- entity extraction;
- person/company/event/topic detection;
- task detection;
- promise detection;
- source-of-truth placement recommendation;
- initial routing to machines;
- confidence scoring;
- duplicate detection;
- privacy classification suggestion;
- original capture preservation;
- initial AI employee assignment recommendation.

### Winton does not own

- Network OS final record authority;
- human-specific follow-through;
- strategic priority arbitration;
- knowledge promotion;
- external sending;
- final relationship judgment;
- final compliance approval.

### Winton’s working relationship with other employees

```text
Winton = intake brain
Porter = Network OS sync and verification
Wendy = Scooter-specific assistant follow-through
Willa = Sequoia-specific assistant follow-through
Walker = Scooter-side priority and escalation
Wren = Sequoia-side priority and escalation
Wells = durable memory and knowledge promotion
Waverly = community and connection opportunity operator
```

---

## 5. ASSISTANTS AS CONVERSATIONAL FRONT DOORS

Wendy and Willa are the conversational front doors for Scooter Taylor and Sequoia Taylor.

Scooter should be able to tell Wendy what he needs.
Sequoia should be able to tell Willa what she needs.

The assistant does not need to personally execute every downstream step. The assistant receives intent, invokes the right machine, coordinates with Winton and specialized employees, and packages the final human-facing approval or next action.

### Canonical Law — Assistant Front Door

```text
Wendy and Willa are the conversational front doors for Scooter Taylor and Sequoia Taylor.

They receive natural-language requests, package human-specific follow-through, prepare approval cards, and keep their human from needing to operate internal workflow mechanics.
```

### Example

Scooter says:

```text
“Wendy, get this Jordan intro handled and make sure we remember the cow farm detail.”
```

West Peek OS handles:

- relationship parsing;
- Network OS matching;
- relationship memory creation;
- connection workflow;
- sender assignment;
- draft preparation;
- approval card;
- touchpoint logging.

Scooter sees only what requires his approval or judgment.

---

## 6. GLOBAL + CAPTURE BUTTON

The + Capture button must be global in West Peek OS.

It must not live only inside Relationship OS because field captures may relate to any machine.

A capture may be about:

- a founder;
- an LP;
- a sponsor;
- an event;
- a legal item;
- a fund admin item;
- a portfolio need;
- a secondaries opportunity;
- a meeting note;
- a task;
- a person;
- a connection idea;
- a strategy note;
- an external helper request.

### Canonical Law — Global Capture

```text
+ Capture lives globally in West Peek OS.
Relationship-related captures route into Relationship OS and Network OS.
```

### Desktop placement

The + Capture button appears in the global app header.

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ West Peek OS        Search West Peek...              + Capture  Approvals │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The + Capture button appears in the center of the bottom nav.

```text
│                                    │
│           Today View               │
│                                    │
├─────────┬─────────┬──────┬─────────┬────────┤

│         │         │Capture│         │        │
```

```text
│                                              │

│                                              │
```

### Post-submit message

```text
Captured. Winton is processing this. You can keep moving.
```

### Product Law — No Structured Entry During the Moment

```text
Never make the human do structured data entry while the relationship moment is happening.
```

## 7. FIELD CAPTURE INBOX FLOW

Every capture enters the Field Capture Inbox before routing.

### Field Capture statuses

- New;
- Parsing;
- Needs match confirmation;
- Routed;
- Waiting on approval;
- Completed;
- Archived;
- Failed and escalated.

### Standard capture flow

```text
Scooter / Sequoia captures messy note
↓
Field Capture Inbox
↓
Winton parses + classifies + matches records
↓
Source-of-Truth Resolver decides where things belong
↓
Porter updates/queues Network OS changes when relationship data is involved
↓
Relevant machine receives work
↓
Assigned AI employee prepares next output
↓
Wendy/Willa package human-specific actions
↓
Walker/Wren escalate if priority/risk requires
↓
Approval Queue
↓
Human approves external action
↓
System logs outcome + updates records
↓
Wells promotes durable knowledge if needed
```

### Field Capture Inbox UI sketch

```text
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Field Capture Inbox                                      Filters: Mine ▾   │
├───────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ All Captures           │ Jordan / BrightPath — AfroTech                     │
│ Mine                   │ Source: Scooter voice note                         │
│ Scooter                │ Status: Routed                                     │
│ Sequoia                │                                                     │
│ Needs Review           │ Detected                                           │
│ Needs Match            │ • Existing Network OS contact                      │
│ Routed                 │ • Founder follow-up                                │
│ Approval-Ready         │ • 3 intro tasks                                    │
│ Sensitive              │ • Founder Salon fit                                │
│ Failed Parse           │ • Q3 fundraising signal                            │
│                        │                                                     │
│                        │ Assigned                                           │
│                        │ • Winton: intake complete                          │
│                        │ • Porter: Network OS update                        │
│                        │ • Wendy: intro drafts                              │
│                        │ • Waverly: event fit review                        │
│                        │                                                     │
│                        │ Approvals                                          │
│                        │ • 3 intro emails need Scooter approval             │
│                        │                                                     │
│                        │ [Approve Routing] [Edit Summary] [Open Audit]      │
└───────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 8. RAW CAPTURE PRESERVATION

West Peek OS must never lose the original human input.

Every capture has three layers.

### Layer 1 — Raw Capture

The original voice note, text note, photo, screenshot, forwarded email, or uploaded file.

Lives in:

```text
Field Capture Archive
```

### Layer 2 — Structured Interpretation

The AI-parsed summary.

Includes:

- people;
- companies;
- tasks;
- dates;
- promises;
- topics;
- machines;
- sensitivity;
- confidence;
- proposed routing.

Lives in:

```text
West Peek OS intake record
```

### Layer 3 — Destination Records

The system outputs created from the capture.

Examples:

- Network OS profile update;
- relationship memory item;
- task;
- intro draft;
- event suggestion;
- deal note;
- LP note;
- sponsor note;
- knowledge candidate;
- meeting prep item;
- approval item;
- connection suggestion.

Lives in the relevant system.

---

## 9. NETWORK OS VS. RELATIONSHIP OS BOUNDARY

Network OS remains the relationship source of truth.

Relationship OS inside West Peek OS is the relationship intelligence, routing, follow-through, memory, and connection layer.

### Network OS answers

- Who is this person?
- What do we know about them?
- What touchpoints exist?
- What relationship records exist?
- What needs review before becoming final?

### Relationship OS answers

- Why does this relationship matter?
- What should happen next?
- Who owns follow-up?
- What machine does this person touch?
- What introductions should be made?
- What promises did we make?
- What should be surfaced to Scooter or Sequoia?

### Canonical Law — Network OS Boundary

```text
Network OS remains the relationship source of truth.

Relationship OS inside West Peek OS is the relationship intelligence, routing, follow-through, memory, and connection layer.

West Peek OS must not rebuild Network OS. It must integrate with Network OS through shared IDs, sync events, deep links, and approved writebacks.
```

---

## 10. NETWORK OS INTEGRATION MODEL

Network OS and West Peek OS communicate through shared IDs and approved sync events.

### Shared IDs

```text
network_os_person_id
west_peek_os_relationship_id
related_company_id
related_event_ids
related_task_ids
```

### Network OS → West Peek OS

Network OS sends:

- new person created;
- intake approved;
- touchpoint added;
- profile updated;
- relationship tag changed;
- approval completed.

### West Peek OS → Network OS

West Peek OS sends:

- proposed profile update;
- new touchpoint summary;
- approved relationship note;
- new tag recommendation;
- follow-up completed;
- intro sent;
- event attendance signal;
- birthday / unique detail update if approved;
- relationship memory item if allowed for sync.

### Writeback rule

West Peek OS may propose updates to Network OS. Final writeback follows Network OS permissions, approval rules, and sync contracts.

## 12. RELATIONSHIP MEMORY SYSTEM

Relationship OS must preserve unique human details that make West Peek thoughtful and effective.

Relationship memory includes:

- birthdays;
- hometowns;
- family context;
- where someone grew up;
- schools;
- past jobs;
- personal interests;
- favorite causes;
- favorite sports teams;
- favorite restaurants;
- personal milestones;
- important dates;
- communication preferences;
- things they care about;
- things to avoid;
- how West Peek met them;
- who introduced them;
- what they are building;
- who they want to meet;
- who they can help;
- who they admire;
- what they are looking for;
- what West Peek promised them;
- what they promised West Peek.

### Relationship memory data model

```text
relationship_memory_item
- person_id
- memory_type
- detail
- source
- date_captured
- captured_by
- confidence
- sensitivity
- allowed_use
- review_date
```

### Memory types

```text
birthday
hometown
education
family
interests
industry_experience
personal_background
communication_preference
relationship_preference
current_need
can_help_with
do_not_use
```

### Allowed-use values

```text
internal_context_only
may_use_for_follow_up
may_use_for_connection_suggestion
do_not_reference_directly
sensitive_do_not_surface
```

### Canonical Law — Relationship Memory

```text
Relationship OS must preserve unique human details that improve thoughtful follow-through, including birthdays, background, interests, preferences, milestones, current needs, and ways the person can help or be helped.

Each memory item must include source, confidence, sensitivity, and allowed use.
```

## 14. CONNECTION INTELLIGENCE

Relationship OS must not only remember people. It must notice relationship opportunities.

### Purpose

Connection Intelligence continuously scans relationship memory, needs, interests, industries, backgrounds, companies, community signals, and current work to identify thoughtful, high-value connection opportunities.

### Canonical Law — Connection Intelligence

```text
Relationship OS must continuously identify high-value, thoughtful connection opportunities across the relationship graph.

Connection suggestions must be based on likely mutual value, timing, trust, relevance, low awkwardness, and privacy safety.
```

### Example

```text
John grew up on a cattle farm.
Mary is building cattle operations software.
Suggested connection: John may be useful for customer discovery context.
```

---

## 16. CONNECTION SUGGESTION STANDARDS

West Peek OS must not suggest low-quality introductions based on weak similarities.

### Usefulness standard

```text
A connection should be suggested only when there is likely mutual value, clear context, low awkwardness, and no sensitivity issue.
```

### Ranking factors

- relevance;
- timing;
- trust level;
- mutual benefit;
- relationship warmth;
- strategic value;
- low awkwardness;
- permission/sensitivity safety.

### Good connection reasons

```text
industry experience
founder problem area
customer segment
geography
school/alumni
professional background
event interest
investment thesis
operator skill
community support
hiring need
sponsor fit
```

### Restricted reasons

```text
health
family details
religion
politics
financial stress
personal hardship
confidential company information
private LP notes
legal/compliance issues
anything marked sensitive
```

Restricted details default to internal context only.

---

## 17. MAKE CONNECTION WORKFLOW

The Make Connection button should not require the human to manually choose sender, intro type, employee, system destination, or delivery mode.

West Peek OS automatically chooses:

- best sender;
- intro type;
- drafting employee;
- approval owner;
- delivery method;
- risk level;
- whether permission-first is required;
- whether extra review is needed.

### Canonical Law — No Manual Workflow Mechanics

```text
West Peek OS must not require Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to manually operate internal workflow mechanics.
```

---

## 18. SENDER ASSIGNMENT ENGINE

The Sender Assignment Engine chooses who should send an introduction.

### Sender assignment factors

```text
relationship ownership
last touchpoint
who promised the intro
who has warmest connection
who has most credibility for the context
who has lowest compliance/reputation risk
natural host of the relationship
```

### Canonical Law — Sender Assignment

```text
West Peek OS must not require Sequoia Taylor or Scooter Taylor to manually choose the sender for every connection.

The system assigns the sender based on relationship ownership, last touchpoint, promise origin, warmth, context, credibility, and risk.

The human may override the sender, but sender selection is not a required step.
```

### Example

If Scooter met John and promised the intro:

```text
Sender: Scooter
Drafting employee: Wendy
Approval owner: Scooter
```

If Sequoia owns an LP relationship:

```text
Sender: Sequoia
Drafting employee: Willa
Approval owner: Sequoia
```

---

## 19. INTRO DELIVERY PREFERENCES

Each human should have an intro delivery preference.

### Options

```text
Send automatically after approval
Create Gmail draft after approval
Ask each time
```

### Category-level overrides

```text
Community intros: send after approval
Founder support intros: send after approval
LP intros: draft after approval or extra approval
Sponsor intros: draft after approval
Legal/compliance-sensitive intros: draft only
Investment-sensitive intros: draft only or extra approval
```

### Canonical Law — Delivery Preference

```text
Each human has an intro delivery preference:
1. send automatically after approval,
2. create Gmail draft after approval,
3. ask each time.

Sensitive, LP, fund, legal, compliance, investment, or unclear-context introductions may override the default and require draft-only or additional approval.
```

---

## 20. INTRO TYPES

The system should distinguish between permission-first intros and direct double-opt-in intros.

### Permission-first intro

Default when both parties have not clearly opted in.

```text
Hi John — I thought of you because [reason]. I know Mary, who is working on [context]. Would you be open to a short intro?
```

### Direct double-opt-in intro

Used when both sides have clearly agreed.

```text
Mary <> John — wanted to connect you both.

Mary is building [company/context].
John has [relevant background].

I thought there might be useful overlap around [specific reason].
I’ll let you two take it from here.
```

### Canonical Law — Permission-First Default

```text
Permission-first unless both sides have clearly opted in or the relationship context makes a direct intro appropriate.
```

West Peek OS needs a deeper Command Center view in addition to the calm Today view.

```text
Show the entire operating state of the firm when Sequoia or Scooter deliberately wants to inspect it.
```

### Canonical Law — Today and Command Center

```text
West Peek OS must provide both a calm default Today view and a full Command Center view.

Today view shows only what needs human attention now.

Command Center view shows the full state of machines, AI employees, queues, approvals, risks, privacy modes, external helpers, weekend work, and blocked items.
```

```text
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 24. PRIVACY MODEL IN PLAIN ENGLISH

West Peek OS has multiple AI brains, but not every brain is allowed to see every kind of information.

Before any AI work begins, West Peek OS must answer:

```text
1. What kind of information is this?
2. Which AI/model/tool is allowed to touch it?
```

### Canonical Law — Model Routing

```text
Model routing follows data classification.

No AI employee, machine, assistant, or workflow may send data to an external model, public web tool, plugin, or frontier API unless the data classification and approval policy allow it.
```

---

## 25. DATA CLASSIFICATION MATRIX

Every object should have a sensitivity label.

### Data classifications

```text
PUBLIC
PUBLIC_IMPORTED
INTERNAL
CONFIDENTIAL
RESTRICTED
LEGAL_PRIVILEGED
BROKERAGE_SEPARATION_SENSITIVE
FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
LP_CONFIDENTIAL
PORTFOLIO_CONFIDENTIAL
SPONSOR_CONFIDENTIAL
PERSONAL_RELATIONSHIP_SENSITIVE
NEVER_SEND_EXTERNAL
```

### Examples

Public sponsor website:

```text
PUBLIC → Frontier allowed
```

Founder deck:

```text
FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL → Local or Lockdown
```

LP private note:

```text
LP_CONFIDENTIAL → Local or Lockdown
```

Scooter field note about someone’s birthday:

```text
INTERNAL or PERSONAL_RELATIONSHIP_SENSITIVE → Local
```

Legal advice memo:

```text
LEGAL_PRIVILEGED → Lockdown
```

Secondaries source note:

```text
BROKERAGE_SEPARATION_SENSITIVE → Lockdown
```

Brand sponsorship trend research:

```text
PUBLIC → Frontier allowed
```

---

## 26. LOCAL MODE

Local Mode is the default for private firm work.

Plain English:

```text
Local Mode means the work stays inside West Peek-controlled infrastructure as much as possible.
```

### Use Local Mode for

- Network OS records;
- relationship notes;
- founder confidential information;
- LP notes;
- IC memos;
- secondaries;
- private meeting notes;
- internal strategy;
- board notes;
- legal/fund admin packets;
- private sponsor negotiation notes;
- portfolio support;
- private partner discussions.

### Local Mode can use

- local models;
- private hosted models;
- West Peek-controlled databases;
- approved internal tools;
- Network OS integration;
- internal retrieval.

### Local Mode cannot silently send data to

- OpenAI;
- Claude;
- Gemini;
- Perplexity;
- external web tools;
- public search APIs;
- random plugins;
- unapproved SaaS tools.

---

## 27. FRONTIER MODE

Frontier Mode is for using outside AI or public web intentionally.

Plain English:

```text
Frontier Mode means an outside model or external web system may be used, but only with approved public, sanitized, or non-confidential information.
```

### Use Frontier Mode for

- public market research;
- public company research;
- public sponsor research;
- public LP landscape research;
- public industry primers;
- generic diligence question generation;
- public event trend scanning;
- non-confidential writing frameworks;
- public-source summaries.

### Frontier Mode UI disclosure

```text
FRONTIER MODE ACTIVE
Only public, approved, or sanitized data will be sent.
```

### Frontier Mode must show

- provider/model;
- purpose;
- data being sent;
- sensitivity level;
- sanitization status;
- approval status;
- retention/policy note if known.

---

## 28. LOCKDOWN MODE

Lockdown Mode is the strictest mode.

Plain English:

```text
Lockdown Mode means no external AI, no frontier models, no public web unless explicitly authorized, and no external reasoning APIs.
```

### Use Lockdown Mode for

- sensitive secondaries;
- brokerage-separation-sensitive matters;
- legal/compliance;
- LP confidential information;
- founder confidential materials;
- board materials;
- private partner strategy;
- restricted relationship notes;
- anything marked highly sensitive.

### Lockdown Mode can use

- local models;
- internal data only;
- approved local search;
- approved internal tools;
- manual human review.

### Lockdown Mode cannot use

- frontier LLMs;
- public search;
- external summarizers;
- external embeddings;
- external transcription;
- third-party plugins.

---

## 29. DOUBLE AIRLOCK SYSTEM

The Double Airlock governs all movement between West Peek-controlled infrastructure and external intelligence systems.

### Architecture

```text
West Peek Local Core
↓
Outbound Airlock
↓
Frontier Chamber
↓
Inbound Airlock
↓
Knowledge Promotion Gate
↓
West Peek Local Core
```

### Canonical Law — Double Airlock

```text
West Peek OS uses a double-airlock architecture.

Outbound Airlock governs what may leave West Peek-controlled infrastructure.

Inbound Airlock governs what may return, be trusted, stored, or promoted.

Nothing leaves without permission.
Nothing becomes permanent truth without promotion.
```

---

## 30. WEST PEEK LOCAL CORE

West Peek Local Core is the protected internal environment.

It contains:

- Network OS data;
- Relationship OS memory;
- LP records;
- founder records;
- IC memos;
- secondaries notes;
- private meeting notes;
- external helper packets;
- legal/compliance docs;
- event/sponsor private details;
- portfolio support notes;
- AI employee state;
- approval history;
- task history;
- Knowledge Bank.

Default rule:

```text
Local Core does not leak outward by default.
```

---

## 31. OUTBOUND AIRLOCK

The Outbound Airlock checks anything before it leaves West Peek-controlled infrastructure.

### Outbound Airlock questions

```text
What is being sent?
Why is it being sent?
Which provider/model/tool will receive it?
What data classification applies?
Is the payload public, sanitized, abstracted, or confidential?
Has confidential information been removed?
Is human approval required?
Is this allowed by policy?
Should the task be blocked or converted to Local Mode?
```

### Bad outbound payload

```text
Analyze this confidential BrightPath founder deck and tell us if we should invest.
```

### Allowed sanitized payload

```text
We are evaluating a seed-stage company in cattle operations software. Without using private company data, generate a public diligence framework for this market.
```

The frontier model gets market/framework help. It does not get the confidential deck.

---

## 32. FRONTIER CHAMBER

The Frontier Chamber is where approved outside AI and public web research happen.

### Allowed in Frontier Chamber

- public-source research;
- generic analysis;
- market maps;
- public competitor summaries;
- public sponsor research;
- public LP landscape research;
- public event trend research;
- general drafting frameworks.

### Forbidden in Frontier Chamber by default

- private founder docs;
- LP notes;
- secondaries details;
- legal privileged docs;
- private relationship notes;
- board notes;
- internal strategy;
- non-public financials;
- unapproved attendee data.

---

## 33. INBOUND AIRLOCK

The Inbound Airlock checks what comes back from external models, web tools, or public research.

### Inbound Airlock questions

```text
What claims did the model make?
Are there sources?
Is the output public research, generic advice, or unsupported speculation?
Does it contain hallucinated facts?
Does it need citation/source verification?
Should it be used once, archived, or promoted?
Does it contain anything that should not be stored?
```

Inbound content is not automatically trusted.

---

## 34. KNOWLEDGE PROMOTION GATE

External output does not automatically become firm memory.

### Promotion options

```text
Use once
Archive raw
Promote as public intelligence
Send for review
Reject
Delete
Refresh later
```

### Promoted knowledge must include

- topic;
- summary;
- claims;
- sources;
- source dates;
- model used;
- created date;
- reviewer;
- confidence;
- expiry date;
- related markets;
- allowed uses;
- privacy label.

---

## 35. PUBLIC INTELLIGENCE PACKET PATTERN

Before private analysis, West Peek OS can use Frontier Mode to build public intelligence packets.

### Example

```text
Task: Build public market packet on cattle operations software.
Data allowed: public only.
Mode: Frontier.
Output: public market map, diligence questions, customer segments, red flags, source list.
```

The packet is reviewed and stored internally.

Later, Local Mode can compare private company details against the approved public packet without sending private data outside.

### Flow

```text
Frontier/public research
→ Public Intelligence Packet
→ Source review
→ Knowledge Bank
→ Local/private use with confidential deal data
```

---

## 36. SANITIZED ABSTRACTION PATTERN

Private context can be abstracted before external research.

### Private note

```text
Mary is building CowOpsAI and has $420K ARR from 12 farms.
```

### Sanitized version

```text
A seed-stage agriculture software company sells workflow software to livestock operators. Generate diligence questions around adoption, buyer pain, sales cycles, and data integration.
```

The sanitized version removes:

- company name;
- private traction;
- customer names;
- private deck context;
- relationship notes;
- confidential identifiers.

---

West Peek OS needs a dedicated Privacy Center.

Every external model or public web call must be logged.

```text
timestamp
employee_or_user
provider
model
purpose
mode
input_classification
sanitization_status

output_destination

```

```text
Every external model, public web, plugin, or frontier API call must be logged with purpose, provider, data classification, sanitization status, approval source, and output destination.
```

## 40. PROVIDER REGISTRY

West Peek OS must maintain a registry of AI/model/web providers.

```text
allowed_modes

forbidden_use_cases
api_key_owner
cost_limits
weekend_allowed
human_approval_required
```

### Example provider record

```text
Allowed: public/sanitized/internal only if approved
Forbidden by default: legal privileged, LP confidential, founder confidential
Use cases: public research, drafting, generic analysis
Weekend allowed: public/sanitized only
Human approval: required for non-public payloads
```

## 41. WEEKEND AI WORK SAFETY

AI employees may work seven days per week, but weekend mode changes notification policy, not privacy policy.

### Canonical Law — Weekend Privacy

```text
Weekend mode changes notification policy, not privacy policy.

AI employees may continue internal work seven days per week, but they may not bypass data classification, approval gates, model routing rules, airlock review, or external-action limits.
```

### Weekend internal work allowed

AI employees may:

- process field captures;
- parse relationship notes;
- prepare Network OS update proposals;
- draft intro emails;
- build approval cards;
- classify tasks;
- summarize internal notes;
- prepare Monday briefings;
- organize external helper requests;
- update task queues;
- flag birthdays/moments;
- identify possible connections.

### Weekend frontier work allowed

Only for public/sanitized tasks:

- research public sponsor brands;
- build public event trend packet;
- scan public company websites;
- summarize public market sources;
- prepare public industry primer.

### Weekend frontier work blocked

Blocked or held if the task contains:

- founder confidential data;
- LP private information;
- legal/compliance material;
- secondaries-sensitive information;
- private relationship notes;
- sponsor negotiation terms;
- portfolio confidential data.

### Weekend queue labels

```text
Internal processing — continue
Approval-ready — batch for next business day unless urgent
Urgent review — notify
Sensitive privacy flag — notify
External action blocked — batch unless urgent
Low-priority archive — no notification
```

### Weekend summary UI sketch

```text
│ Weekend AI Summary                                                          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Processed                                                                   │
│ • 12 field captures                                                         │
│ • 4 possible connections                                                    │
│ • 3 intro drafts                                                            │
│ • 5 Network OS update proposals                                             │
│ • 2 public sponsor packets                                                  │
│                                                                             │
│ Blocked for Privacy                                                         │
│ • 1 founder note attempted public research; held in Local Mode              │
│ • 1 LP note marked Lockdown                                                 │
│                                                                             │
│ Needs Attention                                                             │
│ • 1 urgent founder intro approval                                           │
│ • 1 sponsor follow-up before Monday event                                   │
│                                                                             │
│ Safe to Wait                                                                │
│ • 17 items batched for Monday                                                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## 42. WEEKEND EMPLOYEE AUTHORITY LIMITS

### Winton weekend authority

Can:

- parse captures;
- classify data;
- route internally;
- assign employees;
- flag privacy mode;
- preserve raw capture.

Cannot:

- send external messages;
- push data to frontier without airlock;
- promote sensitive memory without approval.

### Porter weekend authority

Can:

- prepare Network OS updates;
- dedupe records;
- verify matches;
- queue sync proposals.

Cannot:

- write sensitive updates without required approval;
- export relationship data externally.

### Wendy / Willa weekend authority

Can:

- draft emails;
- prepare approval cards;
- batch human tasks;
- create calendar/task suggestions.

Cannot:

- send externally unless approval/send preference allows;
- send sensitive items automatically.

### Waverly weekend authority

Can:

- find connection opportunities;
- prepare community follow-up suggestions;
- identify event fit.

Cannot:

- make introductions without approval;
- use sensitive personal details as intro hooks.

### Wells weekend authority

Can:

- archive raw notes;
- propose memory items;
- classify knowledge.

Cannot:

- promote sensitive/private knowledge automatically;
- send internal knowledge to frontier models.

### Walker / Wren weekend authority

Can:

- prioritize;
- escalate;
- batch;
- hold until Monday;
- flag risk.

Cannot:

- override approval gates;
- approve on behalf of Scooter/Sequoia unless explicitly delegated.

---

## 43. NO-MISS QUEUE RULES

West Peek OS must prevent inputs from disappearing.

Every incoming item must end in one of these states:

```text
routed
archived
needs review
blocked
duplicate
approval-ready
```

No input may remain indefinitely unknown.

### Inputs monitored

- Field Capture Inbox;
- assistant front doors;
- command bar;
- email forwarding;
- calendar integration;
- Network OS sync;
- meeting notes;
- touchpoint ingestion;
- daily digest;
- weekend digest;
- stale queue detection;
- unprocessed item alerts.

### Canonical Law — No-Miss Queue

```text
Every inbound item must reach a terminal queue state: routed, archived, needs review, blocked, duplicate, or approval-ready.

No captured item, assistant request, Network OS sync event, meeting note, or external-helper request may remain unclassified indefinitely.
```

---

## 44. ANTI-BOTTLENECK RULES

Approval gates should block external action, not internal preparation.

### AI employees may continue preparing

If an intro needs approval, Wendy may still:

- draft the permission-first email;
- prepare context;
- attach source note;
- prepare follow-up reminder;
- queue Network OS update.

The only blocked action is sending.

### Canonical Law — Anti-Bottleneck

```text
AI employees continue preparing work up to the approval boundary.

Approval gates block external action, not internal preparation.
```

---

## 45. CONNECTION BOARD PRIVACY APPLICATION

Connection Intelligence runs primarily in Local Mode because it uses relationship memory.

### Allowed locally

```text
John grew up on cow farm
Mary building cattle software
Suggest connection
Draft intro internally
```

### Frontier use only with sanitized abstraction

If external help is needed for phrasing or public context, the outbound payload must be generic.

Allowed:

```text
Draft a concise permission-first intro between a person with agriculture background and a founder building livestock operations software.
```

Forbidden by default:

```text
John’s full profile
Mary’s company deck
private relationship notes
emails
birthdays
LP details
```

---

## 46. FINAL PRODUCT TRUTH

West Peek OS should let Sequoia Taylor and Scooter Taylor move fast in the real world without losing signal.

```text
They capture what happened.
Winton routes it.
The right machine owns it.
The right employees work it.
Network OS remains the relationship source of truth.
Relationship OS adds intelligence, memory, follow-through, and connection opportunities.
Privacy rules protect the data.
The double airlock controls external intelligence.
AI employees keep working on weekends without bypassing privacy or approval gates.
Today view stays calm.
Command Center shows the whole control room when wanted.
```

The system’s relationship advantage is not that it stores contacts.

The advantage is that it helps West Peek:

- remember the human detail;
- make the right intro;
- follow up at the right time;
- protect privacy;
- route work correctly;
- avoid bottlenecks;
- never let a hallway promise disappear.

That is the difference between a CRM and an operating system.

# Integrated Storage Architecture and AI Workforce Naming System

## 1. Purpose of This Integrated Requirement

This integrated requirement updates West Peek OS with two approved architectural decisions:

1. West Peek OS should use a simple, familiar storage foundation in v1, centered on Google Drive, Google Sheets, Gmail, Google Calendar, Network OS, and West Peek OS metadata.
2. West Peek AI employees must use W or P first names only, with names organized by workforce family and exact authority shown by badges rather than implied by names.

This integrated requirement supersedes prior roster references where they conflict with the v3.0 naming system.

## 2. Storage Philosophy

West Peek OS should not begin with an overbuilt storage architecture that makes ordinary work harder.

West Peek already knows Google Drive, Google Sheets, Gmail, and Calendar. Network OS already uses Google Sheets as its lightweight relationship database. The first version of West Peek OS should lean into this familiarity while adding the missing command, metadata, privacy, routing, and AI-work layers.

The core storage model is:

```text
Google Drive = human-facing file vault
Google Sheets / Network OS = lightweight structured relationship store
West Peek OS = command layer, metadata layer, routing layer, AI work layer, approval layer, privacy layer, audit layer
```

In plain English:

```text
Google Drive stores the files.
Google Sheets / Network OS stores lightweight relationship data.
West Peek OS understands what everything is, routes the work, assigns employees, enforces privacy, and tracks what happened.
```

## 3. Google Drive as the Document Vault

Google Drive should be the v1 document vault for West Peek OS.

It should hold:

- founder decks;
- LP materials;
- fund documents;
- legal documents;
- fund admin documents;
- sponsor decks;
- event assets;
- West Peek Live assets;
- meeting recordings and transcripts where appropriate;
- diligence files;
- IC memos;
- portfolio support files;
- screenshots;
- business cards;
- photos;
- public intelligence packets;
- internal planning documents.

The human workflow should stay simple.

Example:

```text
Founder sends deck
↓
Deck goes into Google Drive
↓
West Peek OS registers the file
↓
Winton classifies and routes the item
↓
Porter matches the founder/company where needed
↓
Relevant machine receives the work item
↓
AI employees analyze or summarize only under privacy rules
↓
Original file remains in Drive
```

A founder deck should not require a complicated upload ceremony. It should be enough to forward, upload, drag into a dropzone folder, or link the Drive file.

## 4. Drive Dropzone Intake

West Peek OS should support simple Drive dropzones.

Recommended v1 folder structure:

```text
West Peek Ventures
  00_Admin
  01_Fund
  02_LPs
  03_Deals
    Early_Stage
    Secondaries
  04_Portfolio
  05_Community
  06_Events
  07_Sponsors
  08_Legal_Compliance
  09_Operations
  10_Knowledge_Bank
  11_Public_Intelligence
  12_Intake_Dropzones
    Founder_Decks
    LP_Materials
    Sponsor_Materials
    Event_Assets
    Meeting_Recordings
    Business_Cards
    Unknown
```

The human should not be forced to navigate this full tree while moving fast.

West Peek OS should expose simple actions:

```text
Upload founder deck
Upload LP doc
Upload sponsor material
Upload meeting note
Drop unknown file
```

Behind the scenes, West Peek OS places or registers the file in the correct folder and creates the correct metadata record.

## 5. Founder Deck Intake Example

### Gmail-triggered intake

A founder emails Scooter a deck.

Wendy or Winton detects:

```text
Email from founder
Attachment: deck.pdf
Possible company: BrightPath
Possible person: Jordan
```

West Peek OS creates or proposes:

```text
Drive folder:
West Peek Ventures / 03_Deals / Early_Stage / BrightPath / 01_Intake / Decks

File:
BrightPath_Deck_2026-06-24.pdf

OS record:
company = BrightPath
source = founder email
file_id = Google Drive file ID
privacy = FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
machine = Early-Stage Deal Machine
owner = Scooter
AI employees = Pierce / Priya / Paige / Wendy / Walker as needed
```

### Manual Drive drop

A human drags a deck into:

```text
West Peek Ventures / 12_Intake_Dropzones / Founder_Decks
```

Winton processes:

```text
New file detected.
Looks like founder deck.
Company name extracted.
Founder may match Network OS.
Privacy label: FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL.
Route to Early-Stage Deal Machine.
```

Review card:

```text
New Founder Deck Detected

Company: BrightPath
Source: Drive upload
Matched person: Jordan Ellis
Suggested machine: Early-Stage Deal Machine
Suggested owner: Scooter
Privacy: Founder Confidential
Recommended action: Create intake packet

[Approve routing]
[Change owner]
[Mark sensitive]
[Archive]
```

## 6. Document Object Model

Every file registered in West Peek OS should become a document object.

Minimum fields:

```text
document_id
google_drive_file_id
file_name
file_type
source
uploaded_by
uploaded_at
related_person_id
related_company_id
related_machine
privacy_label
model_mode_allowed
summary_status
extraction_status
current_owner
approval_status
canonical_location
version
linked_tasks
linked_meetings
linked_decisions
```

The system must be able to answer:

- What is this file?
- Who sent it?
- What company or person is it tied to?
- Is it confidential?
- Can AI read it?
- Can frontier AI touch it?
- Has it been summarized?
- Is it part of IC?
- Was it used in a decision?
- Where is the original?

## 7. Google Sheets and Network OS

Network OS may continue to use Google Sheets as its lightweight structured relationship database in v1.

Sheets are appropriate for:

- people records;
- companies;
- tags;
- touchpoints;
- relationship statuses;
- intake records;
- approval status;
- human-readable mirrors;
- easy manual repair;
- early-stage structured data.

But Google Sheets should not be treated as the final database for all of West Peek OS forever.

The likely evolution is:

```text
Phase 1:
Google Drive + Google Sheets + Network OS + West Peek OS metadata layer.

Phase 2:
West Peek OS gains a proper backend database while preserving Drive as the document vault and Sheets as mirror/export/admin layer where useful.

Phase 3:
West Peek OS adds more formal data rooms, permissions, audit trails, and provider integrations.
```

## 8. Data Room Machine

West Peek OS should eventually include a Data Room Machine.

Data rooms may include:

- fundraising data room;
- founder diligence data room;
- secondaries diligence room;
- LP materials room;
- sponsor materials room;
- portfolio support room.

Each room should have:

- source folder;
- approved docs;
- draft docs;
- access rules;
- sharing status;
- expiry;
- approval logs;
- version control;
- safe-to-send flag.

The system must distinguish storing a document from sharing a document.

```text
Store internally ≠ safe to send externally.
```

## 9. Storage and Privacy

Drive is the vault. Privacy rules still govern use.

A file being in Google Drive does not mean every model, employee, or integration may read it.

Every file should receive a privacy label:

```text
PUBLIC
INTERNAL
FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
LP_CONFIDENTIAL
LEGAL_PRIVILEGED
BROKERAGE_SEPARATION_SENSITIVE
PORTFOLIO_CONFIDENTIAL
PERSONAL_RELATIONSHIP_SENSITIVE
NEVER_SEND_EXTERNAL
```

Example:

```text
File: BrightPath Deck
Privacy: FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
Allowed mode: Local Mode by default
Frontier allowed: no
External web allowed: no
Automatic sharing: no
```

If West Peek OS needs outside public context, it must use the public/sanitized pattern:

```text
Private file stays local.
Public research request is sanitized.
Frontier model receives only public/generic context.
Local model combines approved public packet with private file internally.
```

## 10. AI Workforce Naming Law

All West Peek AI employees must use W or P first names only until and unless the final implementation canon changes this rule.

Real humans use full names.

AI employees use first names only and must always be labeled as AI employees in the UI.

No AI employee should use initials to distinguish duplicate names. Duplicate first names should be avoided.

```text
Correct: Winnie
Correct: Whitney
Incorrect: Winnie C.
Incorrect: Winnie P.
```

## 11. W and P Workforce Families

The W/P naming system is a brand and cognition system. It is not the permission system.

### W names — Watchful layer

W names generally represent the watchful, connective, coordinating, protective, memory, privacy, relationship, and governance layer.

W names are appropriate for:

- chiefs of staff;
- executive assistants;
- intake;
- relationship intelligence;
- warm path detection;
- community;
- knowledge;
- compliance;
- privacy;
- systems;
- learning;
- coordination.

W names should feel:

```text
watchful
relational
connective
protective
coordinating
```

### P names — Producer layer

P names generally represent the production, packets, processing, research, execution, drafting, finance, events, operations, and specialist-work layer.

P names are appropriate for:

- investment analysis;
- research;
- memo drafting;
- outreach packets;
- marketing;
- PR;
- content;
- finance;
- fund admin;
- events;
- operations execution;
- portfolio support.

P names should feel:

```text
production
preparation
packets
precision
execution
```

## 12. Names Do Not Encode Authority

Names may hint at operating family, but authority is controlled by explicit badges, approval gates, privacy modes, and human settings.

West Peek OS must not rely on a first initial to determine whether an employee can act externally.

Every AI employee profile must show:

```text
Name
Role
Human owner
Primary machines
Mode access
Data access
External authority
Approval requirement
Weekend authority
```

## 13. Employee Authority Badges

Every AI employee should have one or more authority badges.

Recommended badges:

```text
Internal-only
Draft-only
Approval-send capable
Human-only external
Lockdown-authorized
Frontier-authorized
Local-only
Weekend-authorized
Blocker / reviewer
```

### Internal-only

Can work internally but cannot contact outsiders.

Typical employees:

```text
Winton
Porter
Wells
Willow
Wilson
Preston
```

### Draft-only

Can prepare external communications but cannot send.

Typical employees:

```text
Penn
Pippa
Prue
Parker
Waverly
Wesley
Winter
```

### Approval-send capable

Can send after explicit human approval or delegated category settings.

Typical employees:

```text
Wendy
Willa
```

Other employees may become approval-send capable only through explicit policy.

### Human-only external

AI may create drafts or packets, but the human must personally send or approve through a stricter path.

Use for:

```text
legal-sensitive items
LP terms
fund documents
investment interest
secondaries-sensitive matters
conflict-sensitive matters
```

## 17. Storage-to-Privacy-to-Agent Access Example

If a founder deck is uploaded to Drive:

```text
File: BrightPath Deck
Privacy: FOUNDER_CONFIDENTIAL
Mode: Local
Machine: Early-Stage Deal Machine
```

Then:

- Winton can classify and route it;
- Porter can match founder/company to Network OS;
- Pierce, Priya, and Paige can analyze internally if permitted;
- Wendy can prepare Scooter’s approval card;
- Walker can prioritize;
- Wells can preserve an approved summary;
- Penn cannot use it for outreach;
- frontier AI cannot read it;
- Public Intelligence Forge can only use sanitized market-category prompts.

This is the intended interaction between storage, privacy, and employee permissions.

## 18. Canonical Laws Installed by v3.0

### Storage law

```text
Google Drive stores files.
Google Sheets / Network OS stores lightweight relationship data where appropriate.
West Peek OS stores metadata, routing, approvals, privacy labels, AI work state, audit logs, and institutional memory.
```

### Drive vault law

```text
A file may live in Google Drive while West Peek OS owns the intelligence around what it is, where it belongs, who may use it, and what actions it triggers.
```

### Document privacy law

```text
A document being stored in Drive does not make it available to every AI employee, external model, or integration.

Document access follows privacy label, model mode, employee authority badge, and approval policy.
```

### Naming law

```text
All AI employees use W or P first names only.

W names generally indicate the watchful operating layer.
P names generally indicate the producer execution layer.

Names do not determine authority. Badges, privacy modes, approval gates, and human settings determine authority.
```

### Employee badge law

```text
Every AI employee must display exact role, primary machines, data access, mode access, external authority, approval requirements, and weekend authority.
```

### No duplicate AI first names law

```text
AI employee first names should not be duplicated. If two employees would share a name, rename one before implementation.
```
