# Integrated Hardening Architecture

This section is the canonical v3.2.4 hardening layer. It is not a detached brainstorming note. It defines the operating, governance, UX, diagnostics, implementation, and product-architecture corrections required to transform the v3.1 vision into a buildable, auditable, capital-disciplined, vendor-realistic, compliance-aware, and debuggable firm operating system.

The governing v3.2.4 law is:

```text
West Peek OS builds the firm brain.
It buys, integrates, wraps, or manually references mature commodity infrastructure when that is safer, cheaper, faster, or more reliable.
It builds native infrastructure only when security, cost, control, data ownership, workflow edge, or strategic differentiation clearly justify it.
```

## 37. v3.2.4 Product Hardening Overview

### 37.1 Why v3.2.4 exists

v3.1 established the integrated operating vision:

- Odysseus fork foundation;
- Managing Partner command center;
- AI employee workforce;
- meeting sidecar and Meeting Capture Adapter;
- Research & Intelligence Machine;
- Approval Center;
- Relationship OS and Network OS integration;
- Portfolio Support OS;
- Portfolio Performance / Follow-On Decision Center;
- West Peek Live portfolio-value layer;
- privacy, airlock, and governance concepts.

v3.2.4 hardens that vision by adding the missing institutional spine:

- fund construction and capital allocation math;
- investment mandate and exclusion logic;
- IC learning loops;
- compliance rules engine;
- source-of-truth conflict resolution;
- data license and quality contracts;
- build-vs-buy governance;
- vendor-risk control;
- data-room control layer;
- relationship capital protection;
- portfolio support service levels;
- LP diligence and LP proof workflows;
- governance broadcast through +Capture and assistants;
- activity and audit visibility;
- private assistant scoping;
- developer diagnostics;
- AI debug copilot;
- UX compression;
- phased implementation sequencing.

### 37.2 What v3.2.4 must prevent

v3.2.4 exists to prevent these failure modes:

- building a giant command center without hard fund math;
- producing research outputs without license, source, confidence, freshness, or conflict handling;
- relying on no-API products as hidden sources of truth;
- rebuilding mature infrastructure such as external data rooms before a build-vs-buy review;
- overusing relationship capital because every intro seems useful;
- making portfolio support feel unlimited or unpriced;
- letting every internal assistant interaction become firm-wide surveillance;
- creating opaque AI employee behavior that cannot be debugged;
- hiding system failures behind agent theater;
- building 40 dashboards before proving the first operating spine;
- treating transcripts, provider data, or AI summaries as truth without approval;
- letting compliance live only inside human memory.

### 37.3 v3.2.4 main operating principle

```text
The first product should prove command, capture, routing, approval, memory, activity, audit, privacy labels, and diagnostics.
Everything else attaches to that spine.
```

---

# 38. Fund Construction + Capital Allocation OS

## 38.1 Purpose

The Fund Construction + Capital Allocation OS is the financial decision spine of West Peek OS. It exists because West Peek OS cannot intelligently support investment, reserve, pro-rata, follow-on, concentration, or secondaries decisions without understanding the fund model.

The OS may prepare recommendations, scenarios, and decision packets. It may not autonomously decide whether to invest fund capital.

### Capital decision law

```text
West Peek OS may recommend, analyze, and prepare capital decisions.
Humans decide whether to invest more capital.
```

## 38.2 Required fund construction objects

West Peek OS must support structured objects for:

- fund size scenario;
- management fee assumption;
- carry assumption;
- stage allocation;
- primary investment allocation;
- secondaries allocation;
- initial check band;
- follow-on reserve pool;
- reserve ratio;
- ownership target;
- maximum company concentration;
- maximum sector/theme concentration;
- deployment pacing;
- investment-period assumption;
- opportunity cost score;
- liquidity expectation;
- reserve stress scenario;
- follow-on priority score;
- pro-rata rights record;
- secondaries exposure record;
- portfolio construction model version.

## 38.3 Portfolio construction model

The portfolio construction model must answer:

- how many companies the fund expects to invest in;
- how many initial checks are available;
- how much capital is reserved for follow-ons;
- what ownership targets matter by stage;
- how secondaries exposure affects risk, liquidity, and concentration;
- how much capital can be deployed into one company;
- how much capital can be deployed into one category;
- whether the fund is ahead or behind deployment pace;
- whether a follow-on is affordable under reserve strategy;
- whether a follow-on is strategically justified even when affordable;
- whether support should be provided without more capital.

## 38.4 Follow-on and pro-rata rules

When a portfolio company appears to be pulling ahead, West Peek OS must not jump directly to “invest more.” It must produce a capital-aware packet.

The packet must include:

- current ownership;
- pro-rata rights;
- available allocation;
- company performance signals;
- fund reserve impact;
- concentration impact;
- stage and valuation context;
- support outcomes to date;
- relationship signals;
- downside risk;
- alternative use of capital;
- recommendation;
- human decision field;
- final rationale;
- follow-up action.

## 38.5 Follow-on decision outputs

Valid outputs include:

- protect ownership;
- prepare follow-on IC packet;
- support but do not invest more;
- monitor;
- triage;
- pass on pro-rata;
- request more information;
- escalate to Scooter;
- escalate to Sequoia;
- legal/compliance review required;
- reserve-strategy review required.

## 38.6 Capital allocation workflow

```text
Company performance signal
↓
Portfolio Performance Center
↓
Capital Allocation OS
↓
Reserve / ownership / concentration analysis
↓
Follow-On IC Packet
↓
Human decision
↓
Decision recorded
↓
Outcome reviewed later
```

## 38.7 Capital allocation UI sketch

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Capital Allocation OS                                      │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Fund Scenario: Fund I — $30M                               │
│ Primary / Secondary: 60% / 40%                             │
│ Reserve Pool: Draft model                                  │
│ Deployment Pace: On track / Review                         │
│                                                            │
│ Follow-On Watch                                            │
│ ┌─────────────┬──────────┬─────────────┬────────────────┐ │
│ │ Company     │ Signal   │ Reserve Hit │ Recommendation │ │
│ ├─────────────┼──────────┼─────────────┼────────────────┤ │
│ │ BrightPath  │ Strong   │ Moderate    │ Prepare IC     │ │
│ │ Rootline    │ Medium   │ Low         │ Monitor        │ │
│ │ HavenEd     │ Weak     │ N/A         │ Support Only   │ │
│ └─────────────┴──────────┴─────────────┴────────────────┘ │
│                                                            │
│ [Scenario Simulator] [Reserve Model] [Build IC Packet]     │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

# 39. Investment Mandate + Exclusion Policy

## 39.1 Purpose

The Investment Mandate + Exclusion Policy helps West Peek say no faster and avoid wasting research, meeting, and IC capacity on opportunities that do not fit the firm.

### Fast-no law

```text
West Peek OS should not make every opportunity smarter.
It should help the firm say no faster, with receipts.
```

## 39.2 Mandate categories

Every opportunity must be classified into one of these states:

- in-scope;
- out-of-scope;
- requires partner review;
- requires legal/compliance review;
- automatic pass;
- watchlist/nurture eligible;
- relationship-only;
- community-only;
- secondaries-only;
- primary-only;
- insufficient information.

## 39.3 Required mandate tests

Every investment opportunity should run through:

- venture-scale test;
- founder-quality test;
- market-quality test;
- stage-fit test;
- ownership/return potential test;
- West Peek edge test;
- conflict test;
- compliance test;
- brokerage/fund separation test;
- reputational risk test;
- fund construction fit test.

## 39.4 Exclusion policy

Excluded and restricted categories must be counsel-reviewed and partner-approved. The OS should treat the written Investment Policy as source of truth once adopted.

Until formal legal policy exists, the OS must route questionable categories to human review rather than declaring them investable.

## 39.5 Investment mandate workflow

```text
Opportunity enters system
↓
Winton classifies intake
↓
Investment Mandate filter runs
↓
Fast-no / nurture / research / partner review / compliance review
↓
Only qualified opportunities move to deeper research or IC workflow
```

---

# 40. IC Learning Loop and Decision Feedback System

## 40.1 Purpose

The IC Learning Loop turns every investment decision, pass, follow-on decision, and major portfolio outcome into institutional learning.

West Peek OS must not only record what the firm decided. It must preserve what the firm believed at the time, what evidence supported the belief, what dissent existed, what happened later, and what the firm learned.

## 40.2 Assumption Ledger

Every IC decision record must include:

- core investment thesis;
- top three assumptions;
- key evidence;
- key uncertainties;
- bull case;
- bear case;
- dissent;
- decision rationale;
- expected milestones;
- review cadence;
- owner;
- outcome review dates.

## 40.3 Review cadence

The OS must create outcome-review checkpoints at:

- 3 months;
- 6 months;
- 12 months;
- 24 months;
- exit;
- shutdown;
- major follow-on event;
- major negative event;
- major positive inflection point.

## 40.4 Pass-review loop

Passed opportunities should not disappear. When a passed company later breaks out, raises a major round, shuts down, pivots, or becomes strategically relevant, the OS should compare the original pass rationale against the outcome.

This builds judgment without shame.

## 40.5 IC learning workflow

```text
IC decision made
↓
Assumptions captured
↓
Evidence and dissent recorded
↓
Outcome review scheduled
↓
Actual results compared to original beliefs
↓
Lessons promoted to Knowledge OS
↓
Future IC prompts improve
```

---

# 41. Research Data License + Quality Contract

## 41.1 Purpose

The Research Data License + Quality Contract prevents third-party data from becoming unauthorized, stale, conflicted, or falsely authoritative institutional truth.

### Data truth law

```text
Third-party data does not automatically become West Peek truth.
It becomes usable only according to license, provenance, confidence, freshness, and approval rules.
```

## 41.2 Provider contract fields

Every data provider adapter or manual data source must define:

- provider name;
- data category;
- license restrictions;
- storage permission;
- AI processing permission;
- IC memo permission;
- LP material permission;
- export permission;
- retention period;
- refresh cadence;
- rate limits;
- source priority;
- confidence default;
- freshness default;
- contradiction behavior;
- expiration behavior;
- manual review trigger;
- vendor risk link.

## 41.3 Sources covered

The contract applies to:

- PitchBook;
- Crunchbase;
- Preqin;
- CB Insights;
- Dealroom;
- Tracxn;
- Harmonic;
- SEC/public records;
- company websites;
- Google/news/search;
- Network OS;
- meeting transcripts;
- founder documents;
- LP documents;
- emails;
- internal notes;
- human-sourced intelligence.

## 41.4 Claim object fields

Every research claim should have:

- claim ID;
- claim text;
- source;
- source type;
- date captured;
- date last verified;
- confidence;
- freshness;
- license status;
- internal/external-safe status;
- LP-safe status;
- IC-only status;
- contradictions;
- missing information;
- reviewer;
- expiration date;
- related entity.

## 41.5 Conflict handling

When providers disagree, the OS must not silently choose one as truth. It must:

- mark conflict;
- show competing sources;
- apply source-priority rules if available;
- show date/freshness;
- route material conflicts to Porter/Wells/Willow or human review;
- preserve final resolution;
- record what source was used in outputs.

---

# 42. Compliance Rules Engine

## 42.1 Purpose

The Compliance Rules Engine moves compliance from “Willow reviews later” into machine-detected routing, blocking, and logging.

This system is not legal advice. It enforces counsel-approved rules once defined and routes ambiguous or high-risk cases to humans.

### Compliance memory law

```text
Compliance cannot live only in human memory.
The OS must detect, block, route, and log compliance-sensitive actions.
```

## 42.2 Required compliance gates

The Compliance Rules Engine must support gates for:

- brokerage/fund separation;
- MNPI/confidentiality;
- LP marketing claims;
- general solicitation caution;
- conflicts of interest;
- side letters;
- secondaries confidentiality;
- valuation / marks;
- external communication;
- portfolio company confidential information;
- data room access;
- AI model mode;
- vendor data restrictions;
- personal vs firm activity;
- restricted persons/entities;
- disclosure requirements.

## 42.3 Compliance actions

The engine may:

- allow;
- allow with disclosure;
- route to Willow;
- route to legal/counsel;
- require Sequoia approval;
- require Scooter approval;
- require both partner review;
- block;
- mark as Lockdown;
- create audit event;
- require revised language;
- require source/evidence attachment.

## 42.4 Brokerage/fund separation

West Peek OS must preserve brokerage/fund separation by detecting when:

- secondaries brokerage work is being treated as fund work;
- fund activity is being attributed to brokerage channels;
- external communications blend roles;
- confidential secondary transaction information could enter fund records incorrectly;
- fee/carry/investment/fundraising language risks confusion;
- a person or entity appears in both contexts.

---

# 43. Source-of-Truth Resolver

## 43.1 Purpose

The Source-of-Truth Resolver prevents the OS from becoming confidently wrong when Google Drive, Network OS, Gmail, Calendar, provider data, enrichment, meeting notes, and human notes disagree.

## 43.2 Field-level authority

For every major object type, the OS must define:

- authoritative source;
- fallback source;
- trusted human override rules;
- stale-data rule;
- conflict state;
- merge rule;
- review trigger;
- audit trail.

## 43.3 Entity types covered

The resolver covers:

- person;
- company;
- founder;
- LP;
- portfolio company;
- document;
- meeting;
- decision;
- support request;
- investment;
- event;
- vendor;
- AI employee;
- policy/bulletin/context update.

## 43.4 Conflict object fields

Every conflict should record:

- conflict ID;
- entity;
- field;
- sources involved;
- values asserted;
- timestamps;
- confidence;
- freshness;
- materiality;
- suggested resolution;
- assigned reviewer;
- resolution status;
- final value;
- audit trail.

## 43.5 Source conflict UI sketch

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Source-of-Truth Conflict                                   │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Entity: Maya Chen                                          │
│ Field: Current Company                                     │
│                                                            │
│ Network OS: Atlas Health                                   │
│ Email Signature: BrightCare                                │
│ LinkedIn Enrichment: Atlas Health                          │
│ Last Meeting Notes: “joining BrightCare next month”        │
│                                                            │
│ Suggested Resolution: BrightCare, effective next month      │
│ Confidence: Medium                                         │
│                                                            │
│ [Accept] [Ask Porter to Verify] [Keep Conflict Open]       │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

# 44. Build-vs-Buy / Adapter-First Procurement Law

## 44.1 Purpose

The Build-vs-Buy / Adapter-First Procurement Law prevents West Peek OS from wasting time rebuilding mature commodity infrastructure when an external product, API, whitelabel path, export/import path, or manual tool can do the job better.

## 44.2 Build-vs-buy law

```text
West Peek OS builds the firm brain.
It buys, integrates, wraps, or manually references commodity infrastructure.
It only builds native infrastructure when security, cost, control, data ownership, workflow edge, or strategic differentiation clearly justify it.
```

## 44.3 Build natively

West Peek OS should build or own these native layers:

- AI employee orchestration;
- Approval Center;
- Global Capture;
- +Capture Governance Routing;
- Knowledge OS;
- Research Evidence Ledger;
- IC Decision Portal;
- Capital Allocation OS;
- Compliance Rules Engine;
- Source-of-Truth Resolver;
- Relationship intelligence;
- LP Proof Engine;
- AI Employee Performance Ledger;
- West Peek Taste Layer;
- activity/audit/memory layer;
- developer diagnostics;
- Debug Copilot.

## 44.4 Buy, integrate, wrap, or manually reference first

West Peek OS should avoid rebuilding these unless a strong reason exists:

- meeting transcription;
- external data rooms / VDRs;
- email/calendar infrastructure;
- LP fundraising CRM;
- private-market datasets;
- fund admin;
- e-signature;
- KYC/AML;
- cap table tools;
- document storage;
- video meeting infrastructure;
- document signing;
- payment/capital call tools.

## 44.5 Vendor evaluation matrix

Every vendor/tool must be evaluated for:

- API availability;
- webhook availability;
- durable export;
- durable import;
- external ID support;
- data ownership;
- security posture;
- privacy posture;
- cost;
- workflow fit;
- whitelabel or embed possibility;
- exit path;
- manual fallback;
- canonical source risk;
- integration type;
- admin owner;
- review date.

## 44.6 No-API dependency law

```text
If a third-party product has no API, webhook, durable export, or reliable sync path, West Peek OS may use it tactically but may not depend on it as core infrastructure.
```

# 45. FundingStack Status and LP Fundraising Tool Policy

## 45.1 Locked operator knowledge

FundingStack is currently treated as having no API based on user-provided operator knowledge.

## 45.2 FundingStack rule

```text
FundingStack may be evaluated or used tactically/manual-only.
It cannot be a core integrated West Peek OS dependency unless API, webhook, durable export/import, or reliable sync becomes available.
```

## 45.3 FundingStack may be used for

- manual fundraising workflow support;
- LP list/reference support;
- temporary external workspace;
- deck sharing or investor tracking if useful;
- short-term tactical fundraising assistance.

## 45.4 FundingStack may not be

- canonical LP CRM;
- source of institutional memory;
- required fundraising infrastructure;
- automated integration layer;
- source of truth for LP relationship state.

## 45.5 Canonical LP rule

```text
West Peek OS maintains canonical LP records, LP pipeline state, relationship ownership, touch history, diligence requests, approved materials shared, follow-up tasks, approval cards, fundraising evidence, and LP-safe notes.
```

---

# 46. Data Room Control Layer

## 46.1 Purpose

West Peek OS should not build a native external-facing data room in v1. It should govern data-room activity through a Data Room Control Layer.

## 46.2 Data room architecture

```text
Google Drive = internal document vault / working storage
External VDR or fundraising platform = LP-facing diligence-grade data room when needed
West Peek OS = Data Room Control Layer
```

## 46.3 Data Room Control Layer responsibilities

West Peek OS tracks:

- document registry;
- privacy labels;
- approval status;
- LP-safe status;
- provider links;
- version tracking;
- access ledger;
- expiration reminders;
- revocation reminders;
- audit history;
- legal/compliance review status;
- who saw what;
- which version was shared;
- whether download was allowed;
- whether watermarking was required;
- whether access was revoked;
- whether material should be refreshed.

## 46.4 Data room law

```text
West Peek OS does not need to be the data room.
It needs to know what was shared, with whom, when, under what permission, and whether it was approved.
```

## 46.5 LP diligence and data-room flow

```text
LP requests materials
↓
LP Diligence Request Machine captures request
↓
Data Room Control Layer identifies approved docs
↓
Compliance Rules Engine reviews claims/materials
↓
Approval Center routes to human
↓
External VDR or Drive share is created/updated
↓
West Peek OS records access, version, approval, expiration, and follow-up
```

---

# 47. Vendor Risk Registry

## 47.1 Purpose

Every external product is a risk surface. The Vendor Risk Registry ensures West Peek OS understands what each vendor touches, what it is allowed to do, and what happens if it fails.

## 47.2 Vendor fields

Every vendor record must include:

- vendor name;
- vendor category;
- data touched;
- privacy mode allowed;
- license restrictions;
- retention policy;
- export path;
- shutdown fallback;
- admin owner;
- contract status;
- risk rating;
- integration type;
- manual fallback;
- canonical source risk;
- API/webhook/export/import status;
- cost model;
- renewal/review date;
- security notes;
- compliance notes.

## 47.3 Vendors tracked

The registry should include, at minimum:

- Google Drive;
- Gmail;
- Google Calendar;
- Google Sheets / Network OS;
- Fireflies;
- Recall;
- FundingStack;
- PitchBook;
- Crunchbase;
- Preqin;
- CB Insights;
- Dealroom;
- Tracxn;
- Harmonic;
- external VDR provider;
- fund admin tools;
- LLM providers;
- e-signature tools;
- KYC/AML tools.

## 47.4 Vendor risk law

```text
No vendor becomes institutional truth without a West Peek OS mirror or approved exception.
```

```text
│                                                            │
```

# 48. Relationship Capital Budget

## 48.1 Purpose

The Relationship Capital Budget prevents Relationship OS from overusing the network. West Peek OS should not merely identify possible introductions; it should protect trust.

### Relationship capital law

```text
West Peek OS should protect relationship capital, not just exploit it.
```

## 48.2 Relationship budget fields

For important relationships, track:

- recent asks;
- recent value given;
- recent value received;
- trust level;
- relationship strength;
- social-capital cost;
- cooldown period;
- overuse risk;
- preferred ask type;
- do-not-overuse flag;
- last intro requested;
- last intro made;
- last ignored request;
- last meaningful value exchange;
- relationship owner.

## 48.3 Intro recommendation rules

Before recommending an intro, the OS should evaluate:

- whether the intro creates real value;
- whether the relationship has been overused;
- whether West Peek has recently given value;
- whether the ask is too one-sided;
- whether the intro risks trust;
- whether the target prefers warm intros, brief emails, or no unsolicited introductions;
- whether sensitive personal details are being used improperly;
- whether the intro should be human-only.

---

# 49. Portfolio Support Service Levels

## 49.1 Purpose

Portfolio Support Service Levels help all portfolio companies without promising unlimited labor or unlimited follow-on capital.

### Portfolio support law

```text
Not every portfolio company gets more money.
Every portfolio company can still receive thoughtful support.
```

## 49.2 Support levels

### Base Support

Default support available to portfolio companies:

- founder update review;
- event access;
- basic intro consideration;
- resource library;
- office hours when available;
- light strategic triage.

### Active Support

For companies with current needs and plausible West Peek leverage:

- support plan;
- targeted relationship matching;
- customer/investor/operator intro planning;
- fundraising prep;
- hiring support;
- GTM support;
- founder coaching support.

### Priority Support

For companies with strong signal, strategic importance, or potential follow-on relevance:

- partner-led support;
- structured support sprint;
- follow-on readiness review;
- customer/investor sprint;
- enhanced relationship activation;
- IC update packet if capital decision is near.

### Crisis Support

For companies under pressure:

- triage;
- strategic options review;
- morale/community support;
- bridge/shutdown/acquisition planning support;
- graceful next-step planning;
- non-capital help where more capital is not appropriate.

## 49.3 Support boundaries

The OS must define:

- what support includes;
- what support excludes;
- response expectations;
- partner approval triggers;
- relationship-intro limits;
- non-capital support options;
- follow-on eligibility relationship;
- escalation triggers;
- outcome recording.

---

# 51. West Peek Live Portfolio Value Loop

## 51.1 Purpose

West Peek Live should become a portfolio value, relationship intelligence, research, and LP proof engine, not merely a programming calendar.

### Event value law

```text
Every West Peek Live portco event should produce relationship, support, research, or LP-proof value.
```

West Peek Live portfolio programming may include:

# 52. LP Diligence Request Machine

## 52.1 Purpose

The LP Diligence Request Machine manages LP diligence as a workflow rather than a scramble.

## 52.2 LP diligence workflow

```text
LP asks for materials
↓
Request captured through +Capture, email, meeting, or assistant
↓
LP Diligence Request Machine classifies materials
↓
Approved docs are pulled from Drive / Data Room Control Layer
↓
Missing docs are flagged
↓
Legal/compliance review is routed if required
↓
LP-safe response is drafted
↓
Approval Center routes to human
↓
Documents are shared through approved channel
↓
Data Room Control Layer records what happened
↓
Follow-up task is created
```

## 52.3 Request types

The machine must handle requests for:

- fund deck;
- terms;
- strategy;
- team bios;
- references;
- service providers;
- legal docs;
- portfolio construction;
- pipeline examples;
- platform support evidence;
- community proof;
- track record or attribution where applicable;
- diligence questionnaire responses;
- side letter discussion routing.

## 52.4 LP diligence UI sketch

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ LP Diligence Request                                       │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ LP: Northstar Family Office                                │
│ Request: Fund deck, terms, portfolio construction, pipeline │
│ Owner: Wesley                                              │
│ Compliance: Willow review required                         │
│                                                            │
│ Materials                                                  │
│ ✓ Fund Deck — approved                                     │
│ ✓ Team Bios — approved                                     │
│ ⚠ Portfolio Construction — needs update                    │
│ ⚠ Pipeline Examples — LP-safe review needed                │
│                                                            │
│ Data Room: External VDR / Google Drive link pending         │
│                                                            │
│ [Prepare Response] [Send to Willow] [Open Data Room Layer] │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

# 53. LP Proof Engine

## 53.1 Purpose

The LP Proof Engine converts West Peek’s actual operating activity into LP-safe evidence.

### LP proof law

```text
West Peek should be able to prove platform value with evidence, not vibes.
```

## 53.2 Inputs

The engine ingests:

- portfolio support outcomes;
- community-powered sourcing;
- event outcomes;
- relationship graph value;
- founder support completed;
- research insights;
- pipeline quality;
- decision discipline;
- follow-on discipline;
- capital allocation discipline;
- West Peek Live outcomes;
- portfolio company support stories;
- founder feedback;
- LP diligence activity.

## 53.3 Outputs

The engine produces:

- LP update inputs;
- fundraising proof points;
- platform-value reports;
- case study candidates;
- community-powered investing evidence;
- support outcome summaries;
- data room evidence objects;
- approved language candidates;
- compliance review queue items.

## 53.4 LP-safe boundary

No proof point becomes LP-safe until it passes:

- approval check;

# 54. Meeting Consent + Transcript Reliability Layer

## 54.1 Purpose

The Meeting Consent + Transcript Reliability Layer makes meeting capture safer, more accurate, and less likely to promote bad transcript data into institutional truth.

### Transcript truth law

```text
A transcript is an input, not truth.
Walter produces a proposed meeting record.
Humans approve the durable record.
```

## 54.2 Meeting capture fields

Every captured meeting should track:

- recording consent status;
- capture provider;
- transcript confidence;
- speaker confidence;
- off-record sections;
- AI processing allowed;
- Knowledge promotion allowed;
- human review needed;
- external attendee sensitivity;
- confidential content labels;
- source artifacts;
- transcript version;
- approved meeting record status.

## 54.3 Reliability states

Valid transcript reliability states:

- high confidence;
- medium confidence;
- low confidence;
- speaker labels uncertain;
- incomplete transcript;
- off-record sections present;
- human review required;
- not approved for Knowledge OS;
- approved meeting record finalized.

## 54.4 Meeting sidecar reliability UI sketch

```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────────────┐
│ Zoom / Google Meet            │ │ West Peek OS Meeting Room  │
│                               │ │                            │
│ Human conversation happens    │ │ Prep | Live | Notes | Tasks│
│ here                          │ │                            │
│                               │ │ Walter — Meeting Buddy     │
└──────────────────────────────┘ └────────────────────────────┘

Meeting Capture
Consent: Confirmed
Provider: Fireflies
Transcript Confidence: Medium
Speaker Confidence: Low
Knowledge Promotion: Human review required
```

# 55. AI Employee Performance + Cost Ledger + Lifecycle Control

## 55.1 Purpose

The AI Employee Performance + Cost Ledger + Lifecycle Control system prevents agent sprawl by measuring whether AI employees actually save time, produce useful work, behave safely, and remain worthy of continued active authority.

## 55.2 Ledger fields

Track by AI employee:

- tasks completed;
- approval acceptance rate;
- revision rate;
- error rate;
- human time saved;
- human time consumed;
- privacy flags;
- model cost;
- API cost;
- value created;
- work-card completion rate;
- rejected-output rate;
- hallucination/error incidents;
- compliance flags;
- pause/restrict/PIP/retrain/retire/fire recommendation.

## 55.3 Agent quality outputs

The ledger should be able to say:

- Winter is high-value;
- Penn drafts need too much editing;
- Perry enrichments are low-confidence;
- Walter has strong post-meeting acceptance;
- Willow blocks too many low-risk actions and needs rule calibration;
- Pippa produces good content but requires Taste Layer review;
- a role should be paused, restricted, placed on PIP, retrained, scoped down, merged, retired, or permanently decommissioned.


## 55.4 v3.2.6 upgrade — Cost Dashboard and Budget Governor

Machine #41 is upgraded from a passive performance/cost ledger into the canonical AI Employee Performance, Cost Ledger, Cost Dashboard, and Budget Governor Machine.

This machine owns the visible AI cost-control layer in coordination with Finance OS, Approval Center, Activity/Audit Ledger, Model Governance + Privacy Airlock, Loop Runtime, and Opportunity Radar.

It must provide:

- AI Workforce Cost Dashboard;
- Cost Command Center;
- firmwide Cost Mode control;
- domain-level override control;
- Cost Class assignment visibility;
- AI Run Ledger;
- Cost Estimate records;
- Actual Usage records;
- Value / Output Ledger;
- recurring loop budget panel;
- Strategic Surge setup and expiration;
- spend alerts;
- budget recommendations;
- low-value loop pause recommendations;
- model-routing recommendations;
- monthly AI spend report;
- cost-by-domain and cost-by-machine reporting.

## 55.5 Cost Command Center UI requirements

The Cost Command Center must show:

- current firmwide Cost Mode;
- domain overrides;
- today spend;
- week spend;
- month-to-date spend;
- projected month-end spend;
- daily cap status;
- weekly cap status;
- monthly cap status;
- top spending AI employees;
- top spending machines;
- top spending domains;
- top spending loops;
- provider/model spend;
- paid tool/vendor spend;
- Opportunity Radar spend;
- CoVE / verification spend;
- requested-work spend;
- regular-workflow spend;
- blocked spend requests;
- upcoming expensive scheduled work.

The UI must include authorized controls for:

- switch firm to Normal Mode;
- switch firm to Cheapo Mode;
- switch firm to Critical-Only Mode;
- create Strategic Surge Mode;
- set daily / weekly / monthly caps;
- pause proactive loops;
- require approval for frontier runs;
- require approval for paid data calls;
- pause or downgrade a domain;
- grant domain override;
- pause, downgrade, or kill a recurring loop;
- view actual cost ledger;
- view value/outcome ledger.

## 55.6 Cost alerts

The Budget Governor should alert authorized users when:

- daily budget is exceeded;
- weekly budget is exceeded;
- monthly projection exceeds cap;
- a domain exceeds budget;
- a machine exceeds budget;
- a loop exceeds budget;
- an expensive run is requested;
- a paid tool or paid data call is requested;
- frontier-model use is requested;
- Strategic Surge budget is close to exhaustion;
- two low-value loop outputs occur in a row;
- Cheapo Mode exception is requested;
- Critical-Only Mode blocks or defers material work;
- provider/model pricing changes require threshold review;
- actual provider invoice differs materially from estimates.

## 55.7 Cost authority

Only Managing Partners or explicitly authorized administrators may change firmwide Cost Mode.

Domain overrides must be permissioned, time-bounded when appropriate, and audit-logged.

Strategic Surge Mode must require owner, scope, budget, duration, success metric, and kill condition.

The system must treat cost estimates as estimates until reconciled against provider billing or actual usage records.

## 55.8 v3.2.7 upgrade — AI Employee Lifecycle Control Panel

Machine #41 also owns the AI Employee Lifecycle Control Panel in coordination with Governance Center, Approval Center, Activity/Audit Ledger, Model Governance + Privacy Airlock, Security/Access Control, and each domain owner.

The Lifecycle Control Panel must make it possible for authorized users to:

- view every AI employee and current lifecycle state;
- suspend an AI employee;
- restrict an AI employee;
- place an AI employee on PIP;
- require retraining;
- place an AI employee on probation;
- retire an AI employee;
- permanently fire / decommission an AI employee;
- revoke tool access;
- revoke data access;
- pause or reassign loops;
- reroute active work cards;
- transfer domain responsibilities;
- review incidents;
- view performance scorecards;
- view cost/value history;
- view approval rejection history;
- view hallucination/compliance incidents;
- restore or reactivate an employee only with recorded authorization.

The UI should be calm and operator-readable. It should not anthropomorphize discipline decisions into drama. The operating question is practical:

```text
Should this AI employee keep this authority, lose authority, be retrained, or be removed?
```

## 55.9 Lifecycle records

The system must maintain these records or equivalent fields:

- AIEmployeeStatus;
- AIEmployeeRestriction;
- PIPPlan;
- PerformanceIncident;
- SuspensionRecord;
- RetrainingRecord;
- ProbationRecord;
- RetirementRecord;
- DecommissionRecord;
- PermissionRevocation;
- RoleReassignment;
- LifecycleAuditEvent.

Each lifecycle record should capture:

- employee ID;
- affected domain/machine/workflow;
- triggering reason;
- human approver or authorized actor;
- effective timestamp;
- scope of change;
- access changes;
- active work reassignment;
- loops paused or reassigned;
- review date;
- restoration conditions;
- audit event ID.

## 55.10 Discipline triggers

The system should recommend restriction, PIP, retraining, suspension, retirement, or decommissioning when repeated or severe patterns appear, including:

- unsupported claims;
- failure to apply CoVE;
- failure to apply the correct POV;
- approval-boundary violations;
- external-action risk;
- privacy/compliance incidents;
- repeated bad judgment;
- low usefulness ratings;
- high revision burden;
- poor cost/value ratio;
- repeated low-value loop output;
- excessive model/tool spend;
- stale role scope;
- duplicate role responsibility;
- harmful or off-brand tone;
- failure to preserve West Peek Taste Layer.

The system may recommend lifecycle action automatically. Permanent decommissioning requires authorized human approval.

# 56. West Peek Taste Layer

## 56.1 Purpose

The West Peek Taste Layer prevents generic AI-generated VC slop.

It ensures West Peek communications remain warm, accessible, high-touch, unconventional, serious, LP-credible, and founder-friendly.

## 56.2 Taste checks

Before external-facing materials go out, the OS should ask:

- Does this sound like West Peek?
- Is this too generic?
- Is this too corporate?
- Is this too unserious?
- Is this warm but still institutional?
- Is this founder-friendly?
- Is this LP-credible?
- Is this accurate?
- Is this overclaiming?
- Is this too AI-sounding?
- Does this preserve West Peek’s community-powered edge?

## 56.3 Applies before

The Taste Layer applies before:

- LP materials;
- founder emails;
- event pages;
- portfolio support communications;
- PR drafts;
- website copy;
- social/content drafts;
- sponsor materials;
- community announcements;
- internal employee-facing communication templates when tone matters.

# 57. UX Compression Law

## 57.1 Purpose

The UX Compression Law keeps the system usable.

The user should not operate 40 dashboards. Machine complexity should exist underneath, while the daily human surface stays calm.

### UX compression law

```text
Hide machine complexity until needed.
```

## 57.2 Default UI

The default UI should show only:

- Today;
- Approvals;
- Capture;
- Meetings;
- Pipeline;
- Relationships;
- Search.

Other areas may be available through drill-down, role-specific views, or developer/admin settings.

## 57.3 Command Center UI sketch

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ West Peek OS — Today                                       │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Good morning, Sequoia                                      │
│                                                            │
│ Priority: LP diligence + founder follow-ups                │
│                                                            │
│ [ + Capture ]                                              │
│                                                            │
│ Approvals Due: 7                                           │
│ Meetings Today: 3                                          │
│ Portfolio Alerts: 2                                        │
│ Follow-On Watch: 1                                         │
│ Governance Updates: 1 pending                              │
│ System Health: Warning                                     │
│                                                            │
│ ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐         │
│ │ Approvals    │ │ Meetings     │ │ Portfolio    │         │
│ │ 7 waiting    │ │ 3 today      │ │ 2 alerts     │         │
│ └──────────────┘ └──────────────┘ └──────────────┘         │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```


## 57.4 Cost Mode UX Compression Law

Cost controls must be powerful but calm.

The user should not need to inspect raw provider dashboards to understand AI spend. The West Peek OS interface should compress cost governance into a small number of understandable controls while retaining drill-down detail for operators.

Default controls:

```text
Firmwide Cost Mode: Normal / Cheapo / Critical-Only / Strategic Surge
Daily cap
Weekly cap
Monthly cap
Domain overrides
Pause proactive loops
Require approval for expensive runs
Require approval for frontier models
Require approval for paid data calls
```

Default dashboard questions:

- What did the AI workforce spend today?
- What is this week costing?
- What is month-to-date spend?
- What is projected month-end spend?
- Which employee, machine, domain, loop, or provider is driving spend?
- What did the spend produce?
- What should be paused, downgraded, killed, or approved?

The UX should make the safe/cheap option easy without hiding the ability to authorize high-value strategic work.

# 58. +Capture Governance Routing + AI Workforce Broadcast System

## 58.1 Purpose

The +Capture Governance Routing + AI Workforce Broadcast System allows Sequoia or Scooter to quickly update the AI workforce without deciding ownership, routing, seriousness level, or which employee needs to know.

### Governance capture law

```text
Company-wide AI workforce updates should be easy enough for Sequoia or Scooter to issue through +Capture or their assistant.

The OS, not the human, owns classification, routing, propagation, acknowledgement tracking, and historical recordkeeping.
```

## 58.2 Entry points

A governance or workforce update may enter through:

- +Capture;
- Willa;
- Wendy;
- Wren;
- Walker;
- Command Center;
- Governance Center;
- meeting debrief;
- approval-card comment;
- human typed instruction.

## 58.3 Example human inputs

- “Tell everyone: FundingStack is manual-only and not a system of record.”
- “Make this a rule: no AI employee can send external emails without approval.”
- “Keep in mind: Scooter is focused on founder quality this week.”
- “Update the team: we are not building native data rooms in v1.”
- “Tell the portfolio team: prioritize customer intros over generic advice this month.”
- “For now, any no-API tool is manual-only unless I explicitly approve otherwise.”

## 58.4 Classification types

The OS classifies updates as:

- Constitution / permanent rule;
- policy update;
- operating bulletin;
- context note;
- risk alert;
- vendor status;
- temporary priority;
- partner preference;
- task instruction;
- team brief;
- machine-specific instruction.

## 58.5 Routing scopes

The OS may route to:

- all AI employees;
- W employees only;
- P employees only;
- investment team;
- fundraising team;
- portfolio team;
- research team;
- compliance/ops team;
- meeting team;
- product/build team;
- specific employees only;
- specific machine only.

## 58.6 Historical record fields

Every update records:

- update ID;
- original text;
- clean summary;
- sender;
- submitted by;
- timestamp;
- source channel;
- classification;
- scope;
- affected employees;
- affected machines;
- approver if needed;
- effective date;
- expiration/review date;
- status;
- acknowledgement state;
- applied-in-work references;
- supersedes / superseded by.

## 58.7 AI workforce update law

```text
AI employees are not updated by vibes.
Every firm-wide instruction becomes a classified update object with scope, authority, effective date, owner, acknowledgement state, and audit trail.
```

# 59. Governance Center

## 59.1 Purpose

The Governance Center is the human-friendly interface for Constitution, policies, operating bulletins, context notes, AI workforce briefs, acknowledgements, and change history.

It must support both serious rule updates and lightweight “keep in mind” notes without collapsing them into the same memory layer.

## 59.2 Governance object levels

### Constitution

Permanent rules and final authority principles.

### Policy

Durable operating standards.

### Operating Bulletin

Active guidance that matters across teams or machines but may expire, be reviewed, or be promoted.

### Context Note

Softer memory or current emphasis.

### Task Instruction

One-off action or work direction.

## 59.3 Governance Center UI sketch

```text
│ Governance Center                                          │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [Constitution] [Policies] [Bulletins] [Context Notes]      │
│ [AI Workforce Briefs] [Acknowledgements] [Change Log]      │
│                                                            │
│ Active Bulletins                                           │
│ ┌────┬──────────────────────────────┬─────────┬──────────┐ │
│ │ ID │ Title                        │ Scope   │ Status   │ │
│ ├────┼──────────────────────────────┼─────────┼──────────┤ │
│ │ 14 │ FundingStack manual-only     │ LP/Fund │ Active   │ │
│ │ 15 │ Avoid native data rooms v1   │ Product │ Active   │ │
│ │ 16 │ Founder quality emphasis     │ IC      │ 7 days   │ │
│ └────┴──────────────────────────────┴─────────┴──────────┘ │
│                                                            │
│ [Create Update] [Promote to Policy] [Expire] [View Log]    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Every AI employee should have a current brief showing applicable rules, policies, bulletins, context notes, and active constraints.

# 60. Activity Feed + Audit Ledger System

## 60.1 Purpose

The Activity Feed + Audit Ledger System shows what happened in West Peek OS without turning partner assistant usage into company-wide surveillance.

### Activity visibility law

```text
West Peek OS must show what happened in the system, but not expose every private assistant interaction as company-wide surveillance.

Firm-relevant actions are visible to the appropriate firm authorities.
Personal assistant interactions remain scoped to the requesting partner unless promoted, routed, or converted into firm work.
```

## 60.2 Three log layers

### Recent Activity Feed

Operational awareness.

### Audit Ledger

Durable historical proof for business, governance, compliance, approvals, external actions, IC decisions, data-room activity, LP materials, and policy changes.

### Private Assistant Log

Scoped personal assistant history for the requesting partner.

### Developer Diagnostics

Technical/debugging truth layer for authorized admins.

## 60.3 Activity views

The activity system must support:

- My Activity;
- Agent Activity;
- Firm Activity;
- Audit Ledger;
- Developer Diagnostics;
- search;
- filters;
- last 100 view;
- export where permitted.

## 60.4 Visibility levels

Valid visibility levels:

- private to requester;
- private assistant history;
- partner-visible summary;
- firm-visible operational activity;
- compliance/audit-visible;
- governance-visible;
- restricted / Lockdown.

## 60.5 Activity object schema

Every activity records:

- activity ID;
- timestamp;
- actor type: human / AI employee / system / vendor;
- actor name;
- requesting user;
- machine;
- object touched;
- activity type;
- summary;
- visibility scope;
- sensitivity level;
- related approval ID;
- related document / company / person;
- status;
- audit required;
- diagnostic trace link.

## 60.6 Private-to-firm promotion rule

```text
Private assistant interactions become firm-visible only when promoted, routed, approved, sent externally, attached to firm records, or converted into institutional work.
```

# 61. Developer Settings + System Diagnostics Panel

## 61.1 Purpose

The Developer Settings + System Diagnostics Panel gives authorized admins a technical truth layer for debugging, audit investigation, sync troubleshooting, permission tracing, source-of-truth conflict review, integration failures, memory promotion failures, and agent behavior review.

### Debuggability law

```text
West Peek OS must be debuggable from day one.
Every meaningful system action should create structured logs that can be reviewed by authorized admins without exposing private assistant conversations unnecessarily.
```

## 61.2 Diagnostic sections

The Developer Settings panel must include:

- System Logs;
- Agent Runs;
- API / Webhooks;
- Permissions;
- Memory;
- Source Conflicts;
- Errors;
- Costs;
- Debug Tools;
- AI Debug Copilot.

## 61.3 Diagnostic event types

Developer diagnostics records:

- system logs;
- agent runs;
- routing decisions;
- classification decisions;
- failed tasks;
- API calls;
- webhook events;
- sync jobs;
- permission checks;
- approval transitions;
- memory promotion attempts;
- source conflicts;
- error logs;
- latency/cost/model usage;
- policy blocks;
- privacy/Lockdown decisions;
- retries;
- manual overrides.

## 61.4 Developer diagnostics must answer

- Why did this happen?
- Who triggered it?
- Which agent touched it?
- Which machine routed it?
- Which data source was used?
- Which rule was applied?
- Which API failed?
- Was this blocked by policy?
- Did this write to memory?
- Did this create an approval card?
- What should be tried next?

## 61.5 Privacy boundary

```text
The system may know that an event occurred.
Not every authorized user may see the private content of that event.
```

Technical logs may preserve event metadata. Human-readable content remains permission-scoped.

## 61.6 Developer Settings UI sketch

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Developer Settings                                         │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [System Logs] [Agent Runs] [API/Webhooks] [Permissions]    │
│ [Memory] [Source Conflicts] [Errors] [Costs] [Debug Tools] │
│                                                            │
│ System Health: Warning                                     │
│ Open Issues: 3                                             │
│                                                            │
│ ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ Recent Issues               │ │ AI Debug Copilot       │ │
│ ├─────────────────────────────┤ ├────────────────────────┤ │
│ │ ⚠ Fireflies import failed   │ │ Issue selected:        │ │
│ │ ⚠ Network OS sync warning   │ │ Fireflies import failed│ │
│ │ ⚠ Perry enrichment timeout  │ │                        │ │
│ │                             │ │ Likely cause:          │ │
│ │                             │ │ Transcript not ready.  │ │
│ │                             │ │                        │ │
│ │                             │ │ Recommended fix:       │ │
│ │                             │ │ Retry in 15 minutes.   │ │
│ │                             │ │                        │ │
│ │                             │ │ [Retry Now]            │ │
│ │                             │ │ [Create Work Card]     │ │
│ │                             │ │ [Explain More]         │ │
│ └─────────────────────────────┘ └────────────────────────┘ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Every AI employee action should have a trace with:

- run ID;
- agent;
- trigger;
- requested by;
- machine;
- privacy mode;
- model mode;
- input references;
- steps taken;
- rules applied;
- outputs created;
- approval cards created;
- memory writes attempted;
- errors;
- cost/latency;
- status.

```text
│ 2. Extracted support request                               │
│ 3. Queried Relationship OS                                 │
│ 4. Asked Winnie for intro candidates                       │

│                                                            │
│ Rules Applied                                              │

│                                                            │

│                                                            │
│ [View Inputs] [View Outputs] [View Logs] [Re-run]          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 61.8 API / Webhook Diagnostics

Track:

- API provider;
- endpoint/job type;
- timestamp;
- status;
- latency;
- rate limit status;
- error code;
- retry count;
- object ID;
- source object;
- destination object;
- last successful sync.

## 61.9 Permission Trace

The Permission Trace explains why a human, AI employee, machine, or integration could or could not access something.

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Permission Trace                                           │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Object: BrightPath IC Notes                                │
│ Privacy Label: IC / Confidential                           │
│                                                            │

│ Paige: Allowed — Research Analyst                          │
│ Willow: Allowed — Compliance                               │

│ Waverly: Denied — Community role not permitted             │
│                                                            │
│ Rule Applied: AI Workspace Access / IC Confidential Policy │
│                                                            │
│ [View Policy] [Request Override] [Revoke All]              │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 61.10 Memory / Knowledge Diagnostics

Memory diagnostics tracks:

- what got promoted to Knowledge OS;
- what stayed as a note;
- what expired;
- what was rejected;
- what was superseded;
- what source supported this memory;
- where a rule/bulletin/context note was applied.

# 62. AI Debug Copilot / System Doctor

## 62.1 Purpose

The AI Debug Copilot / System Doctor is the built-in AI assistant inside Developer Diagnostics. It helps Sequoia, Scooter, and authorized admins understand what broke, why it broke, what risk it creates, and what safe action should happen next.

### Debug Copilot law

```text
Developer Diagnostics must include AI assistance that explains failures in plain English, identifies likely root causes, recommends safe fixes, and prepares repair actions.
```

## 62.2 Supported issue types

The Debug Copilot supports:

- failed agent runs;
- failed sync jobs;
- permission denials;
- missing documents;
- source-of-truth conflicts;
- failed memory promotions;
- meeting transcript problems;
- API/webhook failures;
- vendor integration issues;
- approval-routing errors;
- model/cost/latency spikes;
- privacy/Lockdown conflicts;
- data room access issues;
- AI employee quality issues;
- stale data;
- policy enforcement events;
- user-error recovery;
- vendor delay triage.

## 62.3 Safe actions

The Debug Copilot may:

- explain issue;
- classify severity;
- show root cause trace;
- recommend next step;
- retry failed job;
- schedule retry;
- create repair work card;
- route to Wilson;
- route to Willow;
- open related object;
- export diagnostic bundle;
- compare before/after state;
- suggest patch plan;
- generate reproduction steps;
- identify whether a block is expected behavior.

## 62.4 Approval-required actions

The Debug Copilot requires explicit human approval before it may:

- force re-sync;
- override permission;
- delete object;
- purge memory;
- disable agent;
- change policy;
- change model mode;
- replay workflow with external side effects;
- send external communication;
- change vendor configuration;
- promote a diagnostic finding into policy.

## 62.5 Repair authority law

```text
The Debug Copilot may explain, recommend, prepare, and route fixes.
It may not silently perform destructive, privacy-sensitive, policy-changing, or external-impacting repairs without explicit human approval.
```

## 62.6 Issue classifications

The Debug Copilot must distinguish between:

- true system failure;
- expected permission block;
- vendor delay;
- stale data;
- source conflict;
- user error;
- policy enforcement;
- integration failure;
- AI employee quality issue;
- privacy/Lockdown conflict;
- no action needed.

## 62.7 Example: expected permission block

```text
Issue:
Pippa could not access BrightPath IC notes.

Debug Copilot:
Pippa was denied because BrightPath IC notes are labeled IC / Confidential.
Marketing AI employees do not have access to IC-confidential objects.

Recommended action:
No fix required unless you want to create an LP-safe or marketing-safe summary.

Options:
[Create LP-Safe Summary]
[Request Access Override]
[View Policy]
[Dismiss]
```

## 62.8 Example: vendor delay

```text
Issue:
Fireflies transcript import failed.

Debug Copilot:
Fireflies transcript import failed because the transcript is not ready yet.

Likely cause:
The provider webhook arrived before transcript processing completed.

Recommended action:
Retry in 15 minutes.

Risk:
Low. No data was lost. Meeting record is still pending.

Options:
[Retry Now] [Schedule Retry] [Create Work Card] [Ignore] [Open Provider Logs]
```

## 62.9 Example: agent quality issue

```text
Issue:
Penn’s LP drafts have 62% revision rate this month.

Debug Copilot:
Penn is overusing generic VC language and underusing West Peek Taste Layer.
Most revisions involve tone, specificity, and LP credibility.

Recommended action:
Update Penn’s drafting instructions.
Route future LP drafts through Taste Layer before approval.

Options:
[Update Penn Brief]
[Create Training Note]
[Require Taste Layer]
[Pause Penn for LP Drafts]
```

# v3.2.4 Compiler Ledger and Integration QA

## Requirement Parse

Target audience:

- Sequoia Taylor;
- Scooter Taylor;
- future West Peek operators;
- product architects;
- software engineers;
- AI employees;
- compliance reviewers;
- future repository builders.

Document purpose:

- establish the v3.2.4 canonical planning baseline for West Peek OS;
- preserve v3.1 canonical architecture;
- integrate approved v3.2.4 hardening decisions into the canonical body;
- make the OS more buildable, governable, capital-disciplined, source-of-truth aware, vendor-realistic, debuggable, audit-ready, and UX-contained.

Deliverable format:

- Markdown canonical master plan suitable for DOCX or Google Docs export.

Operational scope:

- institutional operating system for West Peek Ventures;
- Odysseus fork;
- two Managing Partner offices;
- AI workforce;
- approvals;
- capture;
- governance broadcast;
