# 65. v3.2.7 Cumulative Governance Addendum — AI Employee Lifecycle Control

## 65.1 Purpose

v3.2.7 closes the AI employee accountability gap.

The system already required AI employees to be safe, useful, senior, evidence-aware, cost-aware, visible, mode-governed, and approval-controlled. v3.2.7 adds the missing institutional consequence layer: authorized humans must be able to suspend, restrict, PIP, retrain, probate, retire, or permanently decommission AI employees when behavior, performance, cost, authority, or strategy requires it.

## 65.2 Canonical distinction

This is a canonical governance requirement, not a deployment decision.

v3.2.7 does not canonize MyClaw.ai, Twin.so, Cloudflare, Railway, Render, DigitalOcean, or any external runtime provider. Vendor/runtime evaluation belongs in the Odysseus implementation plan.

## 65.3 Lifecycle control summary

Required lifecycle controls:

- Active;
- Restricted;
- Suspended;
- PIP / Performance Improvement Plan;
- Retraining Required;
- Probation;
- Retired;
- Permanently Fired / Decommissioned.

Required lifecycle capabilities:

- status visibility;
- permission revocation;
- work-card rerouting;
- loop pause/reassignment;
- incident tracking;
- performance review;
- retraining requirements;
- role reassignment;
- audit logging;
- human approval for permanent decommissioning;
- no orphan responsibility;
- no silent reactivation.

## 65.4 Lifecycle control relationship to cost governance

Lifecycle controls interact with the Budget Governor but are not the same thing.

The Budget Governor controls spend posture, model routing, cost caps, and work-class approvals.

Lifecycle Control governs whether a specific AI employee should retain authority, be restricted, be improved, be retrained, be retired, or be removed.

An AI employee may be cost-restricted without being on PIP. An AI employee may be on PIP because of quality even if it is cheap. An AI employee may be decommissioned because it creates risk even if it saves money.

## 65.5 Lifecycle control relationship to Opportunity Radar

Opportunity Radar may continue only through employees with valid lifecycle status and appropriate authority.

A Restricted or PIP employee may not promote opportunity briefs unless explicitly permitted. A Suspended, Retired, or Decommissioned employee may not surface opportunities, trigger follow-up work, or participate in autonomous loops.

## 65.6 Lifecycle control relationship to approval queues

Opportunity Review and Workflow Approval must display when a proposed action, surfaced opportunity, or draft was produced by an employee under restriction, PIP, probation, or other elevated review state.

This allows Sequoia and Scooter to distinguish:

- good work from trusted employees;
- good work from employees under review;
- risky work from employees with active incidents;
- outputs that require extra human scrutiny.

## 65.7 Canonical lifecycle law

```text
AI employees are revocable operating roles, not permanent autonomous entities.

Performance, usefulness, cost discipline, safety, evidence quality, approval compliance, and strategic fit determine continued authority.

The system may recommend lifecycle action, but permanent decommissioning requires authorized human approval.
```



---

# 66. External Agent Runtime, Owned Agent Core, and Vendor Execution Adapter Law

## 66.1 Canonical purpose

West Peek OS may use internal AI employees, model APIs, firm-owned tool adapters, computer-use environments, browser automation, and external agent platforms. The canonical question is not whether West Peek should build or rent every capability. The canonical question is who owns judgment, memory, approvals, evidence discipline, cost control, and institutional authority.

The answer is permanent:

```text
West Peek OS owns the institutional brain.
External tools may provide temporary execution power.
```

This law governs the boundary between:

- West Peek-owned AI employees;
- model/API-powered internal agents;
- firm-owned tools and adapters;
- browser/computer-use sandboxes;
- external agent runtimes;
- tactical automation vendors;
- hosted always-on agents;
- open-source agent frameworks;
- coding agents and repo agents;
- future equivalents.

## 66.2 Owned Agent Core law

The core West Peek AI employee layer must remain owned by West Peek OS.

Owned core includes:

- AI Employee Registry;
- AI Employee Contract;
- senior POV Layer;
- CoVE / Verification Layer;
- Opportunity Radar governance;
- Opportunity Review Queue;
- Workflow Approval Queue;
- Approval Center;
- AI Workforce Cost Dashboard;
- Budget Governor;
- Cost Mode Switchboard;
- AI Employee Lifecycle Control Panel;
- suspend, restrict, PIP, retrain, retire, and decommission controls;
- Memory Promotion Queue;
- Knowledge OS and source-of-truth resolution;
- Activity Feed and Audit Ledger;
- IC decision hygiene;
- LP diligence proof discipline;
- fund/community/production boundary enforcement;
- relationship intelligence authority;
- compliance routing;
- vendor risk routing;
- taste, brand, and strategic-fit review.

No external platform may replace this owned core.

## 66.3 External Agent Runtime Adapter law

West Peek OS may use external agent platforms such as Twin, MyClaw, OpenClaw, Claude Code, Codex, Hermes-style agents, browser agents, no-code automation platforms, hosted agent platforms, or future equivalents as tactical execution runtimes.

External agent platforms are execution adapters, not institutional authorities.

They may perform scoped tasks such as:

- browser automation;
- web research support;
- scraping where permitted;
- file generation;
- website interaction;
- draft creation;
- inbox triage support;
- scheduled monitoring;
- tactical operations;
- temporary workflow execution;
- low-risk repetitive tasks;
- coding assistance inside controlled repo workflows;
- test-environment computer-use tasks;
- vendor-specific automation tasks.

They may not become:

- the canonical West Peek OS brain;
- source of truth;
- approval authority;
- canonical memory authority;
- compliance authority;
- fund decisioning authority;
- IC decision maker;
- LP proof authority;
- relationship intelligence authority;
- cost governor;
- AI employee boss;
- external communication authority;
- sponsor promise authority;
- data-room authority;
- fund/community/production boundary interpreter.

## 66.4 Build-vs-rent operating rule

West Peek should build or own what makes the firm institutionally smarter, safer, more valuable, more defensible, or more compliant.

West Peek may rent what merely accelerates execution.

Build/own by default:

- governance;
- memory;
- approvals;
- AI employee identity;
- cost controls;
- firm-specific POV;
- CoVE and evidence rules;
- investment decision hygiene;
- LP diligence logic;
- relationship intelligence;
- community/fund/production boundary enforcement;
- source-of-truth resolution;
- lifecycle discipline;
- institutional audit.

Rent or integrate when useful:

- browser automation;
- tactical scraping;
- low-risk scheduled monitoring;
- hosted agent runtime capacity;
- one-off operational workflows;
- sandboxed computer-use tasks;
- non-core no-code automations;
- temporary vendor-specific execution.

## 66.5 Model/API neutrality law

West Peek OS should not be structurally dependent on a single model provider.

Internal AI employees may use model APIs and tool frameworks, including Claude API, OpenAI API, Gemini API, Perplexity or search providers, local models, or future equivalents, through the Model Router, Budget Governor, Privacy Airlock, Cost Dashboard, and Approval Center.

Model selection must obey:

- current firmwide cost mode;
- domain-level overrides;
- data sensitivity;
- evidence requirements;
- approval state;
- task severity;
- least-expensive-adequate model/tool selection;
- privacy and source-of-truth rules;
- lifecycle restrictions on the assigned AI employee.

## 66.6 External agent run requirements

Every external agent run must have:

- named owner;
- task scope;
- business purpose;
- assigned domain;
- assigned machine;
- responsible internal AI employee or human owner;
- permission boundary;
- data boundary;
- account boundary;
- cost class;
- estimated cost;
- approval state;
- allowed tools;
- disallowed tools;
- output destination;
- audit log;
- termination condition;
- review requirement;
- vendor identity;
- fallback plan if the vendor fails.

External agent runs must be temporary unless explicitly approved as a recurring loop. Recurring external-agent loops must follow loop governance, budget caps, review dates, success metrics, and kill conditions.

## 66.7 Sandbox and least-privilege law

Any agent that can browse, click, type, install skills, read files, operate websites, access email, access calendar, run code, use shell tools, or interact with external accounts must operate under least-privilege and sandbox principles.

The default posture is:

```text
No unrestricted external agent access.
No silent credentials.
No unreviewed skill installation.
No direct fund-sensitive access by vendor agents.
No autonomous consequential action without approval.
```

External agents must not receive unrestricted access to:

- Gmail;
- Google Calendar;
- Google Drive;
- GitHub;
- LP materials;
- fund documents;
- sponsor communications;
- relationship data;
- private founder data;
- bank, brokerage, payment, or accounting tools;
- investor pipeline data;
- data rooms;
- production secrets;
- environment variables;
- API keys;
- credential stores.

Access may be granted only through approved scoped adapters, sandbox accounts, test accounts, masked data, or least-privilege service accounts where feasible.

## 66.8 Vendor truth boundary

External agent vendor claims, pricing, uptime, feature availability, security posture, data-handling posture, API availability, model compatibility, and compliance posture are not canonical facts until verified against current vendor documentation, contract terms, security review, and testing.

The canonical rule is:

```text
Treat vendor platforms as candidates, not assumptions.
```

## 66.9 External agent lifecycle control

External agent integrations must obey the same lifecycle discipline as AI employees.

West Peek OS must be able to:

- disconnect a vendor;
- suspend a vendor integration;
- revoke a vendor token;
- pause a vendor-run loop;
- downgrade a vendor workflow;
- require human approval before the next vendor run;
- quarantine vendor output;
- mark a vendor workflow as failed or unsafe;
- reassign the work to an internal AI employee;
- preserve audit history after disconnection.

No external vendor may become impossible to remove.

## 66.10 External agent output handling

External agent output must return to West Peek OS before becoming institutional output.

Default handling:

1. External agent completes scoped task.
2. Output returns to West Peek OS.
3. Assigned internal AI employee or machine validates format and scope.
4. CoVE / evidence review is applied where required.
5. Approval Center routes any consequential action.
6. Memory Promotion Queue decides whether anything becomes durable memory.
7. Audit Ledger records the run, cost, output, approvals, and disposition.

External agents may create drafts, evidence packets, scraped summaries, file outputs, or workflow artifacts. They may not silently publish, send, commit, spend, promise, or update canonical memory unless specifically authorized.

## 66.11 Vendor-assisted does not mean vendor-governed

The existence of MyClaw, Twin, OpenClaw, Claude Code, Codex, Hermes-style agents, or future agent platforms does not reduce the need for West Peek OS.

It increases the need for West Peek OS.

The more external execution power West Peek uses, the more important it becomes that West Peek OS controls:

- authority;
- approvals;
- cost;
- memory;
- scope;
- evidence;
- security;
- lifecycle;
- audit;
- source-of-truth rules.

## 66.12 Canonical external agent runtime law

```text
West Peek owns judgment, memory, approval, governance, cost control, lifecycle discipline, and source-of-truth authority.

External agent platforms may supply temporary execution power.

External agents may not become the West Peek OS brain, source of truth, approval authority, memory authority, compliance authority, investment decision authority, relationship intelligence authority, or cost governor.

Every external agent run must be scoped, sandboxed, least-privilege, cost-visible, approval-governed, audit-logged, revocable, and terminated when the task or authorization ends.
```



# 67. v3.2.10 INTENT-TO-EXECUTION INTELLIGENCE + INSTITUTIONAL LENS BENCH

## 67.1 Purpose

v3.2.10 upgrades the existing Prompt Enhancer + Intent-to-Execution Machine from a prompt-improvement surface into a firm operating standard.

The institutional problem is not that Scooter or Sequoia need better prompt wording.

The problem is that Managing Partners will often give rough, compressed, partial, emotional, strategic, or exploratory intent, and the system must translate that intent into firm-ready work without losing judgment, authority, evidence discipline, cost control, or approval boundaries.

Canonical law:

```text
Managing Partners provide intent.
West Peek OS converts intent into governed, role-aware, evidence-aware, cost-aware execution packets.
AI employees do not execute serious work from raw prompts alone.
```

This layer sits above:

1. AI Employee Registry;
2. Prompt Enhancer + Intent-to-Execution Machine;
3. POV Intelligence Layer;
4. Institutional Lens Bench;
5. CoVE / verification layer;
6. Cost Dashboard and Budget Governor;
7. Model Router;
8. Vendor Adapter Bench;
9. Approval Center;
10. Audit Ledger;
11. Memory Promotion Queue.

## 67.2 West Peek Translation of the Boss OS Prompt Intelligence Source

The uploaded Boss OS v10.14 prompt-intelligence material is useful as a source input, but it is not copied into West Peek OS as personal Boss OS language.

Correct translation:

```text
Boss OS version:
Sequoia gives the need. Boss OS becomes the prompt engineer.

West Peek OS version:
Managing Partners provide intent. West Peek OS becomes the institutional intent-to-execution translator.
```

West Peek OS is a firm operating system. Therefore the output is not merely an enhanced prompt. The output is a governed work packet that can be assigned to a domain, machine, AI employee, model, backend, vendor adapter, or human helper.

## 67.3 Intent-to-Execution Intelligence Law

```text
West Peek OS must not require Scooter or Sequoia to be expert prompt engineers, systems designers, workflow architects, security reviewers, or model-routing specialists before work can be done well.

For ordinary low-risk work, West Peek OS may invisibly improve task framing and proceed.

For serious work, West Peek OS must classify the task, select the correct domain/machine, choose the correct institutional lens stack, define evidence and approval standards, assign a cost class, select the least risky adequate backend, and generate a Universal Tier-1 Work Packet before execution.

Serious work includes canonical changes, repo/app work, investment analysis, IC work, LP/fundraising materials, legal/compliance-sensitive work, external communications, sponsor/brand materials, data-room work, vendor/tool adoption, automation changes, AI employee lifecycle decisions, recurring loops, budget-governed work, and any action that may affect money, reputation, compliance, relationships, source-of-truth records, or external commitments.
```

## 67.4 Prompt / Assignment Enhancement Modes

West Peek OS supports three levels of intent enhancement.

### Level 1 — Invisible Intent Enhancement

Default for normal low-risk work.

The MP speaks naturally. West Peek OS quietly improves the request enough to answer, draft, route, or summarize.

Used for:

1. quick discussion;
2. simple summaries;
3. low-risk drafts;
4. basic planning;
5. non-sensitive internal edits;
6. casual brainstorming;
7. ordinary clarification.

User experience:

```text
No portal required.
No machinery displayed unless requested.
The system simply behaves like a capable operator.
```

### Level 2 — Automatic Tier-1 Work Packet

Triggered automatically for serious work.

West Peek OS does not execute the raw prompt directly. It creates a structured work packet first.

Trigger categories include:

1. repo/app work;
2. canonical document updates;
3. investor materials;
4. LP communications;
5. IC/deal work;
6. external/outbound emails;
7. sponsorship/brand partnership work;
8. legal/compliance-adjacent work;
9. financial decisions;
10. vendor/backend changes;
11. paid tool or data calls;
12. recurring automation loops;
13. private data handling;
14. human helper assignments;
15. external agent assignments.

Visible summary should be short.

Example:

```text
Treating this as Tier-1 work.
Lead Lens: Emerging Manager Platform Operator
Counter-Lens: Compliance-Sensitive Skeptic
Cost Class: Standard
Approval: required before external action
```

### Level 3 — Prompt / Assignment Enhancer Portal

Manual workspace for deliberate control, reuse, export, or comparison.

Use when an MP wants to:

1. see the enhanced work packet;
2. edit the work packet;
3. save a reusable prompt;
4. export a prompt to Claude, Codex, Gemini, OpenAI, Browserbase, n8n, a human helper, or another backend;
5. compare backend-specific prompt adapters;
6. choose or override lens stack;
7. force Tier-1 mode;
8. promote a proven work packet to the firm prompt library;
9. inspect cost and backend recommendation;
10. score prompt quality before execution.

Portal law:

```text
The portal exists for visibility, control, reuse, comparison, and export.
It is not required for ordinary expert assistance.
```

## 67.5 Institutional Lens Bench

West Peek OS uses an Institutional Lens Bench, not a passive library of famous names.

Definition:

```text
The Institutional Lens Bench is a governed registry of expert principles, schools of thought, operator archetypes, professional judgment frames, and named source influences that West Peek OS may retrieve when a task requires higher-order judgment.
```

The bench exists so West Peek can operate like a more experienced institution without blindly imitating any one person, firm, investor, founder, writer, strategist, operator, or framework.

Potential West Peek lens categories include:

1. Emerging Manager GP;
2. Skeptical LP;
3. Venture Platform Operator;
4. Secondaries Operator;
5. Institutional Buyer;
6. Fund Counsel / Compliance Reviewer;
7. Community-Led Growth Operator;
8. Brand Partnerships Strategist;
9. Event Revenue Operator;
10. Portfolio Support Lead;
11. Research Analyst;
12. IC Memo Reviewer;
13. Capital Allocator;
14. Fund Admin / Finance Operator;
15. Relationship Capital Strategist;
16. Premium Taste Reviewer;
17. Founder Operator;
18. Product Architect;
19. Security Engineer;
20. AI Systems Architect.

## 67.6 No Pedestal Law

```text
West Peek OS does not pedestalize masters.

Named investors, founders, operators, writers, strategists, engineers, funds, frameworks, models, vendors, and schools of thought are principle sources, not authorities.

No master, model, vendor, investor, founder, operator, writer, or framework overrides source documents, evidence, compliance, MP authority, data classification, approval gates, West Peek strategy, or reality.
```

Supporting law:

```text
West Peek OS may borrow judgment from masters.
It does not submit the firm to masters.
```

Examples:

1. Buffett/Munger lenses may help with incentives, temperament, durability, downside protection, and capital allocation, but they are not universal for venture, AI, platform, community, media, or power-law opportunities.
2. Direct-response advertising lenses may improve clarity and conversion, but they cannot override truth, compliance, taste, or investor-facing claim standards.
3. Founder/operator lenses may increase speed and product imagination, but they cannot bypass governance, security, or approval gates.
4. Security-engineer lenses may prevent catastrophic risk, but they cannot turn every small workflow into an overbuilt security bureaucracy.
5. Premium/luxury lenses may improve trust and taste, but they cannot excuse vague economics or weak operations.

## 67.7 Lens Stack Requirement

For serious work, West Peek OS should not rely on a single lens.

The standard stack is:

```text
Lead Lens
Supporting Lens
Counter-Lens
Hostile Reviewer
Truth / Compliance Gate
```

### Example — West Peek Rooms

```text
Lead Lens:
Brand Partnerships Operator

Supporting Lens:
Community-Led Growth + Event Revenue Operator

Counter-Lens:
Fund-Compliance Skeptic

Hostile Reviewer:
Scooter saying this feels like busywork instead of leverage

Truth / Compliance Gate:
Legal / Compliance Rules Machine
```

### Example — Late-Stage Secondary Opportunity

```text
Lead Lens:
Secondaries Operator

Supporting Lens:
Institutional Buyer + Capital Allocator

Counter-Lens:
VC Power-Law / Technology Adoption Skeptic

Hostile Reviewer:
Buyer looking for reasons to walk

Truth / Compliance Gate:
Compliance / MNPI / Brokerage Rules Review
```

### Example — LP Update

```text
Lead Lens:
Emerging Manager GP

Supporting Lens:
Skeptical LP + Proof Engine

Counter-Lens:
Compliance-Sensitive Claims Reviewer

Hostile Reviewer:
Allocator assuming the update is fluff

Truth / Compliance Gate:
Legal / Fundraising Claims Review
```

Canonical law:

```text
Every powerful lens has a blind spot.
West Peek OS must identify the blind spot before relying on the lens.
```

## 67.8 Institutional Lens Card Schema

Every lens should be stored as a structured card.

Fields:

```text
lens_name
lens_category
named_sources
schools_of_thought
role_archetypes
domain
when_to_use
when_not_to_use
core_principles
questions_it_asks
failure_modes_it_catches
blind_spots
counter_lenses
output_standard
truth_or_compliance_limits
source_notes
version
review_date
promotion_status
```

This prevents the system from becoming a celebrity-name list or a generic advice library.

## 67.9 Universal Tier-1 Work Packet

The Universal Tier-1 Work Packet is the model/vendor-agnostic assignment object created before serious execution.

Canonical law:

```text
West Peek OS creates the universal task standard first.
Then it adapts that standard to the selected model, backend, machine, vendor adapter, or human helper.
```

Required fields:

```text
objective
raw_intent
context
source_materials
firm_domain
machine
assigned_ai_employee
requested_by
owner
output_format
audience
lead_lens
supporting_lenses
counter_lens
hostile_reviewer
truth_or_compliance_gate
lead_POV
supporting_POVs
definition_of_done
evidence_standard
truth_mode
data_classification
allowed_tools
forbidden_tools
allowed_context
forbidden_context
forbidden_actions
review_requirements
validation_requirements
artifact_requirements
approval_queue
approval_requirement
cost_class
current_cost_mode
budget_cap
backend_recommendation
model_backend_adapter
observability_trace_required
memory_promotion_candidate
prompt_library_promotion_candidate
completion_criteria
kill_condition
```

## 67.10 Model / Backend Prompt Adapter Law

West Peek OS must not send the same prompt shape to every backend.

Different backends need different instruction formats.

Adapter classes include:

1. Claude / Claude Code adapter;
2. Codex / OpenAI agent adapter;
3. Gemini adapter;
4. Perplexity / research adapter;
5. DeepSeek / GLM / Qwen / open-weight model adapter;
6. Browserbase / Stagehand browser execution adapter;
7. n8n / workflow automation adapter;
8. Pipedream / Composio tool-access adapter;
9. Cloudflare Agents / owned runtime adapter;
10. human helper adapter.

Canonical law:

```text
The Tier-1 Work Packet defines the standard.
The backend adapter changes only execution formatting, not authority, truth, cost, evidence, or approval rules.
```

Claude Code, Codex, Cloudflare Agents, Browserbase, n8n, MyClaw, Twin, OpenClaw, GLM, and future tools may be useful exemplars or execution surfaces.

None becomes permanent authority.

## 67.11 Prompt Quality Scorecard

Before serious prompt/work-packet execution, West Peek OS should score quality.

Score fields:

```text
objective_clarity
output_specificity
source_grounding
constraint_clarity
lens_selection_quality
counter_lens_quality
POV_fit
risk_boundary_clarity
truth_mode_fit
data_classification_fit
approval_boundary_clarity
validation_plan_quality
backend_fit
cost_fit
ambiguity_risk
hallucination_risk
rework_risk
```

Score levels:

```text
Draft
Usable
Tier-1
Reusable
Canonical
```

No prompt or work packet becomes reusable firm canon without approval and evidence from successful use.

## 67.12 Prompt / Work Packet Observability and Evaluation

Every serious prompt-enhanced run must be traceable.

Trace fields:

```text
raw_request
enhanced_work_packet
lens_stack
POV_card
backend_adapter
prompt_version
enhancement_level
whether_packet_was_shown_to_MP
whether_MP_edited_it
whether_portal_generated
whether_auto_generated
whether_promoted_to_library
tools_used
output
cost
latency
review_result
hallucination_flags
approval_status
memory_promotion_status
usefulness_score
rework_required
```

This enables West Peek OS to learn:

1. which lenses improve outcomes;
2. which prompts reduce rework;
3. which backend adapters perform best;
4. which enhancements create approval spam;
5. which enhanced prompts are worth promoting;
6. whether the cost of better prompting is justified;
7. where AI employees still need lifecycle discipline.

## 67.13 Cost Governor Integration

Prompt intelligence must obey the Cost Dashboard and Budget Governor.

Canonical rule:

```text
Use the cheapest model/backend that can meet the required standard under the correct authority envelope.
Escalate only when stakes, quality, risk, privacy, evidence, or context require it.
```

Prompt enhancement itself has a cost.

Therefore the Budget Governor must be able to downgrade:

1. deep prompt enhancement to light invisible enhancement;
2. multi-lens review to single lead lens;
3. frontier-model packet generation to cheaper model drafting;
4. immediate execution to batched packet generation;
5. verbose portal preview to compact summary;
6. non-urgent enhancement to deferred work.

Cheapo Mode must preserve safety boundaries. It may reduce elegance, breadth, or depth. It may not remove compliance gates, data classification, approval requirements, or external-action boundaries.

## 67.14 Upgrade to Machine #42

Machine #42 remains:

```text
Prompt Enhancer + Intent-to-Execution Machine
```

v3.2.10 upgrades Machine #42 to own:

1. raw-intent capture;
2. enhancement-level selection;
3. Institutional Lens Bench retrieval;
4. Lens Stack generation;
5. POV Card generation;
6. Universal Tier-1 Work Packet generation;
7. backend prompt adapter selection;
8. Prompt Quality Scorecard;
9. prompt/work-packet observability fields;
10. prompt library promotion recommendations;
11. Cost Governor coordination;
12. Approval Center coordination.

No new machine is required.

This avoids machine-registry bloat while strengthening the existing intent-to-execution lane.

## 67.15 Relationship to Opportunity Radar

Opportunity Radar can surface ideas.

Intent-to-Execution Intelligence turns approved or requested intent into executable work packets.

The distinction:

```text
Opportunity Radar:
Should the firm consider this opportunity?

Intent-to-Execution Intelligence:
How should the firm structure the work once the opportunity or request exists?
```

Surfaced opportunities still enter Opportunity Review first.

Approved opportunities then receive Universal Tier-1 Work Packets before execution.

## 67.16 Relationship to Approval Queues

Prompt intelligence does not bypass approval.

It strengthens approval by making the proposed work clearer.

```text
Opportunity Review Queue:
Should we pursue this surfaced idea?

Workflow Approval Queue:
May we execute this specific requested or approved action?

Prompt / Assignment Enhancer Portal:
What exact work packet or backend prompt should execute the work?
```

All serious packets must display:

1. estimated cost;
2. cost class;
3. selected lens stack;
4. data classification;
5. approval requirement;
6. backend recommendation;
7. forbidden actions;
8. completion criteria.

## 67.17 Canonical Final Law

```text
West Peek OS becomes the institutional prompt engineer and assignment architect.

Managing Partners may speak naturally.

For ordinary work, intent enhancement is invisible.
For serious work, intent enhancement becomes Tier-1 automatically.
For reusable, exportable, inspectable, comparable, or backend-specific work, the Prompt / Assignment Enhancer Portal provides manual control.

Masters become a bench, not a pedestal.
Models become competitors, not idols.
Vendors become adapters, not authorities.
West Peek OS owns the standard.
```


# 67. Capability Intelligence and Adapter Selection Law

## 67.1 Canonical Purpose

West Peek OS must not merely collect vendors, model providers, prompt templates, Claude Skills, workflows, reference repositories, validators, browser tools, research tools, or human SOPs.

West Peek OS must classify, benchmark, select, combine, observe, improve, retire, and replace capabilities.

The purpose of Capability Intelligence is to answer one operating question before serious work begins:

```text
What is the smallest approved capability stack that can complete this work at the required standard, under the correct data, cost, approval, compliance, and audit boundaries?
```

This law sits above the Vendor Adapter Bench, Model Bench, Prompt / Work Packet Intelligence layer, External Agent Runtime Adapter, Browser Execution Adapter, Workflow Automation Adapter, Observability + Evals layer, AI Employee Lifecycle system, and Cost Governor.

## 67.2 Core Capability Law

```text
Own the institution.
Benchmark the capabilities.
Use the smallest effective stack.
Retire what stops working.
```

Operational law:

```text
Discover broadly.
Default narrowly.
Combine sparingly.
Review periodically.
Replace without drama.
```

Capability support does not create authority.

```text
Capabilities may support execution.
Capabilities may not become institutional authority.
```

## 67.3 Capability Definition

A capability is any reusable operating asset that helps West Peek OS produce a reliable outcome.

Capability types include:

```text
model provider
open-weight model
vendor platform
agent runtime
Claude Skill
OpenAI / Codex instruction set
Gemini workflow
MCP server
n8n workflow
Pipedream / Composio integration
Browserbase / Stagehand script
research adapter
repo validator
document production pipeline
prompt adapter
eval harness
human SOP
reference repository
agent launch template
project governance document
firm operating rule
```

Capability Intelligence prevents these assets from becoming a tool museum, a vendor pile, or a shadow authority system.

## 67.4 Active Default / Bench / Archive Model

Every important capability lives in one of three operating layers.

### Active Default

The current best approved capability or capability stack for a defined job type.

Example:

```text
Long-form document production:
Active Default → Document Compiler Mode v2.0 discipline until a better governed document pipeline is tested and promoted.
```

### Bench

Promising alternatives, reference implementations, patterns, tools, workflows, or models that may be watched, tested, or used in limited lanes.

Examples:

```text
new Claude Skills
Claude Managed Agent launch references
new OpenAI / Codex agent patterns
new MCP servers
new coding agents
new open-weight models
new browser execution providers
new workflow/integration tools
```

### Archive / Retired

Capabilities that are stale, underperforming, too expensive, too risky, superseded, or no longer aligned with West Peek standards.

Canonical law:

```text
No capability becomes permanent default by habit.
Defaults are earned and re-earned.
```

## 67.5 Capability Status Model

Every capability must be assigned one of the following statuses:

```text
Discovered
Watched
Bench Testing
Approved-Limited
Approved-Production
Rejected
Blocked
Deprecated
Retired
```

No capability may move into Approved-Production without:

1. purpose fit;
2. data sensitivity review;
3. cost review;
4. approval compatibility;
5. observability compatibility;
6. failure-mode review;
7. rollback or replacement path;
8. MP or authorized admin approval where required.

## 67.6 Smallest Effective Stack Law

West Peek OS must not combine every available tool, skill, model, vendor, workflow, and framework by default.

For each serious job, West Peek OS selects a capability stack:

```text
Lead Capability
Support Capability
Verifier / Reviewer
Fallback
```

Canonical law:

```text
The best capability stack is the smallest stack that reliably meets the required standard.
```

Supporting law:

```text
Do not combine tools to feel sophisticated.
Combine only when each added capability performs a distinct necessary function.
```

## 67.7 Capability Authority Boundary

No capability may override:

```text
Managing Partner authority
source documents
firm compliance boundaries
Investment Committee standards
LP / fund communication standards
relationship intelligence authority
memory promotion rules
approval queues
cost governor
audit ledger
AI employee lifecycle controls
vendor adapter boundaries
model routing rules
truth / evidence requirements
```

A capability may help produce a draft, run a workflow, research a source, generate code, launch a pilot agent, or validate an artifact.

It may not become the firm.

## 67.8 Capability Selection Questions

Before serious execution, West Peek OS should determine:

```text
Do we already have an approved capability for this job?
Is the current Active Default still strong enough?
Has it failed recently?
Is there a Bench candidate with better evidence?
Does the task justify a capability search before execution?
What is the data classification?
What is the cost ceiling?
What approval gate applies?
What observability trace is required?
What verifier or hostile reviewer is required?
What fallback exists if the selected capability fails?
```

Selection rule:

```text
Use Active Default if still strong.
Use Bench Candidate only when evidence or task fit justifies it.
Use Fallback if the active default fails.
Search only when no known capability is good enough, cost is too high, a new link/tool is supplied, or the review cadence requires it.
```

## 67.9 Capability Review Cadence

West Peek OS should review capabilities on a governed cadence.

```text
Monthly:
scan for major new capabilities relevant to active firm work.

Quarterly:
review active defaults by job type.

On-demand:
review when a capability fails, a task is high-risk/high-value, cost pressure rises, benchmark data suggests a better option, or an MP supplies a new tool/link/reference.
```

Canonical law:

```text
No nonstop tool-chasing.
Capability search is triggered by review cadence, failure, cost pressure, benchmark signal, new task need, or MP-provided source.
```

## 67.10 Capability After-Action Discipline

After a serious capability-assisted execution, West Peek OS should record whether the selected stack worked.

After-action fields include:

```text
task
capability_stack_used
model_backend_used
vendor_or_adapter_used
outcome_quality
cost
latency
human_corrections_required
failure_modes
missing_instructions
observability_trace_id
suggested_patch
promotion_recommendation
retirement_recommendation
```

Capability improvement law:

```text
West Peek OS does not silently rewrite critical capabilities.
It proposes improvements, versions them, tests them, and promotes them with approval.
```

## 67.11 Reference Repositories and Agent Launch Templates

Reference repositories, including Claude Managed Agent launch references, are capabilities, not authorities.

They may be used to extract:

```text
agent launch workflow patterns
agent build sheet fields
definition-of-done templates
eval scaffold requirements
wrap-up reports
next-direction plans
scheduled-task gate patterns
```

They may not become:

```text
West Peek OS brain
default runtime
canonical governance
approval authority
source of truth
replacement for AI Employee Registry
replacement for Cloudflare-first owned runtime exploration
replacement for firm-owned observability, cost, and audit layers
```

## 67.12 Final Capability Intelligence Law

```text
West Peek OS does not adopt capabilities by hype.
West Peek OS does not preserve old capabilities by inertia.
West Peek OS compares, tests, governs, promotes, and retires capabilities.

The institution owns the standard.
Capabilities compete to serve the standard.
```

---

# 68. MOBILE-FIRST COMMAND + CLOUD EXECUTION ARCHITECTURE

Canonical section label: Mobile-First Command + Cloud Execution Architecture

## 68.1 Purpose

West Peek OS must be usable as a real firm operating system when the managing partners are not sitting at an open laptop.

The target operating posture is:

```text
iPhone / mobile web app = command surface and approval cockpit
Cloud runtime = orchestration and execution engine
Laptop = optional workstation, local validation machine, private-file node, and fallback environment
Dedicated isolated runtime = high-risk or special-purpose execution only
```

This section does not make any specific cloud provider permanently canonical. It adds the durable law that West Peek OS must be mobile-commandable, cloud-executable, approval-governed, evidence-preserving, and laptop-independent for routine firm work.

## 68.2 Laptop Is Not the System Law

```text
The laptop is not West Peek OS.
The laptop is a workstation.
```

The laptop may be required for:

```text
local repo validation
local browser proof
private local files
local-only credentials
manual debugging
private/local model inference
source packaging
local development workflows
fallback continuity mode
```

The laptop must not be required for routine:

```text
task intake
approval review
AI employee routing
scheduled jobs
document jobs
research jobs
browser jobs
meeting prep
relationship intelligence
sponsor workflow review
LP prep
artifact review
notifications
audit and cost logging
```

Canonical law:

```text
Routine firm execution must not depend on a partner laptop being awake.
```

## 68.3 Mobile Command UI Law

West Peek OS must support a mobile-first command surface.

The mobile surface must allow authorized partners and operators to:

```text
submit tasks
review work cards
approve / edit / reject actions
review artifacts
inspect evidence
view risk cards
view cost cards
view AI employee status
view approval inbox
receive critical notifications
inspect mobile-safe redaction and permission envelopes
```

Initial product direction:

```text
Phase 1: responsive mobile-first web app
Phase 2: installable iPhone PWA
Phase 3: native iOS app only if needed
```

Native iOS may become useful later for:

```text
Face ID approvals
secure device-local vaults
critical push notifications
Apple Shortcuts
home-screen widgets
offline capture
deeper notification controls
```

But v1 should not require App Store-native delivery before the firm can operate.

## 68.4 Cloud Orchestration Runtime Law

West Peek OS requires an always-available cloud orchestration runtime for routine execution.

The cloud runtime owns:

```text
task intake
assignment queue
work queue
approval state
agent routing
capability routing
scheduled jobs
background jobs
evidence storage
audit logging
cost logging
notification dispatch
artifact storage
worker coordination
```

Cloud runtime does not own:

```text
MP authority
canonical documents
compliance judgment
source-of-truth authority
approval override authority
relationship intelligence final truth
IC or LP decision authority
memory promotion authority
```

Cloudflare-first owned runtime remains the preferred candidate posture until contradicted by implementation validation, but canonical law is provider-agnostic.

## 68.5 Runtime Layer Model

West Peek OS should be designed around four primary runtime layers:

```text
1. Mobile Command UI
2. Cloud Orchestration Runtime
3. Execution Worker Layer
4. Evidence + Approval Layer
```

Expanded flow:

```text
Mobile Command UI
↓
Cloud Orchestration Runtime
↓
Capability / Agent / Worker Router
↓
Execution Workers
↓
Evidence + Approval Layer
↓
Audit / Memory / Cost / Lifecycle Ledgers
```

## 68.6 Execution Worker Layer Law

Execution workers are interchangeable capabilities, not authorities.

Worker categories may include:

```text
owned West Peek AI employees
model API workers
repo/code workers
document compiler workers
research/extraction workers
browser execution workers
workflow automation workers
human helper workers
external hosted-agent workers
```

Every worker remains governed by:

```text
data classification
capability selection
authority envelope
approval rules
cost governor
observability traces
audit ledger
memory promotion rules
lifecycle controls
```

## 68.7 Non-Repo Work Parity Law

Repo work is one execution lane.

It is not the whole West Peek OS architecture.

Non-repo work must use the same governed operating spine:

```text
Partner command
↓
Task intake
↓
Intent-to-Execution Intelligence
↓
Universal Tier-1 Work Packet
↓
Capability Stack Selector
↓
Permission / data / cost envelope
↓
Worker execution
↓
Draft / artifact / evidence
↓
Approval if needed
↓
Audit + memory promotion decision
```

This applies to:

```text
LP meeting prep
sponsor target research
West Peek Rooms partner lists
portfolio support briefs
founder diligence memos
relationship intelligence updates
email drafts
calendar prep
event ops checklists
market maps
document compilation
data cleanup
dashboard review
vendor review
```

## 68.8 Protected Self-Modification Law

West Peek OS may assist with its own repo, product, documentation, and runtime evolution only through a protected self-modification lane.

Rules:

```text
No silent self-editing.
Separate branch or isolated work area required.
Diff summary required.
Artifact package required when source changes are delivered.
Validation status required.
Rollback path required.
Approval required before merge, deploy, or authority change.
System-law changes require explicit MP/admin approval.
No self-expansion of permissions.
No silent edits to approval, memory, cost, compliance, lifecycle, or security rules.
```

West Peek OS may propose and prepare changes.

It may not quietly alter its own authority.

## 68.9 Mobile Policy Airlock Law

Mobile can supervise an airlock.

Mobile is not the full private compute engine.

Mobile may support:

```text
approval review
redaction review
permission envelope approval
external model warning
result inspection
approve / reject / edit decisions
```

The strongest private compute airlock requires one of:

```text
laptop
private server
secure cloud / VPC
Mac Studio or other local hardware
local/private model runtime
```

Canonical law:

```text
iPhone may supervise the policy airlock.
iPhone is not the private compute engine.
```

## 68.10 High-Risk Runtime Isolation Law

High-risk financial, trading, regulated, production-critical, or highly confidential execution must use isolated runtime boundaries and may not casually share infrastructure with general firm-agent work.

West Peek-specific high-risk data includes:

```text
LP data
founder data
secondaries deal data
brokerage communications
fund materials
MNPI-sensitive context
private diligence materials
regulated or compliance-sensitive correspondence
```

General West Peek OS runtime must remain separated from any dedicated trading, brokerage-sensitive, regulated, or high-risk financial runtime unless explicitly approved by governance.

## 68.11 Final Mobile-Cloud Law

```text
West Peek OS is a mobile-commandable firm operating system.
It runs through an always-available cloud orchestration backend.
Agents and workers execute in approved cloud, GitHub, browser, workflow, local, or dedicated runtime environments.
The laptop is a workstation, fallback, private-compute node, and local-validation machine.
It is not the system.
```

# 69. Legal + Compliance AI Adapter Governance

## 69.1 Purpose

West Peek OS must distinguish legal-work support from compliance-heavy workflow supervision. Legal and compliance tools are capability adapters, not institutional authorities. The system may route work to specialized legal or compliance AI platforms only after the work packet has been classified, data sensitivity has been checked, approval requirements have been applied, and the evidence/audit layer has been prepared.

The approved operating rule is:

```text
Use Harvey when legal work is involved.
Bring in Norm AI only for compliance-heavy workflows.
```

This rule is a routing default, not a delegation of legal authority.

## 69.2 Vendor Classification

| Vendor | Canonical Classification | Default Status | Primary Lane | Not Allowed To Become |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Harvey | Legal Workbench / Legal AI Adapter Candidate | Bench, legal-work preferred | legal drafting support, legal research support, legal document analysis, contract intelligence, diligence support, transaction support, fund-formation support | legal counsel, MP authority, compliance officer, investment decision-maker, source-of-truth authority, approval authority |
| Norm AI | Compliance + Supervisory AI / Regulatory Workflow Adapter Candidate | Bench, compliance-heavy reserved | regulatory mapping, compliance-heavy workflow review, supervisory AI for agents operating under law, policy-to-workflow translation, risk posture encoding | legal counsel by default, MP authority, final compliance officer, source-of-truth authority, approval authority |

## 69.3 Harvey Legal Workbench Routing Law

Harvey is the preferred legal-work AI adapter candidate when a West Peek work packet involves legal drafting, legal research, contract review, transaction support, fund formation support, due diligence, legal document analysis, legal issue spotting, or counsel-review preparation.

Harvey may be considered for:

```text
NDA review packets
side letter issue spotting
fund formation support packets
subscription-document support packets
LP document review preparation
sponsor agreement draft support
contract clause extraction
transaction diligence summaries
legal memo first-draft support
legal document comparison tables
legal research support
counsel-review packet preparation
```

Harvey output status must remain:

```text
draft
issue-spotting aid
legal-work support packet
counsel-review packet
evidence assistant output
not final legal advice
```

## 69.4 Norm AI Compliance + Supervisory Routing Law

Norm AI is reserved for compliance-heavy, regulatory, supervisory, policy, rule-translation, compliance-monitoring, and AI-agent-governance workflows. It should be brought into the bench only when the work is meaningfully compliance-heavy or when an AI employee, workflow, public output, fund material, brokerage-sensitive communication, or regulated process needs explicit compliance supervision.

Norm AI may be considered for:

```text
fund marketing compliance screen
LP material compliance checklist
brokerage communication compliance review
MNPI-sensitive workflow review
regulated communication issue spotting
AI employee compliance supervision
policy-to-workflow translation
sponsor disclosure compliance review
external claims compliance checklist
regulatory rule mapping
compliance-heavy workflow audit packet
```

Norm output status must remain:

```text
compliance-support aid
supervisory review packet
risk-screening packet
policy-to-workflow aid
human-review-required output when regulated or external-facing
not final legal advice unless routed through licensed counsel outside West Peek OS
```

## 69.5 Legal + Compliance Overlap Rule

When work contains both legal-work and compliance-heavy elements, West Peek OS must not collapse the distinction.

Routing rule:

```text
Legal work product -> Harvey-first candidate.
Compliance-heavy supervision -> Norm AI candidate.
External-facing regulated or reputation-sensitive action -> human approval required.
```

Examples:

| Work Packet | Legal Adapter | Compliance Adapter | Human Review |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sponsor agreement draft | Harvey | Norm only if disclosure/compliance obligations are central | required before external use |
| LP deck compliance screen | Harvey only for legal drafting questions | Norm | required |
| Brokerage email review | Harvey only for legal language support | Norm | registered-person / compliance review required |
| Fund formation question | Harvey | Norm if regulatory operating workflow is implicated | counsel review required |
| AI employee external communication policy | Harvey for legal drafting support | Norm for supervisory policy/workflow | MP/admin approval required |

## 69.6 Authority Boundary

Harvey and Norm may support work. They do not approve work.

They may not override:

```text
source documents
MP authority
firm strategy
approval queues
legal/compliance gates
data classification
brokerage rules
MNPI controls
LP communication controls
human counsel review
registered-person review
source-of-truth records
audit/event logs
Cost Governor decisions
```

No Harvey or Norm output may be treated as final legal advice, final compliance approval, final business decision, or final external-facing communication.

## 69.7 Data Classification Gate

Before routing any work packet to Harvey or Norm, West Peek OS must classify data sensitivity.

Required classification checks:

```text
Does this packet include LP data?
Does this packet include founder data?
Does this packet include secondaries deal data?
Does this packet include brokerage communications?
Does this packet include MNPI-sensitive material?
Does this packet include confidential fund materials?
Does this packet include private diligence materials?
Does this packet include credentials or secrets?
Does this packet include personal data or regulated data?
```

If yes, routing requires an approved data boundary and explicit permission envelope before vendor use.

## 69.8 Legal / Compliance Adapter Selection Flow

```text
MP or AI employee intent
-> Intent-to-Execution Intelligence Layer
-> Universal Tier-1 Work Packet
-> data classification
-> legal-work classification
-> compliance-heavy classification
-> Harvey / Norm / both / neither recommendation
-> human approval requirement check
-> Cost Governor check
-> approved adapter execution
-> evidence packet returned
-> approval gate before external action
-> audit and cost ledgers updated
```

## 69.9 Prohibited Use

West Peek OS may not:

```text
send sensitive data to Harvey or Norm without data classification approval;
treat Harvey as West Peek legal counsel;
treat Norm AI as West Peek legal counsel by default;
treat vendor output as legal advice;
treat vendor output as compliance approval;
use vendor output to bypass MP approval;
use vendor output to bypass counsel review;
use vendor output to bypass registered-person review;
use vendor output to approve LP/fund/brokerage/sponsor/external communications;
allow a legal/compliance vendor to alter source documents, approval rules, memory, policy, or governance without explicit authorization;
allow any vendor to become the canonical Legal/Compliance OS authority.
```

## 69.10 Machine and Domain Impact

This update strengthens the following domains and machines without creating unnecessary machine bloat:

```text
Legal/Compliance OS
Governance OS
Investment OS
Fundraising / LP OS
Brokerage-Sensitive Runtime
Brand / Marketing OS
Event OS / West Peek Rooms sponsorship lane
Machine #42 — Prompt Enhancer + Intent-to-Execution Machine
Capability Intelligence / Adapter Selection layer
Cost Dashboard / Budget Governor
Observability + Evaluation layer
```

## 69.11 Canonical Routing Summary

```text
Harvey is the legal-work AI adapter candidate.
Norm AI is the compliance-heavy / supervisory AI adapter candidate.
Both are subordinate to West Peek OS governance.
Neither is final authority.
Human review governs regulated, external, fund, brokerage, LP, legal, and compliance-sensitive work.
```

## 69.12 Acceptance Criteria

This canonical update is satisfied when future implementation defines:

```text
LegalComplianceAdapterSelectionRun object
Harvey Legal Workbench Adapter capability card
Norm AI Compliance + Supervisory Adapter capability card
routing matrix for legal vs compliance-heavy work
data classification checks before vendor routing
human review requirement flags
output status labels that prevent final-advice claims
audit ledger events for Harvey/Norm usage
Cost Governor tracking for Harvey/Norm usage
observability trace for adapter selection and output evaluation
tests proving legal/compliance vendor output cannot bypass approval gates
```
