# FUND AI OPERATING MODEL — HYBRID FUND OPERATIONS, HUMAN AUTHORITY, AND CROSS-SLEEVE INTELLIGENCE

## 1. Purpose

This section establishes the canonical operating contract for what AI does for West Peek Ventures as a hybrid venture fund and what remains reserved to the Managing Partners, authorized investment professionals, counsel, compliance personnel, fund administrator, accountants, auditors, and other designated human authorities.

West Peek is not designing a generic CRM with AI features. West Peek is designing an institutional operating system for a hybrid venture platform that may combine:

- early-stage primary investing;
- follow-on investing;
- concentrated reserve deployment into proven winners;
- secondary purchases;
- secondary sales or liquidity decisions;
- portfolio support;
- LP fundraising and reporting;
- fund administration coordination;
- relationship and market intelligence;
- firmwide learning and decision calibration.

The system must reduce administrative exhaustion without transferring fiduciary, investment, legal, compliance, banking, or relationship authority to AI.

Canonical law:

```text
AI prepares the decision.
The Managing Partners and authorized humans make the decision.
```

AI exists to protect partner judgment from avoidable operational drag. AI does not become an unaccountable shadow partner.

---

## 2. Four Authorized AI Work Categories

Across the fund, AI may perform four broad categories of work.

### 2.1 Information Intake

AI may:

- ingest approved emails, decks, documents, data-room files, meeting notes, portfolio updates, provider data, and structured forms;
- extract entities, facts, dates, metrics, terms, claims, commitments, and missing fields;
- normalize inconsistent source formats into governed records;
- detect duplicate records and identity conflicts;
- classify data sensitivity and source provenance;
- create evidence-linked intake packets;
- route unresolved identity or source conflicts for human review.

Information intake does not authorize AI to treat every extracted statement as true. Extracted statements retain their source, status, date, and confidence.

### 2.2 Repeatable Analysis

AI may:

- calculate and compare configured financial scenarios;
- compare documents and identify contradictions;
- identify missing diligence evidence;
- calculate period-over-period portfolio changes;
- prepare return, dilution, ownership, reserve, and concentration scenarios;
- compare early-stage, follow-on, and secondary capital-allocation options;
- reconcile internal records against official provider or administrator records;
- generate risk, exception, and unresolved-question reports;
- produce recommendations under explicit assumptions.

Repeatable analysis remains decision support. AI-generated calculations must identify formulas, inputs, assumptions, source dates, and validation status.

### 2.3 Workflow Coordination

AI may:

- create WorkCards;
- assign approved owners;
- route tasks to machines and AI employees;
- track deadlines, promised follow-ups, missing documents, stale opportunities, and unresolved issues;
- prepare approval cards;
- coordinate recurring monitoring loops;
- update workflow status after evidence-backed completion;
- generate alerts and escalation packets;
- prepare handoffs among investment, portfolio, finance, legal, compliance, LP, event, and relationship functions.

Workflow coordination does not permit AI to expand its own authority, create new binding obligations, or bypass approval gates.

### 2.4 First-Draft Production

AI may produce first drafts of:

- diligence requests;
- meeting briefs;
- investment memoranda;
- IC packets;
- portfolio monitoring summaries;
- support plans;
- introduction drafts;
- follow-on analyses;
- secondary transaction comparisons;
- quarterly letters;
- portfolio summaries;
- valuation commentary;
- deployment and reserve summaries;
- annual meeting materials;
- legal review packets;
- compliance review packets;
- fund-administration reconciliation reports.

A first draft is not final authority. External use, legal reliance, investment approval, compliance approval, banking action, LP commitment, or public representation requires the appropriate human review and approval.

---

## 3. Human Judgment and Reserved Authority Register

The following actions and judgments remain human-reserved unless a future approved canonical update establishes a narrower, legally permitted authority envelope.

### 3.1 Investment and Capital Authority

AI may not independently:

- approve or reject an investment;
- determine final conviction;
- make final founder-character judgments;
- interpret reference calls as final truth;
- approve valuation;
- approve scenario probabilities;
- approve ownership targets;
- approve reserve allocations;
- approve capital allocation across sleeves;
- approve follow-on participation;
- approve a secondary purchase or sale;
- approve a partial or full exit;
- change the fund strategy;
- change sleeve targets;
- change concentration limits;
- move or commit capital;
- initiate or authorize wire instructions.

### 3.2 Legal and Compliance Authority

AI may not independently:

- issue final legal conclusions;
- act as legal counsel;
- act as the compliance officer;
- approve fund or brokerage communications;
- resolve conflicts of interest;
- determine MNPI treatment;
- approve regulatory interpretations;
- approve sponsor disclosures;
- approve legal documents for execution;
- approve LP marketing claims;
- waive required counsel, compliance, or registered-person review.

Harvey may support legal work. Norm AI may support compliance-heavy and supervisory workflows. Human legal and compliance authority remains final.

### 3.3 LP, Banking, and Fund Administration Authority

AI may not independently:

- make promises to LPs;
- commit fund terms;
- send sensitive LP communications without approval;
- issue capital calls;
- approve distributions;
- change bank accounts;
- approve payment instructions;
- change official valuations or capital accounts;
- override the fund administrator, accountant, auditor, or authorized finance operator;
- certify financial statements or performance metrics.

### 3.4 Relationship and Public Authority

AI may not independently:

- make relationship-sensitive introductions;
- contact founders, LPs, brokers, buyers, sellers, sponsors, or portfolio companies without authority;
- make material public statements;
- promise access, introductions, capital, liquidity, or outcomes;
- provide portfolio-company termination advice as final guidance;
- impersonate a Managing Partner.

Canonical rule:

```text
AI may prepare, extract, compare, calculate, draft, flag, recommend, monitor, route, and record.
AI may not independently approve, commit, promise, wire, call capital, change legal position, change fund strategy, or make final investment decisions.
```

---

## 4. Hybrid Fund Sleeve Architecture

West Peek may operate a hybrid fund strategy with separate early-stage and secondaries sleeves. Current planning examples may use a 60% early-stage / 40% secondaries allocation and a $30 million target fund, but these values are configurable planning assumptions, not immutable canonical facts.

### 4.1 Configurable Fund Parameters

The system must support configurable fields for:

- target fund size;
- committed capital;
- investable capital;
- management-fee assumptions;
- early-stage sleeve target;
- secondaries sleeve target;
- initial-check allocation;
- follow-on reserve allocation;
- recycling assumptions;
- target company count;
- target check-size ranges;
- ownership targets;
- concentration limits;
- reserve policy;
- deployment pace;
- liquidity assumptions;
- fund-life assumptions.

Illustrative scenarios must be labeled as scenarios. They must not silently become policy.

### 4.2 Early-Stage Sleeve Purpose

The early-stage sleeve may seek to:

- acquire ownership before major value creation;
- source asymmetric upside;
- establish durable founder relationships;
- obtain pro-rata or follow-on rights;
- create access to company and market intelligence;
- build future portfolio-support and secondary-opportunity pathways.

### 4.3 Secondaries Sleeve Purpose

The secondaries sleeve may seek to:

- acquire exposure to more mature private companies;
- underwrite with greater operating evidence;
- exploit pricing dislocations;
- reduce duration risk relative to earlier-stage exposure;
- create a differentiated liquidity profile;
- use West Peek relationship and market intelligence to identify attractive blocks.

### 4.4 Separate Underwriting Standards, Shared Institutional Truth

Canonical law:

```text
Separate underwriting standards.
Shared institutional truth.
Shared capital-allocation framework.
```

Early-stage underwriting should emphasize:

- founder quality and founder-market fit;
- market formation;
- product insight;
- early traction and retention quality;
- go-to-market and distribution;
- ownership potential;
- dilution and follow-on rights;
- venture-scale outcome potential;
- power-law upside;
- the fund's ability to support the company.

Secondary underwriting should emphasize:

- company maturity and operating evidence;
- price, discount, or premium;
- share class and security rights;
- transfer restrictions and ROFR risk;
- information rights;
- block quality and seller motivation;
- buyer demand and market depth;
- liquidity horizon and catalysts;
- comparable pricing;
- downside protection;
- documentation sufficiency.

Both sleeves must feed one fund-level view of:

- company identity;
- exposure;
- ownership;
- reserves;
- concentration;
- deployment pace;
- liquidity;
- return contribution;
- risk;
- opportunity cost.

---

## 5. One Canonical Company, Many Transactions Law

There must be one canonical Company entity for each company.

A company may have many related records, including:

```text
Company
├── Founders and People
├── Organizations and Relationships
├── Opportunities
│   ├── Early-Stage Primary
│   ├── Follow-On
│   ├── Secondary Purchase
│   └── Secondary Sale
├── Transactions
├── Positions
├── Ownership History
├── Valuation and Pricing History
├── Diligence Claims and Evidence
├── IC Decisions
├── Portfolio Updates
├── Support Requests and Outcomes
├── Meetings and Commitments
├── Documents
└── LP-Safe Reporting Evidence
```

The same company must not exist as separate canonical records in:

- the early-stage pipeline;
- the secondary pipeline;
- the portfolio tracker;
- Network OS;
- the CRM;
- IC files;
- research records;
- LP reporting.

Different machines and domains may present different views, but all views must resolve to one canonical company identity.

Duplicate detection must distinguish:

- true duplicate company records;
- multiple opportunities for the same company;
- multiple transactions in the same company;
- multiple share classes;
- multiple broker blocks;
- historical names or aliases;
- affiliated but distinct legal entities.

No deduplication routine may collapse distinct economics, share classes, sellers, transaction terms, or legal entities merely because the issuer is the same.

---

## 6. Fund AI Operating Map

The complete fund flow is:

```text
Deal Intake and Pipeline Machine
↓
Diligence and Research Machine
↓
Meeting Intelligence and Walter Sidecar
↓
Investment Memo and IC Preparation
↓
IC Decision Machine
↓
Transaction / Closing Workflow
↓
Portfolio Monitoring and Support Machine
↓
Follow-On / Secondary / Reserve Decision Center
↓
Fund Construction and Cross-Sleeve Capital Allocation Machine
↓
LP Reporting and Fund Administration Machine
↓
Learning, Calibration, and Institutional Memory Loop
```

Shared across the entire flow:

- Canonical Company Record;
- Network OS;
- Research Evidence Layer;
- Meeting Intelligence;
- VentureDeals / Deal Math Packet;
- Legal and Compliance OS;
- Human Reserved Authority Register;
- Approval Center;
- Cost Governor;
- Audit Ledger;
- Knowledge and Memory Promotion;
- Agent Observability and Evals;
- Source-of-Truth Resolver.

No machine may create a private duplicate record when a governed shared entity already exists.

---

## 7. Deal Intake and Pipeline AI Duties

For each inbound opportunity, AI should perform an approved first normalization pass.

### 7.1 Intake Inputs

Inputs may include:

- email;
- referral or introduction;
- founder submission;
- broker message;
- deck;
- data-room link;
- meeting note;
- Network OS relationship signal;
- event or community signal;
- provider or research signal.

### 7.2 Required AI Intake Actions

AI should:

- resolve or propose the canonical company identity;
- extract company name, sector, stage, round size, valuation, traction, geography, and source;
- identify whether the opportunity is primary, follow-on, secondary purchase, secondary sale, or other;
- extract seller, buyer, broker, share class, block size, and pricing terms where applicable;
- classify thesis and mandate fit;
- identify missing information;
- identify duplicate or related opportunities;
- create the governed opportunity record;
- attach source evidence;
- assign the next action;
- draft the first diligence request when appropriate;
- start staleness and follow-up monitoring.

### 7.3 Deal Card Minimum Output

A normalized deal card should show:

- canonical company;
- opportunity type;
- source and relationship path;
- stage;
- round or block size;
- valuation or implied valuation;
- key traction;
- current ownership where relevant;
- thesis fit;
- primary risks;
- missing information;
- evidence status;
- next action;
- assigned human and AI owners;
- approval requirements.

AI makes the opportunity legible. It does not decide whether West Peek invests.

---

## 8. Diligence Reconciliation and Contradiction Function

Diligence AI must do more than summarize documents. It must reconcile claims across evidence.

### 8.1 Diligence Sources

The function may compare:

- pitch decks;
- financial models;
- cap tables;
- customer lists;
- cohort reports;
- contracts;
- product materials;
- data-room documents;
- prior meeting notes;
- founder statements;
- provider data;
- public research;
- reference calls;
- legal documents;
- portfolio or transaction records.

### 8.2 Required Outputs

AI should produce:

- verified claims;
- founder-stated claims;
- third-party claims;
- AI-inferred claims;
- unverified claims;
- missing evidence;
- direct contradictions;
- numerical inconsistencies;
- date or period mismatches;
- definition mismatches;
- version conflicts;
- questions required to reconcile the record;
- source-linked diligence gaps;
- change log from prior diligence rounds.

### 8.3 Contradiction Record

Each material contradiction should retain:

- claim topic;
- source A statement;
- source B statement;
- additional conflicting sources;
- metric definitions;
- reporting periods;
- source dates;
- confidence;
- materiality;
- assigned owner;
- required question;
- resolution status;
- resolution evidence;
- human disposition.

Example:

```text
Metric: Gross retention
Deck: 95%
Data room: 82%
Founder note: “minimal churn”
Status: Unresolved contradiction
Required action: verify metric definition, measurement period, and authoritative source.
```

AI may flag the contradiction. The investment team determines whether the explanation is credible and whether the risk is acceptable.

---

## 9. Meeting Intelligence Operating Contract

### 9.1 Before the Meeting

AI should prepare:

- one-page meeting brief;
- canonical company and person identity;
- prior interaction summary;
- open questions;
- unresolved diligence contradictions;
- commitments and promised follow-ups;
- relationship context;
- relevant fund exposure;
- the three highest-value questions;
- legal, compliance, confidentiality, or MNPI warnings where applicable.

### 9.2 During the Meeting

Approved meeting intelligence may capture:

- key facts;
- claims requiring verification;
- commitments;
- questions and answers;
- objections;
- contradictions;
- changes to the investment thesis;
- support requests;
- next steps;
- decision signals;
- relationship signals.

### 9.3 After the Meeting

AI should prepare:

- meeting summary;
- decisions and non-decisions;
- commitments by party;
- WorkCards;
- follow-up draft;
- diligence record updates;
- thesis-change summary;
- Network OS relationship updates;
- memory-promotion candidates;
- evidence and approval packet.

No meeting record becomes institutional truth without source, provenance, correction rights, and the applicable memory or record-promotion rules.

---

## 10. Investment Memo and IC AI Duties

AI may draft the first investment memo from structured, cited evidence.

### 10.1 Memo Coverage

The first draft may include:

- company overview;
- founders and team;
- market;
- product and technology;
- traction and customer evidence;
- distribution and go-to-market;
- business model and unit economics;
- competition and moat;
- financing history;
- cap table;
- ownership and dilution;
- return scenarios;
- fund-construction impact;
- key risks;
- contradictions;
- unanswered questions;
- strongest case;
- strongest counterargument;
- recommendation framing.

### 10.2 Recommendation Boundary

Canonical law:

```text
AI may draft the investment memo and recommendation framing from structured evidence.
AI may not own or manufacture the final recommendation.
```

The authorized human investment decision-makers must enter, edit, or affirm:

- final recommendation;
- conviction;
- founder assessment;
- scenario probabilities;
- valuation judgment;
- risk acceptance;
- conflicts disposition;
- final IC rationale;
- decision and conditions.

Any AI-generated return statement must show the assumptions that produce it. A partner must decide whether those assumptions are reasonable.

---

## 11. Portfolio Monitoring and Alert Loop

AI should operate a recurring portfolio-monitoring loop for the portfolio company count approved by the fund strategy.

### 11.1 Portfolio Metric Record

Each company may track:

- revenue;
- growth;
- burn;
- cash;
- runway;
- headcount;
- gross margin;
- retention;
- pipeline;
- customer concentration;
- financing status;
- hiring needs;
- material risks;
- requested introductions;
- board dates;
- milestones;
- forecast versus actual;
- support requests;
- potential secondary opportunities.

### 11.2 Required Delta Analysis

For each material metric, AI should calculate or display:

- current value;
- prior-period value;
- absolute change;
- percentage change;
- target variance;
- trend;
- source date;
- confidence;
- alert severity;
- recommended human action.

### 11.3 Portfolio Alerts

The system should support configured alerts for:

- runway deterioration;
- growth deceleration;
- margin degradation;
- retention deterioration;
- customer concentration increase;
- delayed fundraising;
- missed milestones;
- financing expected within 90 or 180 days;
- material hiring need;
- emerging winner signals;
- support-request urgency;
- potential secondary opportunity;
- stale or missing company data.

Alerts are decision-preparation packets. They do not authorize intervention, follow-on investment, or external communication.

### 11.4 Capacity Law

AI does not make 30 companies equivalent to 10 by magic. AI expands operating capacity only when:

- updates arrive in structured or ingestible form;
- one canonical company record exists;
- monitoring rules are explicit;
- alert thresholds are configurable;
- support requests are routed;
- partner review bandwidth is respected;
- low-value administrative work is automated;
- high-value founder and investment judgment remains human.

The system must track review burden and alert quality so automation does not create a larger supervision problem.

---

## 12. Portfolio Support Routing and Outcome Measurement

AI may maintain a structured portfolio-support request queue.

### 12.1 Support Request Types

Requests may include:

- customer introductions;
- hiring support;
- capital introductions;
- vendor recommendations;
- product feedback;
- strategic introductions;
- board preparation;
- marketing support;
- legal or compliance referrals;
- event or community support.

### 12.2 Support Workflow

For each request, AI may:

- classify request type and urgency;
- identify the desired outcome;
- match the request against Network OS and approved resources;
- suggest contacts;
- identify relationship risks;
- draft introduction language;
- prepare the human approval card;
- track whether the introduction or support occurred;
- request outcome feedback;
- record value created and relationship impact.

Required lifecycle:

```text
Requested
→ Classified
→ Suggested Match
→ Human Match Approval
→ Introduction / Support Draft
→ Sent or Delivered
→ Response Received
→ Outcome Recorded
→ Value and Relationship Impact Reviewed
```

Number of introductions is not the primary KPI. The system should measure useful outcomes, founder satisfaction, relationship impact, time to resolution, and whether the support changed a meaningful company outcome.

---

## 13. Cross-Sleeve Capital Allocation Engine

The early-stage and secondaries sleeves must not operate as isolated capital worlds.

The Cross-Sleeve Capital Allocation Engine prepares comparable options across:

- skip;
- initial early-stage check;
- pro rata;
- super pro rata;
- primary follow-on;
- secondary purchase;
- partial secondary sale;
- full exit;
- reserve retention;
- capital held back;
- reallocation within approved policy.

### 13.1 Required Comparison Fields

Each option should compare:

- capital required;
- current and projected ownership;
- diluted ownership;
- reserve assigned;
- reserve consumed;
- sleeve capacity;
- total fund capacity;
- current valuation;
- entry price or secondary price;
- discount or premium;
- expected exit values;
- incremental MOIC;
- incremental IRR;
- liquidity duration;
- downside cases;
- concentration;
- correlation and exposure;
- information quality;
- rights and restrictions;
- opportunity cost;
- expected contribution to the fund;
- confidence and unresolved assumptions.

### 13.2 Decision Boundary

The engine may rank or compare options under approved scoring and scenario assumptions. It may not approve the allocation.

Any change to:

- reserve policy;
- sleeve targets;
- concentration limits;
- decision weights;
- capital authority;
- investment mandate;

requires the appropriate human approval and governance update.

### 13.3 Same-Company Cross-Sleeve View

When West Peek has or considers multiple exposures to the same company, the system must show all primary, follow-on, secondary, and sale options together. It must not hide cross-sleeve concentration behind separate dashboards.

---

## 14. Secondary Opportunity Normalization and Duplicate-Block Detection

Secondary opportunities often arrive in inconsistent formats. AI should normalize them into a governed schema.

### 14.1 Normalized Fields

The record should support:

- canonical issuer;
- seller and seller type;
- broker or source;
- share class;
- price per share;
- implied valuation;
- last primary valuation;
- discount or premium;
- block size;
- minimum purchase;
- fees and carry;
- transaction structure;
- ROFR risk;
- transfer restrictions;
- information rights;
- expected liquidity window;
- available documentation;
- likely buyers;
- known bids and asks;
- source date;
- confidence;
- status.

### 14.2 Duplicate-Block Detection

The system should identify when:

- multiple brokers circulate the same block;
- one block is presented with different fees or economics;
- a block is split across intermediaries;
- the same seller appears through multiple sources;
- share classes differ;
- a new indication is actually an update to a prior opportunity.

The system must preserve distinct economics and provenance. Duplicate detection may link records but may not silently merge materially different offers.

### 14.3 Legal and Documentation Boundary

AI may extract and compare terms from SPAs, transfer agreements, subscription documents, fund documents, cap tables, share certificates, audit reports, and pricing schedules.

Canonical workflow:

```text
AI extracts and flags.
Counsel interprets.
Partners decide.
```

---

## 15. Private-Market Pricing History and Institutional Moat

The durable moat is not merely the use of AI.

Canonical law:

```text
West Peek's private-market intelligence moat comes from structured proprietary observations, provenance, outcomes, and retained institutional memory.
```

For each issuer, West Peek should retain where legally and contractually permitted:

- observed bid;
- observed ask;
- transaction or indication date;
- block size;
- share class;
- source and relationship provenance;
- actual versus indicated status;
- fees and carry;
- seller type;
- buyer appetite;
- transaction status;
- liquidity catalyst;
- eventual outcome;
- confidence and restrictions.

The system must distinguish:

- observed indication;
- verified executed transaction;
- broker representation;
- internal estimate;
- public financing data;
- stale information.

No private-market observation may be presented externally without applicable confidentiality, legal, compliance, and source-permission review.

---

## 16. LP Reporting and Fund Administration AI Duties

### 16.1 LP Reporting Drafts

AI may prepare first drafts of:

- quarterly letters;
- portfolio summaries;
- valuation commentary;
- deployment summaries;
- reserve summaries;
- concentration summaries;
- pipeline summaries;
- annual meeting materials;
- capital-account explanations;
- LP diligence response packets.

All numbers, claims, performance statements, valuations, and forward-looking statements require human and provider review before external distribution.

### 16.2 Fund Administration Reconciliation

AI may assist in reconciling:

- capital calls;
- distributions;
- ownership records;
- investment cost;
- management fees;
- fund expenses;
- portfolio valuations;
- DPI;
- TVPI;
- RVPI;
- concentration;
- deployment pace;
- reserve usage;
- official provider schedules.

Canonical law:

```text
AI drafts and reconciles.
The fund administrator, accountant, auditor, authorized financial operator, Managing Partners, counsel, and compliance personnel remain authoritative within their roles.
```

A discrepancy must create an exception record rather than silently overwrite either source.

Example:

```text
Portfolio schedule investment cost: $2.4M
Capital ledger investment cost: $2.1M
Difference: $300K
Status: Unresolved reconciliation exception
Action: investigate source documents and obtain authorized resolution.
```

---

## 17. Fund AI Employee Standing Duties

The following duties supplement the existing AI Employee Registry and machine assignments.

### 17.1 Deal and Investment Employees

Daily or triggered duties:

- normalize inbound opportunities;
- resolve company identity;
- summarize materials;
- identify missing information;
- identify stale deals;
- prepare meeting briefs;
- update diligence status;
- create contradiction reports;
- prepare IC packets;
- update Deal Math Packets;
- draft diligence requests;
- prepare pass-and-nurture recommendations;
- maintain source and evidence links.

### 17.2 Portfolio Employees

Daily, weekly, monthly, or triggered duties:

- ingest company updates;
- compare KPI periods;
- monitor runway and financing timing;
- identify support asks;
- track promises and follow-ups;
- surface deterioration and outperformance;
- prepare financing-readiness packets;
- prepare follow-on packets;
- prepare pro-rata reviews;
- prepare support plans;
- suggest introduction matches;
- flag potential secondary opportunities;
- record support outcomes.

### 17.3 Secondaries Employees

Daily or triggered duties:

- normalize broker opportunities;
- deduplicate blocks;
- preserve distinct economics;
- update issuer pricing history;
- track bids and asks;
- monitor ROFR and transfer risk;
- maintain documentation status;
- prepare secondary IC packets;
- compare transactions;
- flag seller, buyer, or source conflicts;
- identify pricing dislocations.

### 17.4 LP and Finance Employees

Recurring duties:

- prepare deployment and reserve summaries;
- draft reporting materials;
- reconcile provider records;
- detect discrepancies;
- prepare capital-call and distribution calendars for review;
- assemble LP diligence response packets;
- maintain evidence and approval status;
- track unresolved reconciliation items.

### 17.5 Legal and Compliance Employees

- route legal work to the Harvey bench candidate when approved;
- route compliance-heavy and supervisory work to the Norm AI bench candidate when approved;
- prepare human counsel, compliance, or registered-person review packets;
- enforce data classification and vendor-output quarantine;
- block final-authority claims;
- record review status and unresolved issues.

No AI employee may convert a standing duty into an expansion of authority.

---

## 18. Fund AI Learning and Calibration Loop

Every material fund workflow should improve institutional judgment without silently changing policy.

The learning loop should capture:

- original thesis;
- key assumptions;
- prediction;
- decision;
- decision rationale;
- actual outcome;
- timing;
- evidence quality;
- errors and omissions;
- false positives and false negatives;
- support outcomes;
- model and capability performance;
- human corrections;
- lessons;
- proposed rule or capability improvement.

AI may propose:

- diligence checklist improvements;
- alert-threshold adjustments;
- data-quality improvements;
- prompt or work-packet improvements;
- capability-stack changes;
- new contradiction checks;
- source-quality updates.

AI may not silently alter:

- the investment mandate;
- underwriting standards;
- sleeve policy;
- reserve policy;
- human reserved authority;
- legal or compliance rules;
- approval requirements.

Changes require versioning, evidence, review, and approval.

---

## 19. Fund AI UX Requirements

West Peek OS should provide the following coherent views rather than forcing operators to assemble the fund picture from unrelated screens.

### 19.1 Fund AI Command Map

Shows:

- current opportunities;
- diligence status;
- upcoming IC decisions;
- portfolio alerts;
- support requests;
- follow-on and secondary decisions;
- sleeve capacity;
- reserve status;
- LP and fund-administration exceptions;
- human-reserved approvals;
- cost and agent status.

### 19.2 Canonical Company 360

Shows all governed company records in one place:

- people and relationships;
- opportunities;
- transactions;
- positions;
- ownership;
- pricing history;
- diligence;
- IC decisions;
- portfolio updates;
- support requests;
- documents;
- LP-safe reporting evidence.

### 19.3 Cross-Sleeve Capital Allocation Center

Shows comparable initial, follow-on, reserve, secondary, and exit options under one fund-capacity model.

### 19.4 Portfolio Alert Center

Shows metric deltas, alert severity, evidence, recommended human action, and review status.

### 19.5 Diligence Contradiction View

Shows unresolved claim conflicts, source links, materiality, assigned owner, and resolution evidence.

### 19.6 Secondary Pricing History

Shows observed bids, asks, transactions, indications, source provenance, and restrictions.

### 19.7 Fund Reconciliation Exceptions

Shows discrepancies among internal records, official provider records, administrator schedules, and source documents.

### 19.8 Human Reserved Authority Queue

Shows actions that require human judgment or approval and prevents AI from representing those actions as complete.

---

## 20. Fund AI Acceptance Criteria

The Fund AI Operating Model is correctly represented in the system only when:

1. one canonical Company entity supports multiple opportunity and transaction types;
2. separate early-stage and secondary underwriting standards coexist with one fund-level allocation model;
3. AI work categories and human reserved authority are machine-readable and enforceable;
4. deal intake produces standardized, evidence-linked opportunity records;
5. material cross-document contradictions create unresolved records;
6. investment memos preserve evidence status and human-owned recommendations;
7. portfolio monitoring produces period-over-period deltas and configurable alerts;
8. support requests retain match approval and outcome measurement;
9. early-stage, follow-on, secondary, reserve, and exit options can be compared together;
10. duplicate secondary blocks are linked without collapsing distinct economics;
11. private-market pricing observations preserve provenance and status;
12. LP reporting remains draft until authorized review;
13. fund-administration discrepancies cannot silently overwrite records;
14. AI employees have explicit fund duties without expanded authority;
15. learning loops propose improvements without silently changing investment policy;
16. all material work remains observable, auditable, cost-visible, and approval-aware.

---

## 21. Truth and Validation Boundary

This section defines planning and governance requirements only.

It does not prove:

- the validity of any fund-size or sleeve assumption;
- the suitability of a 60/40 strategy;
- the performance of any portfolio construction model;
- the correctness of any return calculation;
- legal or regulatory compliance;
- provider integrations;
- Odysseus implementation;
- live data ingestion;
- production alert behavior;
- fund-administrator reconciliation;
- any investment outcome.

All financial calculations, legal conclusions, compliance decisions, provider records, and external materials require the applicable validation and human authority.
